On trade, Trump announced a U.S.-India agreement lowering tariffs on Indian imports from 25% to 18%, contingent on [[india|India]] ceasing purchases of Russian crude and increasing U.S. energy and goods purchases — though New Delhi remained silent and Kremlin spokesman [[dmitry-peskov|Dmitry Peskov]] said Moscow had received no indication of any oil cutoff.[47] U.S. trade partners accelerated alternative arrangements including an India-EU free trade deal and an EU-Mercosur bloc covering over 700 million people, while Trump threatened 100% tariffs on Canada over electric-vehicle policy alignment with China and raised tariffs on South Korea.[47] With the [[new-start-treaty|New START treaty]] set to expire, Trump declined to respond to Russia's September offer of a one-year extension, saying he preferred a new deal including China: "If it expires, it expires. We'll just do a better agreement."[47]

Trump hosted Colombian President [[gustavo-petro|Gustavo Petro]] at the [[white-house|White House]] on February 3 for a two-hour Oval Office meeting attended by [[jd-vance|JD Vance]], [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]], and [[bernie-moreno|Bernie Moreno]], gifting Petro a modified "Make Americas Great Again" hat and a signed copy of *The Art of the Deal*.[47] Commenting on the scheduled Epstein depositions of [[bill-clinton|Bill]] and [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]], Trump called them "a shame," expressed personal liking for Bill Clinton, and praised Hillary Clinton as a "smart woman."[47]

## February 2026: energy, Iran, elections and the border

In February 2026 Trump's administration executed the full repeal of the Obama-era [[endangerment-finding|endangerment finding]], described in Badlands coverage as the largest act of deregulation in American history, eliminating motor vehicle emission standards along with associated compliance programs, credit schemes and reporting obligations.[48] On the same day, reports surfaced that the administration was courting Big Tech firms to sign an [[ai-data-center-compact|AI data center compact]] requiring them to bear the full infrastructure cost of their AI buildout so that household electricity prices, water supplies and grid reliability are not compromised.[48] Badlands contributors read the pairing as a deliberate counter-deployment against a media narrative casting Trump's abundance agenda as the driver of energy scarcity.[48]

Trump met Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] in Washington on February 11 in a closed-door session — their seventh meeting since Trump returned to office — with no joint press conference afterward.[48] On Truth Social Trump reported "nothing definitive reached other than I insisted that negotiations with Iran continue to see whether or not a Deal can be consummated," said a deal would be his preference, and recalled that Iran's earlier refusal ended with it being "hit with Midnight Hammer."[48] He also cited "tremendous progress being made in Gaza, and the Region in general," declaring "there is truly PEACE in the Middle East."[48]

Also that week, anonymous sources told *NBC News* and *Politico* that Trump had instructed US intelligence agencies, including the [[cia|CIA]], to provide 2020 election-related intelligence — specifically material concerning claims of foreign interference — to attorney [[kurt-olsen|Kurt Olsen]], whom he had appointed a special advisor on elections and who became a federal special government employee in October.[48] Intelligence officials reportedly raised concerns about sharing classified information with a private attorney.[48]

On the southern border, the administration confirmed that cartel drones had entered US airspace near El Paso and were disabled by the [[federal-aviation-administration|FAA]] and the [[department-of-war|Department of War]], an episode Badlands linked to Trump's posture toward the Mexican government and his cooperation with Colombian President [[gustavo-petro|Gustavo Petro]] against cartels and their financiers.[48]

## AI accelerationism and the 2026 GOP split

Trump has opposed most artificial intelligence regulation, arguing that US companies must be free to innovate in order to compete globally, particularly with China; his administration issued an executive order in December 2025 seeking to block states from adopting their own AI rules.[49] Politico reported in February 2026 that Florida Governor [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]], Senator [[josh-hawley|Josh Hawley]] and Utah Governor [[spencer-cox|Spencer Cox]] were breaking from that deregulatory approach, while Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] and Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] had publicly aligned with it.[49]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued the outlet was rightly framing Trump as, at his core, an accelerator — and considered that a good thing.[49] In this reading Trump's executive orders on nuclear energy, AI development and supersonic flight point to a consistent [[accelerationism|accelerationist]] stance: preempting a patchwork of state AI regulations creates a unified national framework letting American firms move faster, while the concurrent nuclear push powers the data centers AI requires and brings energy independence home.[49]

## Iran rhetoric and the July 2026 strikes

After the United States and [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]] launched coordinated airstrikes on Iran-backed militia targets in [[iraq|Iraq]], Trump said the US would hit [[iran|Iran]] hard, telling Fox News, "we are going to beat the f*cking shit out of them" if attacks continue, while insisting negotiations with Tehran could still proceed if Iran changes course.[50] The comments came as he met with Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] amid renewed concerns over Iran's nuclear program, and the escalation rattled energy markets as oil prices surged on fears of disruption through the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]].[50]

Badlands writer [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued Trump has no intention of restarting the war, citing dwindling interceptor supplies and the impact of the war on global energy costs, and read the bombast as Trump feeding the hubris of the NeoCon/Zionist spectrum so that its members make fatal errors while believing they can trick him.[50] In this framing Trump's endorsement of Netanyahu ahead of the [[2026-israeli-elections|October 2026 Israeli elections]] traps Netanyahu's rivals, who must attack Trump to campaign effectively.[50]

During the conflict Trump also reduced tariffs and sanctions on Russian oil bound for India and [[china|China]], a step [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described as mirroring [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]]'s silent "move of no move" and as evidence of [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] coordination.[50]

## Pardons and the Fauci question (2026)

Following [[anthony-fauci|Anthony Fauci]]'s refusal to testify at the [[fauci-senate-covid-origins-hearing-2026|Senate Covid origins hearing]], Trump said presidential pardons are very powerful and that Fauci could not be touched aside from the Autopen angle, remarks that generated significant controversy.[50] Separately, a February 2025 executive order signed by Trump had imposed sanctions on the [[international-criminal-court|International Criminal Court]] over its investigations of [[israel|Israel]], a measure cited amid the 2026 [[icc-withdrawal-wave|ICC withdrawal wave]].[50]

## Kentucky primaries, Beijing summitry and the Iran pause (May 2026)

In May 2026 Trump's endorsed challenger [[ed-gallrein|Ed Gallrein]] defeated Rep. [[thomas-massie|Thomas Massie]] in Kentucky's 4th District after Trump campaigned in the state and attacked Massie as "a bad guy" and "an obstructionist and a fool," while his endorsed candidate Rep. [[andy-barr|Andy Barr]] won the GOP Senate nomination — results reported as underscoring his tightening grip on the Republican Party.[51]

The same period saw [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] travel to Beijing to meet [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] directly on the heels of Trump's own Beijing summit, prompting the [[center-for-european-policy-analysis|Center for European Policy Analysis]] to urge his administration to abandon hopes of splitting the Russia–China pair.[51] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] instead read the convergence as granting Trump "the mandate he needs for a new era of American Acceleration," with [[ford-motor-company|Ford]]'s WWII-style mobilization talk and Defense Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]]'s "wartime footing" language as the industrial expression of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] framework.[51]

On [[iran|Iran]], Trump paused planned renewed airstrikes at the request of Gulf allies seeking to revive talks; Israeli analyst [[raz-zimmt|Raz Zimmt]] argued Tehran read the pause as proof that Washington fears renewed war more than Iran does and that Trump's statements are unreliable.[51] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] credited Trump with locking the region into "a perpetual holding pattern that allows neither peace nor war to break out" and concluded that he intends to make peace with Iran.[51] His administration also paused participation in the [[permanent-joint-board-on-defense|Permanent Joint Board on Defense]] with Canada, and drew opposition from New York Governor [[kathy-hochul|Kathy Hochul]], who signed laws decoupling state vaccine policy from federal guidance.[51]

## Iran-Israel war and the MAGA foreign-policy split (March 2026)

In March 2026 Trump publicly intensified his rhetoric around the [[iran-war|Iran-Israel conflict]], signaling support for Israeli military action while warning of broader escalation.[52] The war exposed divisions within Trump-aligned political and media circles over U.S. involvement in foreign conflicts, splitting factions favoring strong U.S.-Israel alignment from those advocating a restrained or non-interventionist reading of "America First."[52] The divide escalated following the resignation of counterterrorism chief [[joe-kent|Joe Kent]] over the U.S. decision to attack [[iran|Iran]] and the strike on the [[south-pars-gas-field|South Pars gas field]].[52] Trump used Truth Social to blame [[israel|Israel]] for the gas field attack, stating it acted "without American coordination," and warned that further attacks on the field would cease unless Iran hit [[qatar|Qatar]] again, threatening an overwhelming U.S. response on his own terms; [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] then confirmed Israel "acted alone."[52]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued Trump "sends cognitive rangers to the outer edges of the Overton window—and follows them partway," comparing the episode to Operation Midnight Hammer, where Trump blessed pro-Israel optics while making clear he alone was calling the shots — what the contributor called Narrative Disarmament.[52] He read Kent's resignation and Trump's simultaneous hardening as a deliberate dialectic rather than a loss of control, noting the second-order effects as Turkey's Hakan Fidan blamed Israel for an "unprecedented crisis" and the fragile Gulf alignments Trump had been forging showed strain, letting Trump adjust his geopolitical stance.[52] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] likewise called the sequence coordinated and predicted Trump will create distance between himself and Netanyahu.[52]

## U.S.-Japan energy summit (March 2026)

Trump met Japanese Prime Minister [[sanae-takaichi|Sanae Takaichi]] at the [[white-house|White House]] for a bilateral summit on energy, security, and economic cooperation.[52] The two countries advanced a nuclear energy initiative including small modular reactor development in the United States estimated around $40 billion, with Japan pledging tens of billions in U.S. energy sector investment covering nuclear, natural gas, and infrastructure, plus cooperation on critical minerals and rare earth supply chains to reduce reliance on [[china|China]].[52] The leaders discussed energy security tied to global instability, including oil supply concerns linked to the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]].[52]

## February 2026: State of the Union, Mar-a-Lago breach and the cartel war

Trump was scheduled to deliver the 2026 State of the Union address to a joint session of [[congress|Congress]] at 9PM ET on February 24, 2026, in what Badlands billed as the first true State of the Union of the Golden Age, coming amid tariff policy disputes and international tensions.[54] Dozens of House and Senate Democrats planned to skip the speech — reportedly including Ed Markey, Chris Van Hollen, Ruben Gallego, Tina Smith and Jeff Merkley — while more than a dozen Democratic congresswomen planned to wear white in protest.[54] The US women’s Olympic ice hockey team declined Trump’s invitation to attend, citing academic and professional commitments following the Milan Games, while the gold-medal-winning US men’s team appeared in a viral video to accept; Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] said leadership would work to accommodate the group despite chamber space limits.[54]

Responding to the Mexican military operation in Jalisco that killed [[el-mencho|Nemesio Oseguera Cervantes]], Trump posted, “Mexico must step up their effort on Cartels and Drugs!”[54] Reporting cited by the Brief said his administration had quietly secured the transfer of roughly 100 cartel leaders from Mexico into US custody before the raid, and Trump affirmed that he had offered to send US troops to fight the cartels but was turned down by Mexican President [[claudia-sheinbaum|Claudia Sheinbaum]].[54]

On February 22, 2026, an armed man breached the perimeter of [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]] and was shot dead by [[us-secret-service|Secret Service]] agents and a sheriff’s deputy; Trump and First Lady [[melania-trump|Melania Trump]] were at the White House at the time.[54] Addressing the incident the next day, Trump said, “I don’t know how long I’ll be around, got a lot of people gunning for me, don’t I, hon?”[54]

In the same period Trump hosted the first [[board-of-peace|Board of Peace]] meeting in Washington, where he indirectly rebuked [[laura-loomer|Laura Loomer]] and [[mark-levin|Mark Levin]] for their attacks on Emir Tamim of [[qatar|Qatar]]; Badlands argued that Israeli figures including [[bezalel-smotrich|Bezalel Smotrich]] and [[ze-ev-elkin|Ze’ev Elkin]] were openly undermining his peace efforts, and predicted no war with [[iran|Iran]] because Trump had given his word to his Arab partners.[54]

## Iran diplomacy and the Netanyahu call (June 2026)

In an interview released June 3, 2026, Trump said [[iran|Iran]] had already agreed in principle not to obtain a nuclear weapon — "They've already agreed they're not gonna have a nuclear weapon" — while stressing that preventing an Iranian bomb remains his top condition for any final agreement.[53] He said Iran's supreme leader is directly involved in approving the talks and that he would "probably" meet him if it helped secure a deal, a shift from months of military escalation toward a more diplomatic posture.[53] Reports indicated talks were complicated by continued Israeli operations in Lebanon and disputes over sanctions and regional security, and no finalized deal was announced by either capital.[53]

Trump and Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] both confirmed a tense phone call over Israel's military actions in Lebanon, with Trump acknowledging frustration at continued strikes and saying he sharply criticized Netanyahu while maintaining that their relationship remains strong.[53] He was reportedly concerned that escalation in Beirut could undermine regional stabilization and the Iran track, highlighting a widening divergence between Washington's push for de-escalation and Jerusalem's focus on pressuring [[hezbollah|Hezbollah]] militarily.[53] Concurrently his administration backed an [[israel-lebanon-ceasefire|Israel-Lebanon ceasefire framework]] requiring Hezbollah to withdraw north of the [[litani-river|Litani River]] in favor of the [[lebanese-armed-forces|Lebanese Armed Forces]].[53]

Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued Trump had run the "Hegelian machine's" own script long enough to expose its authors before "quietly seizing the pen," and that the leader he signaled willingness to meet is [[mojtaba-khamenei|Mojtaba Khamenei]] within a reconfigured Iranian leadership structure — an application of [[chris-paul|Chris Paul]]'s "drafting" concept in the info war.[53] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] called the openness to meeting the Ayatollah the real tell, tying it to Trump's description of the Iran conflict as "The Venezuela Model."[53]

In domestic matters that week, Trump-aligned attorney [[kurt-olsen|Kurt Olsen]] — a White House official who assisted Trump's examination of the 2020 election results — joined the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] to work on the [[grand-conspiracy-probe|grand conspiracy inquiry]] into prior investigations and prosecutions of Trump.[53]

## February 2026

Following his 2026 State of the Union address, Trump criticized Reps. [[ilhan-omar|Ilhan Omar]] and [[rashida-tlaib|Rashida Tlaib]] in a [[truth-social|Truth Social]] post, writing that they had "screamed uncontrollably" during the "very elegant" event, calling them "Crooked and Corrupt Politicians" who should be "sent back from where they came," and extending his attack to the actor Robert De Niro and to Rosie O'Donnell.[55]

A federal judge declined to block construction of Trump's privately funded [[white-house-ballroom|White House ballroom]], and Trump celebrated in a Truth Social post noting that "not one dollar of Taxpayer money" was being spent and that the project was "ahead of schedule, and under budget."[55] In the courts, a Ninth Circuit panel revived his [[executive-order-14251|Executive Order 14251]] on federal collective bargaining, and his [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] expanded its voter-roll litigation to 29 states plus the District of Columbia.[55]

On foreign policy, indirect US–[[iran|Iran]] nuclear negotiations concluded in Geneva without an agreement while the United States deployed two carrier strike groups and more than 100 fighter jets to the Middle East; Reuters cited anonymous US intelligence sources disputing Trump's claim that Iran was close to fielding missiles able to reach the United States.[55] Badlands contributor [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] defended Trump as "the President of peace," crediting him with no new wars in his first term and eight wars ended in his second, and framing the war-scare coverage as PSYOPs designed to delegitimize him.[55]

## March 2026: Iran war messaging and the World Cup

On March 12, 2026, Trump used [[truth-social|Truth Social]] to rebuke the [[new-york-times|New York Times]] over its coverage of the [[iran-war|Iran conflict]], writing that "we are totally destroying the terrorist regime of Iran, militarily, economically, and otherwise" and that readers of the paper would "incorrectly think that we are not winning."[56] He asserted that "Iran's Navy is gone, their Air Force is no longer, missiles, drones and everything else are being decimated, and their leaders have been wiped from the face of the earth," claimed "unparalleled firepower, unlimited ammunition, and plenty of time," and noted that after 47 years of Iranian killings he, "as the 47th President of the United States of America, am killing them."[56]

Earlier the same day he posted that the Iranian national soccer team was "welcome to The World Cup," but that he did not believe it appropriate for them to attend "for their own life and safety," while separately hyping the [[2026-fifa-world-cup|2026 FIFA World Cup]] as "the Greatest and Safest Sporting Event in American History."[56] Badlands writer Ashe in America noted Trump was "on a roll" that Thursday, also attacking Gavin Newsom and Jerome Powell and sharing a photograph of himself with his parents at military academy, and characterized the day's Truth feed as "entirely discombobulating."[56]

Asked the previous week whether [[israel|Israel]] had "forced his hand" on Iran, Trump replied, "I might have forced theirs."[56] Badlands contributor Burning Bright read the sequence as evidence that Trump had been giving pro-war factions the optics they demanded while quietly executing a disentanglement from the conflict.[56]

## June 2026: enriched uranium, the Ayatollah, and Republican defectors

On June 4, 2026 Trump said the United States does not need a formal agreement with [[iran|Iran]] to obtain that country's enriched uranium, asserting that the US could gain access if it chose to but that there is currently "no need" because the material is effectively "entombed" at damaged nuclear sites.[57] He said the United States is closely monitoring the sites and that any recovery operation would be difficult and risky.[57] The remarks marked a notable shift from earlier negotiations, in which the transfer or surrender of Iran's enriched uranium stockpile had been reported as a central element of a potential agreement and a key US objective.[57]

Trump simultaneously struck a conciliatory tone toward Iran's leadership, saying he was not currently seeking a meeting with Supreme Leader Ayatollah [[mojtaba-khamenei|Mojtaba Khamenei]] but would consider one if a deal were reached and would approach it respectfully; he also said Iran had already agreed not to obtain nuclear weapons, describing that commitment as the central issue in the talks.[57] Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] called the pivot "a rebranding exercise at Warp Speed," noting Trump's reply to a question from Fox News correspondent Peter Doocy in which he moved from "I don't want to meet" to saying he would be "honored" to meet.[57]

In the same period Trump criticized a small group of Republican lawmakers who opposed portions of his agenda, calling them "unpatriotic" and "grandstanders" following [[united-states-senate|Senate]] debates over administration-backed legislation.[57] The comments followed votes on spending and executive authority in which several Republicans sided with Democrats, though most congressional Republicans continued to back the administration's major legislative priorities and defections remained limited enough for Trump-backed proposals to advance.[57] Media coverage, including an Atlantic podcast, highlighted Republican officials who had publicly broken with the president on issues ranging from spending to redistricting.[57] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] characterized the phenomenon as an internal breach of the [[america-first-movement|MAGA coalition]] rather than an external assault, tracing it to the 2022 cycle and to figures aligned with the [[uniparty|Uniparty]] architecture.[57]

## March 2026 Iran campaign

In late February 2026 Trump ordered coordinated US and Israeli air and missile strikes on [[iran|Iran]], an operation described in Badlands commentary as [[operation-epic-fury|Operation Epic Fury]], which killed Supreme Leader [[ali-khamenei|Ali Khamenei]] and struck more than 1,000 targets.[58] Trump said the campaign aimed to halt Iran's nuclear and long-range missile capabilities, could last several weeks, and that he remained open to talks with Iranian officials while military pressure continued.[58] Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] said the strikes were preemptive, undertaken because Israel was preparing its own attack that would have precipitated Iranian retaliation against American forces.[58]

Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump had told *The New York Times* he was following the [[venezuela-model|Venezuela Model]] used against [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]] — removing a figurehead while leaving the governing structure intact and open to deals — rather than pursuing the occupation-style regime change favored by neoconservatives.[58] The unease of [[john-bolton|John Bolton]], who warned of "turmoil and bloodshed" and complained of inadequate planning, was cited as evidence that the hawks were not getting the forever war they expected.[58] Trump was also reported to have asked the [[kurds|Kurds]] to assist with the war effort.[58]

## Epstein testimony reference (2026)

In congressional testimony released in March 2026, former President [[bill-clinton|Bill Clinton]] said that Trump had once told him he had "some great times" with [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]].[58] The release coincided with broader congressional debate over executive privilege and subpoena compliance involving Trump and the authority of the House of Representatives to compel testimony.[58]

## Beijing summit with Xi Jinping (May 2026)

The [[white-house|White House]] confirmed in March 2026 that Trump would meet Chinese President [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] in Beijing on May 14 and 15, a summit originally postponed because of the US military operation in [[iran|Iran]].[58] Announcing the trip on [[truth-social|Truth Social]], Trump wrote that his meeting with "the Highly Respected President of China" had been rescheduled and that he and First Lady [[melania-trump|Melania Trump]] would also host Xi and Madame Peng for a reciprocal visit in Washington, D.C., later in the year.[58] Press secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] confirmed the reciprocal visit, with the date still to be determined.[58] Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] noted that the dates fall on the one-year anniversary of Trump's May 13-16 trip to Arabia and wondered whether another Riyadh-style address would follow.[58]

## Remarks on the Ayatollah and the CIA (March 2026)

In an interview on Fox News' "The Five," Trump said the [[cia|CIA]] had told him that Iran's new supreme leader, [[mojtaba-khamenei|Mojtaba Khamenei]], is gay, acknowledging the agency relayed the information while indicating uncertainty and noting that others had made similar claims.[58] He then reminisced about his support among gay voters in the 2024 election, saying, "I did very well with the gay vote. Okay? I even played the Gay National Anthem as my walk-out for my campaign. And I think it probably helped me."[58] GhostofBasedPatrickHenry, observing that Trump had spent weeks questioning whether the Ayatollah was even alive, concluded that "we are being trolled by Trump."[58]

Badlands commentary tied the episode to Trump's broader posture toward [[israel|Israel]], recalling his earlier suggestion that he may have forced Israel into the conflict and noting his announcement of a ten-day ceasefire, in contrast to Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]]'s insistence that Israel would fight on until achieving its objectives - a sequence read as Trump baiting Israel into an untenable military scenario while heading for the exit ramp.[58]

## Ukraine security guarantees (March 2026)

Reporting in March 2026 indicated that the United States had linked long-term security guarantees for [[ukraine|Ukraine]] to concessions on territory and alliances.[58] [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelenskyy]] said that "the Middle East definitely has an impact on President Trump," and that Trump "still chooses a strategy to put more pressure on the Ukrainian side," adding that the Americans were prepared to finalize guarantees once Ukraine was ready to withdraw from Donbas.[58] See [[globalist-fragility|globalist fragility]].[58]

## Iran talks dispute and energy politics (March 2026)

In March 2026 Trump said the United States was engaged in talks with [[iran|Iran]] to end the ongoing conflict, citing recent "productive" discussions and delaying planned strikes on Iranian energy infrastructure for five days; Iran immediately denied that any negotiations were taking place and accused Washington of attempting to influence markets.[59] Oil prices dropped and stocks rose on expectations of de-escalation even as military operations continued.[59] Asked about opening the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]], Trump suggested he and the Ayatollah might control it together, prompting the Iranian government to post a picture of a child's toy steering wheel with his quote and emojis.[59]

Domestically, the [[2026-gasoline-price-surge|gasoline price surge]] brought the national average within a few cents of $4 per gallon, and Trump's administration met directly with oil executives and diplomats to coordinate higher domestic output while Energy Secretary [[chris-wright|Chris Wright]] urged immediate production increases at CERAWeek in Houston.[59] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed this as a return to Trump's "Drill, Baby, Drill" campaign promise and as the first stage of a longer arc toward sovereign energy independence, noting that Trump's sanctions relief on trapped Russian energy combined with the Iran crisis was delivering billions in additional revenue to [[vladimir-putin|Putin]]'s Russia.[59]

## Election integrity and the 2026 midterms

In May 2026 Trump announced that Republicans would field an expanded [[election-integrity-army|Election Integrity Army]] for the [[2026-midterm-elections|2026 midterm elections]], writing that the party had deployed such a network in every state during his 2024 victory and would do so again "much bigger and stronger," and assuring Americans that "this Election will be fair."[61] He framed the effort as a response to a Democrat-led "Election Integrity Group" he said [[chuck-schumer|Chuck Schumer]] was assembling with [[eric-holder|Eric Holder]] and [[marc-elias|Marc Elias]], accusing it of preparing to suppress Republican voters.[61] Badlands commentator [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] rejected the assurance, arguing that a fair election cannot be run on the same systems and that the administration itself knows how fake past elections have been.[61]

## Beijing summit and gas tax (May 2026)

Trump traveled to China in May 2026 for a summit with [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] framed by US media as legacy-defining, accompanied by a large delegation of American industrial and financial executives and focused on trade, investment, [[iran|Iran]], AI coordination, and [[taiwan|Taiwan]]; the trip is covered at [[2026-trump-xi-beijing-summit|the 2026 Trump–Xi Beijing summit]].[61] In the same period he told CBS News that he planned a temporary suspension of the federal gasoline tax to be phased back in "when gas goes down," and said Iran's response to the US peace proposal had included concessions on its nuclear program but was still a "very stupid proposal" that fell short.[61] He was also the target of the alleged attack underlying [[whca-dinner-assassination-attempt-prosecution|the WHCA dinner assassination attempt prosecution]], in which the defendant pleaded not guilty in May 2026.[61]

## May 2026: clemency advocacy, Gulf warning, Beijing and Nigeria

Trump repeatedly called for the release of former Mesa County Clerk [[tina-peters|Tina Peters]] and claimed in 2025 that he had pardoned her, despite state officials noting that presidential pardons do not apply to state convictions; her case became a national rallying point among his supporters before Colorado Governor [[jared-polis|Jared Polis]] commuted the sentence in May 2026.[62]

Amid stalled negotiations tied to the [[iran-war|Iran conflict]] and drone attacks on the [[united-arab-emirates|United Arab Emirates]] and [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]], Trump warned Iran in a social media post that "the clock is ticking."[62] Days earlier he had concluded a state visit to [[china|China]] that included meetings with [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] on trade, Taiwan and the Iran conflict; Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] was scheduled to arrive in Beijing on 19–20 May, a sequence Chinese state media called unusual and indicative of Beijing's growing diplomatic influence.[62] Badlands commentator [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the pairing as confirmation of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] thesis and the [[power-paradigm|Power Paradigm]], arguing that the longer the "sovereign flirtation" between Trump, Xi and Putin continued, the more pressure built on the globalist system and the more room the [[multipolar-world|Multipolar World]] had to form.[62]

Trump also announced, alongside Nigerian President [[bola-tinubu|Bola Tinubu]], that the Islamic State figure [[abu-bilal-al-manuki|Abu-Bilal Al-Manuki]] had been killed in northeastern [[nigeria|Nigeria]] in a joint operation coordinated with U.S. [[africom|Africa Command]].[62] Separately, his administration ran at least 67 million voter registrations through the [[save-program|SAVE program]] operated by the [[department-of-homeland-security|Department of Homeland Security]].[62]

## Ankara NATO summit (July 2026)

Trump arrived at the [[nato|NATO]] summit in Ankara still seething over European allies' refusal to support the recent U.S.-led military campaign against [[iran|Iran]].[61] He repeatedly expressed disappointment that allies had declined to open air bases for strikes or contribute forces to help reopen the Strait of Hormuz, casting their hesitation as disloyalty despite decades of American protection for Europe.[61] He posted memes targeting Italian Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni, dismissed U.K. Prime Minister Keir Starmer as weak, and brushed off Secretary-General [[mark-rutte|Mark Rutte]]'s praise for increased allied spending, emphasizing that the United States does not need Europe's money or assistance and declaring, "I just want loyalty," while refusing to rule out further reductions in the U.S. military footprint in Europe.[61]

The [[pentagon|Pentagon]] had already scaled back some U.S. forces in the region and launched a six-month review of its presence, with Defense Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]] calling allies' refusal to provide base access "shameful."[61] Trump and Turkish President [[recep-tayyip-erdogan|Recep Tayyip Erdoğan]] exchanged warm compliments ahead of the summit, and Trump said the only reason he attended was that it was hosted by Erdoğan.[61] Badlands commentary read the visit as evidence that Trump is prioritizing [[turkey|Turkey]] over Europe and as proof of a "Sovereign Alliance" supplanting unipolar hegemony with a multipolar order.[61]

Rutte separately credited Trump with driving the alliance's burden-sharing shift, saying what the president is doing for NATO is "great news."[61] In domestic matters, proponents of the [[lab-leak-theory|lab-leak theory]] within the [[trump-administration|Trump administration]] pushed the 2026 legal actions against NIAID-affiliated scientists as accountability for COVID-19 origins.[61] [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] described his relationship with Trump as exceptionally strong while acknowledging occasional disagreements, such as over potential U.S. arms sales to Turkey.[61]

## April 2026: Iran re-escalation and the Lebanon prohibition

On April 19, 2026, following the [[mv-touska-seizure|M/V Touska seizure]] by [[us-central-command|U.S. Central Command]], Trump's earlier attempt to mandate calm in the [[iran|Iran]] conflict appeared overtaken by events; he had said U.S. negotiators would head to [[pakistan|Pakistan]] on Monday, but after the ship seizure it was unclear where those talks stood.[64] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump remained the only major actor on the board wholly opposed to the war the escalation narratives were designed to make appear inevitable, and framed the interpretive test as whether Trump's saber-rattling constitutes provoking a deployment or countering one — searching for an on-ramp or an off-ramp.[64]

On [[lebanon|Lebanon]], Trump posted to [[truth-social|Truth Social]]: "Israel will not be bombing Lebanon any longer. They are PROHIBITED from doing so by the U.S.A. Enough is enough!"[64] Axios reporter Barak Ravid reported that [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] and his advisers were surprised by the remarks, which were not expected in Jerusalem, and that Israel's ambassador to the US scrambled for clarification.[64] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read the pattern as Trump diffusing the [[greater-israel|Greater Israel]] project before it could become the regional hegemon, and characterized Trump as waging a war against Israel using Iran as his proxy, while Finance Minister [[bezalel-smotrich|Bezalel Smotrich]]'s call to resettle Gaza ran directly against Trump and the [[board-of-peace|Board of Peace]].[64]

## April 2026: NATO exit threat, citizenship order and Artemis II

In remarks reported from an April 1, 2026 interview with [[the-telegraph|The Telegraph]], Trump said he was "strongly considering" withdrawing the United States from [[nato|NATO]], tying the threat to allies' refusal to back US military action and maritime operations connected to the [[iran-war|Iran war]], including pressure around the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]].[65] He had said on March 27 that the United States does not "have to be there for NATO," and described the alliance as a "paper tiger"; on March 31 Defense Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]] declined to reaffirm the US commitment to collective defense, saying the decision was Trump's.[65] The administration simultaneously promoted a "sovereign nations" framework — reflected in the White House summary of [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]]'s February Munich speech and in Trump's January 7 memorandum withdrawing from international organizations judged contrary to US interests.[65] Badlands commentators read the sequence through the [[sovereign-pincer|Sovereign Pincer]] frame of coordinated convergence with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] against the [[invisible-enemy|Invisible Enemy]].[65]

The same week, the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] heard [[trump-v-barbara|Trump v. Barbara]], the challenge to Trump's executive order limiting [[birthright-citizenship|birthright citizenship]] under the [[fourteenth-amendment|Fourteenth Amendment]].[65] Trump also issued a new executive order on elections addressing citizenship verification and voter eligibility in federal elections.[65]

On the evening of April 1, Trump marked the launch of [[artemis-ii|Artemis II]] with a statement declaring that "for the first time in over 50 YEARS, America is going back to the Moon," praising [[nasa|NASA]] and the astronauts and asserting American dominance "Economically, Militarily, and now, BEYOND THE STARS."[65] Separately, [[cuba|Cuba]] signalled willingness to negotiate an economic deal with his administration in talks led by Rubio.[65]

## July 2026: election declassification, Netanyahu freeze, and the Brazil tariffs
On July 16, 2026 the [[white-house|White House]] launched the [[white-house-election-integrity-portal|White House Election Integrity Portal]], and Trump announced the underlying declassification effort during a televised primetime address that network television refused to air.[66] He argued the released documents demonstrated a broader pattern of intelligence suppression surrounding election security, describing the moment in terms of a crisis worse than Pearl Harbor and 9/11 and declaring that "the time for empty talk is over. Now arrives the hour of action."[66] Critics disputed the interpretation, arguing the material does not establish that foreign actors altered vote totals or changed the outcome of the 2020 election.[66]

The same week, senior White House officials told Axios that no meeting had been scheduled with Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]], with one official describing Israeli reports as an effort to "will the meeting into existence."[66] Badlands commentary held that Trump had been baiting Netanyahu into an indefensible position, and recounted how Trump had previously rolled out the red carpet for Turkish President [[recep-tayyip-erdogan|Recep Tayyip Erdogan]] while giving Netanyahu a cold reception, signing a nuclear trade deal with Turkey the day before Netanyahu spoke at the UN and unveiling the 20-point Gaza Peace Plan during his visit.[66]

Trump also directed U.S. Trade Representative [[jamieson-greer|Jamieson Greer]] to impose a 25% tariff on certain Brazilian imports under [[section-301-of-the-trade-act-of-1974|Section 301 of the Trade Act of 1974]] following a yearlong investigation into unfair trade practices.[66]

## January 2026: Minneapolis, Davos, Venezuela and Canada

Trump used his Truth Social platform to support federal agents after the January 24, 2026 fatal shooting of U.S. citizen [[alex-jeffrey-pretti|Alex Jeffrey Pretti]] in Minneapolis, questioning the absence of local police involvement and accusing Minnesota officials of "inciting insurrection," while linking the unrest to alleged "theft and fraud" involving billions of dollars tied to state officials.[67] The episode intensified the national argument over [[sanctuary-cities|sanctuary jurisdictions]], with Badlands commentary invoking Trump's campaign-trail comparison of his four indictments to Al Capone's single one.[67]

At the [[world-economic-forum|World Economic Forum]] the same month, Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] and [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] took the lead in defending Trump's aggressive trade and [[greenland|Greenland]] policy, with Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] describing them as "two lions" Trump brought into "the den of vipers" and as instruments of an accelerating Golden Age.[67] Trump announced a "framework of a future deal" with [[nato|NATO]] Secretary General [[mark-rutte|Mark Rutte]] that temporarily paused the tariff actions rattling global markets.[67]

In a January 25 interview Trump said U.S. forces had used a classified device he called the [[discombobulator|"Discombobulator"]] to disable Venezuelan defenses during the January 3 raid that captured [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]] and [[cilia-flores|Cilia Flores]], claiming it prevented the launch of Russian and Chinese missiles.[67] He enlisted commodity traders [[vitol|Vitol]] and [[trafigura|Trafigura]] to broker sales of Venezuelan oil, and had previously dismissed opposition leader [[maria-corina-machado|María Corina Machado]]'s prospects as leader even as the [[house-foreign-affairs-committee|House Foreign Affairs Committee]] endorsed her.[67] He also mused on social media about invoking NATO's Article Five to compel allied troops to help secure the U.S. southern border.[67]

Toward Canada, Trump threatened a 100 percent tariff on Canadian imports if Prime Minister [[mark-carney|Mark Carney]] finalized a free trade agreement with China, characterizing Ottawa's growing economic ties with Beijing as a strategic concern.[67] Separately, the administration formally invited Italy to become a founding member of the [[international-stabilization-force|International Stabilization Force]] for Gaza, while Prime Minister [[giorgia-meloni|Giorgia Meloni]] asked Trump to amend the governance terms of the [[board-of-peace|Board of Peace]].[67]

## June 2026: pressuring Netanyahu, purging the IC, walking out on Welker

In June 2026 Trump urged Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] not to retaliate against [[iran|Iran]] following an Iranian missile attack, according to [[axios|Axios]] reporting citing a senior U.S. official and an Israeli source.[68] He told the *Financial Times* that Netanyahu would have "no choice" but to accept whatever agreement Washington negotiates with Tehran, saying: "He won't have any choice. I call the shots. I call all the shots. He doesn't call the shots."[68] Trump said Iran's strikes had not changed his desire to conclude US-Iran negotiations — "It's not going to have any impact on the deal" — and dismissed the exchange as part of a conflict "going for 3,000 years, or 47 years, depending on how you count."[68] Badlands read the sequence as game theory: Trump baiting Netanyahu into standing alone during Israeli election season, then negotiating peace with Iran and exiting the stage.[68]

On June 4 Trump said the United States does not need a formal agreement with Iran to obtain the country's enriched uranium, asserting the US could access it if it chose but that there was "no need" because the material is effectively "entombed" at damaged nuclear sites, which the US is closely monitoring.[68] The remarks marked a shift from earlier negotiations in which surrendering the enriched uranium stockpile had been reported as a central US objective.[68] He struck a conciliatory tone toward Iran's leadership, saying he was not currently seeking a meeting with Supreme Leader [[mojtaba-khamenei|Ayatollah Mojtaba Khamenei]] but would consider a respectful one if a deal were reached, and stating that Iran had already agreed not to obtain nuclear weapons.[68]

Trump also told *The Wall Street Journal* that he had privately instructed acting Director of National Intelligence [[william-pulte|Bill Pulte]] to "start the process" of cutting personnel at the [[office-of-the-director-of-national-intelligence|Office of the Director of National Intelligence]], which he called "unnecessary and/or too big."[68] *Reuters* quoted him saying, "I'd like to see it smaller. I think there are a lot of people in there that shouldn't be there."[68] Pulte had been named acting DNI after the announced June 30 departure of [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]], and Trump indicated he would not nominate him permanently.[68]

Separately, Trump walked out of a *Meet the Press* interview with [[kristen-welker|Kristen Welker]] after a heated exchange over his claim that American elections are fake, a viral moment that [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] defended as both true and justified.[68]

## April 2026: ceasefire extension and Defense Production Act

Trump unilaterally extended a fragile US–Iran ceasefire due to expire on April 22, 2026, after a second round of [[pakistan|Pakistan]]-mediated talks in Islamabad collapsed over the [[naval-blockade-of-iran|naval blockade]] of Iranian ports and the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]].[69] He kept the blockade active, had U.S. forces board a sanctioned Iranian-linked tanker, warned of renewed bombing absent a deal, accused [[iran|Iran]] of multiple violations, and urged Tehran to release several women reportedly on death row as a goodwill gesture; Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] canceled a planned trip.[69] The extension followed a direct request from Field Marshal [[asim-munir|Asim Munir]] and Prime Minister [[shehbaz-sharif|Shehbaz Sharif]], relayed in a Truth Social post on April 21, 2026.[69]

Separately, on April 20, 2026, Trump invoked the [[defense-production-act|Defense Production Act]] and signed five presidential determinations directing the Energy Department to support coal-fired power, liquefied natural gas, petroleum refining, and grid infrastructure, aiming to overcome financing, regulatory, and supply-chain barriers amid AI-driven electricity demand and Iran-related price pressure.[69] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the sequence as the deliberate execution of an energy strategy rather than the capitulation implied by the "[[taco-tuesday|Trump Always Chickens Out]]" refrain.[69]

## June 2026: Iran deal and the G7 summit

Trump arrived in Evian-les-Bains, France, on Monday, June 15, 2026, for the [[2026-g7-summit|G7 summit]], having just announced a framework agreement aimed at ending the recent conflict with [[iran|Iran]].[70] He announced that the peace agreement with Iran was complete, describing it as bringing "Peace and Security" to the region and saying it included reopening the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]], removal of the US naval blockade, and mine-removal operations; ships loaded with oil had already begun moving through the Strait, which he called "totally safe, secure, and pristine."[70] The [[white-house|White House]] said Trump, Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]], and Iranian Parliament Speaker [[mohammad-bagher-ghalibaf|Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf]] digitally signed the agreement on Sunday, with an official ceremony scheduled for June 19 in Switzerland.[70]

At the summit Trump said that he and Iran "get along very well," and in a meeting with [[emmanuel-macron|Emmanuel Macron]] said he wanted to talk to [[hezbollah|Hezbollah]] to resolve the conflict in Lebanon.[70] American Jewish organizations and Israeli commentators expressed alarm at the deal's undisclosed terms, while Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] avoided direct criticism.[70] Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued the vagueness of the agreement was by design, since nobody knowing the exact terms makes effective media criticism impossible, and compared Trump's warm rhetoric toward Tehran to his earlier pivot on Venezuela.[70]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the summit as evidence that Trump's true target is globalism itself rather than the proxies made of Iran, Venezuela, Ukraine, Cuba, or Taiwan, and read his earlier framing of a [[g2|"G2"]] with [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] as an acknowledgment of a pincer closing on the post-war order.[70] Meanwhile a docket of [[weaponization-of-government|weaponization]] cases at the [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]] - the [[james-comey|Comey]] prosecution, the [[carter-page|Carter Page]] denial, the [[gavin-newsom|Newsom]] probe, and the [[doj-school-board-memo-2021|2021 school board memo]] disclosures - advanced during the same week.[70]

## April 2026: Germany, Putin call, and Iran pressure

In late April 2026 Trump indicated the United States could reconsider its military presence in [[germany|Germany]] following criticism of US policy in the [[iran-war|Iran conflict]] by Chancellor [[friedrich-merz|Friedrich Merz]], saying continued deployments depend on allied support and burden-sharing and reiterating long-standing concerns about the value of US commitments within [[nato|NATO]].[71]

His administration escalated economic pressure on [[iran|Iran]], with the [[us-department-of-the-treasury|Treasury Department]] under [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] sanctioning 35 individuals and entities tied to Iran's shadow banking network under [[operation-economic-fury|Operation Economic Fury]], alongside the [[naval-blockade-of-iran|naval blockade]] begun that month.[71] Trump rejected Iran-backed diplomatic proposals and insisted nuclear issues be addressed immediately as a condition for negotiations.[71]

On 29 April 2026 Trump held a roughly 90-minute phone call with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]], described by both sides as "productive" and "businesslike," covering both the Ukraine war and the Iran conflict.[71] Putin proposed a temporary Ukraine ceasefire tied to Russia's May 9 Victory Day celebrations and offered assistance on Iran's nuclear program, while Trump said he encouraged Putin to end the Ukraine war first.[71]

Separately, acting Attorney General [[todd-blanche|Todd Blanche]] stated that Trump had no role in directing or initiating the prosecution of [[james-comey|James Comey]], saying the matter was handled independently through the [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]].[71]

## Iran gambit and the Saudi PIF summit (2026)

Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump's "Iranian Gambit" was emboldening a New Axis that he considered the real allies of the United States, and that the [[global-energy-war|Energy War]] was not about [[iran|Iran]] but about what Iran represents.[72] In his reading, Trump and the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] — including Russia, China and elements within Iran — were executing a Sovereign Disentanglement operation against a Globalist Hegemon exposed as powerless by the proxy conflict, with the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]] shocks functioning as the "problem" phase of a Reverse Hegelian Dialectic whose synthesis was a tri-polar energy renaissance.[72] He described the Iranian Knot as a snare Trump laid for the Hegemon rather than one set for him, and characterized the operation as the inversion of the [[kobayashi-maru|Kobayashi Maru]] and as Narrative Disarmament cloaked in heightened tensions.[72]

[[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] speculated that Trump might actually be waging war on [[israel|Israel]] using Iran as a proxy, noting that Trump's public commentary never mentions Israeli hardships and fixates on Iran, and wondering whether some of his posts obliquely refer to Israel.[72] He also noted Trump's optimism about a possible peace deal, joking that it was considerate of him to wrap the conflict up before [[gart-nashville|GART Nashville]].[72]

In February 2026 Trump delivered remarks at the [[future-investment-initiative|Future Investment Initiative]] Priority Summit in Miami — an event affiliated with Saudi Arabia's [[public-investment-fund|Public Investment Fund]] — addressing global investors, policymakers and business leaders, discussing economic policy and emphasizing the United States as a destination for international investment.[72] It was the second consecutive year he attended and spoke, and the address resembled his Riyadh speech of the prior year in touting Gulf Arab states' diversification into technology, infrastructure, tourism and the U.S. economy.[72] Reporting noted scrutiny in Florida of connections between U.S. political figures and foreign-backed investment initiatives.[72] Viral clips showed Trump inviting the audience to ask him about "anything" and teasing Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] — remarks Badlands read as a sign of close friendship rather than insult.[72]

## March 2026: Iran ultimatum, Mueller's death and the Japan summit

On March 22, 2026, Trump threatened to strike [[iran|Iran]]'s power plants unless Tehran fully reopened the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]] within 48 hours, escalating a conflict that had begun on February 28 and entered its fourth week by March 23.[73] Iran's Revolutionary Guards replied that the strait would be "completely" closed if the United States followed through, and Iranian officials warned that energy infrastructure in countries hosting U.S. military bases would become legitimate targets.[73] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the episode in his [[the-iranian-knot|Iranian Knot]] analysis as Trump deliberately allowing chaos to expose the fragility of the Western protectorate, advancing Sovereign Disentanglement and the revival of [[pax-americana|Pax Americana]].[73]

Asked in the Oval Office about a New York Post report that he had been briefed that Iran's new supreme leader [[mojtaba-khamenei|Mojtaba Khamenei]] was gay, Trump replied "Well, nobody knows…" while appearing to hold back laughter, an exchange Badlands treated as evidence of media credulity.[73]

After the March 20, 2026 death of former FBI director and special counsel [[robert-mueller|Robert Mueller]], accessible reports said Trump posted on [[truth-social|Truth Social]], "Good, I'm glad he's dead."[73]

At a March 2026 [[white-house|White House]] meeting with Japanese Prime Minister [[sanae-takaichi|Sanae Takaichi]], Trump drew criticism for invoking [[japan|Japan]]'s role in the 1941 attack on Pearl Harbor while defending his decision not to notify allies ahead of U.S. military actions against Iran.[73] The same meeting produced the [[us-japan-energy-and-nuclear-agreement-2026|U.S.-Japan energy and nuclear package]], with Trump announcing $40 billion in Japanese investment in small modular reactors in Tennessee and Alabama and $33 billion for natural gas generation in Pennsylvania and Texas.[73]

## Leaked Jack Smith report portion and the multipolar summit (2026)

In May 2026 former federal prosecutor [[carmen-lineberger|Carmen Lineberger]] was indicted for allegedly emailing herself a sealed, unreleased portion of Special Counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]]'s report on the classified-documents investigation into Trump, disguising the file under names such as "Bundt_Cake_Recipe.pdf."[74] The indictment surfaced alongside a referral of former Deputy Attorney General [[lisa-monaco|Lisa Monaco]], whom Badlands commentary described as an alleged architect of the campaign against Trump during his first administration.[74]

Trump's proposed [[golden-dome|Golden Dome]] missile-defense system was singled out for criticism in the joint declaration signed by [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] in Beijing on May 20, 2026, which called for a "[[multipolar-world|multipolar world]]" and came just days after Trump's own visit to the Chinese capital.[74] Badlands contributor Burning Bright read the sequence as public confirmation of the theorized [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] between Trump's America, Xi's China, and Putin's Russia.[74]

## February 2026 Truth Social statements

Following the [[house-judiciary-committee|House Judiciary Committee]] hearing with Attorney General [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]] on the [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]] matter, Trump posted on Truth Social that Bondi "was fantastic" under "intense fire from the Trump Deranged Radical Left Lunatics," and claimed the hearing proved he had been "100% exonerated" of what he called "ridiculous Russia, Russia, Russia type charges."[75] He asserted that "SLIMEBALL Democrats, many of them big Donors and Politicians," had been "proven GUILTY," attacked Representative [[thomas-massie|Thomas Massie]] as a "Sanctimonious RINO" trailing challenger [[ed-gallrein|Ed Gallrein]] in Kentucky polls, and said "nobody cared about Epstein when he was alive."[75] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the post as Trump cutting through "Epsteinian noise" to expose distraction and division as the operation's true purpose.[75]

In a separate post the same week, Trump described relations with [[venezuela|Venezuela]] as "extraordinary," said Washington was "dealing very well with President [[delcy-rodriguez|Delcy Rodriguez]], and her Representatives," and reported that "oil is starting to flow" with "large amounts of money, unseen for many years" soon to help the Venezuelan people.[75] He praised [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] and other representatives while stressing that businessman [[harry-sargeant-iii|Harry Sargeant III]], the subject of a Wall Street Journal story, had "no authority, in any way, shape, or form, to act on behalf of the United States," as no one unapproved by the [[state-department|State Department]] may represent the country.[75] Energy Secretary [[chris-wright|Chris Wright]] toured Venezuelan oil fields the same day.[75] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] wryly observed that "all Venezuela needed was to get discombobulated to be set straight," noting the silence of pro-war commentators.[75]

## Netanyahu meeting and the Herzog pardon dispute (February 2026)

Trump hosted Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] for a closed-door Washington meeting focused on [[iran|Iran]]'s nuclear program, after which no agreement, joint framework, or policy shift was announced.[75] Netanyahu said Trump believed the Iranians "already understand who they are dealing with" and that the conditions he was setting could lead to a good deal.[75] Trump separately attacked Israeli President [[isaac-herzog|Isaac Herzog]] for refusing to pardon Netanyahu, saying "that man should be ashamed of himself ... he's disgraceful."[75] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued the pressure campaign generated leverage for Trump regardless of Herzog's decision, corralling Netanyahu like a cornered animal.[75] The same week the [[uss-gerald-r-ford|USS Gerald R. Ford]] carrier strike group was repositioned from Venezuela to the Middle East.[75]

## Minnesota surge conclusion (February 2026)

Border Czar [[tom-homan|Tom Homan]] announced the end of [[operation-metro-surge|Operation Metro Surge]] in [[minnesota|Minnesota]], saying he had proposed concluding the surge and that Trump concurred, and thanking the president for "ongoing leadership and commitment to ensuring law and order, public safety, and prosperity."[75]

## Pulte appointment and the Iran talks "fake news" (June 2026)

In early June 2026 Trump appointed [[william-pulte|William J. Pulte]] Acting Director of National Intelligence, allowing him to continue serving as director of the [[federal-housing-finance-agency|Federal Housing Finance Agency]] and chairman of [[fannie-mae|Fannie Mae]] and [[freddie-mac|Freddie Mac]]; Trump praised Pulte's experience overseeing some of the nation's most sensitive financial matters, including the safety and soundness of U.S. housing markets.[76]

The same period saw Trump publicly dismiss media reports that talks between the United States and [[iran|Iran]] had stalled as fake news.[76] Badlands writer [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued that Trump had "tricked the MSM into questioning whether any of this is real" and expected the dynamic to erode public trust in every outlet until no news organization is treated as a reliable narrator.[76]

## Late July 2026 developments

A federal appeals court blocked key portions of Trump's executive order on election administration in 23 Democrat-led states, ruling that the administration could not enforce several provisions — including proof-of-citizenship measures and restrictions affecting mail voting — while litigation continued, and setting up a likely [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] fight over presidential authority in federal elections.[77] The order had directed the [[department-of-homeland-security|Department of Homeland Security]] to compile lists of confirmed citizens eligible to vote, instructed the [[united-states-postal-service|Postal Service]] to establish new standards for mail-in ballots, and told the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] to prioritize investigations of election officials issuing federal ballots to ineligible people.[77]

In the dispute over [[open-source-artificial-intelligence|open-source artificial intelligence]], Badlands commentary held that Trump has pinned a substantial part of his futurism goals on the acceleration of AI, and that his administration had signaled alignment with the open-source camp through remarks by Commerce Secretary [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] and former AI and Crypto Czar [[david-sacks|David Sacks]].[77] China's strategically timed open-weight model releases were described as granting Trump the narrative shielding required to resist the regulation push, prompting the observation that "Trump and [[xi-jinping|Xi]] are Sovereign Bros."[77]

On Israel, Trump praised [[turkey|Turkey]] as a "tremendous ally" while publicly criticizing Israeli military operations during a meeting with [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]], a juxtaposition Badlands writer [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read as a deliberate signal repeating the September 2025 pattern of hosting [[recep-tayyip-erdogan|Erdoğan]] ahead of Netanyahu's UN address.[77] On Mexico, the administration indicted ten Mexican officials over alleged [[sinaloa-cartel|Sinaloa cartel]] ties amid tension with President [[claudia-sheinbaum|Claudia Sheinbaum]], while media coverage framed the anti-cartel campaign as a prelude to possible military action inside [[mexico|Mexico]].[77]

## February 2026: alien declassification, the Board of Peace and Iran

Asked aboard Air Force One by [[fox-news|Fox News]] correspondent [[peter-doocy|Peter Doocy]] whether he had seen evidence of non-human visitors to Earth, Trump deflected onto [[barack-obama|Barack Obama]]'s handling of classified material, saying "He made a big mistake. He took it out of classified information," before adding, "Maybe I'll get him out of trouble by declassifying."[78] Hours later he posted on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that he would direct the Secretary of War and other departments and agencies to identify and release government files on alien and extraterrestrial life, UAP and UFOs, a move treated in Badlands commentary as the [[alien-disclosure-op|Alien Disclosure Op]].[78]

On February 19, 2026, Trump convened the first meeting of the [[board-of-peace|Board of Peace]] in Washington, telling attendees, "What we're doing is very simple: peace."[78] He pledged $10 billion from the United States toward Gaza while [[qatar|Qatar]], [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]] and the [[united-arab-emirates|United Arab Emirates]] each committed at least $1 billion, for roughly $17 billion in total from more than 40 participating countries, and announced plans for a southern Gaza base housing 5,000 personnel of the [[international-stabilization-force|International Stabilization Force]].[78] Britain and Germany sent ambassadors as observers while Canada and France declined to attend.[78]

On [[iran|Iran]], Trump said, "Now we may have to take it a step further, or we may not… You're going to be finding out over the next, probably, 10 days," as an assembled armada of two aircraft carriers, a dozen warships, hundreds of jets and advanced air defenses gathered in the region, with over 150 US military cargo flights delivering weapons that month in what some described as approaching Iraq War levels.[78] He also called [[cuba|Cuba]] "a failed nation" that lacks jet fuel for its aircraft.[78]

Separately, Trump announced $10 million through the [[economic-development-administration|Economic Development Administration]] for East Palestine, Ohio's recovery from the 2023 [[east-palestine-train-derailment|train derailment]], writing that its residents "were disgracefully betrayed and forgotten about by Crooked Joe Biden" and pledging support "for the LONG HAUL."[78]

## March 2026: "practically nothing left to target"

In a five-minute telephone interview with Axios published on March 11, 2026, Trump said the war with [[iran|Iran]] would end "soon" and that there was "practically nothing left" to target, adding, "Any time I want it to end, it will end."[79] He described the campaign as "way ahead of the timetable" and said US and allied forces had "done more damage than we thought possible, even in the original six-week period."[79]

Trump's framing stood in open conflict with [[israel|Israeli]] Defence Minister [[israel-katz|Israel Katz]], who said the operation would continue "without any time limit," a divergence Badlands writers treated as the most consequential signal of the week.[79]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the interview as a deliberate "pre-emptive strike into the heart of the central narrative," arguing Trump operates on two levels at once — a surface read that "screams escalation" over a subtext that "whispers de-escalation."[79] In this reading, feeding de-escalation lines to legacy outlets as exclusives traps the [[media-industrial-complex|media industrial complex]] in a contradictory feedback loop, forcing it to amplify rhetoric that starves its own "Forever War" script of oxygen — a technique the piece called "the discombobulator."[79] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] speculated that Trump and Iranian leadership may already have reached an understanding and were jointly exposing NeoCon and Zionist influence over Western governments, likening the US–Israel split to an older brother leaving an arrogant younger brother to face the consequences of his own bullying.[79]

## SAVE America Act and the 2026 midterms

Trump told lawmakers in March 2026 that the [[save-america-act|SAVE America Act]] was a top priority ahead of the [[2026-midterm-elections|2026 midterms]], urging Senate Republicans to pass legislation requiring documentary proof of US citizenship to register to vote in federal elections and directing states to purge non-citizens from voter rolls.[79]

## Demand for James–Cohen communications

Also in March 2026, Trump demanded that New York Attorney General [[letitia-james|Letitia James]] turn over all communications between her office and his former attorney [[michael-cohen|Michael Cohen]], after Cohen claimed he had been coerced into providing damaging statements about Trump.[79] The demand formed part of Trump's continuing challenge to the New York civil fraud judgment against him and his business, a case Badlands commentary characterized as weaponized justice and the substance of his 34-felony conviction.[79]

## Response to Lindsey Graham's death (July 2026)

Following the announcement that Senator [[lindsey-graham|Lindsey Graham]] had died on July 11, 2026, Trump issued a public tribute.[80] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described the statement as succinct and broadly respectful yet tonally odd for a supposed close friend and ally, observing that it opened by announcing bluntly that Graham was dead before the kind words followed.[80] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] noted that Trump made several further posts about the death which were widely shared, and argued that many readers missed their tongue-in-cheek nature.[80]

Earlier the same week, on July 9, Trump removed the final three members of the U.S. [[election-assistance-commission|Election Assistance Commission]], leaving the four-seat bipartisan agency with no sitting commissioners; a [[white-house|White House]] official said he had authority to remove officials not aligned with the administration's election-security priorities, citing [[trump-v-slaughter|*Trump v. Slaughter*]].[80]

## Victory Day, hantavirus and UAP disclosure (May 2026)

In May 2026 Trump celebrated a mutual "Victory Day" with both Russia and Ukraine, a convergence [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read — alongside another Trump–[[vladimir-putin|Putin]] phone call conflating the Ukrainian and Iranian proxies — as proof of a closing geopolitical "pincer" with references to timelines and deals possibly already done.[81] Days earlier, on May 7, Trump said he had been briefed on the [[hantavirus-outbreak-2026|hantavirus cluster]] aboard the M/V Hondius and declared the situation under control while ruling out any renewed lockdown, a response [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] praised for "sparking outrage" simply by refusing another round of restrictions.[81] On May 8 his administration launched the [[pursue|PURSUE]] UAP disclosure portal through the [[department-of-war|Department of War]], to which Trump responded "Have fun."[81] Separately, [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] recounted on [[60-minutes|60 Minutes]] that the first thing Trump said to him when Bibi visited presidential candidates in 2016 and 2024 was that "Iran will never have a nuclear weapon."[81]

## June 2026: Lebanon rupture with Netanyahu and the Iran endgame
In a reported June 1, 2026 phone call, Trump delivered an expletive-filled rebuke to Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]], calling him "crazy," demanding "What the f*** are you doing?" and blaming [[israel|Israel]]'s actions in [[lebanon|Lebanon]] for increasing international hostility toward both Netanyahu and Israel.[82] According to [[axios|Axios]], Trump intervened directly to stop a planned Israeli strike on Beirut, arguing further escalation would undermine regional diplomacy and isolate Israel; Israeli officials subsequently confirmed the Beirut operation was shelved.[82] Trump later posted that he had held a "very productive" call with Netanyahu, that Israeli troops would not enter Beirut, that forces already en route had been turned back, and that [[hezbollah|Hezbollah]] had agreed through intermediaries to halt all shooting.[82]
On the negotiating track he wrote that "Iran really wants to make a deal, and it will be a good one for the USA and those that are with us," while complaining that "Dumocrats, and various seemingly unpatriotic Republicans" made his job harder by constantly "chirping" that he should move faster or slower, go to war or not go to war, and advising critics to "just sit back and relax, it will all work out well in the end - It always does!"[82] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read Trump's repeated invocations of Venezuela — "the results in Iran will be amazing" and "this is being perfectly executed, on the scale of Venezuela, just a bigger, more complex operation" — as confirmation that [[iran|Iran]] was following the [[venezuela-model|Venezuela Model]] at greater scale.[82]
Badlands also revisited Trump's December 2021 call with [[barak-ravid|Barak Ravid]], in which he trashed Netanyahu and heavily implied Netanyahu played a role in the 2020 stolen election through his prerecorded video congratulating Biden "before the ink was even dry."[82] Separately, the administration was reported to be preparing to eliminate the Justice Department [[weaponization-fund|weaponization fund]], a move [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] disbelieved and predicted Trump would answer by doubling the fund instead.[82] See [[june-2026-trump-netanyahu-lebanon-call|the June 2026 Lebanon call]] and [[iran-suspension-of-us-talks-2026|Iran's suspension of US talks]].[82]

## Tariff pivot after the February 2026 Supreme Court ruling

After the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] ruled on February 20, 2026 that his administration's use of the [[international-emergency-economic-powers-act|International Emergency Economic Powers Act]] to impose global tariffs was unconstitutional, Trump moved swiftly to preserve high tariffs under an alternative authority.[84] The administration announced a new global tariff of up to 15 percent under the Trade Act of 1974 — the statutory maximum — after initially setting it at 10 percent, while [[customs-and-border-protection|U.S. Customs and Border Protection]] stopped collecting under the struck-down authority.[84] European and other international partners criticized the approach and urged adherence to existing trade agreements, though trade officials said core policy goals were unchanged.[84] Trump reportedly reacted angrily to two of his three Supreme Court appointees, calling them horrible and saying they embarrassed their families.[84] Details are covered at [[supreme-court-ieepa-tariff-ruling-2026|the February 2026 tariff ruling]].[84]

[[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] cited his line that "what truly matters is not which party controls our government, but whether our government is controlled by the people" in arguing that the loss served the constitutional order.[84]

In the same Brief, Badlands writers credited Trump's pre-game trash talk before the [[2026-winter-olympics-mens-hockey-final|U.S.–Canada Olympic hockey final]] as "bold leadership" pairing presidential confidence with a team that delivered, and noted continuing speculation that Mexico — as the real hub of the cartels — may be the actual target of his military operations in Latin America following the killing of [[el-mencho|El Mencho]].[84] Commerce Secretary [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] was described as central to his 2026 push to reclaim American production and leverage.[84]

## War on Fraud and Venezuela partnership (February 2026)

During his February 2026 [[state-of-the-union|State of the Union]] address, Trump announced a federal "[[war-on-fraud|war on fraud]]" to be led by Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]], and the administration suspended roughly $259 million in [[medicaid|Medicaid]] payments to [[minnesota|Minnesota]] the following day.[85] In the same address Trump discussed his strong partnership with Venezuelan President [[delcy-rodriguez|Delcy Rodriguez]], remarks [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] set against the news that Delcy's brother [[jorge-rodriguez|Jorge Rodriguez]] was pushing a limited [[venezuela-amnesty-law|amnesty bill]] to release hundreds of political prisoners.[85]

## Iran war signals (April 2026)

In a Fox Business interview in April 2026 Trump declared that the U.S.-Israel war with Iran was "very close to being over," saying Iran "wants to make a deal very badly" and warning that any agreement must prevent Tehran from retaining nuclear capabilities, since pulling out then would leave Iran needing "20 years to rebuild."[86] Weekend talks in Pakistan had collapsed after 21 hours with no breakthrough on denuclearization or enrichment, prompting a U.S. naval blockade of Iranian ports the same day, though Trump indicated indirect negotiations could resume within two days, possibly back in Islamabad.[86] Maria Bartiromo posted video of herself leaving the White House after the interview, saying Trump had told her the Iran War was over.[86] Badlands commentator GhostofBasedPatrickHenry read this against Mossad chief David Barnea's insistence on regime change as evidence that U.S. and Israeli interests were finally splitting.[86]

## The Trump Dialectic

The same commentator argued that Trump has a long history of using agent provocateurs to instigate conflict so he can force resolution, citing his deployment of Omarosa on The Celebrity Apprentice as the clearest example, and casting Giorgia Meloni's break with Israel as the current geopolitical instance of the pattern.[86]

## April 2026: Iran talks, blockade, and clash with the Pope

Trump confirmed the breakdown of the April 2026 Islamabad negotiations with [[iran|Iran]], stating on Truth Social that Tehran's refusal to abandon its nuclear program was the central obstacle to any agreement, after Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] said more than twenty hours of talks had failed to bridge critical gaps.[87] The United States then announced a [[naval-blockade-of-iran|maritime blockade]] in the [[gulf-of-oman|Gulf of Oman]] and Arabian Sea east of the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]].[87]

In the same period Trump intensified a public clash with [[pope-leo-xiv|Pope Leo XIV]], criticizing the pontiff's leadership and amplifying social-media content that portrayed himself in religious imagery many observers read as depicting Jesus Christ.[87] The posts prompted backlash from religious commentators, Catholic voices, and political observers who called the imagery provocative and inappropriate, adding to a broader pattern of tension between Trump and Catholic leadership over immigration, nationalism, and moral authority in global politics.[87] Iranian President [[masoud-pezeshkian|Masoud Pezeshkian]] publicly defended the Pope against Trump's criticisms.[87]

Documents released the same week by DNI [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] alleged that Intelligence Community actors manufactured the conspiracy underlying the [[first-impeachment-of-donald-trump|first impeachment]] of Trump in 2019.[87] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] separately argued that Trump champions a multipolar realignment "in secret, though not yet in public practice," strengthening the American people by weakening the US position in the System of Systems, and noted that Trump's public embrace of [[viktor-orban|Viktor Orbán]] preceded Orbán's electoral defeat in [[hungary|Hungary]].[87]

## July 2026 election address and Middle East energy moves
