Trump was scheduled to deliver a prime time national address on July 16, 2026, which he confirmed would address in part foreign interference in US elections and declassified intelligence on the subject; Democrats attempted to convince prime time networks to black out coverage and refuse to carry the speech.[88] Badlands writers expected him to discuss both the 2020 election and the [[2026-midterm-elections|2026 midterms]] while debating whether full disclosure would come from a presidential address at all.[88] See [[july-2026-trump-prime-time-election-address|the address]] for fuller coverage.[88]

In the same period his administration backed the revival of the [[iraq-syria-crude-oil-pipeline|Iraq-Syria crude oil pipeline]], less than a week after he revealed his intention to remove [[syria|Syria]] from the list of state sponsors of terrorism, opening the country to international investors.[88] Separately, his nominee for Director of National Intelligence, [[jay-clayton|Walter "Jay" Clayton III]], faced pointed questioning from the [[senate-intelligence-committee|Senate Intelligence Committee]] over the 2020 election.[88]

## March 2026: Fed succession, energy powers, and Gaza governance

In mid-March 2026, Trump was expected to nominate [[kevin-warsh|Kevin Warsh]] to succeed [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] as Chair of the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]], amid escalating tension over interest-rate policy and Fed independence; he had repeatedly and publicly criticized Powell over rates and monetary policy since his second inauguration.[89] Federal Judge [[james-boasberg|James Boasberg]] separately dismissed the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]]'s Federal Reserve subpoenas, writing that the renovation and testimony issues looked like "a convenient pretext" for "pressuring Powell to knuckle under."[89]

Trump also invoked authorities under the [[defense-production-act|Defense Production Act]] to address energy supply concerns linked to instability involving [[iran|Iran]], directing [[sable-offshore|Sable Offshore]] to restart previously halted petroleum production in California at the Santa Ynez Unit and its pipeline system — an increase of roughly 50,000 barrels per day in in-state output.[89] The White House order adjusted delegations of authority under the statute, clarifying which federal officials may exercise its powers.[89] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the move as a "Reverse [[hegelian-dialectic|Hegelian Dialectic]]" in which foreign chaos supplies the domestic permission structure for energy decentralization, calling it phase one of "Sovereign Disentanglement."[89]

On the Middle East, representatives of [[hamas|Hamas]] met with members of the Trump-backed [[board-of-peace|Gaza Peace Board]] to discuss governance and security arrangements for [[gaza|Gaza]] during a transition period.[89] Domestically, [[politico|Politico]] reported that [[republican-party|Republican]] strategists were divided over whether Trump should keep emphasizing [[2020-election-fraud-claims|2020 election claims]] ahead of the [[2026-midterm-elections|midterms]], while [[democratic-party|Democrats]] debated how to define themselves for a post-Trump 2028 cycle.[89]

## Praise for Putin and Xi (February 2026)

In the Oval Office on February 4, 2026, Trump told reporters that [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] "kept his word" on a cold-weather pause in strikes on Ukraine, explaining that the commitment ran "Sunday to Sunday" before attacks resumed, and adding, "I would like him to end the war. I spoke to him. I want him to end the war."[90] The same day he posted on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that he had completed "an excellent telephone conversation" with [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] covering trade, military matters, an April trip to China, Taiwan, the Russia–Ukraine war, [[iran|Iran]], Chinese purchases of American oil and gas, agricultural purchases including soybean commitments of 20 million tons this season and 25 million next, and airplane engine deliveries.[90] Trump described the relationship as "extremely good" and forecast "many positive results achieved over the next three years of my Presidency"; no additional agreements or timelines were announced.[90]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read both statements as deliberate [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] signalling that cut against Russophobia and Sinophobia in the Western psyche and the NeoCon "Faux MAGA" class, and argued Trump's long-standing need to couch his language about these leaders was abating.[90] He cited [[george-soros|George Soros]]'s identification of Trump, Putin, and Xi as the three paramount threats to global democracy as confirmation that the three are genuine sovereigns.[90]

## Golden Age declaration and Iran normalization (April 2026)

In April 2026 Trump declared a Golden Age for the Middle East, which Badlands commentator [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read as advancing the collective mind toward normalization with [[iran|Iran]] — breaking the public's conditioning to view Iran as a malignant adversary, as had been done with [[venezuela|Venezuela]], and diffusing any attempt by [[israel|Israel]] to subvert the peace process.[91] Days earlier, on a Monday, Trump had simultaneously threatened to nuke Iran and to make peace with Iran, a juxtaposition that "absolutely discombobulated everybody regardless of their position on the Iran War and President Trump," in what Badlands writers labelled a further application of the [[venezuela-model|Venezuela Model]].[91]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued the emerging deal was merely the visible translation of an arrangement already concluded, with Trump "making great progress" with the Iranian leadership per his own words, and that the process redeemed supposed villains in the Western psyche while casting doubt on longtime allies.[91]

## Data centers and fraud enforcement

Trump was named as a backer, alongside [[openai|OpenAI]] and [[oracle|Oracle]], of a proposed $15 billion, 1.3-gigawatt AI data center campus in [[port-washington|Port Washington]], Wisconsin, part of the [[project-stargate|Stargate]] megaproject that became the target of a municipal referendum.[91] His administration also launched the [[national-fraud-enforcement-division|National Fraud Enforcement Division]] on April 7, 2026, and Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] traveled to Budapest to support [[viktor-orban|Viktor Orbán]] ahead of the [[2026-hungarian-parliamentary-election|Hungarian election]], consistent with a National Security Strategy of empowering nationalist leaders across Europe.[91]

## US-Iran escalation and White House ballroom approval (April 2026)

Speaking at a Monday press conference in early April 2026, President Trump warned that [[iran|Iran]] could face overwhelming military force if it did not agree to US terms, including limits on its nuclear program, while simultaneously signaling that he preferred a negotiated resolution and believed a deal remained possible.[92] His criticism of allies and suggestions that the United States could withdraw from [[nato|NATO]] fueled uncertainty about American commitments and prompted European leaders to weigh more independent defense strategies.[92] The [[iran-war|US-led conflict with Iran]] exposed and intensified fractures with allies, with multiple NATO countries declining to support military operations or provide logistical access.[92] A divergence also emerged with [[israel|Israel]]: [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] warned Trump against pursuing a ceasefire in a Sunday call, arguing continued military pressure was necessary to degrade Iran's capabilities.[92]

Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] characterized the rhythm of escalation one day and de-escalation the next as a deliberate pattern of narrative deployments, noting that even segments of the MAGA core appeared momentarily disoriented, and citing [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]]'s framing that one sometimes must escalate in order to de-escalate.[92]

Separately, Trump's [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] asked that the indictment against [[steve-bannon|Steve Bannon]] be dropped, and the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] vacated the ruling that had left Bannon's [[contempt-of-congress|contempt-of-Congress]] conviction in place.[92] In the same period the [[national-capital-planning-commission|National Capital Planning Commission]] voted 8-1 to approve Trump's plan to build a new [[white-house-ballroom|White House ballroom]]; Trump thanked the commission on [[truth-social|Truth Social]], noting the approval was “overwhelmingly, 8-1” and that even Sen. [[rand-paul|Rand Paul]] voted in favor, and said presidents for more than 150 years had envisioned such an addition for state visits, grand events, and inaugurations.[92]

## July 2026 developments

Trump announced new 50% tariffs on nearly $20 billion worth of selected [[canada|Canadian]] imports under Section 338 of the Tariff Act of 1930, citing Canada's discriminatory treatment of American automobiles, dairy products, and alcoholic beverages, with the duties set to take effect August 19 and exemptions for energy, potash, fish, and critical minerals.[93] Canadian Prime Minister [[mark-carney|Mark Carney]] called the [[canada-tariff-escalation-2026|escalation]] inconsistent with the [[usmca|USMCA]] while signaling willingness to negotiate.[93]

He also ordered a federal review into the development of the climate-related guidance in the [[reference-manual-on-scientific-evidence|Reference Manual on Scientific Evidence]], arguing the material contained politically biased and "discredited" climate science and calling for scrutiny of those who prepared it.[93] Separately, his administration activated the never-before-used [[alien-terrorist-removal-court|Alien Terrorist Removal Court]], with the [[justice-department|Justice Department]] filing a sealed, classified application on July 15.[93]

Reports that FBI Director [[kash-patel|Kash Patel]] had traveled to [[russia|Russia]] with a letter from Trump to President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] drew attention amid [[eu-russia-sanctions-resistance-2026|EU divisions over new Russia sanctions]]; neither the FBI nor the White House confirmed details.[93] At the same time, Trump's "Make America Great Again" branding surfaced abroad when [[spain|Spain]]'s World Cup hero [[ferran-torres|Ferran Torres]] wore a "Make Spain Great Again" hat at the Madrid victory parade.[93] Badlands contributor [[bill-maher|Bill Maher]] coverage also noted that Maher, while open to voting for [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] in 2028, reiterated he would not support Trump himself.[93]

## February 2026 coordinated trade, energy, and arms moves

Between January 30 and February 2, 2026, Trump announced a series of coordinated foreign-policy actions spanning trade, energy markets, and arms sales.[94] He confirmed that the United States had cut its reciprocal tariff on Indian imports from 25% to 18%, effective immediately, following a phone call with Prime Minister [[narendra-modi|Narendra Modi]], and eliminated an additional 25% punitive tariff imposed in 2025 over [[india|India]]'s purchases of Russian crude — see [[2026-us-india-tariff-agreement|the 2026 US-India tariff agreement]].[94] Speaking to reporters aboard Air Force One around January 31-February 1, he said the United States welcomes Chinese and Indian investment in [[venezuela|Venezuela]]'s oil sector, stating that [[china|China]] "is welcome" to make deals there and that India is "coming in" to buy Venezuelan oil.[94] In the same period the [[state-department|State Department]] approved roughly $15.7 billion in Foreign Military Sales to [[israel|Israel]] and [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]].[94]

Trump also urged House Republicans to pass the Senate-approved funding measure to reopen the government during the shutdown that began on January 31, 2026.[94]

Following the [[68th-annual-grammy-awards|68th Annual Grammy Awards]], Trump attacked host [[trevor-noah|Trevor Noah]] on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] for saying that Trump and [[bill-clinton|Bill Clinton]] had spent time on Epstein Island, calling the Grammys "the WORST, virtually unwatchable," denying he had ever been to the island "nor anywhere close," and announcing he would send his lawyers to sue Noah.[94] Badlands commentary during the same week framed Trump as organizing [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] meetings in Turkey and the UAE designed to avert wider war.[94]

## June 2026 Middle East "final throes" claim

In June 2026 Trump stated that the United States had mediated an agreement between [[iran|Iran]] and [[israel|Israel]] to halt recent hostilities and that negotiators were in the "final throes" of a broader Middle East peace deal.[95] He indicated the agreement could be finalized within two or three days and described it as "a very, very good deal," urging both sides to cease shooting while warning that final negotiations could still be derailed by miscalculation.[95] The claim followed reports that Iranian forces had shot down a U.S. Apache helicopter with no casualties, amid escalating Israeli attacks on [[lebanon|Lebanon]].[95]

Trump personally intervened with Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]], reportedly telling him to exercise restraint or risk losing U.S. support; Netanyahu subsequently announced that the immediate threat on the Iran front had been contained while affirming Israel's right to self-defense.[95] Israeli operations against [[hezbollah|Hezbollah]] in southern Lebanon and Beirut suburbs continued despite the Iran de-escalation, and Iran maintained that any comprehensive agreement must address the Lebanon theater, with [[pakistan|Pakistan]] playing a mediating role.[95] The episode affected regional shipping through the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]] and contributed to volatility in global oil prices.[95]

Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read the sequence as Trump "playing games with the Zionists," baiting them deeper into war by occupying all sides of the narrative at once: provocative threats to bomb Iran "back to the Stone Age," followed by claims that a deal was imminent.[95] Those claims, he argued, provoke Iranian denials that inflame Israeli leadership while stoking Israeli anxiety that their window for war is closing, accelerating war plans against a peace deal that may not be real.[95] He compared the layered effect to the film *Inception*, noted Trump's telling a Wall Street Journal reporter that the helicopter attack "wasn't a big deal" after promising a strong response, and pointed to Trump openly questioning whether Netanyahu should seek re-election while calling him a "wartime prime minister" — in his reading, daring Netanyahu to shatter the peace while Trump moved toward the off-ramp.[95]

## AI and energy buildout

Trump's [[trump-ai-action-plan|AI Action Plan]] and related executive orders, most from 2025, frame American AI development in national-security terms while advancing the nuclear and permitting reforms required for AI-scale power.[95] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] paired it with [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]]'s [[china-ai-data-center-buildout|Chinese data-center buildout]] as twin "Warp Speed" programs whose rivalry narrative provides mutual [[narrative-shielding|Narrative Shielding]] for the infrastructure of the [[global-golden-age|Golden Age]].[95]

## Election integrity response, June 2026

Trump and congressional Republicans used the late-counting reversal in the [[2026-los-angeles-mayoral-election|Los Angeles mayoral race]] — a case of the [[red-mirage|Red Mirage]] pattern — to renew calls for the [[save-act|SAVE Act]] and other measures requiring proof of citizenship and stricter voter identification.[95]

## 2026: election records, Germany withdrawal, and Israel alignment

The Justice Department's April 2026 grand jury subpoena for Fulton County 2020 election workers followed Trump's repeated claims that the county, a Democratic stronghold, committed widespread voter fraud that cost him Georgia, which he lost by 11,779 votes.[96] Separately, his announced withdrawal of thousands of U.S. troops from Germany surprised European leaders and prompted calls for a stronger European pillar in NATO, a shift Badlands commentary framed as removing the camouflage from an arrangement long underwritten by American power.[96] House Democrats meanwhile pressed his administration to end U.S. silence on Israel's nuclear arsenal, an effort Badlands commentary read as an attempt to damage Trump politically given his close alignment with Israel.[96]

## Davos 2026 address

At the [[world-economic-forum|World Economic Forum]] in Davos, Switzerland on Wednesday, January 21, 2026, Trump addressed a packed audience, boasting about his first-year accomplishments and American exceptionalism and renewing calls for the United States to acquire [[greenland|Greenland]].[97] He threatened tariffs on Europe before announcing that he and [[nato|NATO]] Secretary General [[mark-rutte|Mark Rutte]] had agreed on a "framework of a future deal," after which he dropped the threatened tariffs; the framework would allow Washington to build bases on Greenland considered US territory in the Arctic.[97] Recalling that the United States had held the island as a trustee and returned it to [[denmark|Denmark]] after World War II, he disclaimed any intent to use force while closing with an ultimatum: "You can say yes, and we will be very appreciative, or you can say no, and we will remember."[97]

In a viral passage on the Russia-Ukraine war, Trump said it "wouldn't have started if the 2020 US presidential election weren't rigged," adding "people will soon be prosecuted for what they did. It's probably breaking news, but it should be," and calling for "strong borders, strong elections and ideally, a good press."[97] He also said the United States is "going heavy into nuclear," linking nuclear expansion to power demand from artificial intelligence and industrial growth; nuclear-linked equities rose in premarket trading afterward.[97] The following day he presided over the accession of eight additional countries to the [[board-of-peace|Board of Peace]].[97] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the trip as the [[donroe-doctrine|Donroe Doctrine]] in full bloom, while European institutions responded with a trade-deal freeze and the first invocation of the [[anti-coercion-instrument|Anti-Coercion Instrument]] against Washington.[97]

## Declassification, Saudi rift and Venezuela relief (July 2026)

Trump told reporters on Wednesday, July 1, 2026, that acting DNI [[bill-pulte|Bill Pulte]] would serve for only "a month or two" but is authorized to "declassify almost everything," as a [[white-house|White House]] task force including [[john-solomon|John Solomon]] and [[derek-harvey|Derek Harvey]] gathered 2020 election documents from the [[cia|CIA]], [[national-security-agency|NSA]], [[office-of-the-director-of-national-intelligence|ODNI]], [[fbi|FBI]] and [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] for release.[98]

Reports in *[[new-york-times|The New York Times]]* and the *[[wall-street-journal|Wall Street Journal]]* said disagreements over the [[iran|Iran]] war had created friction between Trump and Saudi Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]], with the administration weighing a repositioning of US forces in [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]]; both governments continued to describe the relationship as an important strategic partnership.[98] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued that Trump had "tricked the Deep State" into withdrawing troops by using its own ego against it, calling the maneuver "5D chess."[98]

The Trump administration also criticised [[maria-corina-machado|María Corina Machado]]'s attempted return to [[venezuela|Venezuela]] after the [[2026-venezuela-earthquakes|2026 earthquakes]], with a senior White House official calling it "grotesque political opportunism" while the administration prioritised search-and-rescue and humanitarian assistance.[98] Separately, [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump understands [[memetics|memetics]] better than perhaps any leader in world history and leveraged the internet's force-multiplier effect during his rise in 2016 — a capability his opponents cannot intentionally replicate.[98]

## January 2026: fraud, Greenland, Iran and Gaza

In mid-January 2026 Trump commented publicly on [[doge|DOGE]] and government efficiency efforts in a [[truth-social|Truth Social]] post that neither announced the dissolution of DOGE nor ended the initiative, and separately denounced alleged food-stamp fraud in Minneapolis as "a giant Democrat SCAM," calling for those involved to be sent back to Somalia or other countries of origin and naming California, Illinois and New York as equally affected.[100] He also posted "FEAR NOT, GREAT PEOPLE OF MINNESOTA, THE DAY OF RECKONING & RETRIBUTION IS COMING!" as [[operation-metro-surge|Operation Metro Surge]] expanded, ordering an additional 1,000 CBP agents to the Twin Cities.[100] Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]]'s "follow the money" framing of dark-money NGO probes mirrored Trump's own language that week, as did his move to cut funding to sanctuary jurisdictions.[100]

On [[greenland|Greenland]], Trump posted that US control was necessary for American national security, framed the island as a strategic outpost for the [[golden-dome|Golden Dome]] missile defense array, and joked that the territory was defended by "two dog sleds" — a line the [[white-house|White House]] amplified in memes.[100] He applauded [[denmark|Denmark]]'s decision to send troops there, which [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read as a maneuver to draw [[nato|NATO]] resources away from Ukraine.[100]

On [[iran|Iran]], Trump publicly questioned whether [[reza-pahlavi|Reza Pahlavi]] could unify opposition support inside the country, saying "He seems very nice, but I don't know how he'd play within his own country," and declined to endorse regime change, disappointing advocates of a strike.[100] Meanwhile [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] announced on Trump's behalf the launch of Phase Two of the [[comprehensive-plan-to-end-the-gaza-conflict|20-Point Plan to End the Gaza Conflict]], with Trump insisting on roles for [[turkey|Turkey]] and [[qatar|Qatar]] in the stabilization force.[100] In the [[united-states-senate|Senate]], administration pressure helped defeat the [[venezuela-war-powers-resolution|Venezuela war powers resolution]] 51–50 on January 14, 2026, with [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] breaking the tie.[100]

## Tribute to Jesse Jackson (February 2026)

Following the death of civil rights leader Rev. [[jesse-jackson|Jesse Jackson]] at age 84 on Tuesday, February 17, 2026, Trump offered warm public praise for him in a [[truth-social|Truth Social]] post, describing Jackson as "a good man" with "lots of personality, grit, and street smarts" who was gregarious and truly loved people, and calling him "a force of nature like few others before him."[100] Trump said he knew Jackson well long before entering politics and had helped him along the way, including providing free office space for the [[rainbow-coalition|Rainbow Coalition]] in the Trump Building at 40 Wall Street, assisting with criminal justice reform, securing long-term funding for Historically Black Colleges and Universities, and supporting Opportunity Zones.[100] He also noted Jackson's uncredited role in helping elect Barack Obama despite their personal differences and extended his deepest condolences to Jackson's family.[100]

## February 2026 developments

The [[justice-department|Justice Department]] issued additional subpoenas in the Florida criminal investigation into the January 2017 intelligence assessment concluding that Russia interfered in the 2016 race to help Trump defeat [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]]; former CIA Director [[john-brennan|John Brennan]] was told he is a target, in one of several inquiries into former officials viewed as Trump adversaries.[100] Simultaneously, the second round of indirect [[2026-iran-deal-negotiations|US–Iran nuclear talks]] concluded in Geneva with a claimed understanding on core principles, even as Supreme Leader [[ali-khamenei|Ali Khamenei]] warned Trump that Iran possessed weapons capable of sinking American warships.[100] On the domestic front, Rep. [[thomas-massie|Thomas Massie]] pressed Attorney General [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]] over [[jeffrey-epstein|Epstein]] file redactions and predicted growing Republican defections from Trump's agenda, while Hillary Clinton accused the administration of a cover-up — a dynamic Badlands writers characterized as an inversion operation redirecting pressure onto Trump.[100]

## Venezuela oil policy and Cuba ultimatum (January 2026)

Trump told The New York Times that the United States would be "using oil" and "taking oil" from [[venezuela|Venezuela]], and said oversight of the country's oil sector would last "much longer" than a year, according to The Guardian.[101] The Wall Street Journal, cited by The Guardian, reported that senior administration officials described a plan aimed at using Venezuela's oil reserves to help cut U.S. consumer prices toward about $50 a barrel by expanding Venezuelan output.[101] Trump also indicated he might block [[exxonmobil|ExxonMobil]] from investing in Venezuela after its CEO described the country as "uninvestable."[101]

On the following Sunday Trump escalated pressure on [[cuba|Cuba]], issuing an ultimatum that Havana must "make a deal" with the United States or face unspecified consequences as its access to Venezuelan oil and financial support was cut off.[101] On Truth Social he declared that "there will be no more oil or money going to Cuba — ZERO!" and urged Cuban leaders to negotiate "before it's too late."[101] He separately reposted a message suggesting that Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] — a Cuban-American and key architect of his Latin America policy — could become president of Cuba, adding the comment "Sounds good to me!"; the repost sparked commentary but did not reflect official policy.[101] Cuban officials responded by asserting their nation's sovereignty and condemning the U.S. approach.[101]

## Approval polling during the Iran war (March 2026)

Polling during the [[iran|Iran]] conflict period showed no dramatic shift in Trump's overall approval rating, with public opinion divided on Trump personally but more consistently negative on the Iran policy itself.[102] An aggregate tracker cited by Newsweek showed his approval improving modestly from about -15 to -11.8 net, with roughly 43% approval overall, while separate polling put his favorability at around 38% favorable against 58% unfavorable in early March 2026.[102] A Trafalgar Group poll found Trump with a +13 net approval on his handling of the Iran war specifically, whereas a PBS/NPR/Marist poll found 54% disapproving and 36% approving of his Iran handling, and another survey found roughly 50% disapproval against 35% approval.[102] Broader public opinion data showed majority opposition to US military involvement in Iran, including low support for strikes or escalation.[102]

[[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] dismissed the numbers, writing that "polls are faker than election results" and wagering that the President is more popular than ever; she argued that if the polls show Trump has not taken a hit, he is likely up, and that the narratives would therefore "get weirder and more intense."[102]

## Iran, Israel and Qatar messaging (March 2026)

After [[joe-kent|Joe Kent]]'s interview with [[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]], Trump posted on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] about [[israel|Israel]]'s subversive behavior while demanding that Iran refrain from retaliating against [[qatar|Qatar]], reiterating that a US response would be faster and more powerful than before.[102] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] noted that Trump did not acknowledge Israel in that capacity, reading the omission as tacit encouragement for Iran to strike Israel instead of Qatar, and suggested Kent had been dispatched by Trump's team to front-run narratives.[102]

On the legislative front, Trump posted on Truth Social that Wednesday: "For all of the Fake News out there, it's called THE SAVE AMERICA ACT, not the SAVE ACT. Nobody knows what the Save Act means!!!" as the [[united-states-senate|Senate]] began debating the [[save-america-act|SAVE America Act]].[102] His September executive order on [[domestic-terrorism|domestic terrorism]], issued after the 2025 assassination of [[charlie-kirk|Charlie Kirk]], also underpinned the [[fbi|FBI]]–[[internal-revenue-service|IRS]] task force on nonprofit terror financing announced in March 2026.[102]

## Early MOU signing and the DNI standoff (June 2026)

In mid-June 2026 Trump and Iranian leaders remotely and digitally signed the [[us-iran-memorandum-of-understanding|US–Iran memorandum of understanding]] ahead of the scheduled ceremony in Switzerland, bringing the interim accord into force early while negotiations on a permanent agreement continued over 60 days.[103] Coverage of the deal focused on [[qatar|Qatar]]'s backchannel role, with Doha's ties to both Washington and Tehran credited for enabling contacts before and during the conflict.[103] Against media and analyst warnings that energy prices would stay elevated for months, Trump wrote on Truth Social that oil prices should come down and claimed that traffic through the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]] had continued throughout the conflict and would normalize rapidly.[103] The agreement deepened divisions within the [[maga-coalition|MAGA coalition]], which Badlands commentary read as a deliberate trap set for hawkish critics.[103] At the 2026 G7 summit Trump announced "I'm the boss!" on entering the official meeting and recounted meeting Egyptian President [[abdel-fattah-el-sisi|Abdel Fattah el-Sisi]], saying "we fell in love, deeply in love."[103] A new [[air-force-one|Air Force One]] aircraft, ordered in his first term and eventually supplied via a Qatari 747 after [[boeing|Boeing]] delays, was delivered that week and expected to enter service on July 4.[103]

Domestically, Trump canceled [[jay-clayton|Jay Clayton]]'s Senate confirmation hearing for director of national intelligence, complicating reauthorization of [[section-702|Section 702]] of the [[foreign-intelligence-surveillance-act|Foreign Intelligence Surveillance Act]] and leaving [[bill-pulte|Bill Pulte]] on track to become acting DNI after [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]]'s departure.[103] He said Republicans had agreed to remove Pulte in exchange for Democratic support on FISA but that "Dumocrats broke the Deal," declined to move Clayton out of the Southern District of New York until [[jamie-mcdonald|Jamie McDonald]] is confirmed as his replacement, and said he would not approve FISA reauthorization without the [[save-america-act|SAVE AMERICA Act]] attached.[103]

## July 2026: Hormuz reversal, Graham eulogy, and pressure on Netanyahu

The 15 July 2026 Badlands Brief covered three Trump-related developments.[104] Trump reversed his [[hormuz-transit-fee-proposal|20% Hormuz transit fee]] in favor of trade and investment deals with Gulf states, announcing free trade in the Persian Gulf during a [[white-house|White House]] meeting with Iraqi Prime Minister [[ali-al-zaidi|Ali al-Zaidi]] — a move the Brief filed under its "[[taco-tuesday|TACO Tuesday]]" rubric while contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] framed it as geopolitical jiu-jitsu converting a levy into an investment opportunity.[104]

On the death of Sen. [[lindsey-graham|Lindsey Graham]], Trump praised him as "a great politician and a kind man," adding that "You know who really lost? Israel. Ukraine. A lot of countries he fought for — they lost somebody who was very special."[104] Politico reported that the resulting "hawkish void," compounded by [[mitch-mcconnell|Mitch McConnell]]'s absence, gave Trump's [[america-first-movement|America First]] allies an opening to press for a more restrained U.S. posture abroad.[104]

In a phone call with Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]], Trump urged the redeployment of Israeli forces out of southern [[syria|Syria]] and southern [[lebanon|Lebanon]], telling him that local populations "don't want you there"; Netanyahu resisted, citing the need for border security zones after October 7.[104] The exchange formed part of a wider U.S. push for phased withdrawals and a Syrian security agreement, which Washington concluded Netanyahu would not accept without stronger guarantees.[104] GhostofBasedPatrickHenry read the episode as Trump siding with the Arabs and drawing a red line that would ultimately expose and end the [[greater-israel-project|Greater Israel Project]].[104]

## Iran "State of Collapse" claim (April 2026)

In late April 2026 Trump posted on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that [[iran|Iran]] had informed the United States it was in a "State of Collapse" and had urgently requested that the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]] be reopened "as soon as possible" while it addressed its leadership situation, which he said he believed Iran could resolve.[105] The statement came during a two-week U.S. blockade of Iranian oil exports and the closure of the Strait, and the same day the [[united-arab-emirates|UAE]] announced its withdrawal from [[opec|OPEC]].[105]

Trump rejected Iran's latest peace proposal, delivered via Pakistan, for failing to address nuclear issues upfront, while U.S. intelligence agencies assessed how Tehran might respond if he unilaterally declared victory and began drawing down forces.[105] Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued the news cycle was seeding the narrative that Trump would exit the war, an outcome Badlands writers had long anticipated.[105] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the episode as part of Trump's "Sovereign Disentanglement" operation and the dismantling of a green globalist energy order in favor of a nationalist realignment.[105]

## "Bomb the Shit Out of Them" Threat to Iran (June 2026)

In June 2026 Trump warned that the United States would resume major military strikes against [[iran|Iran]] if ongoing peace negotiations failed, telling Fox News correspondent Trey Yingst he would "bomb the shit out of them" if Tehran did not immediately agree to a deal.[106] The remarks followed another round of American strikes on Iranian surveillance, communications and air-defense facilities after Washington blamed Iran for downing a U.S. Apache helicopter near the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]]; Defense Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]] said the strikes were meant both to degrade Iranian capabilities and to strengthen Washington's negotiating position.[106] Trump had repeatedly complained about what he called the most violated ceasefire in the history of the world, while insisting that he and his administration still preferred a negotiated settlement and that a peace agreement remained within reach.[106] On [[truth-social|Truth Social]] he described Iran as "the bully of the Middle East," declared it "DEAD" and said it would "now have to pay the price," and in a follow-up post called the naval blockade a "STEEL WALL," closing with "Praise be to Allah!"[106]

Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read the episode as a live demonstration of Trump's narrative method: after a week of pressure aimed at [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] and [[israel|Israel]], Trump "reset the board" by resuming hyper-aggressive posturing toward Iran, slipping into a storyteller persona in the Oval Office and embellishing details about the downed helicopter, unmanned boats travelling at "ludicrous speed," and favors done for the Field Marshal of [[pakistan|Pakistan]].[106] The writer highlighted Trump's claims that the United States had been quietly taking Iranian oil at night and that he now spoke directly with Iranian leaders begging him to stop bombing, treating both as tests of the "verisimilitude" of media-constructed reality.[106] In this reading the purpose of the performance was to condition Netanyahu and the wider public to accept Israel fighting Iran without American participation — a scenario the author argued would end only when Iran strategically defeated Israel, and which Netanyahu's vow to "continue to attack both in Iran and Lebanon" even under a ceasefire appeared to confirm.[106]

Trump also struck a conciliatory posture toward [[turkey|Turkey]] that same week, praising President [[recep-tayyip-erdogan|Recep Tayyip Erdoğan]] as a strong and effective negotiator when asked about the Turkish leader's attacks on Israel and about the F-35 jets Turkey has paid for but not received, emphasizing the importance of maintaining U.S.–Turkish relations.[106]

## Valentine's Day 2026 attack on Bill Maher

On Valentine's Day 2026, Trump used his Truth Social account to deliver a lengthy personal rebuke of comedian [[bill-maher|Bill Maher]] in place of a traditional holiday greeting.[107] He described a 2025 White House dinner with Maher as "a total waste of time," wrote that the comedian had been "extremely nervous" and had asked for a vodka tonic to calm himself, called *Real Time with Bill Maher* "very boring" and "anti-Trump," and labeled Maher "a highly overrated lightweight" while allowing he was slightly more talented than other late-night hosts.[107] The post also included jabs at Canada and China, and Trump faulted Republicans for "using [Maher] to show how the Left is coming over our way," adding that "Our Base, the Greatest of All Time, laughs at your weakness when you do it!"[107]

## China de-escalation and criminal referrals (April 2026)

In April 2026 Trump signaled a tactical de-escalation with [[china|China]], claiming that [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] had agreed not to provide weapons to [[iran|Iran]] following direct communication between the two leaders.[108] On Truth Social he wrote that "China is very happy that I am permanently opening the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]]," said he was doing it for China and the world, and predicted that "President Xi will give me a big, fat, hug when I get there in a few weeks," adding "Doesn't that beat fighting??? BUT REMEMBER, we are very good at fighting, if we have to."[108] The move came as the United States continued aggressive military and economic actions in the Middle East, including a naval blockade of Iranian ports and operations to reopen the strait, and as US officials escalated scrutiny of actors linked to [[russia|Russia]]'s war in [[ukraine|Ukraine]].[108]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the pattern as choreography rather than realpolitik chaos, arguing that Trump escalates with one pillar of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] while de-escalating with the other, and that the trade wars with Xi have provided cover for a joint "Multipolar Margin Call" against the globalist financial architecture.[108]

During the same week, the [[office-of-the-director-of-national-intelligence|Office of the Director of National Intelligence]] referred the whistleblower behind the complaint tied to Trump's 2019 impeachment and former Inspector General [[michael-atkinson|Michael Atkinson]] to the [[justice-department|Justice Department]] for potential criminal violations, and the Justice Department moved to vacate the seditious conspiracy convictions of [[proud-boys|Proud Boys]] and [[oath-keepers|Oath Keepers]] leaders tied to January 6.[108]

## January 7, 2026 actions

On Wednesday, January 7, 2026, Trump signed a Presidential Memorandum directing the [[us-withdrawal-from-66-international-organizations|withdrawal of the United States from 66 international organizations]] deemed contrary to American interests, ending participation and funding for 35 non-UN bodies and 31 [[united-nations|United Nations]] entities.[109] The same day he signed an Executive Order to "prioritize the warfighter" through [[defense-contractor-buyback-restrictions|restrictions on defense contractor stock buybacks]] and excessive corporate payouts.[109]

Trump also announced on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that Venezuela would purchase "ONLY American Made Products" with revenue from a new oil deal, including agricultural products, medicines, medical devices and equipment to improve the country's electric grid and energy facilities, calling the arrangement "a wise choice" for both nations.[109] This followed the US [[seizure-of-the-tanker-marinera|seizure of the Russia-flagged tanker Marinera]] linked to Venezuelan crude.[109] Separately, he renewed public discussion of acquiring [[greenland|Greenland]], reiterating its strategic importance to US national security, while Danish and Greenlandic officials insisted the territory is not for sale.[109]

On Venezuela's political future, Trump said that while he considered opposition leader [[maria-corina-machado|María Corina Machado]] "a nice woman," he did not believe she had sufficient political support and preferred to deal with interim leader [[delcy-rodriguez|Delcy Rodríguez]].[109] Badlands contributors read the sequence as a deliberate strategy: [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued Trump had executed a "fake" regime-change operation to intercept a globalist-planned [[color-revolution|color revolution]], while [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] said Trump had "chummed the waters" to draw out neoconservatives such as [[tom-cotton|Tom Cotton]] and [[rick-scott|Rick Scott]].[109] The [[white-house|White House]] additionally published an [[white-house-january-6-webpage|official January 6 webpage]] disputing the "insurrection" framing of the 2021 Capitol breach and criticising former Vice President [[mike-pence|Mike Pence]].[109]

## June 2026: quantum order and the Reflecting Pool

At a June 2026 quantum executive order signing accompanying the [[office-of-science-and-technology-policy|OSTP]] announcement of a national [[quantum-computing|quantum computing]] initiative, Trump referenced his uncle [[john-g-trump|John G. Trump]], the engineer tasked with reviewing [[nikola-tesla|Nikola Tesla]]'s papers after Tesla's 1943 death.[110] Two posts from the official [[white-house|White House]] account in the same period sparked widespread online discussion about "Q" messaging.[110]

Trump also said repair work would begin immediately on the [[lincoln-memorial-reflecting-pool|Lincoln Memorial Reflecting Pool]] after personally inspecting the site and alleging vandalism had damaged the recently renovated landmark, writing on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that the pool might need to be partially drained.[110] Critics questioned whether the deterioration stemmed from the renovation itself, and authorities had not publicly released evidence supporting all of Trump's claims.[110]

Separately, his administration was reported to be preparing [[dhs-election-grant-conditions|guidance conditioning Department of Homeland Security grants]] on state adoption of hand-marked paper ballots, manual audits, and citizenship verification, and Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] announced a framework agreement with [[iran|Iran]] accompanied by [[iran-general-license-x|Iran General License X]].[110]

## Davos 2026 and the Greenland gambit

Trump traveled to the January 2026 [[world-economic-forum|World Economic Forum]] annual meeting in Davos amid significant concern among European leaders about the U.S.-European alliance, his aggressive demands to acquire [[greenland|Greenland]] — including threats of tariffs on [[denmark|Denmark]] and EU allies — having bewildered attendees and strained transatlantic relations ahead of the visit.[110] Several cabinet members and allies, including Commerce Secretary [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] and Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]], spoke at the event before his arrival, and Badlands Media provided live coverage of his address.[110] Whereas he had attended the previous year's forum by video, in 2026 he appeared in person.[110]

The administration announced a 10% tariff on goods from eight countries effective February 1, rising to 25% by June if unresolved, framed as retaliation for a small European troop deployment to Greenland that included 13 German soldiers.[110] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] characterized the [[greenland-tariff-dispute-2026|Greenland Gambit]] as a pivot to abstract power projection in service of the Golden Age transition, executed with speed that caught the EU bloc off guard after the Venezuela regime-change narrative.[110]

At a press briefing before the forum, Trump was asked directly whether his [[board-of-peace|Board of Peace]] was intended to replace the [[united-nations|United Nations]] and answered ambiguously with "Might," criticizing the U.N. for failing to live up to its potential while adding, "you got to let the U.N. continue because the potential is so great."[110] A signing ceremony for the board was planned at Davos, with invitations extended to nearly 60 nations including [[russia|Russia]].[110]

Domestically, Trump signed an [[executive-order-on-institutional-homebuyers|executive order barring institutional investors from buying single-family homes]], directing agencies to prioritize individual American families and first-time buyers and instructing [[fannie-mae|Fannie Mae]] and [[freddie-mac|Freddie Mac]] to purchase $200 billion in mortgage-backed securities to lower borrowing rates.[110] His administration also intensified federal enforcement in Minnesota, including [[operation-metro-surge|Operation Metro Surge]], grand jury subpoenas to state officials, and a [[department-of-housing-and-urban-development|HUD]] fair-housing probe.[110]

## Iran ceasefire push and EU energy leverage (March 2026)

In late March 2026, as the Iran–Israel exchange of fire entered its fourth week, Trump claimed that active talks with Tehran were underway, described a significant Iranian "present" related to energy flows, asserted that Iran had been "destroyed militarily," and then revealed that Iran had agreed not to pursue a nuclear weapon.[111] Washington was pressing a one-month ceasefire proposal delivered through Pakistani intermediaries that would require Iran to dismantle its nuclear capabilities — including decommissioning Natanz, Isfahan, and Fordow — abandon its proxy militias, forswear nuclear weapons, and keep the Strait of Hormuz open, in exchange for full sanctions relief and civilian nuclear assistance.[111] Israel under [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] remained skeptical, and Iran rejected envoys [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] and [[jared-kushner|Jared Kushner]] as "backstabbers," preferring Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]].[111] Markets reacted swiftly to the ceasefire rumors, with oil prices tumbling and US stock futures surging, while the United States reportedly prepared to deploy elements of the 82nd Airborne Division to the region as a precaution.[111]

In the same period Trump's administration used American energy exports as leverage over the [[european-union|European Union]], with Ambassador [[andrew-puzder|Andrew Puzder]] warning that terms of supply "may not be as favorable" if the European Parliament failed to ratify the transatlantic trade deal committing the bloc to $750 billion in US energy purchases by 2028.[111] Badlands commentary framed this as Trump exploiting Europe's self-inflicted dependency after decoupling from Russian pipeline gas, and predicted the same lever would be applied in Ukraine–Russia peace negotiations.[111]

Trump also formally retracted a Biden-era [[cia|CIA]] assessment that had treated "motherhood" and "homemaking" as potential indicators of violent extremism, a move hailed by critics of the document as reversing the politicized weaponization of federal intelligence against conservative women and families.[111]

## No tax on overtime proposal (September 2024)

At a rally in Tucson, Arizona in September 2024, Trump announced that he would "end all taxes on overtime" if returned to the [[white-house|White House]], extending an earlier pledge to eliminate taxes on tips.[112] "I went to some economists, great ones, I said, 'What do you think?'" he told the crowd. "They said, 'It would be unbelievable, you'll get a whole new workforce by doing that.'"[112] He added that "the people who work overtime are among the hardest-working citizens in our country," and that "when you're past 40 hours a week, your overtime hours will be tax-free."[112]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] called the [[no-tax-on-overtime|tax deployments]] "masterful," arguing they appeal directly to working-class voters, strand the establishment in the untenable position of opposing cuts it cannot caricature as gifts to the rich, and open a national debate over who should be taxed at all — with Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]]'s adoption of the no-tax-on-tips plan offered as proof the strategy was working.[112]

Separately, the federal government designated the January 6, 2025 certification of the presidential vote a [[national-special-security-event|national special security event]], a first for that proceeding, explicitly to prevent a reprise of the 2021 Capitol riot by Trump supporters seeking to overturn the 2020 results.[112] In the same period, [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] and former Rep. [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] campaigned for Trump at Arizona Christian University in Glendale in an event moderated by Substack writer [[jessica-reed-kraus|Jessica Reed Kraus]], weeks after Kennedy ended his independent presidential campaign and endorsed Trump in Arizona.[112]

## October 2024 "enemy from within" controversy

In mid-October 2024, Trump doubled down on his insistence that his political rivals were "the enemy from within," even after similar comments drew backlash and became a centerpiece of a campaign rally by Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]].[113] Speaking at a [[fox-news|Fox News]] town hall before a friendly audience of women voters in Georgia, hosted by [[harris-faulkner|Harris Faulkner]] and aired on "The Faulkner Focus," Trump responded to a clip of his earlier remark that his Democratic opponents were the "enemy within" and that the military could be used to quell unrest.[113] "I don't know, I thought it was a nice presentation. That wasn't unhinged. No, you know what they are, they're a party of sound bites," he said, adding: "It is the enemy from within, and they're very dangerous. They're Marxists and communists and fascists, and they're sick."[113]

Trump singled out Democratic California Representative [[adam-schiff|Adam Schiff]] and former Speaker [[nancy-pelosi|Nancy Pelosi]], saying, "These people — they're so sick, and they're so evil," and insisting, "I'm not threatening anybody. They're the ones doing the threatening. They do phony investigations."[113] Badlands contributor [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued that the oath of office required of U.S. officials explicitly demands defense of the [[us-constitution|Constitution]] against enemies both foreign and domestic, and that the media framing sought to recast domestic enemies as a conspiracy theory.[113]

## Federal workforce reaction to a possible second term

Reporting in October 2024 described federal employees across the executive branch panicking at the prospect of another Trump administration, with [[environmental-protection-agency|EPA]] staff moving to "safer" agencies and an [[department-of-the-interior|Interior Department]] worker studying [[project-2025|Project 2025]].[113] Trump had pledged to "demolish the deep state," and his running mate, Senator [[jd-vance|JD Vance]], said he ought to fire "every civil servant in the administrative state."[113] In the waning days of his first administration Trump had sought to make it easier to fire federal employees, a move quickly reversed by the [[joe-biden|Biden]] administration.[113] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that establishment "narrative schizophrenia" before the November 5 election — shifting from an implied Harris victory tour to "too close to call" to betting markets swinging toward Trump — reflected preparation for attempts to decertify the result, including [[fourteenth-amendment|Fourteenth Amendment]] challenges floated by sitting members of Congress.[113]

## Durham testimony on the Russia allegations (June 2023)

At the [[durham-house-judiciary-testimony-2023|House Judiciary Committee hearing]] of June 21, 2023, Special Counsel [[john-durham|John Durham]] testified that the [[cia|CIA]] knew [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] had approved a plan to smear Trump, then a candidate, with Russia allegations to distract from her email scandal, and that the [[fbi|FBI]] had failed to apply equal scrutiny to allegations about the Clinton and Trump campaigns.[114] Truth Community commentators argued that Democratic efforts to frame the report as exonerating the officials Trump had accused of spying on his campaign were themselves evidence that larger crimes remained unexposed.[114]

## "Bloodbath" hoax and the comedy of the rallies (March 2024)

In March 2024 corporate media outlets including NBC News, [[abc-news|ABC News]] and [[politico|Politico]], along with Biden/Harris campaign social media accounts and Democrat lawmakers, were accused of misconstruing a passage of a Trump speech about the auto industry into a claim that he had promised a "bloodbath" if he lost the election.[115] [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] responded that "Legacy media lies," and MSNBC's Joe Scarborough deleted his own "Bloodbath" post on X after being called out.[115] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] drew two "white pills" from the episode: that the interval between the deployment of a media operation against Trump and its complete exposure by a decentralized collective had shrunk from years to months to minutes, and that the coordination was not one-sided — arguing that anyone who still doubted Trump and Musk were working in concert was "watching a different movie."[115]

The same month, [[politico|Politico]] published a long feature on Trump's use of humor at rallies, opening with his stock line that he had "been indicted more times than Alphonse Capone" — a claim the piece noted was false.[115] The article described the ritualized structure of Trump events, from Lee Greenwood at the start to a [[qanon|QAnon]]-associated closing dirge, and argued that comedy allowed Trump to retain a reputation as a politically incorrect outsider despite his insider status as leader of the GOP.[115] Critics and scholars of rhetoric quoted in the piece said the humor turned opponents into jokes, recast Trump's liabilities as laughing matters and desensitized supporters to proposals such as mass deportations and the abandonment of allies — that "the mirth masks the menace."[115] The piece invoked Joseph Goebbels's belief that "horseplay is necessary," Ruth Ben-Ghiat's comparison of Trump's humor to Benito Mussolini's, and Maya Vinokour's observation that Politburo stenograms from the Stalin era recorded abundant laughter even as the purges began.[115]

Badlands contributor [[simon-esler|Simon Esler]] read the Politico feature as "a true circus of all the mad propaganda that has been fired at Trump over the years," a greatest-hits compilation of histrionics whose chief interest was as a window into the enemy's cognitive battlefield.[115] He noted the irony of Politico likening Trump to communist tyrants while, in his account, anti-Trump demonstrators in Canada were carrying images of Stalin, Lenin and Marx and using the pro-Palestine movement to promote communism.[115]

## April 2025 tariff escalation and Krebs memorandum

On April 9, 2025 Trump raised the tariff on Chinese imports to 125% hours after [[china|China]] boosted its duty on American goods to 84%, citing a lack of respect and setting Beijing apart from other trading partners, for whom he announced a 90-day pause on so-called reciprocal tariffs because they had reached out for talks rather than retaliating.[116] The [[white-house|White House]] said he "has a spine of steel and will not break," while Beijing vowed to "fight to the end," and about $19 trillion had been wiped off world equity markets since February 19 amid the worst Treasury selloff since the pandemic.[116] Trump said of the confrontation, "I don't blame China for what happened. I blame the people sitting right at this desk."[116] Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] said the pause had benefited more than 75 non-retaliating countries and praised the president's courage in staying the course.[116]

The same day Trump signed the memorandum [[addressing-risks-from-chris-krebs-and-government-censorship|"Addressing Risks from Chris Krebs and Government Censorship"]], directing agencies to revoke the security clearance of former [[cybersecurity-and-infrastructure-security-agency|CISA]] director [[chris-krebs|Chris Krebs]], suspend clearances at associated entities including [[sentinelone|SentinelOne]], and review his conduct in office.[116] Michigan Governor [[gretchen-whitmer|Gretchen Whitmer]], whom he praised in a White House meeting that day, was unexpectedly brought into the Oval Office while he signed a series of executive orders.[116]

Trump also said he had a specific deadline for reaching a deal with Tehran over its nuclear program ahead of the [[2025-oman-nuclear-talks|Oman talks]], confirming he would "absolutely" consider military action if negotiations failed.[116] Separately, [[goldman-sachs|Goldman Sachs]] reversed a recession call within 73 minutes of issuing it after his tariff pause was announced.[116]

## Hush-money case gag order (May 2023)

In May 2023, State Supreme Court Justice [[juan-merchan|Juan Merchan]] issued a social-media gag order barring Trump from posting any evidence in his New York [[trump-hush-money-case|hush-money case]], after prosecutors accused him of a history of targeting witnesses.[117] The order also prohibited him from publicly discussing witnesses or other evidence, and came as his legal team sought to move the state criminal case to federal court.[117] Trump had been arraigned on April 4, 2023 on 34 counts of falsifying business records over an alleged $130,000 non-disclosure payment to [[stormy-daniels|Stormy Daniels]] made two weeks before the 2016 election; he denied the affair and any wrongdoing.[117]

A contemporaneous ABC News/Washington Post poll found that only 44% of Americans viewed Trump, then 76, as too old to serve, compared with 68% who said the same of the 80-year-old [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], and showed Trump far surpassing Biden on perceived mental sharpness and physical health.[117]

## February 2025: DOGE, Riyadh diplomacy, and lawfare warnings

In mid-February 2025 Trump floated cutting the defense budget in half after things "settle down" with Russia and China, a sharp turn from Defense Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]]'s same-day insistence that "nobody can or should test the extent of America's willingness to invest in national security" as [[doge|DOGE]] arrived at the [[pentagon|Pentagon]].[118] Six of his 22 Cabinet-level picks still awaited [[united-states-senate|Senate]] confirmation, including [[kelly-loeffler|Kelly Loeffler]], [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] and [[linda-mcmahon|Linda McMahon]].[118]

Trump's administration was reported to have offered key concessions to [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] ahead of Ukraine talks in [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]], ending the Russian president's international isolation, shattering Western unity and casting doubt on U.S. commitments to Europe; the exclusion of European and Ukrainian diplomats from Riyadh prompted France to convene an emergency Paris meeting.[118] Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] framed the talks as a follow-up to Trump's call with Putin, while Kremlin spokesman [[dmitry-peskov|Dmitry Peskov]] said it was "hard to overestimate the significance" of that call.[118] [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelenskyy]] countered that the United States was trying to "please" Putin with a "quick win" ceasefire.[118]

Politico reported that Trump's opponents had little legal protection against the campaign promise, made over 100 times, to investigate and prosecute perceived political enemies, since the law on vindictive prosecutions has little teeth and presumes prosecutorial good faith.[118] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] rejected that presumption, citing revived statutes, buried evidence, witness tampering and [[adam-schiff|Adam Schiff]]'s "fictional fever dreams" as textbook bad faith, and argued that restoring public trust requires real consequences.[118] Meanwhile [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described Trump and [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] as "the two biggest Mind-Movers on the game board," running an inverted [[hegelian-dialectic|Hegelian Dialectic]] in which every solution implemented, from DOGE to peace, weakens the establishment while provoking it further.[118]

## September 2025 policy actions

On September 9, 2025 the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] agreed to hear on an expedited basis Trump's appeal over whether he may use emergency powers to impose tariffs, after the [[us-court-of-appeals-for-the-federal-circuit|Federal Circuit]] ruled in August that he lacked that authority; argument was set for the first week of November.[119] The same week the [[bureau-of-labor-statistics|Bureau of Labor Statistics]] disclosed a record downward revision of 911,000 jobs, weeks after Trump fired Commissioner [[erika-mcentarfer|Erika McEntarfer]] over what he called "phony" statistics.[119] Trump also signed a memorandum on September 9 ordering the health department and the [[food-and-drug-administration|FDA]] to crack down on direct-to-consumer pharmaceutical advertising, aligning with pledges by HHS Secretary [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]].[119] Separately, [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] lost Los Angeles Police Department protection after Trump had earlier cancelled the Biden directive extending her Secret Service detail.[119]

## April 2025 diplomacy: Crimea, Netanyahu call and Gulf tour

In April 2025 Trump's administration advanced a peace proposal that would have the United States formally recognize [[crimea|Crimea]] as Russian territory and freeze the war's front lines, with sanctions on [[russia|Russia]] eventually lifted under a future accord; the proposals were presented to Ukraine in Paris and confronted Ukrainian and European officials meeting in London.[120] Contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] observed that Trump had already publicly conceded two of [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]]'s three prewar demands — Donbas recognition and Ukrainian exclusion from [[nato|NATO]] — and predicted the third would follow "because the deals are already done."[120]

On 22 April 2025 Trump spoke by telephone with Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] and wrote on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that the call covered "numerous subjects including Trade, Iran, etc.," went "very well," and that the two leaders "are on the same side of every issue"; he notably omitted Gaza and the 59 hostages held there amid the ceasefire impasse.[120] The same day press secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] announced that Trump would tour [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]], [[qatar|Qatar]] and the [[united-arab-emirates|United Arab Emirates]] from 13 to 16 May, his second foreign trip since returning to office.[120] Reporting also indicated that Trump had dissuaded [[israel|Israel]] from striking [[iran|Iran]]'s nuclear sites in the near term in favour of diplomacy.[120]

Badlands contributors read these threads together: [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued the stage was set for Trump and Putin to strike a deal in Riyadh, describing Trump as "a master of narrative warfare" who had baited hardline calls for kinetic war against Iran into the open, while [[chris-paul|Chris Paul]] contended the optics showed Netanyahu aligning with Trump's positions rather than the reverse.[120]

## Tariff meeting with retail chief executives

On 21 April 2025 Trump met at the [[white-house|White House]] with the chief executives of three of the largest American retailers — [[walmart|Walmart]]'s Doug McMillon, [[target|Target]]'s Brian Cornell and [[home-depot|Home Depot]]'s Ted Decker — to discuss how his sweeping tariff plans could affect their import-heavy business models.[120] A Lowe's representative had been expected but did not attend, and the three participating companies afterwards issued nearly identical statements calling the meeting productive.[120] Burning Bright argued that firms such as Apple, Walmart and Amazon are better understood as nation states than as companies, and that "capitulation comes in many forms."[120]

## Other April 2025 items

Two federal judges extended blocks on deportations of Venezuelan migrants under the [[alien-enemies-act|Alien Enemies Act]], with Judge [[charlotte-sweeney|Charlotte Sweeney]] imposing a 21-day notice requirement and Judge [[alvin-hellerstein|Alvin Hellerstein]] weighing a 10-day rule.[120] Former Vice President [[al-gore|Al Gore]] compared the administration to Nazi Germany in a San Francisco climate speech, saying it was "trying to create their own preferred version of reality."[120] Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] warned that the US-China trade war was "not sustainable," and the [[food-and-drug-administration|FDA]] under [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] announced the [[2025-fda-synthetic-food-dye-phase-out|synthetic food dye phase-out]].[120]

## November 2025 developments

In mid-November 2025 Trump reversed months of resistance and endorsed the congressional effort to release files related to sex offender [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]], declaring, "We have nothing to hide," as the House prepared to vote on authorizing release of Epstein records in the government's possession.[121] He said the incessant focus on Epstein was undercutting Republicans' ability to tout successes on economic, security, and diplomatic issues, writing on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that "House Republicans should vote to release the Epstein files, because we have nothing to hide, and it's time to move on from this Democrat Hoax perpetrated by Radical Left Lunatics."[121] He added that the [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]] had already turned over tens of thousands of pages and was "looking at various Democrat operatives (Bill Clinton, Reid Hoffman, Larry Summers, etc.) and their relationship to Epstein," and that the House Oversight Committee "can have whatever they are legally entitled to, I DON'T CARE!"[121] [[canncon|CannCon]] argued that every attempt to hurt Trump with limited leaks backfires and buries more of the leakers' own.[121]

On Venezuela, Trump said on Sunday, November 16, in Florida that he was open to talks with President [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]] — "We may be having some discussions with Maduro, and we'll see how that turns out" — while repeating accusations that Caracas helped cartels smuggle drugs and released "hundreds of thousands" of violent criminals across the border, and noting that the [[state-department|State Department]]'s [[cartel-de-los-soles|Cartel de los Soles]] terrorist designation would permit strikes on Venezuelan soil.[121] On Russia, Putin aide [[yury-ushakov|Yury Ushakov]] said the understanding reached at the August Alaska summit between Trump and [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] remained in force and continued to serve as the basis for settlement talks.[121]

Trump's use of a newly installed [[white-house|White House]] speaker system also drew coverage: on Friday, November 14, with nothing on his public schedule except a trip to Mar-a-Lago, music from "The Phantom of the Opera" was audible outside the building, followed later by James Brown's "It's a Man's Man's Man's World."[121] Press Secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] said Trump could control the music from an iPad in the Oval Office and was "testing the speakers for what will be the best event in the history of the White House."[121] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read an accompanying Truth Social image as a Bruce Wayne–League of Shadows reference, arguing that the 1986-and-later Dark Knight was directly inspired by Phantom of the Opera and that both stories feature a masked, troubled genius operating in a lair beneath a grand structure of a once-opulent city, concluding: "We're in a War of Stories."[121]

## April 2025 policy actions

Trump's executive order overhauling U.S. elections placed the [[election-assistance-commission|U.S. Election Assistance Commission]] at its center, directing the agency to update the national voter registration form to add a proof of citizenship requirement, though whether a president may order an independent agency to act was expected to be litigated.[122] On April 23, 2025, he told reporters in the Oval Office that he believed a deal to end the Russia-Ukraine war had been reached — "I think we have a deal with both, I hope they do it" — while conceding, "I think we have a deal with Russia. We have to get a deal with [[volodymyr-zelensky|Zelensky]]."[122]

The same period saw the Department of Education announce the restart of collections on defaulted student loans beginning May 5, 2025, affecting roughly 5.3 million borrowers, a move Badlands contributor [[gmoney|GMONEY]] criticized as a "really dumb move" that targeted students instead of "the real villains."[122] On April 23 Trump also signed seven executive orders on education, covering K-12 discipline, higher education accreditation, foreign funding transparency, AI in schools, support for Historically Black Colleges and Universities, and workforce training.[122] Trump Tower in New York City was closed to visitors that Wednesday after a man believed to be a member of a climate extremist group spray-painted "USA" in green on the seal of Trump as the 45th president and displayed a "Game Over" banner before being handcuffed and removed.[122] Trump administration officials, along with some congressional Republicans, were reported to be working to shield oil and gas companies from legal liability for climate-related damages as researchers published a Nature paper attributing $28 trillion in extreme-heat damage from 1991 to 2020 to emissions from 111 fossil fuel companies.[122]

## Trade, aid and intelligence actions (March 2025)

In March 2025 Trump postponed 25% tariffs on many Mexican imports for a month, citing President [[claudia-sheinbaum|Claudia Sheinbaum]]'s progress on drug smuggling and illegal immigration, prompting a mass celebration in Mexico City rather than the retaliation Sheinbaum had planned.[123] Simultaneously he escalated the [[2025-us-canada-trade-war|trade war with Canada]], pledging to "just get it all back" on April 2 through reciprocal tariffs after Ontario Premier [[doug-ford|Doug Ford]] imposed a 25% surcharge on electricity exported to Michigan, New York and Minnesota and threatened to cut it off entirely.[123] Trump wrote on Truth Social that "Canada is a Tariff abuser, and always has been, but the United States is not going to be subsidizing Canada any longer."[123]

That same week his administration canceled 83% of US foreign aid contracts through Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]], revoked former President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s access to the [[presidents-daily-brief|President's Daily Brief]] along with the security clearances of numerous former officials, and hosted the first-ever [[white-house-digital-asset-summit|White House Digital Asset Summit]], where Trump said: "Last year, I promised to make America the Bitcoin superpower of the world, AND WE'RE TAKING HISTORIC ACTION TO DELIVER ON THAT PROMISE."[123] His envoy [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] prepared to meet [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] in Moscow following US–Ukraine ceasefire talks in Saudi Arabia, while hostage envoy [[adam-boehler|Adam Boehler]]'s unprecedented direct talks with [[hamas|Hamas]] in Doha strained relations with Israel.[123] On currency policy, The Economist noted that Trump and Vice President [[jd-vance|J.D. Vance]] had both argued the dollar's strength was a problem for American industry, even as Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] maintained the strong-dollar policy.[123]

## April 2024 campaign and legal developments

Polling in April 2024 showed Trump had upended much of the black and Hispanic voting support Democrats had enjoyed since at least Lyndon Johnson's "Great Society," and in some instances back to FDR's New Deal.[124] A Wall Street Journal poll showed Trump's support among black men in swing states had moved to 30 percent, compared with just 11 percent of black men nationally in 2020; among black women, support rose from 6 percent in 2020 to 11 percent in April 2024.[124]

Trump met Kayla Montgomery, a young Republican political consultant who engages black professionals and students in the Atlanta area, during an impromptu campaign stop at a Chick-fil-A restaurant; she told him "I don't care what the media tells you, President Trump—we support you!" and video of the pair hugging went viral, even as media and Democrats dismissed the interaction as staged.[124] Days later Trump left his trial in Manhattan to visit a bodega in Washington Heights, a mostly black and immigrant community, where he received what Badlands described as a hero's welcome.[124]

Badlands contributor [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued that black and brown voters were "not moving towards Trump so much as moving away from the uniparty left and its rainbow communism," citing the economy alongside bigotry, the mutilation of children, disarmament, three theaters of war, the open border, the attack on energy and the condition of the sitting president; in her framing voters were "realizing they were with Trump — his policies and platform and vision for America — all along."[124]

Also in April 2024, boxer [[ryan-garcia|Ryan Garcia]] announced from Mar-a-Lago that he would vote for Trump and offered to help the campaign.[124] Unsealed filings in the classified documents case revealed the FBI code name [[plasmic-echo|Plasmic Echo]] for its investigation into Trump's presidential records and alleged coordination between the Biden [[white-house|White House]], the National Archives and the [[department-of-justice|DOJ]] in targeting him since 2021.[124] Commentary in the same brief contrasted Trump's rapid, surgical destruction of [[isis|ISIS]] — carried out without carpet bombing civilian settlements — with the Israeli approach in [[gaza|Gaza]].[124]

## December 2024 transition items

At the close of December 2024, Trump weighed in on the [[h-1b-visa-program|H-1B visa]] fight roiling his coalition, telling the New York Post that he likes the visa program and uses it himself while declining to endorse any expansion — the same president-elect who had fired Tennessee Valley Authority board members for using foreign workers over Americans.[125] He also celebrated a Texas federal judge's ruling blocking the disposal of border wall materials, calling it on Truth Social a "major, crucial WIN for America" and welcoming the judge-ordered investigation into "the illegal selling of the materials"; he pledged to rebuild the wall to protect the country from violent migrant crime, fentanyl smuggling, sex trafficking and terror attacks.[125] See [[texas-border-wall-materials-sale|Texas border wall materials sale]].[125]

Separately, Trump asked the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] to delay the deadline for the potential [[tiktok-ban|TikTok ban]], which was slated for the day before his inauguration, arguing through appellate attorney [[d-john-sauer|D. John Sauer]] that he "alone possesses the consummate dealmaking expertise, the electoral mandate, and the political will to negotiate a resolution to save the platform while addressing the national security concerns."[125] Badlands commentary in the same brief predicted that heaven and earth would be moved to convince Trump that America must go to war with Iran, while casting him as "the infinite player" and "a master negotiator" capable of resolving the Syrian collapse.[125]

## November 2025: tariff case, election losses, and Gaza force

On Tuesday, November 4, 2025, on the eve of Supreme Court oral arguments over the legality of his tariffs, Trump said the United States would be "virtually defenseless" against other nations if the Court struck them down, posting on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that "Tomorrow's United States Supreme Court case is, literally, LIFE OR DEATH for our Country" and crediting tariffs with the country's economic and national security.[126] His officials played down the effects of a possible loss, saying overturned tariffs would be reimposed under other trade authorities.[126] [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] observed that within twelve hours Trump had also said the country's fate depended on the Senate ending the [[filibuster|filibuster]].[126]

That same day, Democrats swept the [[2025-off-year-elections|first major elections of Trump's second term]], with [[zohran-mamdani|Zohran Mamdani]] projected to win New York City, [[abigail-spanberger|Abigail Spanberger]] and [[mikie-sherrill|Mikie Sherrill]] winning the Virginia and New Jersey governorships, and California voters approving a Democratic redistricting push; Trump blamed the losses on the government shutdown and on his own absence from the ballot.[126] Mamdani tied bomb threats at New Jersey polling places to the administration's rhetoric about voter fraud, drawing a rebuke from press secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]].[126]

In the same period the United States circulated a draft UN Security Council resolution creating an [[international-security-force|International Security Force]] for [[gaza|Gaza]] with a mandate through 2027, which [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read as evidence that Trump intends Turkish troops to participate and the [[palestinian-authority|Palestinian Authority]] under [[mahmoud-abbas|Mahmoud Abbas]] to govern Gaza long-term, over [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]]'s objections.[126] Separately, [[verizon|Verizon]] disclosed that Republican lawmakers' call records had been turned over to the Justice Department under [[arctic-frost|Arctic Frost]], the investigation that underpinned Trump's federal indictment.[126]

## November 2025: Mamdani meeting, Xi call, and the Genesis Mission

On Friday, November 21, 2025, Trump met New York mayor-elect [[zohran-mamdani|Zohran Mamdani]] at the [[white-house|White House]] in a session that scrambled the online MAGA ecosystem; instead of the "socialist showdown" Fox News had billed, the meeting produced smiles and Trump's endorsement of Mamdani, with the president saying, "I feel very confident that he can do a very good job."[127]
