On the evening of October 1, 2023, Trump announced on Truth Social that he would appear in court the following morning for the start of his [[new-york-civil-fraud-trial|New York civil fraud trial]], writing, "I'm going to Court tomorrow morning to fight for my name and reputation."[169] Law enforcement and court employees in Lower Manhattan had already been preparing security for his expected appearance on Monday and possibly Tuesday.[169] His plans had become clearer after a federal judge in Florida granted his request to postpone a deposition in a separate matter that conflicted with the New York trial's start.[169] Trump flew to New York City on Sunday evening following a campaign event in Ottumwa, Iowa, and stayed the night at Trump Tower; the case, brought in September 2022 by Attorney General [[letitia-james|Letitia James]] against Trump, his eldest sons, their companies and several executives, was set to begin at 10 a.m. Monday.[169]

The same weekend, Trump called Representative [[jamaal-bowman|Jamaal Bowman]]'s conduct in the [[2023-cannon-building-fire-alarm-incident|Cannon Building fire alarm incident]] egregious.[169] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] answered the "walls are closing in" framing by arguing that it was [[joe-biden|Biden]] on trial — in Congress and in the court of public opinion — and that the [[media-industrial-complex|Media Industrial Complex]] and establishment politicians on both sides of an "illusory aisle" would rally to defend the Biden Crime Family, the [[uniparty|Uniparty]] establishment, and the [[deep-state|Deep State]].[169] He closed with the line that [[barack-obama|Obama]] promised "Hope and Change" while "Donald Trump is going to bring it."[169]

In congressional politics that week, Burning Bright also described the clash between Speaker [[kevin-mccarthy|Kevin McCarthy]] and Representative [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]] as [[kayfabe|kayfabe]], asserting that both men were Trump's choices — one for Speaker, the other for opposition to that Speaker.[169]

## Iranian assassination threat briefing (September 2024)

On Tuesday, September 24, 2024, top U.S. intelligence officials briefed Trump about threats from [[iran|Iran]] to assassinate him, according to campaign spokesman [[steven-cheung|Steven Cheung]].[170] Cheung said Trump had been briefed by the [[office-of-the-director-of-national-intelligence|Office of the Director of National Intelligence]] "regarding real and specific threats from Iran to assassinate him in an effort to destabilize and sow chaos in the United States," and that intelligence officials had identified continued and coordinated attacks that had heightened in the preceding months, with law enforcement across all agencies working to protect Trump and keep the election free from interference.[170] An ODNI spokesperson acknowledged the briefing but declined to address specifics; Iran's permanent mission to the United Nations did not immediately respond to a request for comment.[170]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] highlighted Trump's remark that the plotters "could be Iran-based. They could also be something else," arguing that Trump always signals his real thinking between beats.[170] Burning Bright characterized "Iran" as a "Deep Proxy State" — a nation-state analogue of ISIS, Hamas or [[azov-battalion|Azov]], all of which he said find common footing in Western intelligence — and pointed to the number of [[state-department|State Department]], NSC, intelligence and media figures with ties to Iran.[170] He concluded that two things can be true simultaneously: that "Iran" is attempting to assassinate Trump, and that "Iran" is the mask worn by a clandestine criminal cabal responsible for many modern color revolutions, predicting that once the thread unravels U.S. intelligence, media and NSC figures would face a reformed justice system.[170]

## Commentary on Zelensky and Ukraine aid (September 2024)

In September 2024 Trump called Ukrainian president [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] "the greatest salesman in history" for his ability to extract cash from Washington to finance the war with Russia, telling supporters that "every time he comes into the country, he walks away with $60 billion."[170] The remark followed Zelensky publicly casting doubt on Trump's claim that he could promptly end the conflict, and came after the U.S. approved a $61 billion military aid package for Kyiv in April over months of Republican opposition.[170] Trump added that Zelensky wanted [[kamala-harris|Harris]] to win the election badly, "but I would do differently — I will work out peace."[170]

Burning Bright argued that Zelensky had advanced the cause of public awakening more than almost any other player seeming to be on the other side, noting that globalist media, [[nato|NATO]] and Ukrainian leadership all wanted him ousted, and framing Trump's "perfect phone call" remark as an endorsement of a man conducting an anti-corruption purge in the borderlands: "Control goes both ways."[170]

## Posture during the October 2023 Israel crisis

During the opening weeks of the Israel–Hamas war, Trump adopted what Badlands commentary described as a deliberately mixed public posture: casting doubt on [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]]'s leadership near the start of the Israeli deployments while defending Israel and strongly condemning the terror strikes throughout the week, and reminding Americans that none of it would be happening had he still been in office.[171] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] noted that Trump also said he would fix the situation — in 2025 — which he read as having "'The Plan' written all over it."[171] The same commentary contrasted Trump's positioning with [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s [[kobayashi-maru|no-win scenario]] on the same terrain.[171]

Separately, [[patel-patriot|Patel Patriot]] pointed out that the two-state solution recognizing Palestine promoted by [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] at their October 2023 Beijing meeting had also been proposed by Trump during his time in office.[171] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]], contrasting Biden's record with Trump's, cited the relocation of the US Embassy in Israel and the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]] as evidence that "45 proved that peace is possible" when genuinely pursued.[171] Trump was also identified as a close ally of [[jim-jordan|Jim Jordan]], whose speakership bid stalled that week.[171]

## $175 million bond in the New York civil fraud appeal (April 2024)

On Monday, April 1, 2024, Trump posted a $175 million bond in his New York civil fraud case, halting collection of the more than $454 million he owed and preventing the state from seizing his assets while he appealed, according to a court filing.[172] A New York appellate court had given the former president 10 days to put up the money after a panel of judges agreed the previous month to slash the amount needed to stop the clock on enforcement.[172] The bond was essentially a placeholder meant to guarantee payment if the judgment is upheld; if it is, the presumptive Republican presidential nominee would have to pay the state the whole sum, which grows with daily interest, and if he wins he would owe nothing and recover the money posted.[172] Until the appeals court intervened, New York Attorney General [[letitia-james|Letitia James]] had been poised to begin collecting the judgment, possibly by seizing some of Trump's marquee properties.[172]

Badlands contributor [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] wrote that "the regime players tried everything they could to prevent this moment," but that Trump was now appealing Judge [[arthur-engoron|Arthur Engoron]]'s "egregious abuses of power and discretion" in the trial, describing the penalties as "cruel and unusual by any definition" and noting that the Appellate court had agreed to hear arguments in September — "interesting timing."[172] See [[new-york-civil-fraud-trial]].[172]

## December 2023 ballot and immunity litigation

By late December 2023 Trump faced parallel legal efforts to keep him off state ballots and to try him before the 2024 election.[173] A federal judge in West Virginia, [[irene-berger|Irene Berger]], rejected a bid by candidate [[john-anthony-castro|John Anthony Castro]] to strike him from that state's ballot, holding Castro lacked standing; Castro had filed at least two dozen such suits nationwide under Section 3 of the [[fourteenth-amendment|Fourteenth Amendment]], with an Arizona court dismissing another earlier that month and the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] declining in October to hear his Florida appeal.[173]

On December 19 the [[colorado-supreme-court|Colorado Supreme Court]] barred Trump from the state's ballot on the ground that he had "engaged in insurrection" by inciting supporters to storm the Capitol on January 6, 2021, and his lawyers immediately announced an appeal to the Supreme Court; commentators expected a narrow reversal that would avoid the insurrection question (see [[colorado-ballot-disqualification-case|the Colorado ballot disqualification case]]).[173]

Separately, the Supreme Court declined to expedite consideration of Trump's claim of [[presidential-immunity|presidential immunity]] in the 2020 election interference case, leaving a Washington federal appeals court to review the issue first with argument set for January 9, 2024, and rebuffing Special Counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]]'s request to short-circuit the process.[173]

Meanwhile polling and reporting documented rising [[black-support-for-trump|black support for Trump]], which had more than tripled since 2016 and exceeded 20 percent in some surveys.[173]

## May 2025 Gulf tour and Iran talks

On the third day of a Middle East tour in May 2025, Trump told a business round table in Doha, [[qatar|Qatar]] that his administration was "getting close to maybe doing a deal" with [[iran|Iran]] over nuclear weapons, saying "Iran has sort of agreed to the terms" without providing details.[174] It was not clear whether the comments reflected actual progress or were an exaggeration for political or negotiating reasons, and the claim could not be immediately confirmed with Iranian officials.[174] Trump repeated that he wanted Iran to become a great country but that "they can't have a nuclear weapon," adding, "we're in very serious negotiations with Iran for long-term peace"; the talks had been taking place behind closed doors in [[oman|Oman]], a country not on his itinerary.[174]

In the [[united-arab-emirates|United Arab Emirates]], Trump pledged to strengthen ties in a meeting with President [[mohamed-bin-zayed-al-nahyan|Sheikh Mohamed bin Zayed Al Nahyan]], saying "I have absolutely no doubt that the relationship will only get bigger and better," and referencing the UAE's pledge to invest $1.4 trillion in the United States over ten years conveyed by [[tahnoon-bin-zayed-al-nahyan|Sheikh Tahnoon bin Zayed Al Nahyan]].[174] The visit followed a stop in Qatar, where Doha announced plans to invest $10 billion in a US military facility.[174] In [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]], Trump said "sometimes, you need enemies to do the job, and do it right. Some of the closest friends of the United States of America are nations we fought wars against in generations past" — remarks [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read as all but confirming the existence of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]].[174]

Separately, White House official [[sebastian-gorka|Sebastian Gorka]] said a Trump meeting with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] to finalize a Russia–Ukraine peace agreement was "imminent," while Trump himself mulled travelling to [[turkey|Türkiye]] "if something happened" during expected Russian–Ukrainian discussions there.[174]

During the same trip Trump said he had told [[apple|Apple]] chief executive [[tim-cook|Tim Cook]] he had "a little problem" with the company's plan to manufacture iPhones in [[india|India]], telling him, "We're not interested in you building in India. India can take care of themselves."[174]

Badlands commentary also noted that April 2025 inflation data showed egg prices dropping 12.7 percent, the biggest monthly decline since 1984, vindicating Trump's repeated claims that egg prices were falling even as [[cnn|CNN]] disputed the timing of his statements and Agriculture Secretary [[brooke-rollins|Brooke Rollins]] had offered a more conservative estimate of summer normalization.[174]


## December 2025 developments
Speaking at a White House Christmas reception on Sunday, December 14, 2025, Trump claimed Democrats "rigged" the 2020 presidential election and said his administration possessed overwhelming evidence that would eventually be made public: "We have all the ammunition, all the stuff, and you'll see it come out... It's coming out in truckloads."[175]

Trump's new National Security Strategy rattled European leaders, with the European Union's former top diplomat Josep Borrell calling it "a declaration of political war" and European diplomats in Washington privately describing the Western alliance as over.[175] Italian Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni cast the strategy as a wake-up call underscoring Washington's intention to step back from the continent and force Europe to bolster its own defenses.[175]

On domestic policy, Trump said that Republicans "don't need" another large legislative package to move through Capitol Hill "because we got everything" in the One Big Beautiful Bill Act, even as some Republicans pitched a second, smaller partisan bill on affordability ahead of the midterm elections.[175]

On Venezuela, Trump told reporters at the White House on Friday, December 12, 2025 that U.S. land strikes on alleged Venezuela-linked drug operations could begin "pretty soon," claiming maritime narcotics trafficking had dropped by 92% and that "we knocked out 96% of the drugs coming in by water," while providing no details on targets or scope.[175] Nobel laureate María Corina Machado said in her first U.S. interview after the award that she "absolutely support[s] President Trump's strategy" in Venezuela.[175]

The White House also withdrew the nomination of Joe Francescon as deputy director of the National Security Agency after Trump ally Laura Loomer revealed a 2023 donation by Francescon to a Democratic congressional campaign.[175] Separately, Trump administration lawyers moved to block witness testimony in Judge James Boasberg's revived contempt inquiry into the administration's use of the Alien Enemies Act.[175] A meeting between Trump and Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu was proposed for January 29 to discuss Phase Two of the Gaza Peace Plan, including placing Turkish soldiers in Gaza and a U.S. base in southern Israel.[175]


## December 2025 developments
Speaking at a White House Christmas reception on Sunday, December 14, 2025, Trump claimed Democrats "rigged" the 2020 presidential election and said his administration possessed overwhelming evidence that would eventually be made public: "We have all the ammunition, all the stuff, and you'll see it come out... It's coming out in truckloads."[175]

Trump's new National Security Strategy rattled European leaders, with the European Union's former top diplomat Josep Borrell calling it "a declaration of political war" and European diplomats in Washington privately describing the Western alliance as over.[175] Italian Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni cast the strategy as a wake-up call underscoring Washington's intention to step back from the continent and force Europe to bolster its own defenses.[175]

On domestic policy, Trump said that Republicans "don't need" another large legislative package to move through Capitol Hill "because we got everything" in the One Big Beautiful Bill Act, even as some Republicans pitched a second, smaller partisan bill on affordability ahead of the midterm elections.[175]

On Venezuela, Trump told reporters at the White House on Friday, December 12, 2025 that U.S. land strikes on alleged Venezuela-linked drug operations could begin "pretty soon," claiming maritime narcotics trafficking had dropped by 92% and that "we knocked out 96% of the drugs coming in by water," while providing no details on targets or scope.[175] Nobel laureate María Corina Machado said in her first U.S. interview after the award that she "absolutely support[s] President Trump's strategy" in Venezuela.[175]

The White House also withdrew the nomination of Joe Francescon as deputy director of the National Security Agency after Trump ally Laura Loomer revealed a 2023 donation by Francescon to a Democratic congressional campaign.[175] Separately, Trump administration lawyers moved to block witness testimony in Judge James Boasberg's revived contempt inquiry into the administration's use of the Alien Enemies Act.[175] A meeting between Trump and Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu was proposed for January 29 to discuss Phase Two of the Gaza Peace Plan, including placing Turkish soldiers in Gaza and a U.S. base in southern Israel.[175]

## March 18, 2025: judges, Putin call, and the JFK files

March 18, 2025 saw Trump simultaneously engaged on several fronts covered in the Badlands News Brief. He called for the impeachment of Judge [[james-boasberg|James Boasberg]], who had ruled against his deportation of hundreds of Venezuelans over the preceding weekend, prompting House Republicans to introduce articles of impeachment within hours and drawing a rare public rebuke from Chief Justice [[john-roberts|John Roberts]].[176] Two further judges ruled against the administration the same day: [[ana-reyes|Ana Reyes]] blocked the executive order barring transgender people from military service, and [[theodore-chuang|Theodore Chuang]] indefinitely blocked [[doge|DOGE]] from further dismantling [[usaid|USAID]].[176] Badlands writers argued that Trump was executing constitutional authorities under the [[alien-enemies-act|Alien Enemies Act]] that were not subject to judicial review.[176]

The same day Trump held a telephone call with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] on a Ukraine ceasefire, securing agreement on a limited 30-day halt to strikes on energy facilities though not the full 30-day ceasefire Kyiv had accepted.[176] European capitals were reported to be in a "mood of alarm" ahead of the call.[176] Trump's earlier remark to [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] that he did not "have the cards" was read by Badlands contributor Burning Bright as an assertion of who does hold power at "the advent of the Golden Age."[176]

Tuesday night also brought the release of the last trove of [[jfk-files-release|JFK assassination files]], pursuant to Trump's January executive order requiring unredacted disclosure; he estimated 80,000 pages and told reporters, "I don't believe we're going to redact anything."[176] In bonus coverage, Trump labelled [[canada|Canada]] "one of the nastiest countries to deal with," saying of former prime minister [[justin-trudeau|Justin Trudeau]] — whom he called "Governor Trudeau" — that "his people were nasty and they weren't telling the truth."[176]

## July 2024: Bitcoin conference, Orbán, and Epstein deflection

In July 2024, as the presumptive Republican nominee, Trump was announced as a speaker at the annual [[bitcoin|Bitcoin]] conference in Nashville, Tennessee, scheduled for 30 minutes on July 27, the final day of the event.[177] He had leaned into crypto rhetoric since endorsing the industry at his NFT gala that spring and had written pro-Bitcoin policies into the core Republican Party platform, contrasting himself with the Biden administration despite misgivings about the technology during his first term.[177]

On July 11, 2024, Hungarian prime minister [[viktor-orban|Viktor Orbán]] flew to Mar-a-Lago to meet Trump at 7 p.m. immediately after the [[2024-nato-washington-summit|NATO Washington Summit]], having separately met [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]], [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] and [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelenskyy]] while pursuing his own ceasefire plan.[177] European officials at the summit were reported to be alarmed at the prospect of [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] being replaced in November by a Trump they regarded as hostile to the alliance.[177]

During the same period, some Biden supporters sought to deflect from questions about the president's acuity by reviving Trump's past associations with [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]], both online and in [[congress|Congress]], after documents from Epstein's 2006 Florida investigation were released.[177] The 158-page grand jury transcript, which showed prosecutors heard testimony that Epstein raped girls as young as 14 before granting a lenient plea deal, makes no mention of Trump.[177] Badlands commentary characterized the effort as projection and evidence of panic rather than control.[177]

## July 2025: Obama, Gaza, and the Epstein counter-offensive

On July 22, 2025, following the release of new evidence by Director of National Intelligence [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]], Trump said that [[barack-obama|Barack Obama]] and members of his administration had "manufactured" intelligence to tie him to Russia, describing Obama as the "ringleader" of [[russiagate-hoax|Russiagate]] and calling for a criminal investigation.[178] Three days later, on July 25, he said Obama "owes me big" following the Supreme Court ruling affirming broad [[presidential-immunity|presidential immunity]], adding that "he's done criminal acts, there's no question about it. But he has immunity, and it probably helps him a lot… it doesn't help the people around him at all."[178]

Trump's long-running feud with [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] was revisited in the same period: during his first term he had said the Fed chair had "No guts, no sense, no vision!" while GOP lawmakers acted as a firewall — a check that by mid-2025 was eroding as more Republicans demanded Powell's ouster over the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]]'s refusal to cut rates and the cost of its headquarters renovations.[178] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued Trump neither would nor wished to fire Powell, casting the chair as "a bridge to the Golden Age."[178]

Departing for a four-day visit to Scotland on Friday, July 25, Trump redirected reporters asking about the [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]] case toward former President [[bill-clinton|Bill Clinton]] and former Treasury Secretary [[larry-summers|Larry Summers]]: "You ought to be speaking about Larry Summers. You ought to be speaking about some of his friends that are hedge fund guys… You ought to be speaking about Bill Clinton."[178] He accused Clinton of visiting Epstein's private island and said he himself had never done so.[178] Burning Bright read the maneuver as Trump accelerating rather than fearing the Epstein "psyop," baiting opponents into overcommitting to a largely defanged narrative so that his own exoneration would rise in proportion to their collapse.[178]

On Sunday, July 27, asked in Scotland about images of starving children emerging from [[gaza|Gaza]], Trump called them "terrible" but immediately pivoted to say "They're stealing the food," an apparent allusion to [[hamas|Hamas]], while stating that "Israel will have to make a decision" on the war — even as Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] denied any Israeli role in the aid shortages.[178] See [[rabbis-open-letter-on-gaza-hunger-2025|Rabbis' open letter on Gaza hunger]].[178]

A [[wall-street-journal|Wall Street Journal]] poll released July 26 found Trump's approval rating still underwater, but the [[democratic-party|Democratic Party]] faring considerably worse, with voters trusting Republicans more on the economy, inflation, tariffs and foreign policy despite disapproving of Trump's tariff handling by 17 points.[178] See [[2025-wall-street-journal-poll-on-democratic-party-favorability|the 2025 WSJ favorability poll]].[178]

## Transition Period: Ceasefire Announcement and Inauguration (January 2025)

On January 15, 2025, while still president-elect, Trump announced on his TruthSocial platform that Israel and Hamas had struck a deal securing the release of all hostages held by the group, writing "We have a deal for the hostages in the Middle East" and that "They will be released shortly."[179] The agreement, approved in Qatar, was reported to involve a 42-day ceasefire and an exchange of prisoners including all Israelis taken captive in the October 7, 2023 incursion from Gaza.[179] Trump framed the outcome as a direct product of his election win, stating that the "EPIC ceasefire agreement could have only happened as a result of our Historic Victory in November," because it signaled that his administration would seek peace and negotiate deals to ensure the safety of Americans and allies.[179] His Middle East envoy [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] joined Biden negotiator Brett McGurk in Doha for the final push, and both the incoming and outgoing presidents claimed full credit once the deal was announced.[179]

Trump's swearing-in was scheduled for noon Eastern on January 20, 2025 at the United States Capitol, an event that collided on the calendar with the opening day of the World Economic Forum's annual meeting in Davos, forcing billionaires and chief executives invited to both to choose between face time with the incoming administration and networking in Switzerland.[179] Badlands commentary noted that the stated theme of the inauguration invoked "the Founder's commitment to future generations of Americans to preserve the continuity and stability of our democratic system of government," wording said to closely track the Federal Mission Resilience strategy released in conjunction with Executive Order 13961 — the order central to the [[devolution|devolution]] thesis — prompting the observation that the "Devo term is almost coming to an end."[179] Former first lady [[michelle-obama|Michelle Obama]] announced she would skip the ceremony, leaving her husband to attend alone.[179]

## May 2025: tax bill, Ramaphosa, and Gaza (Badlands News Brief, May 22, 2025)

On May 21, 2025, the [[house-rules-committee|House Rules Committee]] narrowly approved Trump's sprawling tax and immigration package in a late-night vote, clearing the path for the full chamber to pass the measure as soon as the following morning.[180] The legislation would extend trillions of dollars in tax cuts from the first-term Tax Cuts and Jobs Act, advance new campaign promises such as no taxes on tips or overtime, and fund hundreds of billions of dollars in spending on mass deportation and national defense.[180]

The same day, Trump confronted South African President [[cyril-ramaphosa|Cyril Ramaphosa]] at the [[white-house|White House]] with a five-minute video montage alleging discrimination and violence against white farmers, telling staff to "Turn the lights down, and just put this on."[180] Badlands commentary read the presentation as a calculated move to inject "genocide" into the news cycle outside the context of Gaza, noting that Ramaphosa's government is the petitioner in the [[international-court-of-justice|ICJ]] case against Israel.[180]

Badlands also reported signs that Trump was pivoting against [[benjamin-netanyahu|Netanyahu]], whom the commentary described as hellbent on pursuing conflict.[180] Separately, [[benjamin-netanyahu|Netanyahu]] named implementation of Trump's "revolutionary" plan to relocate Gaza's civilians as a condition for ending the war.[180] Trump also announced a Presidential Delegation to Quito, Ecuador for the inauguration of [[daniel-noboa|Daniel Noboa]] on May 24, 2025, led by Health and Human Services Secretary [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]][180]

## October 2025: cartel war powers, shutdown leverage, and Gaza plan

In a confidential notice to congressional committees in early October 2025, the Trump administration informed [[congress|Congress]] that the president had determined the United States was engaged in a formal "armed conflict" with drug cartels designated as terrorist organizations, and that suspected smugglers for those groups were "unlawful combatants" — a rationale advanced to justify three military strikes on boats in the Caribbean Sea that killed all 17 people aboard.[181] Legal specialists said the [[armed-conflict-designation-against-drug-cartels|armed conflict designation]] cemented Trump's claim to extraordinary wartime powers, including the authority to kill enemy fighters posing no immediate threat, detain them indefinitely without trial, and try them in military courts.[181] Badlands commentary tied the move to [[campbell-v-clinton|Campbell v. Clinton]] as precedent for judicial deference to presidential war-making.[181]

During the government shutdown that began October 1, 2025, Trump told One America News Network that he had not wanted a shutdown but warned that "there could be firings, and that's their fault," and that Democrats' favored projects "could be permanently cut."[181] He added that although "a lot of people are saying, 'Trump wanted this,'" he did not, but that he was "allowed to cut things that should have been approved in the first place."[181] Construction of the 90,000-square-foot [[white-house-ballroom|White House ballroom]] continued through the shutdown because it was privately funded by Trump and other donors rather than by a FY26 appropriation.[181]

On the Middle East, Trump unveiled a proposal to end the war in [[gaza|Gaza]] that Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] accepted and that Arab states praised, while Israeli Finance Minister [[bezalel-smotrich|Bezalel Smotrich]] attacked it as a return to "Oslo-era delusions."[181] Badlands contributor GhostofBasedPatrickHenry noted that Trump had amplified both Smotrich's criticism and commentary from Israeli opposition leader [[benny-gantz|Benny Gantz]], reading the juxtaposition as a signal that Trump expected Netanyahu's security cabinet to reject or subvert the deal.[181] [[qatar|Qatar]] said it was "satisfied" with security guarantees obtained after a call with Trump and Netanyahu.[181]

## Gag order and attacks on Jack Smith (October 2023)

In October 2023, days after US District Judge [[tanya-chutkan|Tanya Chutkan]] temporarily lifted her gag order in the 2020 election conspiracy case pending appeal, Trump criticized special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] on Truth Social as "deranged" and accused Smith's team of leaking a story to the New York Times about Australian businessman Anthony Pratt, who claimed Trump had disclosed classified secrets to him; Trump denied the reports.[182] He also wrote that there was "NO WAY I can get a fair trial on a Biden, Election Interference Indictment, in D.C." and said he would demand a change of venue if the case proceeded.[182]

## Position on the Israel-Hamas war

Badlands grouped Trump with Vladimir Putin, Xi Jinping and [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] as major world leaders calling for peace, US neutrality and a two-state solution following the October 7 [[hamas|Hamas]] attack, a stance the outlet described as aligning with the only point of agreement among most Americans.[182] Badlands also reported that US-based Muslims, particularly in Michigan and other swing states, were threatening to vote for Trump in 2024 because of the Biden administration's handling of the crisis.[182]

## Maine and Colorado ballot disqualification (January 2024)

At the end of 2023, Maine Secretary of State [[shenna-bellows|Shenna Bellows]] unilaterally removed Trump from [[maine|Maine]]'s 2024 presidential primary ballot under the [[fourteenth-amendment|Fourteenth Amendment]], an action criticized as resting on accusation rather than conviction or adjudication.[183] Trump's campaign said it would immediately appeal the [[maine-ballot-disqualification|decision]] to the Maine Superior Court, which by statute was required to rule by January 17, 2024.[183]

Trump remained on the [[colorado|Colorado]] primary ballot despite the [[colorado-ballot-disqualification-case|Colorado litigation]], but the trial court's finding that January 6 was an insurrection — upheld by the [[colorado-supreme-court|Colorado Supreme Court]] — was described by Badlands writer [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] as the enabling precedent for the Maine action.[183] His appeal, alongside that of the Colorado GOP, in [[anderson-v-griswold|Anderson v. Griswold]] was headed to the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|U.S. Supreme Court]], where commentators urged the justices to address the underlying insurrection finding rather than the Fourteenth Amendment question alone.[183] Commentary in [[zerohedge|ZeroHedge]] warned that the ballot-removal trend was "not only about Donald Trump" and could later be used against any conservative candidate framed as a "threat to Democracy."[183]

## March 2025 address to Congress and inbound diplomacy

On March 4, 2025, Trump delivered an [[2025-trump-address-to-joint-session-of-congress|address to a joint session of Congress]] days after enacting sweeping 25 percent tariffs on Mexico and Canada, saying he was "fighting every day" to lower consumer prices and promising to tariff other countries at whatever rate they tariff the United States.[184] He pledged to expand energy production, touted dramatically lower illegal border crossings and the largest deportation operation in US history, announced the capture of one of the masterminds of the [[abbey-gate-bombing|Abbey Gate bombing]], and read from a letter in which [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] said Ukraine was ready to negotiate peace and sign a minerals deal.[184] He also proposed scrapping the [[chips-and-science-act|CHIPS Act]] and applying the savings to deficit reduction, and named 13-year-old cancer survivor Devarjaye "DJ" Daniel an honorary Secret Service agent.[184] Representative [[al-green|Al Green]] was removed from the chamber by the Sergeant at Arms amid Democratic protests.[184]

In the same period Trump's tariff threats prompted [[tsmc|TSMC]] to pledge 00 billion in new US investment, bringing its total US commitment to 65 billion, after he accused [[taiwan|Taiwan]] of stealing the American chip industry and threatened duties of up to 100 percent.[184] He reportedly asked [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] during their February phone call to help broker nuclear talks with Iran, a request Moscow signaled willingness to fulfil.[184] Separately, his allegations of Chinese interference at the [[panama-canal|Panama Canal]] preceded the [[blackrock-panama-canal-ports-deal|3 billion BlackRock ports deal]] with [[ck-hutchison|CK Hutchison]], and his administration directed the [[general-services-administration|General Services Administration]] to list 443 federal properties for possible sale.[184]

## "Closer to World War III" warning (October 2023)

Speaking to a crowd in Cedar Rapids, Iowa on Saturday, October 7, 2023, Trump said the United States is "closer to World War III than we've ever been," warning that another conflict could involve "weaponry the likes of which this world has never seen before."[185] The remarks came as the war in Ukraine passed 1.7 years and [[hamas|Hamas]] launched its [[october-7-attacks|invasion of Israel]], and coincided with a spike in Google searches for "Will I get drafted to war?" to the highest level since the 2007 Iraq troop surge.[185] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]], writing as the mother of three sons including one of draft age, credited Trump and the [[america-first-movement|America First]] movement with "rightly calling out the emotional blackmail, war drums, and lack of American interests in the resource prioritization math," and argued that institutional energy was directing Americans to look abroad while the border remained open to unvetted military-aged men.[185]

Separately, [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] observed that the [[two-state-solution|two-state solution]] floated by Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] after October 7 was a position Trump had also floated, and — having previously called Trump "the signal within the noise" — said Putin was proving to play a similar role, to the point that "if you swapped out the quotes of one for the other, you'd have a very difficult time telling them apart."[185] He noted that many refer to Trump as "The Peacemaker" while cautioning that it takes two or more sides both to make and to end a war.[185] Commentary on [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]]'s independent candidacy in the same brief judged that Trump's base remained "solid and aligned in their unwavering support for 45," making the Kennedy family's public denunciation of their brother a net benefit to Trump.[185]

## 2024 Michigan primary victory

Trump easily won the [[2024-michigan-republican-primary|Michigan Republican primary]] on Tuesday, February 27, 2024, continuing his march toward the party's presidential nomination and appearing on a path to roughly 70 percent of the vote.[186] His rival [[nikki-haley|Nikki Haley]] nevertheless took more than a quarter of the vote, a share commentators said raised questions about his general-election standing, though it trailed her 40 percent in South Carolina and 43 percent in New Hampshire.[186] By that point Trump had swept every contest, winning Iowa, New Hampshire, Nevada and South Carolina.[186]

Separately, in the [[state-of-georgia-v-trump|Georgia election interference case]], Trump's attorney [[steve-sadow|Steve Sadow]] cross-examined witness [[terrence-bradley|Terrence Bradley]] at the February 27 hearing on the motion to disqualify District Attorney [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]] over her relationship with [[nathan-wade|Nathan Wade]]; Trump's lawyers accused the prosecutors of lying about when the romance began.[186]

## Carroll verdict and lawfare (May 2023)

On May 9, 2023, a New York City jury found Trump liable for sexual abuse and defamation — but not rape — in the civil case brought by writer [[e-jean-carroll|E. Jean Carroll]], awarding her $20,000 in punitive damages on a battery claim and about $5 million in compensatory damages for defamation after less than three hours of deliberation in U.S. District Court in lower Manhattan.[188] Trump, who had long denied the mid-1990s allegation and accused Carroll of using false claims to promote her book, called the outcome "an affront to justice" and said he would appeal; he had told The Hill at the [[white-house|White House]] in June 2019, "Number one, she's not my type. Number two, it never happened."[188]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] wrote that Trump's demeanor toward the saga was "rather cavalier," that the verdict followed the [[lawfare|lawfare]] campaign brought by Manhattan District Attorney [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]], and that the public was being primed for former heads of state to face criminal and civil trials as elites lost their "Narrative Shielding."[188] He suggested Trump was "taking all of these slings and arrows for a purpose," and asked what would follow when "the real trials start."[188] The same brief recalled that [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] had earlier described a November 2022 "Narrative Pincer Move" between Trump and [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]], in which the mere threat of Trump's return to [[twitter|Twitter]] triggered a mass cultural and memetic event that discredited establishment conservative media.[188]

## July 2024: convention, Zelensky call and Bitcoin

Accepting the Republican nomination on the final night of the [[2024-republican-national-convention|2024 Republican National Convention]] in Milwaukee, Trump declared that "I will end every single international crisis that the current administration has created, including the horrible war with Russia and Ukraine, which would have never happened if I was president."[189] The convention's closing lineup drew attention in alternative media for its wrestling motifs: [[linda-mcmahon|Linda McMahon]], former president and CEO of [[world-wrestling-entertainment|WWE]], recalled Trump joining one of the promotion's highest-rated storylines and revealed she had run the Small Business Administration in his cabinet, while [[hulk-hogan|Hulk Hogan]] coined "[[trumpamania|Trumpmania]]" hours later.[189] Trump had been inducted into the WWE Hall of Fame in 2013, and his ties to the McMahon family dated to WrestleMania IV, held in 1988 at Trump Plaza in Atlantic City; that same year he attended a Republican National Convention for the first time, telling Larry King he was a conservative, fielding questions about a possible vice-presidential nomination from George Bush, and denying that he was an establishment Republican.[189]

On Friday, July 19, 2024, Trump spoke with Ukrainian President [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] for the first time since Russia's full-scale invasion, calling it a "very good call" and saying he would work to negotiate a peace deal between Moscow and Kyiv; a person close to Zelensky said Trump had pledged to "achieve a just peace in Ukraine" if he won back the [[white-house|White House]], and the two agreed to schedule a personal meeting.[189] The call fell days short of the five-year anniversary of the conversation that led to [[first-impeachment-of-donald-trump|Trump's first impeachment]].[189]

That same month, rumors held that Trump might declare [[bitcoin|Bitcoin]] a "strategic reserve asset" at a Nashville conference — a prospect analysts called a watershed moment and which followed his earlier framing of Bitcoin and Bitcoin mining as a national security matter.[189] Separately, the Washington Post reported that the [[us-secret-service|Secret Service]] had repeatedly denied his detail's requests for additional security resources in the two years before the [[attempted-assassination-of-donald-trump-2024|July 13 attempt on his life]], while a [[morning-consult|Morning Consult]] poll found 34 percent of Democrats believed he had faked the shooting.[189]

## November 2025: elections, Central Asia, Nigeria, and SNAP

In the first days of November 2025 Trump was simultaneously active on several fronts covered in the Badlands News Brief. He endorsed [[andrew-cuomo|Andrew Cuomo]] on the eve of the [[new-york-city-mayoral-election-2025|New York City mayoral election]], warning that a victory for "Communist" [[zohran-mamdani|Zohran Mamdani]] would make it "highly unlikely" he would send federal funds to the city, and declaring that a vote for Republican [[curtis-sliwa|Curtis Sliwa]] was a vote for Mamdani.[190]

He prepared to host the five Central Asian heads of state at the White House for the [[central-asia-summit-2025|2025 Central Asia Summit]], a meeting aimed at supply chain security, critical minerals, and transport corridors bypassing Russia and China.[190] In a Truth Social post on November 1 he ordered the [[department-of-war|Department of War]] to prepare for possible military action in [[nigeria|Nigeria]] over the alleged killing of Christians by Islamist militants, warning any attack would be "fast, vicious, and sweet."[190] Regarding [[venezuela|Venezuela]], he had deployed a naval armada to the western Caribbean and authorized strikes on alleged drug-smuggling vessels from September, denying plans for direct strikes inside the country while reportedly reviewing a list of potential targets.[190]

On the domestic front, his administration announced on November 3 that it would maintain [[snap|SNAP]] benefits at 50 percent using contingency funds during the 34th day of the government shutdown, following a court order; Trump wrote, "If we are given the appropriate legal direction by the Court, it will BE MY HONOR to provide the funding."[190]

## Anticipated return to Twitter and Facebook (January 2023)

In January 2023 NBC News reported that Trump was preparing to return to [[twitter|Twitter]] and Facebook.[191] His Twitter account had been reinstated following the platform's purchase by Tesla and SpaceX chief executive [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]], but Trump had continued to post exclusively on his own platform, [[truth-social|Truth Social]].[191] A Republican with knowledge of private discussions, speaking anonymously, said: "Trump is probably coming back to Twitter. It's just a question of how and when. He's been talking about it for weeks, but Trump speaks for Trump, so it's anyone's guess what he'll do or say or when."[191] [[badlands-media|Badlands]] contributor [[absolute-1776|Absolute1776]] cautioned that anonymous GOP sources were not to be trusted, but suggested a return to Twitter could signal a shift in public perception and narrative as Trump geared up for a return to the [[white-house|White House]], noting it came as the Biden administration faced mounting difficulties.[191]

## Turning Point Action speech (July 2023)

Speaking at the [[turning-point-action|Turning Point Action]] conference in West Palm Beach in July 2023, Trump told young American voters that "this election will decide whether your generation inherits a fascist country or a free country—whether you will have the rule of tyrants or the rule of law," adding that the choice was whether "Marxist radicals burn our civilization to the ground" or "young patriots like you propel America to glorious new heights."[192] Vowing to oust "Crooked Joe Biden," he asserted that "under Biden, hope and opportunity for young people are being utterly extinguished" and promised that "the American dream is dead under Biden, we will have it back very soon."[192]

[[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] argued that Trump was "beating the anti-globalist propaganda drum" and that the speech described the core problem precisely rather than poetically: that the weaponization of a false [[rule-of-law|rule of law]] — a politically active population kept incompetent in law and politics — is the most powerful tool of the parasitic elite.[192] In his reading, the goal for Trump and the patriots is not to shift power from one elite class to another but to end the age of extrinsic power altogether, with the real task extending far beyond 2024 to what the people do with a liberated country after the [[great-awakening|great awakening]].[192]

Trump's standing also framed rival campaigns; [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] noted that [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]]' donors professed shock at his inability to cut into Trump's massive lead among the GOP base, concluding that "MAGA is the Captain, now."[192] On trade, Burning Bright argued that the [[joe-biden|Biden]] administration and Treasury Secretary [[janet-yellen|Janet Yellen]] were taking credit for tariffs Trump had implemented, absorbing the short-term costs of decoupling from China while the long-term gains would greet an incoming Trump administration.[192]

## Bond reduction and gag order (March 2024)

On March 25, 2024, a New York appeals court agreed to hold off collection of Trump's more than $454 million civil fraud judgment if he posted $175 million within 10 days, preventing seizure of the presumptive Republican nominee's assets during his appeal.[193] Syracuse University law professor Greg Germain said the reduction showed that the appellate division had "serious concerns about the validity of Judge [[arthur-engoron|Engoron]]'s decision."[193] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] wrote that Trump planned to pay the reduced bond in cash, a plan she connected to [[trump-media-and-technology-group|Trump Media]] shares surging more than 50 percent after the DJT ticker debut.[193]

The following day, Justice [[juan-merchan|Juan Merchan]] issued a gag order in the Manhattan criminal case barring Trump from publicly discussing witnesses, counsel, court and district attorney staff and their families, and any prospective or seated juror, while exempting District Attorney [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]] himself.[193] The order followed a Truth Social post that morning in which Trump attacked Merchan and his daughter, and came a day after the judge set an April 15 trial date in the case, which carries 34 counts of alleged falsification of business records.[193] Ashe in America framed the sequence as election interference, contrasting it with the same administration's condemnation of [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]] for excluding Venezuela's leading opposition candidate.[193]

## Iowa town hall and Breitbart interview (January 2024)

At an Iowa town hall broadcast on Fox News on the evening of January 10, 2024, Trump signaled that he had already decided on a running mate for the 2024 race, telling host Martha MacCallum: "I can't tell you that really, I mean, I know who it's going to be," and deflecting requests for a hint with "We'll do another show sometime."[194] Asked whether he would be open to mending fences with any of the rivals he had run against, he replied, "Oh, sure. I will, I will," adding, "I've already started to like [[chris-christie|Christie]] better."[194] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the remarks as trolling, noting Trump was surely aware of the competing theories about both his eventual pick and his view of his former vice president.[194]

The same day, in a more-than-two-hour exclusive interview with Breitbart News at Mar-a-Lago, Trump argued that had the 2020 election not gone the way it did, every Arab country and possibly [[iran|Iran]] would have signed the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]] by then.[194] He said Iran "was broke with me" but now had $250 billion, leaving "no money for Hamas, no money for Hezbollah" under his sanctions regime, and cited his ordered killing of [[qassem-soleimani|Qassem Soleimani]] as part of the pressure meant to force Tehran to the bargaining table.[194] He maintained that the October 7 attack on Israel "never would have happened" on his watch and that "we would have taken much more decisive action if it did."[194]

## 'Mandate' appeal and Israel remarks (October 2024)

At a rally in Juneau, Wisconsin, on Sunday, October 6, 2024, with Election Day less than a month away, Trump said he needed a "mandate" from the American electorate and urged supporters in the battleground state and nationwide to turn out "in record numbers."[195] "We need, and I hate to use this word because they should have never done it with respect to COVID, they should have never done it, but for this, we need a mandate," he said, referencing pandemic-era restrictions such as masking and social distancing, before adding, "But we need a mandate in the vote, and we're going to get it."[195] The federal government at the time was closely divided, with Democrats holding a 51-49 Senate majority and Republicans a narrow House margin, and national and swing-state polls showing Trump and [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] near even.[195]

Badlands contributor Burning Bright argued the remark was flying under the radar, noting that appeals to "vote harder" do not appeal to much of the MAGA movement, but reading Trump's emphasis on "mandate" — alongside [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] calling 2024 potentially "the last election" — as evidence that the purpose of the Devolution period was to provoke exactly such a demand from the populace.[195] Whatever the systemic capabilities of what he called the fraudulent machine, the commentary held, the Deep State was losing the story of implied public mandate: "He who wins the story wins the war, because he who wins the story wins the mandate."[195]

On Monday, October 7, 2024 — the anniversary of the Hamas attacks that killed more than 1,200 people in Israel — Trump phoned into [[hugh-hewitt|Hugh Hewitt]]'s radio show and said Israel "ha[s] to get smart about Trump, because they don't back me," asserting "I did more for Israel than anybody. I did more for the Jewish people than anybody. And it's not reciprocal."[195] He cited relocating the U.S. embassy to Jerusalem, recognizing the Golan Heights, and the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]] as evidence of his record, though he had previously drawn backlash for saying Jews who back Democrats should "have their head examined" and suggesting Jewish voters would be partly to blame if he lost.[195] He also told Hewitt he would support Israel striking back at Iran after an Iranian missile attack the prior week, while speculating a deal with Tehran might be in progress.[195]

Badlands commentary noted Trump had made a similar complaint in a December 2021 phone interview with Israeli journalist Barak Ravid, and argued the roughly 20 percent he referenced were the Israeli settlers and affiliated NGOs driving settlement of the West Bank, Gaza, and the Golan Heights, with ambitions in southern Lebanon.[195] Trump told Ravid he had stopped [[benjamin-netanyahu|Netanyahu]] from expanding the settlement movement and shut down construction.[195] The writer suggested that although the world believes Trump and Netanyahu are close friends, the two have never liked one another, and speculated that Jewish American elites organize against Trump because he and [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] disrupted trafficking and slavery operations in Africa.[195]

## February 2025: tariffs, aid, and health policy

At the first Cabinet meeting of his second term on February 26, 2025, Trump claimed the [[european-union|European Union]] "was formed in order to screw the United States, that's the purpose of it," adding, "And they've done a good job of it. But now I'm president."[196] He announced plans to impose tariffs on EU imports "very soon," saying "it will be 25%, generally speaking, and that will be on cars and all other things," and described the EU as "a different case than Canada."[196] The move echoed his first term, when he imposed 25% tariffs on European steel and 10% on aluminum, prompting EU countermeasures.[196]

The same week, Trump's administration won an emergency pause from Chief Justice [[john-roberts|John Roberts]] of a district court order requiring roughly $2 billion in [[usaid|USAID]] contractor payments by midnight.[196] Trump also signed a new executive order on Tuesday, February 25 reaffirming his first-term push for health care price transparency, with a White House official signalling more aggressive enforcement after years of halfhearted compliance from hospitals and insurers.[196] Badlands writer Chris Paul argued that without Trump, "ConInc faux-Trumps" would be taking credit for policies he originated years earlier.[196]

At that Cabinet meeting Trump also aligned himself forcefully with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] and the work of [[doge|DOGE]] amid tensions between Musk and senior officials.[196] Separately, he had claimed on February 18 that [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelenskyy]]'s approval rating was 4% and called for Ukrainian elections, a claim Zelenskyy called disinformation.[196]

## July 2024: Nashville, Netanyahu and West Palm Beach

In the last week of July 2024, Trump delivered the keynote at the [[bitcoin-2024-nashville-conference|Bitcoin 2024 conference]] in Nashville, promising to make the United States a "bitcoin superpower" and the "crypto capital of the planet," to build a bitcoin "strategic reserve" out of government holdings, to fire Securities and Exchange Commission chair [[gary-gensler|Gary Gensler]], and to establish a crypto advisory council.[197] "We will have regulations, but from now on the rules will be written by people who love your industry, not hate your industry," he told the audience.[197] Badlands commentator [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the speech, and Trump's aside about his uncle Dr. John Trump, as an invitation to both bitcoin maximalists and anons to board "the same train."[197]

On Friday, July 26, Trump hosted Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] in Florida, where Netanyahu sought an unequivocal pledge of military and financial support should Trump win in November.[197] A Trump campaign readout said only that he would "make every effort to bring peace to the Middle East," and the campaign declined to say whether he would continue supporting the $3 billion annual U.S. weapons package for Israel that Netanyahu's delegation had been pressing to accelerate.[197] Badlands writer [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read Trump's body language — an informal handshake, a two-handed dominance grip, the absence of [[melania-trump|Melania Trump]], the seating of Sarah Netanyahu beside him, and his refusal to touch a gift hat reading "Total Victory" — as a rejection of Israeli war aims and the opening of a long negotiation over whether a third world war would occur.[197]

That same evening, at a [[turning-point-action|Turning Point Action]] rally in West Palm Beach, Trump told supporters, "Christians, get out and vote! Just this time – you won't have to do it any more," adding, "It'll be fixed! ... In four years, you don't have to vote again."[197] Critics including constitutional attorney Andrew Seidel called the remarks overt Christian nationalism and an intimation of ending American democracy.[197]

Meanwhile, polling after [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s withdrawal showed the race tightening against [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]], with Trump leading by one to three points in New York Times/Siena, Wall Street Journal and CNN surveys — down from six-point leads over Biden after the debate.[197]

## April 2025 tariffs, TikTok and market turmoil

In early April 2025 Trump's sweeping global tariffs — a general 10 percent levy on imported goods plus targeted rates on individual trading partners, including 54 percent on China — triggered a severe market selloff, with the Dow Jones Industrial Average dropping 2,200 points and the S&P 500 losing 10 percent over two days.[198] Shell-shocked investors piled into U.S. Treasuries on fears the trade war would trigger a worldwide recession, pricing in more aggressive [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] rate cuts even as analysts warned tariffs could reignite inflation.[198] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the situation as a pincer on the Fed, which would prefer not to cut rates to boost Trump's stock market but now had no choice, and argued that Trump "isn't trying to beat the players — he's trying to break the game."[198]

Trump said he had come "pretty close" to a deal with China for the sale of [[tiktok|TikTok]] before Beijing reversed course over the new tariffs, telling reporters aboard Air Force One that a small tariff concession would get the deal approved "in 15 minutes, which shows you the power of tariffs."[198] He announced an executive order granting [[bytedance|ByteDance]] 75 additional days to divest.[198] The tariff push also drew a nationwide backlash on 5 April, when more than 1,300 [[hands-off-protests|"Hands Off!" rallies]] were held against his administration.[198]

Elsewhere in the period, Trump's newly appointed Africa adviser [[massad-boulos|Massad Boulos]] advanced a [[us-dr-congo-critical-minerals-deal|critical minerals agreement]] with the [[democratic-republic-of-the-congo|Democratic Republic of Congo]]; Senate Republican leaders pressed Trump to endorse [[john-cornyn|John Cornyn]] in the Texas Senate primary against [[ken-paxton|Ken Paxton]] and [[wesley-hunt|Wesley Hunt]]; and his administration was rebuked by Judge [[paula-xinis|Paula Xinis]] over its refusal to return wrongly deported Salvadoran man [[kilmar-abrego-garcia|Kilmar Abrego García]].[198] Washington's engagement with Moscow over Ukraine prompted speculation that Trump was attempting a [[reverse-kissinger|"Reverse Kissinger"]] to isolate China.[198]

## April 2025 tariff standoff and China outreach

Speaking to reporters at the [[white-house|White House]] on April 9, 2025, Trump voiced optimism that a deal with [[china|China]] remained within reach even after he raised tariffs on Chinese goods to 125 percent, praising President [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] as "a smart guy" and "one of the smartest people in the world" and predicting "a very good deal."[199] He said he would consider direct talks with Xi and argued that "investing in the United States of America will be the greatest investment."[199]

The broader [[liberation-day-tariffs|"Liberation Day"]] package drew severe backlash, including from conservatives; after doubling down several times Trump relented and imposed a 90-day pause on the reciprocal tariffs, substituting a 10 percent across-the-board rate with the exception of China.[199] Sen. [[rand-paul|Rand Paul]] publicly pressed for repeal, describing Republican colleagues' private dissent as "quiet whispers."[199] [[apple|Apple]] responded to the deadline by airlifting some 600 tons of iPhones out of [[india|India]] to build U.S. inventory.[199] Italian Prime Minister [[giorgia-meloni|Giorgia Meloni]] prepared to become the first European leader to meet Trump after the announcement, raising expectations of a bilateral Italy–US deal negotiated outside the [[european-union|European Union]].[199]

## Other April 2025 developments

The [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] on April 10, 2025 declined to set aside a district court order requiring the administration to "facilitate" the release and return of [[kilmar-abrego-garcia|Kilmar Abrego Garcia]], a Maryland man deported to [[el-salvador|El Salvador]] through what officials called an administrative error.[199] Trump signed executive orders directing the [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]] to investigate former cybersecurity chief [[chris-krebs|Chris Krebs]] over his conduct during the 2020 election, a day before Director of National Intelligence [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] told a Cabinet meeting that electronic voting systems were vulnerable and urged a nationwide move to paper ballots consistent with the President's mandate.[199] The administration also secured the return of three Americans convicted over the [[2024-dr-congo-coup-attempt|2024 DR Congo coup attempt]], which [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read as a trade for U.S. help resolving the DRC's crisis with [[rwanda|Rwanda]].[199] Separately, Trump was set to open talks with [[iran|Iran]] in Oman, with lawmakers in both parties raising questions about whether he sought to limit Iran's nuclear capabilities or demand full dismantlement.[199]

## February 2024 border visit and Illinois ballot appeal

On Thursday, February 29, 2024, Trump and President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] made [[2024-dueling-border-visits|dueling visits]] to the U.S.–Mexico border, appearing some 300 miles apart in Texas, with Trump blaming the president for lawlessness at the border while Biden challenged him to "join me" in securing the southern frontier.[200] Biden urged Trump to help pass the [[bipartisan-border-bill-2024|bipartisan border bill]] in [[congress|Congress]], legislation Republicans had torpedoed at Trump's urging on the grounds that it was not strong enough.[200] The New York Times treated Biden's dare as an acknowledgment of Trump's power over the Republican Party on immigration.[200] During his appearance Trump spoke about [[laken-riley|Laken Riley]], and in a clip posted by Raheem Kassam migrants could be heard chanting his name, prompting Trump to laugh, "They like Trump, can you believe it?"[200]

The same day, Trump appealed a Cook County judge's ruling ordering his removal from the Illinois primary ballot over his role in the January 6, 2021 attack on the U.S. Capitol, with his attorneys asking the Illinois Appellate Court to overturn Judge Tracie Porter's decision.[200] Porter had found the Illinois State Board of Elections "clearly erroneous" for allowing him to remain on the ballot and held that he "falsely swore" he was "legally qualified" when filing his statement of candidacy on January 4, given the [[colorado-supreme-court|Colorado Supreme Court]]'s earlier insurrection finding.[200] Trump's name remained on ballots pending the [[illinois-ballot-disqualification-case|appeal]], which [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] noted joined many other challenges awaiting the Supreme Court's ruling in Trump v. Anderson.[200]

## Concession to farmers and hospitality on immigration (June 2025)

On Thursday, June 12, 2025, Trump acknowledged in a Truth Social post that his "very aggressive" immigration policies were removing long-time workers from the farming and hospitality industries.[201] "Our great Farmers and people in the Hotel and Leisure business have been stating that our very aggressive policy on immigration is taking very good, long time workers away from them, with those jobs being almost impossible to replace," he wrote, promising that "changes are coming."[201] The concession followed reports of federal immigration raids on farms that stoked fears among agricultural workers during the summer growing season.[201] Badlands contributor [[chris-paul|Chris Paul]] responded that the affected companies "should have no problem purchasing Trump Cards for these very important (otherwise) illegal aliens."[201]

The same week, Trump's federalization of the [[california-army-national-guard|California National Guard]] in Los Angeles was ruled illegal by Judge [[charles-breyer|Charles Breyer]], only for the [[united-states-court-of-appeals-for-the-ninth-circuit|9th Circuit]] to stay that order overnight, leaving the troops under Trump's control through the weekend.[201] Regarding the [[june-2025-israeli-strikes-on-iran|Israeli strikes on Iran]] launched that same night, Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] stated that Trump and the administration had taken all necessary steps to protect U.S. forces while stressing that Washington was not involved in the strikes.[201] Badlands commentator [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] recalled Trump's earlier statement that "we can blow up whatever we want, but nobody's getting killed. We can blow up a lab, but nobody's going to be in the lab," and cited his quote "It's not gonna happen" regarding a third world war, casting him as a "reluctant warrior; resolute peacemaker."[201] Separately, [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] quipped at a New York gala that Trump could serve as U.S. president and Israeli prime minister at the same time.[201]

## Second-term opening week (January 2025)

Addressing the [[world-economic-forum|World Economic Forum]] in Davos by video link on January 23, 2025, Trump promised global elites lower taxes if they moved manufacturing to the United States and threatened tariffs if they did not, telling them the U.S. had "the largest amount of oil and gas of any country on Earth, and we're going to use it."[202] He floated a universal tariff, saying it was necessary because "essentially countries take advantage of the U.S.," while acknowledging the country was "not ready for that yet."[202] Badlands contributors read the address as a [[devolution|Devolution]] signal, especially his remark that "by SKIPPING a term" he would be president during both the Olympics and the 250th anniversary.[202] He also criticized banks including [[bank-of-america|Bank of America]] and [[jamie-dimon|Jamie Dimon]] over the debanking of conservatives, praised Saudi Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] as a "great guy" whom he expected to invest a trillion dollars in the U.S. economy, and called for denuclearization talks with Russia and China.[202]

His opening executive orders withdrew the U.S. from the Paris climate accords, ordered the Gulf of Mexico renamed the Gulf of America, and suspended refugee admissions.[202] On immigration, thousands of migrants with asylum appointments through the CBP mobile app had them cancelled, the humanitarian parole website for Cuba, Haiti, Nicaragua and Venezuela went dark, immigration authorities were permitted to make arrests in schools and places of worship, top immigration court officials were fired, and his [[birthright-citizenship|birthright citizenship]] order drew immediate legal challenges from blue-state attorneys general.[202] The [[pentagon|Pentagon]] announced that the [[department-of-defense|Department of Defense]] would provide airlift support to [[department-of-homeland-security|DHS]], with the [[united-states-air-force|Air Force]] supplying four aircraft to remove up to 5,000 detained aliens, following the deployment of at least 1,500 active-duty service members to the southern border.[202] Trump wrote that "a National Emergency currently exists along the southern border of the United States."[202]

On the same Thursday Trump signed an executive order on cryptocurrency establishing a "Presidential Working Group on Digital Asset Markets," directing evaluation of a national digital asset stockpile, and banning development of a [[central-bank-digital-currency|central bank digital currency]]; he repeated his pledge to make the U.S. the "world capital" of both crypto and artificial intelligence.[202] Reporting also indicated he would pardon pro-life activists imprisoned under the [[face-act|FACE Act]], including [[bevelyn-williams|Bevelyn Williams]], and he pardoned [[proud-boys|Proud Boys]] leader [[enrique-tarrio|Enrique Tarrio]].[202] He criticized [[fema|FEMA]] as having "not done their job over the last four years" ahead of his first trip of the term to North Carolina and Los Angeles, and the Senate confirmed [[john-ratcliffe|John Ratcliffe]] as [[cia|CIA]] director in a 74–25 vote.[202]

## February 2025: tariffs, Venezuela, DOGE and Gaza diplomacy

In early February 2025 Trump doubled down on sweeping tariffs against America's three largest trading partners, with levies of 25% on Canadian and Mexican goods and 10% on Chinese goods due to take effect that Tuesday.[203] On Truth Social he wrote that the United States pays "hundreds of Billions of Dollars to SUBSIDIZE Canada" for no reason, that America has unlimited energy and lumber, and that Canada "should become our Cherished 51st State" with lower taxes, better military protection and no tariffs.[203] Canadian Prime Minister [[justin-trudeau|Justin Trudeau]] responded with a 25% tariff on more than $100 billion of American goods, and Mexican President [[claudia-sheinbaum|Claudia Sheinbaum]] ordered retaliatory tariff and non-tariff measures.[203] Badlands commentary described Trump's tactic as "anchoring" — leading with an endgame offer so that any concession favors the initial position.[203]

Trump sent envoy [[richard-grenell|Richard Grenell]] to Caracas to warn [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]] to accept the unconditional return of deported Venezuelans and release American hostages or face consequences.[203] His administration also granted the [[doge|Department of Government Efficiency]] access to the federal payment system, fired [[consumer-financial-protection-bureau|CFPB]] director [[rohit-chopra|Rohit Chopra]], appointed [[darren-beattie|Darren Beattie]] as acting Under Secretary for Public Diplomacy at the [[state-department|State Department]], and withdrew the [[us-department-of-the-treasury|Treasury]] from the [[network-of-central-banks-and-supervisors-for-greening-the-financial-system|NGFS]] pursuant to his executive orders on international environmental agreements and American energy.[203]

On the Middle East, Trump scheduled a White House meeting with Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] on which the future of the Gaza hostage-release and ceasefire deal was said to hang, and pressed a proposal to relocate Gazans to Egypt and Jordan that surrounding Arab states, including Saudi Arabia, strongly rejected.[203] He also publicly accused Senate Democrats of "purposefully delaying virtually all of my Nominees," noting that only eight of more than 20 had been confirmed.[203]

## October 2025 Asia trip and related developments

Speaking to reporters aboard Air Force One en route to Japan on Monday, October 27, 2025, Trump said he would extend his Asia visit that week if it meant meeting North Korean leader [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong Un]], saying he "got along great" with Kim and remained open to another encounter.[209] "I liked him. He liked me," he said. "If he wants to meet, I'll be in South Korea."[209] The remarks followed comments the previous Friday in which he said he would welcome another meeting with Kim, whom he first met in 2019, and observed that North Korea "has a lot of nuclear weapons but not a lot of telephone service."[209]

In Tokyo, Trump met for the first time with Japan's new prime minister, [[sanae-takaichi|Sanae Takaichi]], and the two governments signed the [[us-japan-critical-minerals-agreement|US–Japan critical minerals agreement]] on cooperation in producing and supplying critical and rare-earth minerals, following China's tightening of export controls in response to his tariffs.[209] The leaders also signed a document directing their governments toward "a new golden age of the ever-growing US-Japan alliance."[209]

Trump's third stop was South Korea, where he was expected to meet newly elected President [[lee-jae-myung|Lee Jae Myung]], address business leaders, and attend a dinner with other heads of state, with Korean shipbuilder [[hanwha-ocean|Hanwha Ocean]] central to his push to revive American shipbuilding.[209] At a Norfolk, Virginia speech marking the Navy's 250th birthday he said America would "soon revitalize our once great shipyards with hundreds of billions of dollars in new investments."[209]

Trump simultaneously escalated against Canada over the [[ontario-reagan-tariff-advertisement|Ontario Reagan tariff advertisement]], calling it "fraudulent," suspending trade talks, and threatening an additional 10 percent tariff, while celebrating the [[argentina-2025-legislative-elections|Argentine legislative election victory]] of [[javier-milei|Javier Milei]] as "a big win" that followed his own "very strong endorsement" and US financial support.[209]

On Sunday, October 26, the [[white-house|White House]] social media account posted an AI-generated image of Trump in Master Chief's armor holding an energy sword and saluting a 40-star flag, captioned "Power to the Players," in what became known as the [[console-wars-declaration|console wars declaration]]; reporting tied it to his Nobel Peace Prize campaign and his claim to have ended seven wars.[209]

Asked aboard the Monday flight about a possible 2028 run, Trump played coy, saying he would "love" to do it and citing "the best numbers ever," while ruling out running as a vice presidential candidate and taking over after the election, and naming Vice President JD Vance and Secretary of State Marco Rubio as potential successors — remarks that followed [[steve-bannon|Steve Bannon]]'s claim that there was a "plan" to keep Trump in office.[209]

## CNN New Hampshire town hall (2023)

In early May 2023, Trump agreed to appear on a live [[cnn|CNN]] town hall forum in New Hampshire, despite having frequently derided the network as "fake news" as president and never having given an interview to a CNN journalist while in office.[205] The [[2023-cnn-trump-new-hampshire-town-hall|event]] was set for May 10 at St. Anselm's College in Goffstown, televised at 9 p.m. Eastern and moderated by [[kaitlan-collins|Kaitlan Collins]], with questions from Republicans and undeclared voters planning to participate in the 2024 GOP primary.[205] Though Trump had recently been interviewed by Sean Hannity and Tucker Carlson on Fox News, it was his first town-hall-style event of the 2024 campaign, and it was read as an attempt to draw a contrast with [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]]; a Trump adviser said stepping outside the traditional Republican comfort zone had been key to his 2016 success.[205]

## October 2024: threats, podcasts, and the closing campaign

By October 2024, U.S. officials had concluded that Iran's repeated threats to kill Trump and several of his former top generals and national security strategists were serious and persistent, with Tehran neither bluffing nor giving up.[206] Iran had threatened Trump and his national security team since January 2020, when he ordered the drone strike that killed Qassem Soleimani, releasing videos depicting their future deaths, pushing for arrest and extradition, and promising revenge.[206] Intelligence officials briefed the Trump campaign in September 2024, which said it was warned the threat had "heightened in the past few months," following two assassination attempts on Trump that summer that were not linked to Tehran by any presented evidence.[206]

Separately, a man carrying a shotgun, a loaded handgun, a high-capacity magazine, and fake press and VIP passes was arrested at a checkpoint near a Trump rally in California's Coachella Valley on October 12, 2024; Riverside County Sheriff Chad Bianco said deputies "probably stopped another assassination attempt," though his office said the incident did not affect Trump's safety.[206]

On the campaign's media front, Trump hinted during an appearance on the Nelk Boys' "Full Send" podcast that he would sit for an interview with Joe Rogan, saying "I think I'm doing it, actually" and then "yeah, I am."[206] Badlands commentary predicted the interview would be the most-watched in American history and a mass culture event of unmatched memetic magnitude.[206]

Trump also featured in the October 2024 Saturday Night Live season, where a Family Feud–style sketch's punchline for the Trump campaign was that he has dementia — a joke the show had also used the prior week.[206] Trump and Elon Musk had likewise both recently claimed that American households would in the near future be powered by individual power packs forming a decentralized energy grid.[206]

An older Trump remark resurfaced as well: in a December 2021 interview with Israeli journalist Barak Ravid, Trump said Benjamin Netanyahu backed out of the Soleimani strike the day before it was scheduled and also tried to back out of the Abraham Accords, adding, "Israel did not do the right thing. People will be hearing about that, at the right time."[206]

## Trudeau "governor" jibe and tariff diplomacy (December 2024)

In December 2024, President-elect Trump mocked Canadian Prime Minister [[justin-trudeau|Justin Trudeau]] as the "governor" of the "Great State of Canada" after warning of tariffs on the country.[207] Posting on Truth Social just after midnight, Trump wrote: "It was a pleasure to have dinner the other night with Governor Justin Trudeau of the Great State of Canada. I look forward to seeing the Governor again soon so that we may continue our in depth talks on Tariffs and Trade, the results of which will be truly spectacular for all!"[207] Trump spokesman Steven Cheung, asked for comment on the quip, said only, "It's so good," while Trudeau's office did not immediately respond.[207] Trump had joked that Canada should become the 51st state, suggesting it directly to Trudeau during their dinner at Mar-a-Lago, and made a similar suggestion to host Kristen Welker on NBC's "Meet the Press" while discussing how tariffs on Canada and Mexico would make the United States "rich," characterizing the US-Canada trade deficit as a form of subsidy.[207]
