In mid-February 2025 the [[white-house|White House]] published a list of weekly wins, noting that Trump had brought home an American citizen wrongfully detained in Russia and another detained in Belarus; restored a 25% tariff on steel imports and elevated the aluminum tariff to 25%; unveiled a plan for fair and reciprocal trade; spoken with Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy in pursuit of peace; hosted Jordan's King Abdullah II, who announced the kingdom would accept 2,000 sick children from Gaza "as quickly as possible"; and joined Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi to announce new deals on immigration, trade, energy and artificial intelligence.[248] [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] called it a great list of accomplishments and good material to share with friends and family who are less tuned in.[248]

The same week saw the [[2025-steel-and-aluminum-tariffs|steel and aluminum tariffs]] formalized alongside a reciprocal tariff measure, [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]]'s push for an [[arab-alternative-to-trump-gaza-plan|Arab alternative to Trump's Gaza plan]], and his administration's first appeal to the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] over the firing of a top ethics official.[248] On Saturday, February 15, Trump posted "[[he-who-saves-his-country-does-not-violate-any-law|He who saves his Country does not violate any Law]]" to Truth Social and X after courts blocked several of his executive actions, drawing warnings that a constitutional crisis was brewing.[248]

Trump also attended the Daytona 500 on Sunday, arriving aboard Air Force One, which flew over Daytona International Speedway before landing at Daytona International Airport to cheers from the crowd; he became the first sitting president to attend two Daytona 500 races, having previously attended in February 2020.[248]

## February 13, 2025: Putin call, Modi deal, and cabinet confirmations

On Wednesday, February 12, 2025, Trump held a call with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] that CNN described as bringing the Russian leader "in from the cold," as the two hatched plans to end the war in Ukraine and agreed to swap presidential visits.[249] Paired with Defense Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]]'s message in Brussels that European allies should "take ownership of conventional security on the continent," the day was cast as a watershed in transatlantic relations that underscored Trump's "America First" ideology and his tendency to treat every alliance as a dollars-and-cents value proposition.[249] Trump invoked the traditional American wariness of foreign entanglements, saying, "We have a little thing called an ocean in between."[249]

The following day Trump announced an energy deal with Indian Prime Minister [[narendra-modi|Narendra Modi]] under which Delhi would import more US oil and gas to shrink the bilateral trade deficit, while his reciprocal tariff order remained in place.[249] At the same press conference announcing reciprocal trade, Trump was asked about New York Mayor [[eric-adams|Eric Adams]] and stated definitively that he had not spoken with the [[justice-department|Justice Department]] about the case at all, after prosecutors including [[danielle-sassoon|Danielle Sassoon]] resigned rather than drop it.[249]

Also that week, the [[us-senate|Senate]] confirmed [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] as Secretary of Health and Human Services and [[brooke-rollins|Brooke Rollins]] as Secretary of Agriculture, [[linda-mcmahon|Linda McMahon]] testified at her confirmation hearing for Education Secretary, and [[kash-patel|Kash Patel]] advanced out of committee before his nomination was reportedly delayed so that senators could attend the [[munich-security-conference|Munich Security Conference]].[249] Trump signed a presidential action establishing the President's [[make-america-healthy-again|Make America Healthy Again]] Commission the same day.[249]

## Reported second-term personnel plans (December 2023)

In December 2023, Reuters reported that Trump in a second term would likely install loyalists in key positions at the [[pentagon|Pentagon]], the [[state-department|State Department]] and the [[cia|CIA]] whose primary allegiance would be to him, giving him more freedom than in his first presidency to enact isolationist policies.[250] Nearly 20 current and former aides and diplomats told the outlet that the result would enable sweeping changes to the U.S. stance on issues ranging from the Ukraine war to trade with China, as well as to the federal institutions that implement and sometimes constrain foreign policy.[250]

The reporting recalled that during his 2017–2021 term Trump struggled to impose his vision on the national security establishment and often voiced frustration at top officials who slow-walked, shelved or talked him out of his plans; former Defense Secretary [[mark-esper|Mark Esper]] wrote in his memoir that he twice objected to Trump's suggestion of missile strikes on drug cartels in Mexico.[250] [[robert-obrien|Robert O'Brien]], Trump's fourth and final national security adviser, said that "President Trump came to realize that personnel is policy," adding that at the outset of the administration "there were a lot of people that were interested in implementing their own policies, not the president's policies."[250] Aides said more loyalists in place would allow Trump to advance his foreign policy priorities faster and more efficiently than before.[250]

Badlands contributor [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued that Reuters' concern amounted to an open admission that the outlet objected to a president employing people committed to executing his agenda, and framed the coverage as evidence that "all the masks are slipping" as a 2024 Trump victory appeared more likely.[250] She contended that foreign policy was significantly more successful under Trump, characterizing his first term as a period of "peace and prosperity."[250]

Trump also figured in December 2023 coverage of the [[united-states-space-command|United States Space Command]], which he directed be created in 2019 and which declared full operational capability that month.[250]

## Colorado ballot appeal and media lawsuit (November 2023)

On Monday, November 20, 2023, Trump appealed to the [[colorado-supreme-court|Colorado Supreme Court]] the ruling of District Court Judge [[sarah-wallace|Sarah Wallace]], which had found that he "engaged in insurrection" on January 6, 2021 while holding that he could nonetheless remain on Colorado's primary ballot.[251] His appeal challenged the insurrection finding itself and questioned whether a state court judge, rather than Congress, should settle the question, while [[citizens-for-responsibility-and-ethics-in-washington|Citizens for Responsibility and Ethics in Washington]] cross-appealed the ballot-access holding.[251] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] wrote that she had "never loved the President more" for appealing a ruling he had nominally won, treating the move as proof "that there is more going on."[251]

The same week, his platform [[truth-social|Truth Social]] and its parent [[trump-media-and-technology-group|Trump Media & Technology Group]] sued 20 media organisations in Florida over reporting that the company had lost $73 million since launch, which the complaint called "an utter fabrication."[251] Separately, Representative [[dan-goldman|Dan Goldman]] said on MSNBC that Trump "has to be eliminated," then apologized the next day for what he called a "poor choice of words."[251]

## January 2025: transition week actions

In the days before his second inauguration Trump moved on several fronts at once.[252] On January 16, 2025 he named Mel Gibson, Sylvester Stallone and Jon Voight as [[hollywood-special-ambassadors|Hollywood special ambassadors]] to serve as his "eyes and ears" in the entertainment capital, saying the goal was bringing back business lost to foreign countries.[252] He said he would use the momentum of the freshly agreed Gaza ceasefire to expand the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]], writing on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that his national security team, through Special Envoy to the Middle East [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]], would work with Israel and allies to ensure Gaza "NEVER again becomes a terrorist safe haven" while continuing to promote "PEACE THROUGH STRENGTH."[252] Having repeatedly threatened "hell to pay" if hostages were not released before his January 20 inauguration, he said he was "thrilled American and Israeli hostages will be returning home."[252] In a podcast interview with [[dan-bongino|Dan Bongino]] he said of the deal, "We changed the course of it, and we changed it fast, and frankly, it better be done before I take the oath of office," adding, "We shook hands, and we signed certain documents, but it better be done."[252] Trump also personally encouraged [[vivek-ramaswamy|Vivek Ramaswamy]] to accept appointment to Ohio's vacant Senate seat if offered.[252]

Reporting at the time characterized "Trump 2.0" as a radically different and potentially more potent figure than the one who arrived in Washington eight years earlier, better versed in how Washington works, backed by a disciplined operation under chief of staff [[susie-wiles|Susie Wiles]], holding the full support of the Republican Party and a popular-vote mandate.[252] Badlands contributor Burning Bright cast Trump as "the James T. Kirk of the Info War and the Shadow War," with the Middle East as his [[kobayashi-maru|Kobayashi Maru]] — an apparently unwinnable test whose parameters he intends to rewrite rather than accept.[252]

## November 2025: sedition accusations, MBS talks, and Venezuela

Trump reacted to the [[seditious-six-video|"Seditious Six" video]] of November 18, 2025 by calling the six Democratic lawmakers "traitors" and accusing them of "SEDITIOUS BEHAVIOR"; press secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] later clarified that he wanted the six to face consequences but did not want members of Congress executed.[253] Defense Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]] separately labelled the video a "politically-motivated influence operation."[253]

Axios reported that Trump's November 18 meeting with Saudi Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] grew tense when he pressed the crown prince to join the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]] and normalize relations with [[israel|Israel]]; MBS pushed back, citing anti-Israel Saudi public opinion after the Gaza war, and Trump was reportedly disappointed even as both men praised each other publicly.[253]

Speaking for the first time about the collapse of the cases against [[james-comey|James Comey]] and [[letitia-james|Letitia James]] — dismissed by Judge [[cameron-mcgowan-currie|Cameron McGowan Currie]] over the illegal appointment of prosecutor [[lindsey-halligan|Lindsey Halligan]] — Trump said they "got out on a technicality" and that "anybody that looks at it very fairly would say, boy, are they guilty," while noting the court had not barred re-bringing or appealing the cases.[253]

On Venezuela, Trump appeared on Truth Social to endorse the discredited theory that [[venezuela|Venezuela]]'s leadership controls electronic voting software worldwide and caused his 2020 defeat, a claim ruled false by a judge in 2023 and central to the [[dominion-voting-systems|Dominion Voting]] suit Fox News settled for $787 million.[253] Days later Axios reported he planned to speak directly with [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolas Maduro]] despite the designation of the [[cartel-de-los-soles|Cartel of the Suns]] as a foreign terrorist organization, a shift suggesting strikes or ground operations were not imminent.[253]

His White House also drew Republican frustration by omitting abortion from its health care framework, leaving the [[hyde-amendment|Hyde amendment]] fight unresolved in negotiations over expiring [[affordable-care-act|Affordable Care Act]] subsidies.[253]

## June 2025: Iran strikes, NATO summit, and media fights

In late June 2025 Trump presided over a cluster of overlapping controversies covered in the Badlands News Brief.[254] His administration disputed a leaked low-confidence Defense Intelligence Agency assessment of the U.S. strikes on Iran's nuclear facilities, and he amplified Secretary of State Marco Rubio's line that "there's no way Iran comes to the table if somehow nothing happened" — a move Badlands analysts read as putting his opponents in a pincer where they became the ones crying "fake news."[254] Commentators also argued Trump was deliberately "up-branding" the B-2 Spirit as an unimaginable superweapon, both as a deterrent and as a narrative inoculation intended to dissolve the nuclear-threat narrative permanently.[254] At the NATO summit in The Hague he lashed out at Israel and Iran for allegedly breaching his brokered ceasefire, saying before departing that the two had "been fighting so long and so hard that they do not know what the f**k they are doing," and the next day likened them to "kids fighting in a schoolyard"; NATO Secretary General Mark Rutte, seated beside him, replied that "daddy has to, sometimes, use strong language."[254] On Truth Social that Wednesday evening Trump called for the corruption trial of Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu to be "CANCELLED, IMMEDIATELY, or a Pardon given," praising him as "a WARRIOR, like perhaps no other Warrior in the History of Israel" and dismissing the charges as a politically motivated "WITCH HUNT."[254] He also urged Republicans to support dismantling Voice of America as partisan and wasteful, and had earlier authorized Attorney General Pam Bondi to investigate the unlawful use of online fundraising platforms for "straw" or "dummy" contributions, a probe running parallel to House subpoenas of ActBlue officials.[254] Separately, envoy Steve Witkoff said expansion of the Abraham Accords remains one of the president's key objectives.[254]

## July 2024: running mate selection and convention

On the third night of the [[2024-republican-national-convention|2024 Republican National Convention]], Republicans welcomed [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] as Trump's running mate, an evening also devoted to attacking [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s leadership on the world stage.[255] Vance's nomination effectively coronated him as heir apparent to Trump's movement, blending Appalachian working-class roots with Trump's economic populism and nationalism, and the night's mix of national security talk and populist attacks was said to encapsulate what Trump's "America First" agenda meant for the GOP and the US role as a global superpower.[255] Vance hailed Trump as a man who "will stand up for American companies" and for "union and nonunion" workers.[255]

On July 16, [[cnn|CNN]]'s [[john-king|John King]] said Trump was on course for a historic landslide, with a plausible path to 330 or more electoral votes against the 270 needed to win.[255] The tech industry also shifted toward Trump, with [[marc-andreessen|Marc Andreessen]] and [[ben-horowitz|Ben Horowitz]] of [[andreessen-horowitz|Andreessen Horowitz]] each planning donations to his re-election effort following [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]]'s endorsement and PAC formation.[255]

## Relationship with Netanyahu

Axios reported in July 2024 that [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] had spent years working behind the scenes to win Trump back, with allies meeting Trump on at least four occasions over three years after ties deteriorated when Netanyahu congratulated Biden on his 2020 victory.[255] Badlands commentary recounted that in a December 21, 2021 interview with [[barak-ravid|Barak Ravid]], Trump revealed that as president he had quietly backed [[benny-gantz|Benny Gantz]] against Netanyahu in the April 2019 Israeli election.[255]

Trump opposed Netanyahu's plans for [[west-bank-annexation|West Bank annexation]] and settlement construction, warning him against further annexation just fifteen days into his term in 2017 and telling Ravid that he had stopped the settlement plan in May 2020.[255] Ravid said that blockage is what leveraged Netanyahu into the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]], which Netanyahu attempted to abandon a day before signing via ambassador [[ron-dermer|Ron Dermer]] before Trump pressed him into signing.[255]

## Bitcoin and energy

Badlands commentator [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] linked Trump's rapid public turn in favor of Bitcoin adoption — particularly mining — to national defense and energy infrastructure, reading it alongside the Department of Energy's [[frontiers-in-artificial-intelligence-for-science-security-and-technology|FASST]] initiative as part of long-term patriot plans.[255]

## January 2024: New Hampshire win and parallel legal fights

Trump won the [[2024-new-hampshire-republican-primary|New Hampshire Republican primary]] on January 23, 2024, defeating [[nikki-haley|Nikki Haley]], his last remaining major rival for the nomination, eight days after crushing the field in the Iowa caucuses; Fox News projected the result within minutes of the final polls closing.[256] "I'm very honored by the result," Trump said in a statement, while Haley told supporters in Concord that Trump had earned the victory.[256] Badlands commentators read the sequence structurally: [[chris-paul|Chris Paul]] argued that the New Hampshire primary serves as an establishment corrective to Iowa, while [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described Trump as using intra-GOP concessions as stepping stones toward the appearance of a mandate and as bluffing the System into shedding its legitimacy mask.[256]

The same day, the [[dc-circuit-court-of-appeals|D.C. Circuit Court of Appeals]] denied Trump's petitions for rehearing — before either the original three-judge panel or the full court en banc — of its decision largely upholding the partial gag order in the Washington, D.C. election-subversion case brought by special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]].[256] In the separate [[trump-classified-documents-case|classified documents case]], Trump's push to unseal materials gained support when a coalition of news organizations asked the court to verify that the government's proposed redactions and withholdings were necessary; his legal team had moved on January 16 to compel Smith's office, before Judge [[aileen-cannon|Aileen Cannon]], to turn over a trove of documents.[256]

Trump also featured in two other contemporaneous controversies: an audio recording published by DailyMail.com in which [[jeff-dewit|Jeff DeWit]], chair of the [[arizona-republican-party|Arizona Republican Party]], appeared to offer [[kari-lake|Kari Lake]] an inducement to stay out of politics while predicting Trump would lose; and a filing by Fulton County District Attorney [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]] accusing the estranged wife of special prosecutor [[nathan-wade|Nathan Wade]] of conspiring with Trump co-defendant [[mike-roman|Mike Roman]] to undermine her prosecution.[256]

## Televised Georgia arraignment (August 2023)

In August 2023, Fulton County Superior Court Judge [[scott-mcafee|Scott McAfee]] ruled that cameras would be allowed in his courtroom for the arraignments of Trump and his co-defendants in the [[state-of-georgia-v-trump|Georgia election case]], marking the first time one of Trump's criminal proceedings would be televised.[257] District Attorney [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]], who had charged Trump the week before alongside 18 co-defendants over their alleged efforts to overturn the 2020 election results, sought to hold the arraignments the week after Labor Day; Trump denied any wrongdoing.[257] The first co-defendant, Atlanta bail bondsman [[scott-hall|Scott Hall]], surrendered at the Fulton County jail on August 22, and former chief of staff [[mark-meadows|Mark Meadows]] moved the same day to block his own arrest pending removal of his case to federal court.[257]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] wrote that Trump had become "the symbol of that rising sovereign sentiment within the American Mind" and that, whatever the old adage about revolutions not being televised, "HIS revolution WILL be televised" — the trial being "not going to be the end of the story, but the beginning of one."[257]

## Victory in the 2024 presidential election

Trump won the [[2024-us-general-election|2024 presidential election]] held on November 5, 2024, though several media organizations declined to call the race for him by the following morning and Michigan, Wisconsin, Arizona and Nevada remained uncalled while the New York Times rated all four as likely Trump states.[258] Badlands contributor [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] described the result as a definitive, landslide win including the popular vote, and argued that slow-walked counts and localized irregularities may have been intended to minimize Trump's mandate rather than to reverse the outcome.[258] Trump delivered a victory speech at Mar-a-Lago in the early hours.[258]

Exit polling supported the mandate framing: AP VoteCast found about 80 percent of voters wanted at least "substantial change" in how the country is run and roughly 25 percent wanted "complete and total upheaval," with the economy and jobs the top issue at 40 percent and immigration second at about 20 percent.[258] An October 25 New York Times/Siena College poll had found 52 percent of likely voters trusted Trump on the economy against 45 percent for [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]].[258] Just under six in ten respondents said they were at least somewhat concerned a second Trump administration would move the country closer to authoritarianism.[258]

On [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]]'s election-night coverage, [[patrick-byrne|Patrick Byrne]] argued that four years of layered "defenses in depth" had made 2024 different from 2020, and called for a returning Trump to reform US election systems outright; [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] wrote that Trump might be declared the winner "but his and our work is only just beginning."[258]

## Israeli anxiety over a second term

Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] highlighted a [[jerusalem-post|Jerusalem Post]] opinion piece published on Election Day arguing that a Trump victory would have "horrendous consequences" for the US, the free world and Israel, and that post-defeat unrest and violence in America would be preferable to a Trump win.[258] He contrasted this with Israeli public opinion, where polling showed well over 60 percent support for Trump against around 20 percent for Harris, and asked why authorities in Israel would fear the return of a president who had demonstrated such loyalty to the country.[258]

He cited an October 7, 2024 interview with [[hugh-hewitt|Hugh Hewitt]] in which Trump, asked about a planned visit to the Chabad in New York City, complained that the Israeli leadership did not reciprocate the loyalty he had shown it.[258] He also pointed to a reported [[ballot-harvesting]] operation by [[ivoteisrael|iVoteIsrael]] in central Israel, which he said proved inconsequential to the result but did not excuse the attempt, and noted the record standing ovations both US parties gave [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] during his 52-minute address to Congress in July.[258]

## Expectations on predators and trafficking

Badlands commentary tied [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]]'s criticism of [[jennifer-lopez|Jennifer Lopez]] over her silence about [[sean-combs|Sean "Diddy" Combs]] to the incoming Trump administration's promise to take on pedophiles and other predators, asking whether "the next phase" of accountability had already begun.[258]

## Woodward's *War* and the Aurora rally (October 2024)

In October 2024, journalist [[bob-woodward|Bob Woodward]]'s forthcoming book *War* reported that Trump had stayed in touch with Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] after leaving office, citing an unidentified Trump aide who said the two had "maybe as many as seven" conversations since 2021, and claiming Trump had secretly sent Putin scarce COVID-19 testing equipment early in the pandemic.[259] Putin reportedly told him, "Please don't tell anybody you sent these to me."[259] Campaign spokesman [[steven-cheung|Steven Cheung]] called the claims "made up stories," said Trump had given Woodward "absolutely no access for this trash book," and described Woodward as "a total sleazebag who has lost it mentally."[259] The book also covered Senator [[lindsey-graham|Lindsey Graham]]'s attempts to persuade Trump to move on from his 2020 election loss — an issue that returned to the campaign spotlight in the closing weeks through Trump's own comments and those of his running mate.[259] Badlands contributor GhostofBasedPatrickHenry read the leak as a narrative deployment that flattered Trump and fit the site's "Sovereign Alliance" framing.[259]

Separately, the campaign confirmed on October 7, 2024 that Trump would rally at the Gaylord Rockies Resort & Convention Center in [[aurora-colorado|Aurora, Colorado]] that Friday, describing the city as a "war zone" beset by an "influx of violent Venezuelan prison gang members from [[tren-de-aragua|Tren de Aragua]]."[259] Governor [[jared-polis|Jared Polis]] countered that Trump would find a city with two straight years of rapidly declining crime, while Representative [[lauren-boebert|Lauren Boebert]] said she was "ready and thrilled" to host him.[259] Badlands writer [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] predicted "a sea of MAGA" and accused the Colorado establishment of concealing a migrant gang takeover of Aurora apartment buildings.[259] Contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] separately noted that President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] had repealed Green New Deal proposals to boost domestic energy and chip production, policies Trump had proposed.[259]

## "Messenger-in-chief" after the Kirk assassination (September 2025)

Following the [[assassination-of-charlie-kirk|assassination of Charlie Kirk]], Trump embraced the role of national spokesman to an extraordinary degree, being first to confirm to a shocked country that Kirk was dead and first to announce that a suspect was in custody, sharing the date of Kirk's funeral and saying he would attend.[260] Before a suspect was detained, he blamed the "radical left" for the murder without presenting evidence, an accusation echoed by many followers who called for vengeance amid a wave of right-wing anger.[260] Reuters noted that Trump delivered information that would ordinarily come from law enforcement or local officials rather than the nation's top leader, contrasting his approach with the more cautious posture of past presidents while noting it accorded with his penchant for direct communication and defiance of convention.[260]

Trump also amplified a viral TikTok video by user Ellie May urging him to revive the [[smith-mundt-act|Smith-Mundt Act]] under the name the "Charlie Kirk Act," sharing it on his Truth Social account; a related petition gathered more than 5,000 signatures within 13 hours.[260] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the moment as an "engineered psychospiritual storm," arguing that debates between PsyOps and PsyActs distract from the narrative emerging from the chaos, and that Kirk had become the embodiment of an assault on the vision of reclaimed sovereignty, economic resurgence and cultural revival that Trump and the [[america-first-movement|America First movement]] represent.[260]

## Tariffs and the Liberation Day framing

Coverage of the April 2, 2025 [[liberation-day-tariffs|"Liberation Day" tariffs]] described the day as producing the largest worldwide market decline since 2020, with the measures — effective April 5 — imposing a 10% baseline tariff on nearly all imported goods plus higher "reciprocal tariffs" on countries running large trade surpluses with the United States.[260] China faced escalations to 34% and in some instances 54%, with plans for further increases to 104%; the European Union saw 20%, Japan 24%, Vietnam 45%, and India 26% later climbing to 50%, while Canada and Mexico also faced new levies.[260] The policy was the most comprehensive imposition of trade barriers in over a century, surpassing the Smoot-Hawley Tariff Act of 1930 and pushing the average effective U.S. tariff rate to roughly 22%; the administration framed it as a "declaration of economic independence," asserting unprecedented tariff authority under the [[international-emergency-economic-powers-act|International Emergency Economic Powers Act]].[260]

Burning Bright interpreted the pattern as a "Reverse [[hegelian-dialectic|Hegelian Dialectic]]" that hijacks globalist crisis cascades, arguing that the [[sovereign-trinity|Sovereign Trinity]] of Trump, Putin, Xi and an emergent mesh around them is dismantling the hegemon's grip without firing the shots the System requires to survive, and that "Liberation Day" functions as a template mapping the unwinding of the Deep State's network rather than a single event.[260]

## Qatar meeting after the Israeli strike on Doha

Trump planned to meet Qatari Prime Minister [[mohammed-bin-abdulrahman-al-thani|Sheikh Mohammed bin Abdulrahman Al-Thani]] in New York, a White House official said, days after [[israel|Israel]] attacked [[hamas]] leaders in Doha in a strike that risked derailing US-backed efforts to broker a truce in Gaza.[260] Trump said he was unhappy with the strike, describing it as a unilateral action that did not advance US or Israeli interests, and Washington continued to count [[qatar|Qatar]] as a strong Gulf ally and principal mediator on ceasefire, hostage and post-conflict questions.[260] Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] noted that Trump had already ordered [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] to draft a defense agreement with Qatar providing military protection in the event of another Israeli strike.[260]

## Bronx rally, May 2024

On May 23, 2024, Trump held a campaign rally at Crotona Park in the Bronx, breaking political norms by campaigning in one of the most heavily Democratic parts of New York City.[261] His campaign estimated the crowd at about 25,000, far above the 3,500 initially expected, counting supporters lined up outside who had waited hours for entry.[261] Trump told the crowd, "I'm here tonight to declare we are going to turn New York City around, and we are going to turn it around very, very quickly," promising renewed safety and better schools and vowing to "make New York bigger, better and greater than ever before."[261] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that the [[2024-trump-bronx-rally|rally]] was historic not for its size or its blue-state setting but for something "intrinsic, American and yes, racial" that he claimed the Deep State fears most, and framed it as evidence the Awakening was shifting from its "very slowly" phase to an "all at once" coda.[261]

## Pomerantz defamation threat (January 2023)

In January 2023, Trump retained trial attorney [[joe-tacopina|Joe Tacopina]] to pursue former Manhattan prosecutor [[mark-pomerantz|Mark Pomerantz]] over allegedly defamatory statements.[262] Tacopina's letter of January 23, 2023, objected to Pomerantz's assertions that Trump was "guilty of numerous felony violations" and that failing to prosecute him was a "grave failure of justice," and warned that Trump's lawyers would "aggressively pursue all legal remedies" against Pomerantz and his publisher, Simon & Schuster, if his forthcoming book *The People vs. Donald Trump* was published.[262]

## Mar-a-Lago press conference (October 29, 2024)

On Tuesday, October 29, 2024, Trump held a press conference at [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]] that ran a little under an hour, opening in a somber tone with the line, "We're here to talk about the character of [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]]."[263] [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] carried special coverage of the event, framing it as Trump's answer to Harris's brief press gaggle the previous week in which she had spent five minutes comparing him to Hitler — but with far more substance, including policy announcements.[263] Trump characterized his opponent's run as a "campaign of destruction" and a "campaign of hate," while the subtext of his remarks pointed to foreign interference in U.S. elections; addressing how he would respond to slander from abroad, he declared, "We will obliterate your entire country."[263]

The central policy announcement was that, as president, he would for the first time seize the assets of criminal gangs and drug cartels and use those assets to create a compensation fund providing restitution for victims of migrant crime.[263] He also doubled down on his call for the death penalty for any migrant who kills an American, including police officers.[263] The announcement followed earlier reporting that Trump had told allies he would covertly deploy Tier 1 U.S. Special Forces operators to Mexico to eliminate cartel leadership if he returned to the White House.[263]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] linked the asset-seizure plan to the Badlands concept of "Drainage," arguing that the collapses of institutions from Silicon Valley Bank and First Republic to Credit Suisse could relate to the activation of [[executive-order-13818|Executive Order 13818]] of December 21, 2017, "Blocking the Property of Persons Involved in Serious Human Rights Abuse or Corruption" — a broad instrument allowing the president, or a military to which he has devolved authority, to pursue enemies foreign and domestic, including those winning fraudulent elections.[263] In that framing, power projection takes narrative, kinetic and financial forms, and applying drainage across all three produces considerable variance in outcomes.[263]

During the same period, transition team leader [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] gave a widely noted financial interview arguing that bitcoin, tariffs and a Trump victory would make Americans rich, which Badlands read as evidence that Trump had long known exactly whom he needed in key positions.[263]

## Reaction to the January 6 footage release, March 2023

Trump responded to [[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]]'s March 6, 2023 broadcast of previously unseen Capitol security footage by declaring "Free at Last," framing the material as portraying a picture of the day different from the one painted by former House Speaker [[nancy-pelosi|Nancy Pelosi]].[264] Reporting on the segment noted that the footage — including scenes of [[jacob-chansley|Jacob Chansley]] being calmly escorted through the Capitol by police officers — cut against narratives advanced by former Rep. [[liz-cheney|Liz Cheney]] and the [[january-6-committee|January 6 committee]], which had characterized participants as taking part in an "insurrection."[264] Badlands contributor [[patel-patriot|Patel Patriot]] argued that January 6 had served as "the primary weapon of the establishment in their attempts to destroy Trump's political future," and that the bipartisan backlash to the [[january-6-capitol-security-footage-release|footage release]] followed from its damage to that narrative.[264]

## January 2025 executive actions and second-term milestones

On January 29, 2025, Trump signed the [[laken-riley-act|Laken Riley Act]] into law, the first bill of his second presidency to become law, expanding the categories of people who can be arrested, detained and deported by federal immigration officers; he thanked Democrats for the bipartisan support, calling it "a landmark law ... it will save countless innocent American lives."[265] The same day he announced he would instruct the [[pentagon|Departments of Defense]] and [[department-of-homeland-security|Homeland Security]] to prepare a migrant detention facility at [[guantanamo-bay|Guantanamo Bay]] for as many as 30,000 migrants, signing a memorandum calling for "additional detention space."[265] He also signed an executive order to combat antisemitism, pledging to deport non-citizen students who joined pro-Palestinian protests and to cancel the student visas of "all Hamas sympathizers on college campuses."[265] A further order created [[task-force-250|Task Force 250]] to organize the celebration of the 250th anniversary of American independence on July 4, 2026, reinstating the National Garden of American Heroes orders and Executive Order 13933 on protecting monuments.[265] His administration also ended [[temporary-protected-status|temporary protected status]] for more than 600,000 Venezuelan nationals, announced by Homeland Security Secretary [[kristi-noem|Kristi Noem]].[265]

Trump's [[office-of-management-and-budget|Office of Management and Budget]] rescinded its controversial federal funding freeze memo on January 29, 2025 after Republican pushback, with acting director [[matthew-vaeth|Matthew Vaeth]] signing the reversal.[265] The [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] held interest rates steady the same day, days after Trump called on the central bank to lower them, with Chair [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] declining to respond to the president's demand.[265] Trump also invited Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] to the [[white-house|White House]] for a February 4 meeting, making him the first foreign leader to visit in Trump's second term, while pressing him to proceed with the second phase of the Gaza hostage deal.[265] Meanwhile, [[vogue|Vogue]] drew Badlands criticism for mocking First Lady [[melania-trump|Melania Trump]]'s official portrait, and [[trump-media-and-technology-group|Trump Media and Technology Group]] launched [[truth-fi|Truth.Fi]] to serve "American patriots" affected by debanking.[265]

## July 2025: Japan trade deal, treason claims, and women's sports

On July 22, 2025, Trump announced a "massive" trade deal with Japan under which Japan would open its market to American cars, trucks, rice and other agricultural products and invest $550 billion in the United States, with Japanese exports facing a 15 percent tariff rather than the 25 percent he had threatened.[266] See [[2025-us-japan-trade-agreement|2025 US–Japan trade agreement]].[266]

The same week, his administration's claims that [[barack-obama|Barack Obama]] administration officials had planned a "treasonous conspiracy" against him drew a rare public rebuke from Obama's office, which called them "a weak attempt at distraction."[266] The claims rested on material released by Director of National Intelligence [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]]; Trump summarized his position as "I caught them all… let's see what happens."[266]

His executive order "Keeping Men Out of Women's Sports," [[executive-order-14201|Executive Order 14201]], produced compliance from the [[us-olympic-and-paralympic-committee|U.S. Olympic and Paralympic Committee]], which in July 2025 replaced its transgender eligibility policy with a reference to an athlete safety policy citing the order.[266] Wyoming Secretary of State [[chuck-gray|Chuck Gray]] separately credited Trump's election integrity priorities when a federal judge dismissed the challenge to [[wyoming-house-bill-156|Wyoming House Bill 156]].[266]

## December 2025 developments

The Supreme Court agreed on December 4, 2025 to let Texas use a Trump-backed congressional map in the 2026 midterms while litigation continues, a boost to the Republican redistricting campaign to maintain control of Congress.[267] The same day Trump hosted DRC President Felix Tshisekedi and Rwandan President Paul Kagame in Washington to sign deals aimed at stabilizing eastern Congo and attracting Western mining investment, part of a diplomatic push that since January had ranged from the Middle East to Ukraine with mixed results and drew criticism that he should focus on domestic cost-of-living discontent.[267] In a Truth Social post he denounced Colorado Governor Jared Polis as a 'SLEAZEBAG' for keeping Tina Peters jailed and demanded 'FREE TINA!', while his special envoy Steve Witkoff and son-in-law Jared Kushner met Vladimir Putin in Moscow amid European fears of an American betrayal on Ukrainian territory.[267]

## Fourth of July weekend 2025

Over the Fourth of July weekend of 2025, Trump signed his [[one-big-beautiful-bill-act|"One Big Beautiful Bill"]] into law at the White House, hours after an hour-long speech in Des Moines, Iowa that capped the America250 event at the Iowa State Fairgrounds and launched a yearlong celebration of the nation's 250th birthday.[268] He touted the bill's passage from the balcony overlooking the South Lawn.[268]

The same weekend, his [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] and the [[fbi|FBI]] concluded in a memo obtained by Axios that there was no evidence [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]] kept a "client list," blackmailed powerful figures, or was murdered — the first time his administration had officially contradicted those theories, which had been promoted by FBI Director [[kash-patel|Kash Patel]] and Deputy Director [[dan-bongino|Dan Bongino]] before their appointments.[268]

Trump also attacked [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] on July 6 after his former adviser announced the [[america-party|America Party]], writing that he was "saddened to watch Elon Musk go completely 'off the rails,' essentially becoming a TRAIN WRECK over the past five weeks" and that third parties "have never succeeded in the United States."[268]

On trade, Trump and Commerce Secretary [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] confirmed that countries that had not struck deals with the United States by August 1 would revert to the tariff levels set in April, with 12 to 15 letters to foreign governments expected to begin going out that Monday at noon.[268]

In foreign policy, Trump prepared to host Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] in Washington, having publicly stated that he expected to negotiate an end to the Gaza war during the meeting, while [[hamas|Hamas]] reportedly responded positively to proposed ceasefire terms.[268] Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] meanwhile highlighted historically friendly Russia–US relations as Trump sought to broker an end to the Ukraine conflict.[268]

Domestically, the administration faced continued [[court-shopping|court shopping]] by litigants seeking favourable district courts, though a Supreme Court decision was reported to offer some relief.[268] Trump was also blamed by some commentators, alongside climate change, for the [[2025-central-texas-floods|2025 Central Texas floods]] on the basis of alleged cuts to NOAA funding — an attribution Badlands contributors rejected as gaslighting.[268] Badlands writer [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] pointed to signals from [[russ-vought|Russ Vought]] and [[steve-bannon|Steve Bannon]] that Trump would use impoundments to strip wasteful spending from the new law, recalling his June 2023 [[agenda-47|Agenda 47]] pledge that "bringing back impoundment will give us a crucial tool with which to obliterate the deep state, drain the swamp, and starve the warmongers."[268]

## Post-conviction surge and pop-culture signals (June 2024)

In the days following his May 30, 2024 conviction on 34 felony counts in the [[trump-hush-money-case|Manhattan hush-money case]], Trump's campaign raised more than $200 million, including roughly $70 million from small-dollar donors and $53 million within the first 24 hours — with Trump telling Fox News's Maria Bartiromo that at least 30 percent of donors were first-time political donors.[269] He also joined TikTok, accumulating 2 million followers against [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s 337,000, prompting [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] to declare narratives that his support was inorganic "deceased."[269] Actor [[dennis-quaid|Dennis Quaid]] announced he intended to vote for Trump, citing "a weaponization of our justice system."[269]

The same weekend, Trump received a hero's welcome at UFC 302 in Newark, New Jersey and was filmed in an apparently impromptu cage-side exchange with former [[ufc|UFC]] champion [[khabib-nurmagomedov|Khabib Nurmagomedov]]; [[dana-white|Dana White]] said afterward that he wanted [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] present should the UFC ever stage an event in Moscow.[269] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] grouped these with Putin's award of the Order of Friendship to [[steven-seagal|Steven Seagal]] as evidence of an [[overton-window|Overton Window]] shift in popular culture.[269] Badlands also noted that on June 18, 2013 — the day Putin proposed Seagal as an honorary consul — Trump joked on Twitter about becoming Putin's best friend at that November's Moscow-hosted Miss Universe pageant.[269] Separately, X and NewsNation were reported to be planning a live "The People's Town Hall" interview with Trump and [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]], which the Biden campaign declined to join.[269]

## "Meet the Press" interview (December 2024)

Trump's first sit-down interview after winning a second term aired on Sunday, December 8, 2024, on NBC's "Meet the Press," offering a view into his mindset as he prepared to return to office.[270] Speaking with moderator [[kristen-welker|Kristen Welker]], Trump discussed his plans for his first day in office, several of his more controversial Cabinet choices, and whether he would like the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] to pursue his critics and rivals.[270]

Trump indicated he would not explicitly order his appointees to go after political rivals, even as he continued to attack special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] and again suggested that lawmakers who investigated the January 6, 2021 attack on the Capitol, such as former Representative [[liz-cheney|Liz Cheney]], should be jailed.[270] Asked about [[kash-patel|Kash Patel]], his choice to lead the [[fbi|FBI]], who had spoken of the need to target the "deep state," Trump said that "if they think that somebody was dishonest or crooked or a corrupt politician, I think he probably has an obligation to do it," but when Welker asked whether he would direct Patel to do so, he answered: "No. Not at all. Not at all."[270] He said similarly that he would leave it to his attorney general nominee [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]] to determine whether to prosecute figures such as Jack Smith: "I will say this: No, I'm not doing that unless I find something that I think is reasonable, but that's not going to be my decision. That's going to be Pam Bondi's decision, and, to a different extent, Kash Patel, assuming they're both there, and I think they're both going to get approved."[270]

[[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] wrote that a defining feature of Trump's first term was that he did the things he campaigned on, and that the regime's alarm stemmed from expecting him to do so again — citing mass deportations, fair trade policies, ending US imperialism, pardoning political prisoners, and unleashing US energy.[270] She predicted that by July 4, 2026 the country would be transformed.[270] Separately, Badlands writer [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] cast the unfolding events in Syria and beyond as a soap opera he called [[trumpamania|TrumpaMania]], "brought to you by Executive Producer President Donald J Trump," and noted Trump's public instruction regarding Syria: do not get involved.[270]

## Post-election exercise of power (November 2024)

In the days after his November 2024 victory, CNN reported that Trump was already flexing raw power, suggesting he might try to subvert Washington's checks and balances and leaving foreign leaders scrambling to come to terms with his win.[271] Early signs from [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]], the Florida club and estate where he was building his new administration, indicated that when he returned to the [[white-house|White House]] in January — bolstered by a thumping win and a democratic mandate — he would act with maximum force.[271] He used social media to press Senate Republicans running in that week's majority leader election to endorse [[recess-appointments|recess appointments]] for his Cabinet nominees, and all three candidates quickly signaled openness; CNN wrote that he planned to rule a GOP monopoly on power with unchallenged authority, treating [[us-congress|Congress]] as a rubber stamp rather than a co-equal branch.[271] Shortly before midnight he posted on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that he had named [[tom-homan|Tom Homan]], former acting director of [[immigration-and-customs-enforcement|ICE]], as border czar.[271]

[[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] wrote that the post-election shock among mainstream analysts had turned to panic and dread, and welcomed the mandate framing, arguing that the administration would need to "move fast and break things" to make America great again in four years.[271] She contended that the overwhelming mandate — Trump winning both the Electoral College and the popular vote — required that bureaucratic obstruction be neutralized, describing him as "triumphantly returning to the swamp with a massive sump pump."[271]

On foreign policy, a senior [[nato|NATO]] official, Admiral [[rob-bauer|Rob Bauer]], warned that any peace deal Trump negotiated allowing [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] to claim victory in [[ukraine|Ukraine]] would undermine U.S. interests; Trump had repeatedly said he could end the war in a day, and a settlement outlined by Vice President-elect [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] in September echoed Kremlin demands.[271] Transition spokeswoman [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] said Trump was re-elected because Americans "trust him to lead our country and restore peace through strength around the world."[271] Separately, the Kremlin's [[dmitry-peskov|Dmitry Peskov]] denied Washington Post reporting that Trump and Putin had spoken by phone, calling it "pure fiction."[271]

Trump also named former New York congressman [[lee-zeldin|Lee Zeldin]] to lead the [[environmental-protection-agency|Environmental Protection Agency]] and adviser [[stephen-miller|Stephen Miller]] as deputy chief of policy.[271] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described a bifurcation in the emerging administration between narrative-level figures such as [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] and [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|RFK Jr.]] and the "lesser" names — [[kash-patel|Kash Patel]], Miller, Homan, [[mike-waltz|Mike Waltz]] and [[susie-wiles|Susie Wiles]] — that he argued mattered most to the Deep State.[271] Meanwhile Republicans were projected to retain the [[us-house-of-representatives|House of Representatives]], delivering Trump a governing trifecta, and at the [[2024-riyadh-arab-islamic-summit|Riyadh Arab-Islamic summit]] Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] hardened Saudi rhetoric toward [[israel|Israel]] days after the U.S. vote.[271]

## Post-2024 Transition: Retribution, Appointments, and Foreign Policy

In the weeks following his November 2024 election victory, Trump's repeated vows to seek revenge against political adversaries took on what The Hill described as a renewed seriousness, alarming those who had crossed him during his first term and the intervening years.[272] Reporting noted that after inauguration he would have new avenues to act on those calls and that he was assembling a team well positioned to carry out any retribution, including the nomination of former Rep. [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]] as attorney general and the placement of several lawyers from his criminal defense team in senior Department of Justice posts.[272] House Republicans, having retained their majority, also vowed to investigate Trump adversaries.[272]

Badlands commentary framed Trump's campaign line "I will be your retribution" as one of his most misunderstood statements, arguing that its meaning was foreshadowed by his 2017 inaugural address, in which he described the transfer of power not from one administration or party to another but from Washington, D.C. back to the American people.[272] In this reading the first Trump administration, the intervening "Biden Pause," and the incoming second term formed a three-act strategy whose appointments had been decided long in advance.[272]

On November 19, 2024 Trump confirmed via Truth Social that he planned to declare a national emergency and use the U.S. military to carry out mass deportations, reposting a comment by Judicial Watch president [[tom-fitton|Tom Fitton]] with the caption "TRUE!!"[272] Estimates cited roughly 11 million undocumented immigrants in the United States, with the program expected to affect around 20 million families.[272]

During the same period Trump announced a rapid series of appointments: [[brendan-carr|Brendan Carr]] as Federal Communications Commission chairman, whom he called a "warrior for free speech"; [[linda-mcmahon|Linda McMahon]] as secretary of education, an agency he had vowed to abolish; [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] as commerce secretary to lead a sweeping tariff program; and former congressman and Fox Business host [[sean-duffy|Sean Duffy]] as transportation secretary.[272] Trump praised Duffy as a "tremendous and well-liked public servant" and said he would eliminate DEI requirements for pilots and air traffic controllers.[272]

MSNBC "Morning Joe" hosts [[joe-scarborough|Joe Scarborough]] and [[mika-brzezinski|Mika Brzezinski]], long among his fiercest critics, traveled to Mar-a-Lago in mid-November 2024 to meet him and reopen lines of communication; Brzezinski described him as "cheerful, upbeat."[272] Trump was also photographed eating McDonald's aboard his plane with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]], [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]], Donald Trump Jr. and House Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] after a UFC event in New York City, an image that went viral and spawned a hoax claiming it had been predicted by a 1721 painting.[272]

On the legal front, a Georgia appeals court canceled December 5 oral arguments in Trump's bid to disqualify Fulton County District Attorney [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]], while New York prosecutors signaled in a court filing that they were open to postponing sentencing in his hush money case, potentially until the end of a second presidential term in 2029.[272] Trump's lawyers had urged Judge [[juan-merchan|Juan Merchan]] to dismiss the case outright "to facilitate the orderly transition of executive power."[272]

## Ballot eligibility and classified documents litigation (December 2023)

On December 6, 2023, the [[colorado-supreme-court|Colorado Supreme Court]] heard argument in [[anderson-v-griswold|Anderson v. Griswold]] over whether Trump could be excluded from the state's Republican primary ballot under Section 3 of the [[fourteenth-amendment|Fourteenth Amendment]] because of his conduct surrounding January 6, one of challenges pending in more than a dozen states.[273] Two days earlier, Judge [[aileen-cannon|Aileen Cannon]] denied special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]]'s request to keep certain documents sealed in the [[trump-classified-documents-case|classified documents case]], directing the clerk to unseal several docket entries.[273] Trump has maintained that he used presidential powers to declassify the materials held at [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]] and has called the prosecution an attempt by political foes to hamper his 2024 run.[273] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] wrote that Trump had told supporters they were in the middle of his "second term," but that he did not want its results.[273]

## October 2025: Portland, Gaza, the shutdown, and the boat strikes

On October 5, 2025, after a federal court blocked his attempt to federalize the Oregon National Guard, Trump deployed 300 [[california-army-national-guard|California National Guard]] troops to Portland, Oregon, in the [[2025-portland-national-guard-deployment|2025 Portland National Guard deployment]], drawing a lawsuit threat from Governor [[gavin-newsom|Gavin Newsom]], who called the order "un-American."[274]

When [[hamas|Hamas]] returned a "yes, but" answer to his Gaza peace proposal, Trump called Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] to share what he considered good news; told by Netanyahu that it was "nothing to celebrate," Trump replied, "I don't know why you're always so f***ing negative. This is a win. Take it."[274]

During the [[october-2025-government-shutdown|October 2025 government shutdown]] he warned Democrats he would decide which "Democrat agencies" to cut and whether the reductions would be permanent, calling the shutdown an "unprecedented opportunity," and promoted [[russ-vought|Russ Vought]] — "he of PROJECT 2025 Fame" — as his budget enforcer, even sharing an AI-generated video depicting Vought as the grim reaper.[274]

Speaking at the [[us-navy|US Navy]]'s 250th anniversary ceremony in Norfolk, Virginia, on October 5, Trump described strikes on alleged drug cartel vessels off [[venezuela|Venezuela]] as "an act of kindness," saying every one of those boats was "responsible for the death of 25,000 American people," praising the military for helping "blow the cartel terrorists the hell out of the water," and noting that "nobody wants to go into the water anymore."[274] He also singled out 100-year-old retired Chief Petty Officer [[felix-maurizio|Felix Maurizio]] during the speech.[274] His administration separately moved to staff immigration courts with military attorneys under the [[military-lawyers-as-immigration-judges-program|military lawyers as immigration judges program]].[274]


## Anticipated Manhattan Arrest (March 2023)

On Saturday, March 18, 2023, Donald Trump announced via [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that he expected to be arrested the following Tuesday in connection with the ongoing investigation by Manhattan District Attorney [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]], an announcement that Badlands Media characterized as having "shocked the world."[275] The matter concerned Trump's alleged 2016 payment of $130,000 in hush money to [[stormy-daniels|Stormy Daniels]] through his former personal attorney [[michael-cohen|Michael Cohen]], whom Trump later reimbursed.[275] Trump has denied claims that he had an affair with Daniels in the early 2000s, and unconfirmed reports at the time alleged that a New York grand jury had been empaneled and might seek an indictment.[275]

Badlands Media contributor [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] argued that Trump was "a master of navigating optics and legal/political battles" and was using the threat of indictment and arrest as a means to campaign and raise awareness, calling it "a brilliant move by Trump and a good step forward for the [[america-first-movement|America First]] agenda."[275] In Deschamps' framing, Trump's purpose was to expose the fraud of the existing legal system by placing himself at the front of the line and standing with a public already harmed by what he called a defunct legal system, thereby compelling the people to rise up and restore the [[rule-of-law|rule of law]].[275] He predicted that [[lawfare|lawfare]] driven by [[deep-state-cabal|Cabal]] actors would continue to destroy the system's credibility, and that efforts by [[uniparty|uniparty]] actors to block Trump's 2024 run through indictment would fail.[275]

## Autopen, Clinton posts and the Putin call (May 2025)

On Saturday, May 17, 2025, Trump used [[truth-social|Truth Social]] to attack [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] after leaked audio of Biden's interview with special counsel [[robert-hur|Robert Hur]] became public, writing that "Whoever had control of the 'AUTOPEN' is looking to be a bigger and bigger scandal by the moment," and adding that "the biggest crime of all is that THE 2020 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION WAS RIGGED!"[276] The same weekend he reshared a 2016 video captioned "THE VIDEO HILLARY CLINTON DOES NOT WANT YOU TO SEE," reviving the long-standing claim that [[bill-clinton|Bill Clinton]] and [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] are connected to a series of deaths; the Washington Post characterized the posts as false conspiracy theories and noted Democratic warnings that such rhetoric could lead to violence.[276] Badlands writer Burning Bright instead read the post as a deliberate signal, noting that a sitting president had amplified "the most direct Clinton Body Count proof he could, including [[jfk-jr|JFK Jr.]]"[276]

Trump also announced he would speak by telephone with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] on Monday at 10:00 a.m. about "stopping the 'bloodbath'" in Ukraine and about trade, followed by separate calls with [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] and members of [[nato|NATO]].[276] His administration's executive order on [[birthright-citizenship|birthright citizenship]] was simultaneously before the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] in the case over [[nationwide-injunctions|universal injunctions]], where Justice [[elena-kagan|Elena Kagan]] told Solicitor General [[d-john-sauer|John Sauer]] that "every court is ruling against you."[276]

## September 2024 remarks on Ukraine

At a campaign event in North Carolina in September 2024, Trump lashed out at Ukrainian president [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] for refusing to make concessions to Russia, in what reporters described as his strongest indication to date that he would stop backing Kyiv if he won the presidential election.[277] He said Ukraine should have "given up a little bit" to appease Moscow and avoid a war that "didnt need to happen," complained that "we continue to give billions of dollars to a man who refuses to make a deal," and argued that "any deal, even the worst deal, wouldve been better than what we have right now."[277] Trump added that "Ukraine is gone, its not Ukraine anymore," saying destroyed cities and the dead could never be replaced.[277] Badlands Media read the remarks as a signal that Trump was preparing to abandon [[nato|NATO]] policy on Ukraine just as [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] was advertising a revised doctrine and hypersonic strike capability, and noted that Zelensky was due to meet Trump the following day.[277]

## Relations with Abbas and Netanyahu

Badlands writers recalled that in July 2024, the night before [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] addressed Congress, Trump publicly posted a letter that Palestinian president [[mahmoud-abbas|Mahmoud Abbas]] had sent him expressing concern over the attempt on Trumps life, complete with a hand-written note.[277] The gesture was cited as evidence of Trumps differing personal assessments of the two leaders.[277]

## July 2025: Golf Force One, Gaza starvation, and the Epstein pressure

During a golf outing at his Turnberry course in Scotland in late July 2025, Trump was shadowed by a new [[united-states-secret-service|Secret Service]] armored escort vehicle — a modified Polaris Ranger dubbed [[golf-force-one|"Golf Force One"]] by the press — which trailed his standard golf cart across the links.[278]

Speaking during a meeting with UK Prime Minister [[keir-starmer|Keir Starmer]] in Scotland, Trump said there was "real starvation" in [[gaza|Gaza]], directly contradicting Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]]'s insistence that claims of Israeli-caused hunger were a "bold-faced lie."[278] Asked whether he agreed with Netanyahu, Trump replied: "I don't know... those children look very hungry... that's real starvation stuff," adding, "Nobody's done anything great over there. The whole place is a mess... I told Israel maybe they have to do it a different way."[278] His comments followed the UN humanitarian chief's warning that "vast amounts" of food were needed to stave off starvation.[278]

Trump also faced sustained pressure from within his own coalition over the [[epstein-files|Epstein files]], with podcaster [[joe-rogan|Joe Rogan]] calling the administration's refusal to release more information a "line in the sand."[278] Simultaneously, [[cia|CIA]] Director [[john-ratcliffe|John Ratcliffe]] escalated the administration's [[russiagate-hoax|Russiagate]] accountability push by suggesting indictments for [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]], [[james-comey|James Comey]] and [[john-brennan|John Brennan]].[278] Badlands commentary argued Trump had anticipated the convergence, gaining bipartisan "narrative shielding" from the parallel disclosure tracks.[278]

Trump's immigration enforcement record was highlighted the same week by [[immigration-and-customs-enforcement|ICE]] arrests of 214 migrants charged with or convicted of child sex offenses in the Houston area over six months, which Badlands contributor [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] contrasted with his 2015 remarks about migrant "rapists," concluding "Trump was right again."[278]

## February 2025: Ukraine minerals, Putin summit talk, and the DECLAS push

With Trump back in the [[white-house|White House]] in February 2025, residents and workers in Washington, D.C. appeared anxious about their legal exposure after a campaign built around a message of "retribution" against those he believed wronged him during the Biden years; Google search-trend data showed searches for "criminal defense lawyer" soaring in the capital in January, nearly five times higher than in Utah, the state with the second-most such searches.[279]

Trump inaugurated an official White House channel on [[rumble|Rumble]], the video platform and cloud services provider, adding the administration to the company's expanding portfolio of content providers.[279] Rumble chief executive [[chris-pavlovski|Chris Pavlovski]] reported that the first briefing carried on the platform peaked around 55,000 live viewers against roughly 22,000 on YouTube.[279]

On Ukraine, Trump dispatched Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] to Kyiv — the first senior administration official to visit — a day after Russia released an American prisoner, which Trump called a positive signal for cease-fire talks.[279] He wrote on Truth Social that "this war must and will end soon — too much death and destruction," and complained that the United States had spent billions of dollars "with too little to show" for it.[279] Trump claimed Kiev had agreed to relinquish $500 billion worth of rare-earth minerals to compensate for aid provided under [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], a claim contested by [[denis-pushilin|Denis Pushilin]] of the [[donetsk-peoples-republic|Donetsk People's Republic]], who said most of the lithium and titanium lies in Russian-held Donbass.[279]

Trump named [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]] as the likely host of his first meeting with Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] of his second term, telling journalists in the Oval Office, "The first time we'll meet in Saudi Arabia, see if we get something done," and indicating several meetings could follow.[279] He noted that both leaders know Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] and that the kingdom could be "a very good place to meet."[279] Badlands commentary also noted that Trump had [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] tell the press pool that he and [[king-abdullah-ii|King Abdullah]] differed on how to handle Gaza, and that he had asked Arab states to return with a better proposal.[279]

On Tuesday, February 11, 2025, Trump signed an executive order formally requiring federal agencies to cooperate with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] and the [[doge|Department of Government Efficiency]]; hundreds of [[small-business-administration|Small Business Administration]] employees received termination notices within hours.[279] North Korean state media, via the [[korean-central-news-agency|Korean Central News Agency]], denounced his proposal to take over Gaza and relocate Palestinians as bluster, accusing Washington of robbery and criticizing calls to take the Panama Canal and Greenland and to rename the Gulf of Mexico the "Gulf of America."[279]

Also in February 2025, Musk's [[x-corp|X]] agreed to pay about $10 million to settle the lawsuit Trump had filed in 2021 over his deplatforming after the January 6, 2021 Capitol riot, making X the second social-media platform to settle such litigation.[279]

## March 2024 developments

On March 13, 2024, Fulton County Superior Court Judge Scott McAfee quashed six counts of the Georgia 2020 election interference indictment, three of them against Trump, while leaving ten counts against him and the overarching racketeering charge intact; it was the first time charges in any of Trump's four criminal cases had been dismissed.[280] The dismissed counts included those relating to the Brad Raffensperger phone call and to the oaths of office, and McAfee held that prosecutors could seek a new indictment to reinstate them.[280]

The same day the House passed the TikTok divestment bill 352-65-1 over Trump's shifting position on the platform.[280] Badlands writer Simon Esler observed that Trump was positioning himself in a grey zone and, citing his recent meetings with ByteDance part-owner Jeffrey Yass and with Elon Musk, speculated that Trump's media conglomerate might be circling TikTok as an acquisition target.[280] Trump's meeting with Musk was one of several he held with donors in Palm Beach during that period as he sought to raise money for a financially strained campaign, and it came up in Don Lemon's interview with Musk.[280]

Also that week, FBI Director Christopher Wray declined to tell Representative Elise Stefanik whether the bureau had counterintelligence investigations open into Trump or Joe Biden, saying there was "no investigation that I could confirm here."[280] Separately, in declining a public House Oversight hearing, Hunter Biden's attorney Abbe Lowell conditioned any appearance on Chairman James Comer also questioning relatives of Trump about the family's financial dealings.[280] Badlands writer Burning Bright argued that with Trump out of public power the international order had shifted toward sovereignty and a multipolar concept advanced by Vladimir Putin, Xi Jinping, Mohammed bin Salman and Trump himself.[280]

## August 2025 Putin summit and DC crime surge

On August 7, 2025 the Kremlin confirmed that Trump and Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] would meet "in the coming days," an agreement reached in principle at the suggestion of the American side.[281] The bilateral followed Trump's growing frustration with Putin's slow-walking of ceasefire negotiations over Ukraine; he had threatened harsh tariffs on Russia and on countries continuing to trade with it, setting Friday as a ceasefire deadline, and had already hit India with 50 percent tariffs over its purchases of Russian oil.[281] Trump briefed European leaders and Ukrainian President [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelenskyy]] on the plan the previous day and floated a possible trilateral meeting.[281] Badlands host [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] speculated the summit would be held in Saudi Arabia, while [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the Trump–Putin–Zelenskyy triangle as the central narrative of the war and Ukraine as the "prime Proxy State Template" for the Deep State's proxy wars and proxy presidents.[281]

Domestically, Trump ordered a seven-day surge of federal law enforcement onto the streets of Washington, D.C. beginning at midnight on August 7, 2025, after saying a day earlier that he was weighing a federal takeover of the D.C. police department in the wake of a violent attack on a former [[doge|Department of Government Efficiency]] staffer.[281] The same week his administration announced a $50 million reward for the capture of [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]], nominated [[stephen-miran|Stephen Miran]] to the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] Board seat vacated by [[adriana-kugler|Adriana Kugler]], and granted six more states waivers to bar junk food from [[snap|SNAP]] purchases.[281] Trump was also reported to be preparing an executive order permitting crypto and other alternative assets in 401(k) retirement plans, news that helped lift [[bitcoin|bitcoin]] back above $117,000.[281] The [[new-york-post|New York Post]] editorial board separately urged Trump, if he entered the New York mayoral race, to endorse [[eric-adams|Eric Adams]] over [[zohran-mamdani|Zohran Mamdani]].[281]

## April 2024 debate challenge to Biden

In April 2024, Trump's campaign publicly pressed for additional 2024 presidential debates and for them to begin "much earlier" than the schedule proposed by the [[commission-on-presidential-debates|Commission on Presidential Debates]], with Trump telling Fox News Digital that he was "totally committed" to debating President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] "anytime, anywhere, anyplace."[282] Co-campaign managers [[susie-wiles|Susie Wiles]] and [[chris-lacivita|Chris LaCivita]] sent a letter to commission co-chairs [[frank-fahrenkopf|Frank Fahrenkopf Jr.]] and [[antonia-hernandez|Antonia Hernandez]] saying they wrote "in agreement with the pending letter" from television networks urging that debates occur in 2024, and that while three presidential debates and a vice-presidential debate had already been announced, the campaign favored "these debates beginning much earlier."[282] Badlands Media commentator [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described the move as a masterstroke of narrative warfare, arguing that Trump's earlier boycott of the Republican primary debates had been "de facto participation" that rendered those events moot, and that targeting Biden during a period of cascading crises was designed to prepare mainstream audiences for a decisive contrast.[282]

## Sweden vindication and the Orban endorsement exchange (September 2023)

When Sweden's government introduced a mandatory requirement for public institutions to report illegal immigrants in September 2023, commentators framed the move as vindication of Trump's February 2017 rally remarks — "You look at what's happening last night in Sweden" and "They're having problems like they never thought possible" — which media outlets at the time claimed referred to a nonexistent terrorism incident but which followed a Fox News report on a documentary about violent immigrants.[283] [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] writer [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued the headline should have read "Trump was right AGAIN," holding up [[sweden|Sweden]] as evidence that the [[nordic-model|Nordic Model]] and globalism had failed everyone except global elites.[283]

The same weekend, Hungarian Prime Minister [[viktor-orban|Viktor Orban]] endorsed Trump's 2024 candidacy during an appearance on [[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]]'s show on X, saying reelecting Trump was "the only way out" of the Russia-Ukraine war and that "Trump is the man who can save the Western world."[283] Trump shared a graphic of Orban and the quote on Truth Social late that Friday and by Saturday offered an endorsement of Orban in return.[283]

## Riyadh Investment Forum and Syria Sanctions (May 2025)

Speaking at the Saudi-US Investment Forum in Riyadh on May 13, 2025 — the first stop of his [[2025-trump-middle-east-tour|first Middle East trip of his second term]], which also took in Qatar and the United Arab Emirates — Trump said it was his "fervent hope" that Saudi Arabia would soon sign a normalization agreement with Israel and join the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]], adding, "But you'll do it in your own time."[284] He predicted that "it will be a special day in the Middle East, with the whole world watching, when Saudi Arabia joins us."[284] At the same event he unveiled a $142 billion defense agreement and a $600 billion Saudi investment package covering AI, energy and infrastructure, and lavished praise on Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] — "an incredible man," "a great guy," "I like him a lot. I like him too much" — without raising the Kingdom's human rights record.[284]

Trump also announced that the United States would lift all sanctions on Syria and normalize relations with its new government, a decision taken over objections from within his own administration and from Israel, and made at the urging of bin Salman and Turkish President [[recep-tayyip-erdogan|Recep Tayyip Erdoğan]]; he remarked, "Oh, what I do for the crown prince."[284] See [[syria-sanctions-relief|Syria sanctions relief]].

Badlands commentators read the trip as a symbolic milestone. [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] highlighted Trump's line that "sometimes, you need enemies to do the job, and do it right. Some of the closest friends of the United States of America are nations we fought wars against in generations past," treating the visit as the "coming out party" of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] and noting that Trump "doesn't roll up to the World Economic Forum. He rolls up to the Kingdom."[284]

In the same week Trump signed an executive order aimed at lowering U.S. drug prices, a step toward a [[most-favored-nation-drug-pricing|most favored nation]] pricing regime described as the pharmaceutical industry's nightmare scenario.[284] Reporting on the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] under [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]] meanwhile described policy decisions being made at the [[white-house|White House]] and pushed down to the department.[284]

## Late February 2025: minerals deal, Epstein files, tariffs, and Starmer visit

Trump repeatedly stated in February 2025 that the United States wanted to "get back" the money it had spent on military and financial aid to Kiev through a minerals deal with Ukraine, and Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] announced that [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] would sign the finalized [[us-ukraine-minerals-agreement|rare earths agreement]] at a White House meeting with no further negotiation.[285]

On February 27, Trump's Justice Department released Phase One of the [[epstein-files|Epstein files]] to a group of conservative influencers; the president's name did not appear in the released contact list.[285] Badlands commentary criticized the rollout, arguing that taking the truth directly to the people was a distinctive feature of Trump's approach and that delegating the narrative to hand-picked influencers behind closed doors abandoned that strategy.[285]

Trump's tariff pressure on [[mexico|Mexico]] — tied to demands that it crack down on cartels and fentanyl — preceded Mexico's transfer of 29 detainees, including [[rafael-caro-quintero|Rafael Caro Quintero]] and the founders of [[los-zetas|Los Zetas]], to U.S. custody.[285]

Trump also hosted British Prime Minister [[keir-starmer|Keir Starmer]] at the White House, where he was visibly pleased by King Charles's invitation to an unprecedented second state visit, calling it "a great honour, because it's never happened before."[285] Trump said he felt he could trust [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] to stick to any peace deal, while Starmer emphasized that a truce must endure and not favour the aggressor; asked whether U.S. forces would back British troops deployed to Ukraine, Trump said the British "can take care of themselves" and "don't need much help."[285] In parallel, his administration opened direct diplomatic contacts with Russia, including six hours of embassy talks in Istanbul, a sharp break from the approach of Joe Biden.[285] Chief Justice [[john-roberts|John Roberts]] also granted his administration an administrative stay of Judge [[amir-ali|Amir Ali]]'s order requiring payment of frozen foreign aid funds.[285]

## Economic Club of Chicago interview (October 2024)
