On October 15, 2024, Trump sat for an interview with Bloomberg Editor-in-Chief [[john-micklethwait|John Micklethwait]] in partnership with the Economic Club of Chicago, an event that began and ended with a standing ovation.[286] Micklethwait opened by noting that [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] had refused to appear to discuss her economic vision.[286] Trump challenged Micklethwait on tariffs, telling him, "It must be hard for you to spend 25 years talking about tariffs as being negative and then have somebody explain to you that you're wrong," to which the crowd erupted in applause.[286] He predicted tariffs would have "a massive effect, positive effect," reasoning that "the higher the tariff, the more likely it is that the company will come into the United States, and build a factory in the United States so it doesn't have to pay the tariff."[286]

Trump also mocked the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]], calling it "the greatest job in government" where "you show up to the office once a month and say, 'Hmm, let's flip a coin,' and everybody talks about you like you're a god," adding that it "sets a horrible tone" and that "there are certain companies you have to have."[286] When Micklethwait cited a Wall Street Journal estimate that Trump's economic plan would increase the national debt by more than $7 trillion, Trump replied, "What does the Wall Street Journal know? They've been wrong about everything. And so have you, by the way."[286] Micklethwait objected that Trump was "trying to turn this into a debate"; Trump answered, "It's not a debate, but you've been wrong all your life on this stuff."[286]

Badlands contributor Burning Bright called the appearance a rare information-war deployment that sent shockwaves through multiple layers of the Collective Mind on both Actual and Narrative levels, noting that [[polymarket|Polymarket]] odds surged toward Trump that afternoon and that his aside about "currency" and his sidestepping of accusations that he had helped destabilize dollar hegemony were signals to those with asymmetric readings of Operation Warp Speed.[286] In his framing, Trump "walked into the den of vampires and told them to their faces he's destroying their system" — and did it to cheers.[286]

## Federal death row preparations and Rogan (October 2024)

Also in October 2024, the Daily Mail reported that the [[justice-department|Justice Department]] was preparing the federal death row at the Terre Haute penitentiary in Indiana, where 40 federal death-sentence prisoners are held, for a resumption of executions should Trump win; no executions had been carried out under [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], and Trump had indicated lethal injections would quickly resume if he retook the White House.[286] Trump had separately indicated he would soon appear on [[joe-rogan|Joe Rogan]]'s podcast without giving specifics, having recently sat for several shows aimed at young male audiences such as *Flagrant*; Badlands contributor Ashe in America predicted he would "crush" in the format because he authentically holds his positions.[286]

Commenting on North Korea's demolition of its road and rail links with South Korea, Burning Bright argued that Trump's three-sentence reaction amounted to an "ultra condensed Hegelian Dialectic" — problem, intended reaction, and then the STORY of the solution set apart in narrative quotes — concluding that "Trump the Peacemaker" is the last story, which [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong Un]], Vladimir Putin and other members of the Sovereign Alliance would help him actualize.[286]

## Late June 2025

Trump said he would name a successor to Federal Reserve Chair [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] "very soon" despite Powell having eleven months left in his term, raising the prospect of an unprecedented [[shadow-fed-chair|"shadow" Fed chair]] amid his months-long attacks on the Fed for refusing to lower interest rates.[287] The Supreme Court's decision in [[trump-v-casa|Trump v. CASA]] on June 27 freed his administration from a wave of district court [[universal-injunction|universal injunctions]] covering matters from government downsizing to immigration.[287]

On foreign policy, Trump insisted that the strikes he ordered on [[iran|Iran]]'s nuclear facilities had "completely obliterated" its uranium enrichment capabilities, and after an initial classified intelligence assessment contradicted him he and his administration attacked both the leakers and the media covering the document, continuing the dispute at the NATO summit in The Hague.[287] Al Jazeera noted this was only the latest instance of Trump publicly disagreeing with his own intelligence community, following similar clashes over Russia, North Korea and Venezuela.[287] The same week he brokered the [[rwanda-drc-peace-agreement-2025|Rwanda–DR Congo peace agreement]], signed in Washington with Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]], which he said gave the United States rights to Congolese mineral wealth.[287]

Trump also announced the termination of all trade talks with [[canada|Canada]] over Ottawa's digital services tax, calling it a "direct and blatant attack on our country," within the broader [[2025-us-canada-trade-war|US–Canada trade war]] that began with 25% tariffs in February.[287] Apollo Global Management chief economist [[torsten-slok|Torsten Sløk]] published a widely read post suggesting Trump may have outsmarted everyone on tariffs by holding rates near 30% on China and 10% elsewhere while producing some $400 billion in annual revenue.[287] Domestically, he pressed Senate Republicans to pass his [[one-big-beautiful-bill-act|budget bill]] by the Fourth of July, badgering Senator [[thom-tillis|Thom Tillis]] into announcing his retirement, and told Fox's Maria Bartiromo that a "very wealthy" group of buyers was set to purchase [[tiktok|TikTok]] pending likely approval from President Xi.[287]

## Ukraine peace plan presented to Trump, June 2024

In June 2024 two key advisers presented Trump with a plan to end Russia's war in Ukraine if he won the November 5 election, under which Ukraine would be told it would receive more US weapons only if it entered peace talks, while Moscow would be warned that refusing to negotiate would result in increased US support for Kyiv.[288] Retired Lieutenant General [[keith-kellogg|Keith Kellogg]], one of Trump's national security advisers, described the approach in an interview, and [[fred-fleitz|Fred Fleitz]] — who like Kellogg served as a chief of staff in Trump's National Security Council during his 2017-2021 presidency — said the plan envisioned a ceasefire based on prevailing battle lines during talks.[288] Fleitz said Trump responded favorably, adding, "I'm not claiming he agreed with it or agreed with every word of it, but we were pleased to get the feedback we did," while spokesman [[steven-cheung|Steven Cheung]] cautioned that only statements by Trump or authorized campaign members should be deemed official.[288] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued the plan was a direct mirror of [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]]'s own peace terms — denazification and annexation of the Donbas and a Ukrainian commitment not to join [[nato|NATO]] — and noted Trump had publicly favored US withdrawal from NATO, concluding "the deal is already made."[288]

## October 2025 Asia tour

In late October 2025, Trump undertook a three-country Asia tour culminating in a bilateral meeting with Chinese President [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] in South Korea, covered at [[2025-trump-xi-summit-in-south-korea|2025 Trump–Xi summit in South Korea]].[289] In Seoul he finalized a long-deadlocked trade deal with South Korean President [[lee-jae-myung|Lee Jae-myung]], telling a dinner of regional leaders, "We made our deal, pretty much finalised it," under a framework in which Seoul would invest $350 billion in the United States in exchange for lower tariff rates.[289] Trump also said scheduling would not permit a rumored meeting with [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong Un]] on this trip.[289]

At the APEC CEO Summit in Korea, Trump praised Malaysian Prime Minister [[anwar-ibrahim|Anwar Ibrahim]], calling Malaysia "fantastic" for its role in the [[kuala-lumpur-peace-accord|Kuala Lumpur Peace Accord]] between Cambodia and Thailand.[289] At the same APEC gathering he repeated his claim to have brokered peace between India and Pakistan through trade pressure, calling Indian Prime Minister [[narendra-modi|Narendra Modi]] "a killer" and performing an impersonation of him.[289] Badlands contributor Burning Bright argued that none of these agreements were actually decided that week: "The deals are done. This is about the show."[289]

During the same period, Trump administration officials told a federal judge they had planned to deport [[kilmar-abrego-garcia|Kilmar Abrego Garcia]] to [[liberia|Liberia]] as early as October 31, and his [[department-of-homeland-security|Department of Homeland Security]] ended the Biden-era policy of automatically extending migrant work permits.[289]

## Gag order loosened, June 2024

On June 25, 2024, Justice [[juan-merchan|Juan M. Merchan]] loosened the gag order in Trump's Manhattan criminal case, allowing him to criticize witnesses who testified against him, including his former fixer [[michael-cohen|Michael Cohen]], and others involved in the trial that led to his conviction.[288] The ruling provided that once Trump was sentenced on July 11 he could publicly assail prosecutors and their relatives as well, though he remained subject to a separate order barring him from releasing jurors' identities or attacking them by name.[288] Merchan appeared conflicted about permitting broad complaints about the jury, writing that "it would be this court's strong preference to extend those protections" but that he felt the law required dropping the restrictions.[288]

Also that week, former Secretary of State [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] wrote that it would be a "waste of time" to try to refute Trump's arguments in a debate, saying it was "nearly impossible to identify what his arguments even are."[288]

## June 2024 items

Badlands commentary in June 2024 highlighted a December 2021 interview Trump gave to Israel's Channel 12, characterized as state media, in which he ran what [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] described as a psychological warfare campaign against Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]].[290] Trump recounted cutting Netanyahu off mid-sentence to say "Bibi, you don't want to make a deal," promoted [[benny-gantz|Benny Gantz]] and [[mahmoud-abbas|Mahmoud Abbas]] as the region's two genuine peacemakers, and stated "I don't think Bibi ever wanted to make peace; never did."[290] Of Abbas he said "I thought he wanted to make a deal more than Netanyahu... he was almost like a father," and concluded "I always thought that the Palestinians were impossible, and that the Israelis would do anything to make a deal... I found that not to be true."[290] The commentary observed that while some of these remarks had surfaced in memoirs reported by obscure outlets, the Channel 12 appearance itself went largely undiscussed, and that Trump said he was polling above 80% among Israeli citizens at the time — offered as partial explanation for civil unrest in Israel against Netanyahu.[290]

Trump's persona was also in the news through Ramin Setoodeh's book Apprentice in Wonderland, which reported that Trump kept the [[the-apprentice|Apprentice]] set intact on the 14th floor of Trump Tower for at least six months after launching his 2015 campaign in case the run failed.[290]

On June 6, 2024, a week after being found guilty on 34 counts in New York, Trump announced at a Turning Point Action town hall in Arizona that his campaign had raised roughly $400 million since the verdict, an unprecedented sum unlikely to be matched by [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] before the election.[290] He described the proceeding as "a rigged trial in New York with a highly conflicted... judge where there was no crime," argued that "they could have brought the case seven years ago" and that "it's only when you run for office they bring cases," and claimed he was beating Biden "by a lot."[290]

## Garbage truck presser and cartel asset seizure proposal (October 2024)

In the final week of the 2024 campaign, Trump turned a Democratic insult into a stunt, speaking to reporters on Wednesday, October 30 from the passenger seat of a campaign-branded garbage truck marked "LOADMASTER" while wearing an orange hi-vis vest.[291] "How do you like my garbage truck?" he asked supporters. "This truck is in honor of Kamala [Harris] and Joe Biden."[291] The sequence had begun with Trump saying immigration had turned the United States into a "garbage can," continued with a speaker at his Madison Square Garden rally calling Puerto Rico a "floating island of garbage," and culminated in [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] appearing to call Trump's supporters "garbage" — "The only garbage I see floating out there is his supporters," Biden said, calling the "demonization of Latinos" unconscionable and un-American.[291] Trump kept the garbageman costume on at a rally in Green Bay, swaying to Lee Greenwood's "God Bless the U.S.A." in the vest over his button-down and tie.[291] Badlands writer [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read the episode as [[trumpamania|TrumpaMania]] at full strength, arguing Trump grasped that troop morale is decisive in a long conflict and had found a way to make his campaign whimsical while humiliating the [[uniparty|Uniparty]].[291]

The previous day, Tuesday, October 29, Trump said at Mar-a-Lago that his administration would seize "the assets of criminal gangs and drug cartels" and direct the proceeds to families affected by "migrant crime" if elected the 47th president.[291] The funds would create a compensation fund for victims of crimes committed by people in the United States illegally, with Trump adding that "the government will help in the restoration."[291] Asset seizure has long been used by the federal government against drug trafficking organizations, and former [[drug-enforcement-administration|Drug Enforcement Administration]] officials described it as an effective tool in the pursuit of justice.[291] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] placed the proposal in a chronology alongside Trump's December 2017 executive order blocking the property of persons involved in serious human rights abuse or corruption, his December 2023 declaration of "war on cartels," the [[toronto-dominion-bank|Toronto-Dominion Bank]] fentanyl laundering case, and the [[bank-of-america|Bank of America]] money-laundering enforcement warning.[291]

## Wuhan origins op-ed and call for accountability (March 2023)

In a March 2023 op-ed published by the Daily Mail, Trump wrote that three years earlier he had declared that [[covid-19-narrative|COVID-19]] almost certainly came from the Chinese [[wuhan-institute-of-virology|Wuhan]] lab, and that the world was finally admitting the truth.[292] He called the cover-up of COVID-19's origins "one of the greatest scandals in the history of the world," noted that millions of people worldwide had died from what he termed the China Virus, and said the cost of the outbreak and the lying about its origins was incalculable, with some estimates in excess of $50 trillion.[292] He argued that it was time to hold China — and "the corrupt forces who have facilitated this colossal suppression of facts" — accountable for the damage inflicted on humanity.[292]

Badlands contributor [[just-human|Just Human]] welcomed the op-ed, observing that it echoed much of what Trump had said in his CPAC speech regarding China and COVID-19, and singling out the lines: "There must now be a reckoning. The sinister censorship regimes in the United States and throughout the West must be dismantled and destroyed. This scandal is the best possible reminder of why we must have free speech."[292]

## Bid to block Pence's grand jury testimony (March 2023)

On Friday, March 3, 2023, Trump asked a federal court, in a filing submitted secretly in a sealed proceeding, to block former Vice President [[mike-pence|Mike Pence]] from speaking to a grand jury about certain matters covered by executive privilege, as part of the criminal investigation into efforts to overturn his 2020 election loss.[292] The request followed earlier signals from Trump's team to special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] that the former president intended to assert privilege over Pence's testimony, after the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] had asked a judge to compel Pence's appearance.[292] Commentary noted that the dispute could be resolved at district court, appealed, or eventually reach the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|US Supreme Court]], with Pence separately arguing that his role as President of the Senate on January 6 was protected by the Constitution's Speech or Debate Clause.[292]

## March 2025: Yemen strikes, deportations, and Canada pressure

On March 15–16, 2025, Trump launched large-scale military strikes against Yemen's Iran-aligned [[houthis|Houthis]] over their attacks on Red Sea shipping, killing at least 31 people at the start of a campaign expected to last many days, and warned [[iran|Iran]] that it must immediately halt support for the group, saying "America will hold you fully accountable and, we won't be nice about it!"[293] Over the same weekend his administration invoked the [[alien-enemies-act|Alien Enemies Act]] to deport hundreds of alleged [[tren-de-aragua|Tren de Aragua]] members, proceeding despite an order from Judge [[james-boasberg|James Boasberg]] blocking the law's use and directing planes in the air to return.[293] He was also expected to speak with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] that week about ending the war in Ukraine, following envoy [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]]'s "positive" Moscow meeting.[293]

Trump's pressure on [[canada|Canada]] dominated the first foreign trip of new prime minister [[mark-carney|Mark Carney]], who sought alliances in Paris and London while insisting Canada would "never, ever, in any way shape or form, be part of the United States"; Trump had posted in December that no one could explain why the United States subsidizes Canada "to the tune of over $100,000,000 a year" and suggested many Canadians would prefer statehood.[293] In a lighter incident at Joint Base Andrews, a wind-muffling "dead cat" microphone struck Trump in the lip and cheek as he leaned in to hear a question about freeing more hostages from Gaza; he glared briefly at the reporter before joking that "she just made television tonight," and the operator was heard apologizing.[293]

## Transition-week developments (January 2025)

In the final week before his second inauguration, President-elect Donald Trump pressed [[aileen-cannon|Judge Aileen Cannon]] to enjoin release of [[jack-smith|Special Counsel Jack Smith]]'s entire report and sought to intervene in the Florida case, while Cannon cleared the way for the [[justice-department|Justice Department]] to publish the volume on the 2020 election case.[294] Trump also hosted Senator [[john-fetterman|John Fetterman]] at [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]] — the first Democratic senator to visit — and afterwards called him "a fascinating man" and "a commonsense person," saying he "couldn't be more impressed."[294] His Middle East envoy [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] pressed Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] to accept a Gaza hostage deal, backed by Trump's warning that "everybody involved" would have hell to pay if the hostages were not released by January 20.[294] On defense burden-sharing, Trump had floated raising the [[nato|NATO]] spending benchmark to 5% of GDP, a level no member then approached.[294]

## July 2025: Russiagate declassification, Redskins ultimatum, and the GENIUS Act

On July 18, 2025, Director of National Intelligence [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] declassified documents that Fox News Digital said showed the Obama administration had "manufactured and politicized intelligence" to create the narrative that Russia was attempting to influence the 2016 presidential election that Trump won against [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]].[295] Gabbard described the material as evidence of a "treasonous conspiracy" and of a "years-long coup against President Trump," and said she could not fathom why the special counsel inquiries of [[robert-mueller|Robert Mueller]] and [[john-durham|John Durham]] had not surfaced it.[295]

On Sunday, July 20, 2025, Trump threatened to interfere with the deal to build a new football stadium in Washington, D.C. unless the [[washington-commanders|Washington Commanders]] readopted the Redskins name, writing on Truth Social that he would not "make a deal for them to build a Stadium in Washington" otherwise.[295] He had previously urged both that franchise and the Cleveland Guardians to restore their former names, and had told reporters in February 2025, "I think we should take over Washington, D.C.," despite limited presidential authority under the District's home-rule law.[295]

Trump signed the [[genius-act|GENIUS Act]] into law on July 18, 2025, creating a federal framework for payment stablecoins requiring 1:1 reserve backing and public disclosure, with the [[white-house|White House]] due to publish its first comprehensive cryptocurrency policy report on July 22 as mandated by Executive Order 14178.[295] Badlands commentary treated the signing as a decisive move against central banking and read Trump's habitual "Too Late" nickname for Fed Chair [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] as a reference to central bankers being outmaneuvered.[295]

Also in July 2025, the White House said it wanted grand jury transcripts in the [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]] case unsealed, after a Department of Justice and FBI review reported finding no incriminating client list; a Wall Street Journal article then reported that Trump had sent Epstein a letter for the financier's 50th birthday, a report Trump denied.[295] Trump also threatened 50 percent tariffs on Brazilian goods beginning August 1 to pressure Brazilian authorities over what he called a "witch hunt" against [[jair-bolsonaro|Jair Bolsonaro]], and his administration imposed visa restrictions on Brazilian Supreme Court Justice [[alexandre-de-moraes|Alexandre de Moraes]] and others.[295] On the same weekend, White House officials told Axios of growing skepticism about Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]], one saying "Bibi is acting like a madman."[295] Marking six months in office, the White House posted: "TRUMP DIDN'T COME TO PLAY. Six months in. All gas. No brakes. The winning will continue."[295]

## Detroit counterprogramming and the second GOP debate (2023)

In September 2023 Trump planned to travel to Detroit on the day of the second Republican primary debate, injecting himself into the labor dispute between striking autoworkers and the nation's leading auto manufacturers.[296] The trip, which was to include a prime-time speech before current and former union members, marked the second consecutive primary debate he skipped in favor of his own counterprogramming; during the first debate in August he had sat for an interview with former Fox News host Tucker Carlson that posted online.[296] The decision to go to Michigan days after the [[united-auto-workers|United Auto Workers]] went on strike was read as evidence of how far Trump wanted to be seen looking past his primary rivals, and of the degree to which he and his political apparatus were already focused on a rematch with [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]].[296]

Rather than attend the [[second-gop-presidential-debate-2023|September 27 debate]] at the Ronald Reagan Presidential Library and Museum in California, Trump intended to address more than 500 workers, with his campaign planning to fill the room with plumbers, pipe-fitters and electricians as well as autoworkers.[296] He had not directly addressed the striking workers' wage demands and had attacked union leadership, while more broadly casting himself on the side of autoworkers; his campaign also weighed a picket-line appearance, though an adviser called it unlikely given the logistical difficulties of his security protections.[296]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] wrote that Trump never does anything by accident from a narrative perspective, arguing that he was demonstrating that the GOP debates — and by extension the conventional framing of the 2024 race — were beneath him, while eschewing legacy political posturing in favor of addressing national issues weighing on the collective mind.[296]

## November 2024 pre-election news cycle

In the final days before the 2024 election, an anonymous television executive told [[cnn|CNN]] media analyst [[brian-stelter|Brian Stelter]] that "a Trump victory means mainstream media is dead in its current form," a remark [[zerohedge|ZeroHedge]] and [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] treated as an admission that half the country had already abandoned corporate news.[297]

[[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] publicly floated a [[doge|Department of Government Efficiency]] and invited [[ron-paul|Ron Paul]] to take part; Musk had stated his willingness to serve in a Trump-led administration and Trump had hinted at appointing him to an advisory or official role if elected.[297] Badlands commentary read Paul's potential inclusion as the merger of the [[tea-party-movement|Tea Party movement]]'s origins with the MAGA platform, and noted that Trump had been regularly raising Paul's signature themes of abolishing the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] and ending the income tax.[297]

Hungarian Prime Minister [[viktor-orban|Viktor Orbán]] told Austrian broadcaster ServusTV that "we know that Donald Trump did not start a war anywhere as president, and wherever he was, he ended it as soon as possible," adding that "if there is a person in the world who can succeed in achieving a ceasefire, it is Donald Trump."[297] Russian Security Council deputy chairman [[dmitry-medvedev|Dmitry Medvedev]] offered the opposite reading, describing "a low-energy Trump" who would be forced to comply with the system, would not stop the Ukraine war, and who — if he genuinely attempted to do so — "could end up becoming the new JFK."[297]

Days before the vote, the Daily Beast published August 2017 recordings made by author [[michael-wolff|Michael Wolff]] in which [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]] called himself Trump's "closest friend" and claimed intimate knowledge of his private life, including the assertion that Trump first slept with [[melania-trump|Melania Trump]] aboard Epstein's plane.[297] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the release as a recycled conflation operation, arguing that the MAGA base already regarded Trump as innocent of criminal association with Epstein while viewing him as a target of an influence operation, and that the campaign had instead shifted the Overton Window toward mass acknowledgement that former presidents associated with pedophiles.[297]

## January 2024 ballot-eligibility brief

Ahead of the February 8, 2024 [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|U.S. Supreme Court]] argument in the [[colorado-ballot-disqualification-case|Colorado Section 3 case]], Trump filed a 59-page brief arguing that "the Court should reverse the Colorado decision because President Trump is not even subject to section 3, as the President is not an 'officer of the United States' under the Constitution," and that even if he were subject to it, he did not "engage in" anything qualifying as "insurrection."[298] The brief made five arguments in all, adding that Section 3 should be enforceable only through congressional implementing legislation such as 18 U.S.C. § 2383, that the Colorado ruling violated U.S. Term Limits, Inc. v. Thornton (1995), and that it violated the Electors Clause.[298] It focused exclusively on his conduct on January 6 and warned that leaving "any wiggle room" would only delay the ballot-disqualification fight given legislators determined to use Section 3 as a cudgel.[298]

Days earlier, a bipartisan group of Massachusetts voters had asked the [[massachusetts-supreme-judicial-court|Massachusetts Supreme Judicial Court]] to declare him ineligible for that state's March primary ballot and to order Secretary of State [[william-galvin|William Galvin]] to remove him.[298]

## July 2024: demands Israel end the Gaza war

Ahead of a Florida meeting with Israeli prime minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] scheduled for Friday 26 July 2024, Trump demanded that Israel bring an end to its war with [[hamas|Hamas]] and that the remaining hostages held in Gaza be released.[299] Speaking to Fox News before the meeting, he said the war should end fast "because they are getting decimated with this publicity, and you know Israel is not very good at public relations."[299] Trump insisted that the Hamas raid that killed 1,200 people in Israel, and the subsequent war, would not have happened had he been in the White House, and earlier in the year had blamed Netanyahu for allowing the attacks to occur, saying intelligence warnings were well known in advance.[299] Netanyahu met President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] in the Oval Office and held a private meeting with Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] before travelling to Florida.[299]

In the same interview Trump proposed a one-year jail term for burning the American flag, a position Badlands commentator [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] respectfully rejected on free-speech grounds, arguing that existing laws on theft, arson and destruction of property already apply and that no one should be punished by government for peacefully expressing contempt for it.[299] The same commentary read Trump's remarks as completing his public "turn" against Netanyahu and suggested Trump was telegraphing doubt that the hostages could be saved, on the theory that Netanyahu needed them in Hamas custody to preserve his moral imperative for war.[299]

## Ukraine peace overtures (July 2024)

Following a phone call with Ukrainian president [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] that both sides characterised positively, Trump wrote that "I, as your next President of the United States, will bring peace to the world and end the war that has cost so many lives and devastated countless innocent families," adding that "both sides will be able to come together and negotiate a deal that ends the violence and paves a path forward to prosperity."[299] The overture prompted warnings from Ukrainian officials, including Kyiv mayor [[vitali-klitschko|Vitali Klitschko]], that Zelensky would risk "political suicide" by ceding territory to Russia.[299] Badlands commentary argued that Trump's stagecraft had disoriented his opponents to the point that radicalised activists originally mobilised against him were now protesting nationalist factions in Ukraine and Israel instead.[299]

## FBI comments on the Butler shooting wound

On 24 July 2024, FBI Director [[christopher-wray|Christopher Wray]] said it was not clear whether Trump had been struck by a bullet or by shrapnel at his 13 July rally in Butler, Pennsylvania, where one rally-goer was killed and two others seriously injured before gunman [[thomas-matthew-crooks|Thomas Matthew Crooks]] was shot dead by the Secret Service.[299] Trump had said that night that he was "shot with a bullet that pierced the upper part of my right ear," describing "a whizzing sound, shots, and immediately felt the bullet ripping through the skin" on Truth Social.[299] A week later his campaign released a note from Texas Rep. Ronny Jackson, a former White House physician, stating that the gunshot wound came "less than a quarter of an inch from entering his head" and that Trump was recovering as expected.[299]

## Bedminster audio recording (June 2023)

On June 26, 2023, [[cnn|CNN]] exclusively obtained and aired the audio of a 2021 meeting at Bedminster, New Jersey in which Trump discussed holding secret documents he had not declassified, including what appeared to be a Pentagon document with plans to attack Iran.[300] Saying "These are the papers" — a quote omitted from the indictment — Trump also joked with aides about [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]]'s emails, replying to a staffer's remark that "Hillary would print that out all the time" with "No, she'd send it to [[anthony-weiner|Anthony Weiner]]."[300] CNN argued the recording could undercut Trump's statement to Fox News' Bret Baier that he did not have any documents with him.[300]

Badlands contributor [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] responded that Trump's status as president allowed him to declassify documents by action rather than declaration, and that the legal theory to the contrary is dubious and unsupported by precedent.[300] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] and [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] argued the audio felt staged, and linked it to Trump's repeated invocation of the [[perfect-phone-call|"Perfect Phone Call"]] and to the [[mar-a-lago-raid|Mar-a-Lago Raid]] as a reverse [[wrap-up-smear|wrap-up smear]].[300] Burning Bright also observed that [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] was forwarding rhetoric parallel to Trump's after the Wagner mutiny, with both framing [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] as a warmonger and themselves as peacemakers.[300]

## Election-interference warning in "Save America" (August 2024)

In late August 2024, Modernity News reported that Trump had warned in his forthcoming book [[save-america|*Save America*]], due out the following week, that [[mark-zuckerberg|Mark Zuckerberg]] and anyone else who illegally interferes in the election would be jailed for life if he won the presidency.[301] Trump wrote that he was "watching him closely" and that "if he does anything illegal this time he will spend the rest of his life in prison – as will others who cheat in the 2024 presidential election."[301] He recounted that Zuckerberg had visited him in office, bringing his wife to dinners and being "as nice as anyone could be," while secretly mounting a "plot against the President."[301] [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] contributor Ashe in America praised the strategy of making the "criminal cartel" fear getting caught, and Burning Bright argued that the book's timing alongside the Brazilian ban of [[x-corp|X]] and the French case against [[pavel-durov|Pavel Durov]] reflected deliberate narrative convergence rather than coincidence.[301]

## Constitutional controversy coverage

A New York Times books essay published August 31, 2024 cited Trump's post-2020 call for the "termination of all rules, regulations and articles, even those found in the Constitution" as evidence of authoritarian inclination, while also arguing that he owed his ascent to the [[united-states-constitution|Constitution]] itself — having won the presidency in 2016 through the Electoral College despite losing the popular vote and having appointed three Supreme Court justices, two confirmed by senators representing 44 percent of the population.[301]

## Late-May 2024 developments

As jury deliberations opened in the [[trump-hush-money-case|New York hush money case]] on May 28, 2024, legal commentators including [[jonathan-turley|Jonathan Turley]] sharply criticized Judge [[juan-merchan|Juan Merchan]]'s instructions permitting jurors to disagree about which predicate crime had occurred while still returning a unanimous verdict.[303] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] characterized the proceeding as the "NY Election Interference" trial and wrote that the world was bracing for a guilty verdict, closing with "Either way, Trump 2024."[303] Trump had been largely confined to New York for the duration of the trial yet continued to lead in most key battleground state polls, prompting the [[joe-biden|Biden]] campaign to abandon its distance from the courthouse and stage its own surrogate event outside.[303]

The same week, The Wall Street Journal reported that Trump and [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] were speaking by phone several times a month and had discussed a possible advisory role for Musk in a second Trump administration — a position compared to the informal influence that former Marvel chairman [[ike-perlmutter|Ike Perlmutter]] exercised over the [[department-of-veterans-affairs|Department of Veterans Affairs]].[303] The two had met privately in March 2024 and exchanged views on immigration, electric vehicles, the [[united-states-space-force|U.S. Space Force]] and Musk's companies.[303] Separately, commentary on Russia's rapprochement with the [[taliban|Taliban]] framed Moscow's reliance on the group to contain [[isis-k|ISIS-K]] as a continuation of Trump's post-[[doha-agreement|Doha Agreement]] vision.[303]

## June 2023 indictment announcement

On June 8, 2023, Trump announced on Truth Social that he had been indicted, describing the classified-documents case as "the Boxes Hoax" and posting his full statements minutes after learning of the charges.[304] Legal commentator [[technofog|TechnoFog]] reported that the [[2023-trump-classified-documents-indictment|indictment]] came from the [[joe-biden|Biden]] [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] that had appointed Special Counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]], and concerned both the handling of documents and possibly Trump's response to a grand jury subpoena for materials bearing classification markings.[304] Badlands writer [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] argued the case would backfire by forcing the release of details that dispel long-cultivated narratives of Trump's guilt, predicting a role reversal in which Trump moves from villain to hero in the public mind.[304]

The same week, Trump posted a doctored video purporting to show rival [[chris-christie|Chris Christie]] launching his presidential campaign at an all-you-can-eat buffet; Christie responded that he had "struggled with my weight for 20 years" but not with his character and accused Trump of behaving like "a child" and "a spoiled baby."[304]

## Al Smith dinner keynote (October 2024)

Trump delivered the keynote at the 79th [[alfred-e-smith-memorial-foundation-dinner|Alfred E. Smith Memorial Foundation Dinner]] on the evening of Thursday, October 17, 2024, cracking jokes, taking shots at Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] for skipping the bipartisan charity event, and vowing to correct "serious problems" in the United States.[305] He arrived with former first lady [[melania-trump|Melania Trump]] just after 8 p.m. and shook hands with guests including Speaker of the House [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]]; comedian Jim Gaffigan emceed.[305]

Referring to Harris's absence, Trump quipped, "If you really wanted Vice President Harris to accept your invitation, I guess you should have told her the funds were going to bail out the looters and rioters in Minneapolis, and she would have been here, guaranteed," alluding to her promotion of a bail fund during the 2020 riots.[305] He also said he was "shocked" that she skipped the dinner, "because we can't get enough of hearing her beautiful laugh."[305]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] treated the appearance as a continuation of Trump's earlier Al Smith Dinner attacks on [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] and the apparatus she represents, arguing that the [[deep-state|Deep State]] had become "a cornered animal" whose death throes Trump was clearly enjoying.[305] In the same brief, [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] echoed Trump's position on the 2020 election, saying, "I think there were serious problems in 2020," while polling showed Trump erasing Harris's leads nationally and in Wisconsin, Michigan, and Pennsylvania.[305] Commentary on the Middle East predicted Trump would end the Gaza war on taking office, following [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s post-[[yahya-sinwar|Sinwar]] call for the fighting to stop.[305]

## October 2024: pardon remarks, Kamala jibes and transition friction

In October 2024 Trump said he was open to pardoning [[hunter-biden|Hunter Biden]] if reelected, telling radio host Hugh Hewitt "I wouldn't take it off the books" while maintaining that "Hunter's a bad boy" and citing "the laptop from hell," a reversal after years of invoking the "Biden crime family."[306] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] of [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] read a prospective pardon as narrative shielding for a second Trump term, arguing that "to unify the nation, Trump needs to be seen as a builder, not a conqueror."[306]

At a Georgia rally Trump questioned why [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] was taking days off with under two weeks until the election, suggesting "maybe she knows something we don't know, that's bad."[306] He also hit back at Harris' description of him as a fascist, writing on Truth Social that she was "a Threat to Democracy" and "not fit to be President of the United States."[306]

Reported early-voting turnout in [[nevada|Nevada]] showing Republicans outpacing Democrats fed Badlands speculation that Trump could carry [[clark-county|Clark County]] and even win the national popular vote, a scenario [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] relished given Colorado's adoption of the [[national-popular-vote-interstate-compact|National Popular Vote Interstate Compact]].[306]

Separately, Rep. [[jamie-raskin|Jamie Raskin]] wrote to Trump and [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] accusing the campaign of "breaking the precedent set by every other presidential candidate since 2010" by missing deadlines for transition memoranda with the [[general-services-administration|General Services Administration]] and the White House.[306] Trump's campaign also organized turnout in [[nebraskas-second-congressional-district|Nebraska's 2nd Congressional District]] through [[trump-force-47|Trump Force 47]], a joint venture with the Republican National Committee.[306]

## Victory Day proclamation and May 2025 news cycle

On 8 May 2025 Trump issued [[victory-day-for-world-war-ii-2025|Victory Day for World War II, 2025]], a presidential action commemorating the 80th anniversary of the Allied triumph over national socialism and fascism and pledging "peace through strength," including the statement that his "proudest legacy will be that of a peacemaker."[307] The same week his administration announced the [[2025-us-uk-trade-deal|US-UK trade deal]], the first trade pact concluded since [[liberation-day-tariffs|Liberation Day]], which left the 10% tariff on most UK goods intact while walking back some sectoral duties on cars and steel.[307] The [[european-union|European Union]] simultaneously moved to challenge his reciprocal tariffs at the [[world-trade-organization|World Trade Organization]] and prepared retaliation against U.S. imports.[307]

Trump nominated [[casey-means|Casey Means]] as Surgeon General after withdrawing [[janette-nesheiwat|Janette Nesheiwat]]'s nomination over credential allegations, a choice defended by [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] amid backlash.[307] He also fired Librarian of Congress [[carla-hayden|Carla Hayden]], a move coverage tied to his broader use of executive orders and the [[doge|Department of Government Efficiency]] to reshape the federal workforce.[307] Israel Hayom reported that Trump had lost patience with [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] and would advance West Asia initiatives — particularly regarding [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]] and the Gulf — without waiting for Israeli approval.[307]

Badlands commentary in this period held that Trump was neither Netanyahu's puppet nor a captive of pro-Israel lobbying, but a "game theory master" who had appointed Zionists to senior posts precisely so that the resulting optics would become undeniable; on that reading, former leaders of [[aipac|AIPAC]] and the [[anti-defamation-league|Anti-Defamation League]] who signed a New York Times letter attacking his administration were reacting to a trap they had already entered.[307]

## October 2025: appeals, Asia diplomacy, and third-term remarks

On October 28, 2025, Trump filed a 96-page appeal seeking to overturn his New York felony conviction, arguing federal law preemption, presidential immunity, defective jury instructions, lack of proof of intent, and bias on the part of Justice [[juan-merchan|Juan Merchan]].[308] The same week, commentary in The Washington Post argued that the sanctions Trump imposed on major Russian oil companies, including [[lukoil|Lukoil]] and [[rosneft|Rosneft]], could seriously damage the Russian economy if rigorously enforced, in part because his order for the first time allowed sanctions against buyers of Russian oil and the banks financing that trade in China, Hong Kong and India.[308]

In Tokyo, Trump and Japanese Prime Minister [[sanae-takaichi|Sanae Takaichi]] signed landmark critical minerals and energy investment agreements expanding Japan's $550 billion investment commitment in the United States.[308] He then traveled to South Korea for meetings with President Lee, a summit with [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]], and speculation about a possible encounter with [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong Un]].[308] Speaking on the Gaza ceasefire, Trump said "nothing is going to jeopardize" the deal but that Israel "should hit back" if its soldiers were killed.[308] Aboard Air Force One en route to South Korea, he appeared to rule out a third term, saying the Constitution is "pretty clear" that he is not allowed to run again despite having "the best numbers for any president in many years"; the 22nd Amendment limits any individual to two elected terms.[308] During the same period his administration was blocked by Judge [[susan-illston|Susan Illston]] from issuing further shutdown-related reductions in force, and House Republicans asked Attorney General [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]] to examine whether [[joe-biden|Biden]]-era autopen pardons could be voided.[308]

## Georgia bond and surrender plans (August 2023)

On Monday, August 21, 2023, a Fulton County judge set Trump's bond at $200,000 in District Attorney [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]]' [[state-of-georgia-v-trump|election interference case]].[309] Trump and the other eighteen defendants were given until noon that Friday to appear at the [[fulton-county-jail|Fulton County Jail]] for processing, and Trump said on his own social media platform that evening that he intended to surrender in Georgia on Thursday.[309] Fulton County Superior Court Judge [[scott-mcafee|Scott McAfee]] also set bond that day for attorneys [[john-eastman|John Eastman]] and [[kenneth-chesebro|Kenneth Chesebro]] at $100,000 each, for [[ray-smith-iii|Ray Smith III]] at $50,000, and for [[scott-hall|Scott Hall]] at $10,000.[309]

Every defendant's bond agreement included a provision that they "shall perform no act to intimidate any person known to him or her to be a codefendant or witness in this case or to otherwise obstruct the administration of justice," and Trump's agreement specified that this covered "no direct or indirect threat" against codefendants or witnesses — language widely read as a restraint on his social media posting.[309] Coverage of the impending booking also focused on the jail itself, with former Watergate prosecutor [[jill-wine-banks|Jill Wine-Banks]] telling [[msnbc|MSNBC]] that Trump would be processed in a facility she called "a really dirty, dangerous, scary place" rather than a clean federal building.[309]

Writing for [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]], [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that the mood among Trump's supporters on the eve of his public arrest and likely mug shot was neither reactive nor muted but "excited," and that whatever anxiety was in the air belonged to his political enemies rather than his allies.[309] He framed the week as a test of narrative rather than law, asking readers to watch its effect on the [[collective-mind|Collective Mind]] with one question in mind: whether Trump would emerge looking like part of the Establishment or an existential threat to its continued existence.[309] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] made a parallel argument, contending that the unlawful and unprecedented treatment of Trump functioned as a rallying cry that accelerated the [[great-awakening|great awakening]].[309]

## September 2025: portraits, shutdown leverage, the UN, and the West Bank

In September 2025 Trump's [[white-house|White House]] unveiled a "Presidential Walk of Fame" near the Oval Office in which [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s portrait was replaced by an image of an autopen, echoing Trump's long-running claim that Biden signed pardons and other important documents by machine.[310] In the same week his budget office, under [[russ-vought|Russ Vought]], told agencies to prepare permanent reduction-in-force plans for programs inconsistent with Trump's priorities should funding lapse on 1 October.[310]

Trump also made overtly pro-Ukraine remarks mocking Russia's failure to win its war quickly and stating that "Putin and Russia are in BIG Economic trouble, and this is the time for Ukraine to act," prompting Kremlin spokesperson [[dmitry-peskov|Dmitry Peskov]] to insist Russia was "a real bear," not a paper tiger.[310] At the [[united-nations|United Nations]], Trump denounced the organization after a stalled escalator and inoperable teleprompter, mocking its costly headquarters and posting on Truth Social; the U.N. attributed both problems to his own delegation.[310]

At a Tuesday meeting with leaders of eight Arab countries, Trump assured them he would not allow [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] to annex the West Bank, presenting a white paper on ending the war that covered governance and postwar security.[310] He was scheduled to meet Turkish President [[recep-tayyip-erdogan|Recep Tayyip Erdogan]] at the White House that Thursday, having earlier teased both F-35 and [[f-16-fighting-falcon|F-16]] deals with [[turkey|Turkey]].[310] Separately, Trump argued that [[james-comey|James Comey]] broke the law by sharing memos containing classified information with the New York Times, and replaced U.S. Attorney [[erik-siebert|Erik S. Siebert]] with [[lindsey-halligan|Lindsey Halligan]] as the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] neared an indictment decision.[310]

## Reaction to Tucker Carlson's Fox News exit (April 2023)

Interviewed on [[newsmax|Newsmax]] on the night of April 24, 2023, Trump said he had been "shocked" and "surprised" to learn that [[fox-news|Fox News]] had ousted [[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]], describing him as "a very good person a very good man and very talented" with "very high ratings" who "has been terrific to me" over the preceding year.[312] Trump, who had grown increasingly critical of Fox, had urged Fox Corp. owner and chairman [[rupert-murdoch|Rupert Murdoch]] — who was set to testify at trial — to embrace his theories about voter fraud in a bid to win the [[dominion-voting-systems|Dominion Voting Systems]] defamation case, and said afterward, "I was surprised they made a settlement in that case. I thought that was a case that should easily be won."[312] He added that it was unclear whether Carlson had been fired or had left because he was not "being given his free rein."[312]

[[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the remarks as "MOABS" following a seeded news cycle, arguing that Trump's phrasing implied a timeline for Carlson's "activation" or turn in favor of the America First agenda, and that his jibe at Fox aligned with the Badlands thesis that the network had been deployed to shield Dominion rather than to fight it, setting legal and psychological precedent harmful to the election reform movement.[312]

## Bicameral ballot cases and December 2023 legal front

On December 27, 2023, the [[colorado-republican-party|Colorado Republican Party]] appealed to the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|U.S. Supreme Court]] the Colorado high court's 4-3 ruling that Trump is ineligible for the presidency under the insurrection clause, an appeal that also had the effect of extending the stay of that ruling.[313] Trump said he intended to file his own appeal to the nation's highest court as well.[313] The same day, the [[michigan-supreme-court|Michigan Supreme Court]] rejected a parallel "insurrectionist ban" challenge and kept Trump on Michigan's 2024 primary ballot, producing dueling state outcomes that heightened the stakes of Supreme Court review.[313]

Also on December 27, special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] moved to bar Trump from advancing theories of selective or vindictive prosecution or otherwise injecting politics into his federal election interference trial.[313] Commentary from [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] noted the tension in seeking a trial date the day before Super Tuesday while simultaneously seeking to restrict the candidate-defendant's speech.[313]

Separately, [[donald-trump-jr|Donald Trump Jr.]] said he would go to "great lengths" to prevent [[nikki-haley|Nikki Haley]] from being selected as his father's running mate, calling her a "puppet of the establishment in Washington, D.C."[313]


## December 2025 cabinet meeting, pardons and Latin America policy

At a cabinet meeting on Tuesday, December 2, 2025, Trump claimed that the federal income tax would be abolished in the near future and replaced entirely by tariffs, after touting his trade policies and potential "dividend" stimulus checks.[314] "We're going to be giving back refunds out of the tariffs because we're taking in literally trillions of dollars," he said, adding that the government would also be able to reduce debt and that "at some point in the not-too-distant future you won't even have income tax to pay because the money we're taking in is so great."[314] The Bipartisan Policy Center estimated the government had collected $258.1 billion in tariffs during the calendar year, and progressive economist Dean Baker of the Center for Economic and Policy Research argued that replacing an income tax that raised roughly $2,600 billion with tariff revenue would add about $2,300 billion to the annual deficit, pushing it to around $4 trillion, or roughly 13 percent of GDP.[314] Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] stopped short of predicting the total eradication of income taxes but claimed the tariffs could deliver "income tax relief."[314]

At the same meeting Trump said the United States would "very soon" begin striking alleged drug traffickers on land inside Venezuela, following months of air strikes on boats in the Caribbean that had killed more than 80 people, and suggested that any country trafficking drugs into the United States was subject to attack, "not just Venezuela."[314] He defended War Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]], saying neither of them had known about a September 2 follow-up strike on a suspected drug vessel carried out after an initial attack failed to kill everyone aboard.[314] Coverage noted that the prospect of force to oust [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]] had put anti-interventionists among Trump's most ardent backers on the back foot, with the specter of the Iraq war ever present.[314] Senator [[rand-paul|Rand Paul]] countered the strike policy by citing Coast Guard records showing that 21 percent of boats stopped off the Venezuelan coast had carried no drugs.[314]

Trump also told reporters at the White House that he had held "a great talk" by telephone with Brazilian President [[luiz-inacio-lula-da-silva|Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva]] covering trade, tariffs, organized crime and the sanctions Washington had imposed on Brazilian officials — including Supreme Court Justice [[alexandre-de-moraes|Alexandre de Moraes]], sanctioned in retaliation for the prosecution of [[jair-bolsonaro|Jair Bolsonaro]].[314] He announced that he had pardoned former Honduran president [[juan-orlando-hernandez|Juan Orlando Hernández]], freeing him from a 45-year cocaine-trafficking sentence, saying he had acted in response to pleas from Hondurans, that he felt "very good" about it, and asserting without evidence that Hernández had been the victim of a witch hunt by the administration of [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]].[314] Democrats including Senator [[dick-durbin|Dick Durbin]] accused him of hypocrisy.[314] Meanwhile Homeland Security Secretary [[kristi-noem|Kristi Noem]] reported to him that half the Minnesota visas her department reviewed were fraudulent, and his envoys [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] and [[jared-kushner|Jared Kushner]] presented his Ukraine peace plan to [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] at the Kremlin.[314]

## Crime and safety remarks in Michigan (August 2024)

On Tuesday, August 20, 2024, while Democrats gathered in Chicago for their convention, Trump delivered remarks on crime and safety in Michigan, speaking for nearly an hour in front of law enforcement officers and equipment.[315] Badlands commentary by [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] described the event as natural, informal, unscripted, and fact-based, paired the same day with [[jd-vance|JD Vance]]'s appearance at the Kenosha County Courthouse in Wisconsin.[315] Both sets of remarks focused on rising crime during the Harris-Biden administration.[315]

Where Vance emphasized a child's right to public safety, Trump addressed systemic challenges — fully funding and outfitting local law enforcement, and the impact of business crime on American workers.[315] The Brief judged the strongest moment of his remarks to be the detailed account he gave of migrant crime, including rapists and child predators, followed by policy plans centered on mass deportation and a renewed focus on public safety at the most local levels.[315] The commentary contrasted this with the convention's insistence on joy, concluding that "truth is always more powerful than gaslighting."[315]

Trump also figured in other items from the same Brief: [[nicole-shanahan|Nicole Shanahan]] said the [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] campaign was "looking at" joining forces with him, and [[hunter-biden|Hunter Biden]]'s lawyers cited the Florida dismissal of the classified documents case against Trump in their unsuccessful bid to have his California tax charges thrown out.[315]

## Polling lead over Biden (October 2023)

In early October 2023, a series of swing state and national surveys showed Trump leading President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], prompting the Daily Caller headline "His Odds Get Better."[316] Polling analysts attributed the lead to voter concern over the Biden administration's policies while cautioning that the surveys were still too early to be predictive of the 2024 election.[316] Nathan Klein, a pollster for OnMessage Inc., told the Daily Caller News Foundation that "as Biden's likeability has waned, we're seeing a real trend of voters being drawn back in by Trump's policies," adding that with a small but persistent national lead, winning back a few thousand of the voters lost in 2020 would leave a clear path to victory.[316]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that polling itself is rife with fraud and constitutes narrative warfare by the media, and criticized [[zerohedge|ZeroHedge]]'s framing of Trump's "astonishing and perhaps unforeseen political resilience" as a misread.[316] In his account Trump was surging not despite but because of the politicized "witch hunt" launched against him, with the trials functioning as the campaign itself amid what he called the Crisis Cascade.[316]

## December 2025 primetime address and the "warrior dividend"

On the evening of Wednesday, December 17, 2025, Trump delivered a roughly 15-to-18-minute primetime address to the nation, framed in advance as a speech about affordability after Democrats won several elections campaigning on cost-of-living issues.[318] In the address he blamed immigrants for high housing and health care costs, took shots at his predecessor [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], and touted his tariff policies, which many economists said were themselves contributing to rising goods prices.[318] Former House Speaker [[newt-gingrich|Newt Gingrich]] called it "one of the most important" speeches of Trump's career on Fox News, drawing mockery from analysts, while California Governor [[gavin-newsom|Gavin Newsom]], Senator [[mark-kelly|Mark Kelly]] and others derided the address as "angry, desperate and defensive" and said it "could have been an email."[318]

During the speech Trump announced that 1,450,000 military service members would receive a special "warrior dividend" of $1,776 before Christmas, in honor of the nation's founding in 1776, saying "the checks are already on the way" and crediting tariff revenue and the One Big Beautiful Bill for the funds.[318] The payments total roughly $2.5 billion out of the administration's $200 billion-plus tariff haul for the year, though it was unclear how the administration would appropriate the money without prior approval from [[congress|Congress]].[318] Badlands contributor [[canncon|CannCon]] summarized the announcement simply as a Christmas bonus for every active-duty service member funded by tariffs.[318]

Badlands contributor [[chris-paul|Chris Paul]] argued that the surrounding coverage was a coordinated messaging campaign to establish an official story about the address, noting that Trump's only participation had been announcing the speech and a single Truth Social post making clear that neocon globalists wanted a war for oil on behalf of transnational energy conglomerates.[318]

## Venezuela pressure campaign, December 2025

[[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]] said on the Judge [[andrew-napolitano|Napolitano]] podcast that members of Congress had been briefed that a war with [[venezuela|Venezuela]] was coming and would be announced in the December 17 address; Trump instead spoke almost entirely about domestic matters and made no mention of Venezuela or Ukraine.[318] Speaking to journalists the same day, Trump said Washington sought to reclaim oil, land, and other assets from Venezuela — "They took our oil rights. We had a lot of oil there... They threw our companies out, and we want it back" — blaming previous administrations for weakness and justifying a naval blockade on the country.[318] He had branded the government in Caracas a foreign terrorist organization and announced a blockade on sanctioned tankers carrying Venezuelan crude, prompting President [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolas Maduro]] to accuse him of "warmongering" and a "diplomacy of barbarism."[318] Representative [[thomas-massie|Thomas Massie]] countered that the assets in question were oil company projects nationalized by Venezuela two decades ago, and that no one had campaigned on using American soldiers to make those companies whole.[318]

## Other December 2025 items

Trump's administration pursued the release of former Mesa County Clerk [[tina-peters|Tina Peters]], offering what *Westword* called an illegitimate pardon given her conviction on state rather than federal charges, and the [[united-states-department-of-justice|Justice Department]] opened a civil rights probe into 33 Colorado corrections facilities that critics called politically motivated.[318] Trump also acknowledged FBI Deputy Director [[dan-bongino|Dan Bongino]]'s January departure, saying "Dan did a great job. I think he wants to go back to his show."[318] Meanwhile his administration pushed talks to end the war in Ukraine, with special envoy [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] and son-in-law [[jared-kushner|Jared Kushner]] expected to meet Russian officials in Miami.[318]

## November 2025 news cycle

Trump used a social media post to urge his supporters and his own administration to direct all their energy toward confronting what he described as widespread election fraud, declaring, "We must focus all of our energy and might on ELECTION FRAUD!!" and accusing federal authorities of failing to act decisively.[319] He also reshared a podcast episode featuring investigative journalist [[lara-logan|Lara Logan]], whose interview included allegations from a former [[cia|CIA]] officer that foreign influence campaigns and intelligence failures had compromised the integrity of US elections.[319]

In a Friday interview with [[fox-news|Fox]], Trump said [[iran|Iran]]'s nuclear program had been significantly set back and that Tehran now wanted an agreement, predicting a deal would probably be reached, while noting that the list of countries interested in the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]] "keeps growing."[319] He also skipped the [[2025-g20-johannesburg-summit|G20 summit]] in Johannesburg, citing what he described as a genocide against white farmers in South Africa.[319]

On the Friday of that week Trump met incoming New York City Mayor [[zohran-mamdani|Zohran Mamdani]] in the Oval Office, and the two said they looked forward to working together after months of bruising public criticism.[319] "The better he does, the happier I am," Trump said, adding that "some of his ideas are really the same ideas that I have" and "we agree on a lot more than I would have thought. I want him to do a great job, and we'll help him do a great job."[319] The tone contrasted sharply with months of hostility during which Trump — a native New Yorker — opposed Mamdani's campaign, threatened to arrest and deport him, and repeatedly called him a "communist," while Mamdani accused the president of acting like a "despot."[319] In the same period, Trump attacked Rep. [[marjorie-taylor-greene|Marjorie Taylor Greene]] as a "traitor," a "lunatic," and "wacky" over her demands for full release of the [[jeffrey-epstein|Epstein]] files, prompting her announced resignation from [[congress|Congress]].[319]

## Space policy executive order (December 2025)

On Thursday, December 18, 2025, Trump issued the executive order [[ensuring-american-space-superiority|"Ensuring American Space Superiority"]], the first major space policy move of his second term, enshrining the goal of returning astronauts to the Moon by 2028 and directing the defense of space against weapon threats.[320] The order was signed hours after [[jared-isaacman|Jared Isaacman]] was sworn in as [[nasa|NASA]]'s 15th administrator, and it reorganized space policy coordination under chief science adviser [[michael-kratsios|Michael Kratsios]] while restructuring the [[national-space-council|National Space Council]] so that the president rather than the vice president would serve as chairman.[320] Badlands commentary quipped that Trump had become "the chairman of going to the moon" and had ousted [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] from the role.[320]

## Carlson interview and remarks on Macron (April 2023)

In April 2023, shortly after his historic indictment in New York by Manhattan District Attorney [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]], Trump sat down with [[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]] on [[fox-news|Fox News]] for his first mainstream media appearance since the charges were filed.[321] In the interview he said the United States had lost influence in the world since he left office, telling Carlson, "You got this crazy world, it's blowing up and the United States has absolutely no say."[321] He said of [[emmanuel-macron|Emmanuel Macron]], then completing the [[2023-macron-state-visit-to-china|2023 Macron state visit to China]], "And Macron, who's a friend of mine, is over with China, kissing his ass. Okay, in China! I said, 'France is now going to China.'"[321]

Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump was "really just laying it all out there," and connected the moment to [[brics|BRICS]] being established as a parallel economy built on the real rather than the "unReal" of the fiat central banking system — noting the timing of such moves while Trump was off center stage.[321] Republicans on the [[house-judiciary-committee|House Judiciary Committee]] separately scheduled the [[2023-house-judiciary-manhattan-crime-hearing|2023 House Judiciary Manhattan crime hearing]] targeting Bragg, whose investigation of Trump had produced the indictment.[321]

## September 2024 town hall remarks on communication style

At a town hall covered live by [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] on September 17, 2024, Trump offered what commentator [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described as his most direct statement yet about the intent behind his rhetorical style, saying: "I give these long, sometimes very complex sentences and paragraphs, but they all come together ... if you couldn't connect the dots, you've got a problem. Many stories were told in that paragraph."[322] Burning Bright argued that Trump's apparent digressions are not asides at all but the substantive core of his narrative deployments, citing as an example a March 2023 Daily Mail op-ed in which Trump wrote that it was "time to hold China—and the corrupt forces who have facilitated this colossal suppression of facts—accountable for the damage they have inflicted upon all of humanity," with the operative claim contained in the parenthetical clause.[322]

The same brief covered a whistleblower report released by Senator [[josh-hawley|Josh Hawley]] documenting security failures by the [[us-secret-service|Secret Service]] during the July 13, 2024 assassination attempt on Trump in Butler, Pennsylvania, noting that Trump had by then been the target of two assassination attempts in two months.[322] Badlands commentary also recalled Trump's 2017 promise to Palestinian Authority president [[mahmoud-abbas|Mahmoud Abbas]] to liberate his people, framing it as a policy that would benefit the world by shutting down what the writer called a factory of radicalization.[322]

## Closing days of the 2024 campaign

In the final week of the [[2024-us-general-election|2024 campaign]], Trump made Pennsylvania the focal point of preemptive claims that the election was being rigged.[323] After voters in Bucks County faced long lines and early cutoffs when requesting and casting mail ballots in person, Trump aides and allies successfully sued to extend the on-demand voting period and simultaneously presented the episode as evidence of voter suppression and intimidation.[323] When Lancaster County said it was reviewing voter registration applications for possible fraud, Trump claimed there were thousands of "fake ballots," and he described voter materials under review in York County as "THOUSANDS of potentially FRAUDULENT applications."[323] "Pennsylvania is cheating, and getting caught, at large scale levels rarely seen before," he posted, following up with "We caught them CHEATING BIG in Pennsylvania. Must announce and PROSECUTE, NOW!"[323] Politico argued that these claims could form the foundation of an attempt to overturn a loss in the state.[323]

Trump's own sit-down with [[joe-rogan|Joe Rogan]] was read by Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] as "full of signal," much of it delivered through the digressions Trump calls "weaves," and its pairing with [[jd-vance|JD Vance]]'s appearance as a one-two punch covering the whole sociopolitical spectrum.[323] The writer argued Trump did not need the additional votes: "Trump doesn't need the votes. He wants the mandate."[323]

Migration policy also featured in the closing stretch. The Telegraph reported that some 2,000 migrants were marching north through Mexico to reach the United States before a possible Trump return to the White House, with a second convoy timed for Election Day; Trump had by then called for the death penalty for migrants who kill US citizens and had made mass deportations central to his platform.[323] Separately, Badlands commentary contrasted the performative belligerence of officials such as US UN envoy [[robert-wood|Robert Wood]] with what it described as Trump's credible but restrained willingness to use force, arguing that "the world respects Donald Trump" because they understand he will not hesitate when necessary.[323]

In the Middle East, Trump's demand that [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] end the war before he returned to office was described as one arm of a pincer known in Badlands analysis as [[trumps-gambit|"Trump's Gambit"]], the other being Arab insistence on a Palestinian state before further normalization under the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]].[323]

Trump's transition apparatus also drew attention: co-chair [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] told CNN that [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] sought federal data to show certain vaccines were unsafe, while denying that Trump had promised Kennedy the Health and Human Services post.[323]

## Indictment watch (March 2023)

In the days before any charges were returned in the [[trump-hush-money-case|Manhattan hush-money case]], law enforcement officials met on March 20, 2023 to plan the logistics of a possible arraignment, expecting it no sooner than the following week; officials cited safety concerns and said a major police presence would shut down the area if Trump came to Manhattan.[324] Trump had called on his supporters to protest ahead of a possible indictment.[324] [[just-human|Just Human]] argued that Trump's [[truth-social|Truth Social]] posts had focused unprecedented attention on the [[manhattan-district-attorneys-office|Manhattan District Attorney's Office]], leaving [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]] in a position where either outcome — indictment or none — would strengthen Trump.[324]

## Indictment watch (March 2023)

In the days before any charges were returned in the [[trump-hush-money-case|Manhattan hush-money case]], law enforcement officials met on March 20, 2023 to plan the logistics of a possible arraignment, expecting it no sooner than the following week; officials cited safety concerns and said a major police presence would shut down the area if Trump came to Manhattan.[324] Trump had called on his supporters to protest ahead of a possible indictment.[324] [[just-human|Just Human]] argued that Trump's [[truth-social|Truth Social]] posts had focused unprecedented attention on the [[manhattan-district-attorneys-office|Manhattan District Attorney's Office]], leaving [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]] in a position where either outcome — indictment or none — would strengthen Trump.[324]

## October 23, 2025 news cycle

On October 23, 2025, Trump denied a Wall Street Journal report that U.S. [[b-1-lancer|B-1 bombers]] had been sent near [[venezuela|Venezuela]], saying "No, that's not accurate. But we're not happy with Venezuela."[327] The same day he insisted he could keep striking alleged drug traffickers abroad without [[congress|Congress]] declaring war — "I'm not going to necessarily ask for a declaration of war... we're just going to kill people that are bringing drugs into our country" — while saying he would notify Congress before any land operations.[327]

The [[white-house|White House]] announced that Trump would meet Chinese President [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] the following week on the sidelines of the [[asia-pacific-economic-cooperation|APEC]] summit in South Korea, their first in-person meeting since he returned to power in January 2025, amid a trade war in which he had threatened an additional 100 percent tariff on Chinese goods.[327] Press Secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] separately said a summit with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] was "not completely off the table" after Trump called off a planned Budapest meeting.[327]

That night Trump announced he was terminating all trade negotiations with [[canada|Canada]] over the [[ontario-reagan-tariff-advertisement|Ontario Reagan tariff advertisement]], accusing Canada of trying to influence a pending U.S. Supreme Court case on his tariffs.[327] In comments published the same day from an October 15 Time interview, he warned that [[israel|Israel]] would lose "all support" from the United States if it pursued [[west-bank-annexation|West Bank annexation]], saying: "It won't happen because I gave my word to the Arab countries."[327]


## "Liberation Day" in Washington, D.C. (August 2025)

On August 11, 2025, Trump declared "This is liberation day in D.C.," reusing a phrase he had first deployed in April 2025 for his tariff announcement, this time to frame an executive action declaring a crime emergency in the capital.[328] The action placed the [[metropolitan-police-department|Metropolitan Police Department]] under federal control and deployed the [[national-guard|National Guard]] to patrol city streets, prompting Mayor [[muriel-bowser|Muriel Bowser]] to renew calls for [[dc-statehood|D.C. statehood]] and protests over fears that [[congress|Congress]] might rescind the [[dc-home-rule-act|Home Rule Act]].[328] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that repeating "[[liberation-day|Liberation Day]]" fused economic and political deliverance into a single narrative aimed at a common globalist adversary.[328]

## Kennedy Center Honors (2025)

Trump confirmed in August 2025 that he would personally host the Kennedy Center Honors, having previously installed [[richard-grenell|Ric Grenell]] to lead the [[john-f-kennedy-center-for-the-performing-arts|Kennedy Center]] and purged its board; he announced honorees including the band Kiss, George Strait, [[sylvester-stallone|Sylvester Stallone]], Michael Crawford and Gloria Gaynor.[328]

## Ceasefire remarks and the June 2025 news cycle

After bombing three of [[iran|Iran]]'s nuclear facilities over the weekend of June 21–22, 2025, Trump demanded that all parties "simmer down," and found that posting online that a ceasefire was in effect was not sufficient to halt military aggression.[332] Speaking to reporters outside the [[white-house|White House]] on Tuesday, June 24, he said he was "not happy" with either [[israel|Israel]] or Iran: "We basically have two countries that are fighting so long and so hard that they don't know what the fuck they're doing," adding that Iran "violated, but Israel violated it too" and that Israel had dropped "a load of bombs — the likes of which I've never seen before" immediately after the deal was struck.[332]

Badlands contributors treated the outburst as a deliberate act of [[narrative-warfare|narrative warfare]]. [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] quipped that "the best bomb of the war so far was this F-bomb."[332] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]], who calls Trump the "Master of Narrative Warfare," argued that Trump was resetting the narrative battlespace by forcing all parties to negotiate in public, that he had destroyed "a nuclear narrative" rather than nuclear weaponry, and that he was providing "off-ramps to war, on-ramps to peace" that left war hawks in [[congress|Congress]] and bureaucrats in [[nato|NATO]] in a whirlpool of indecision.[332] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] said the decision to drop the F-bomb at the end ensured the clip would go "super-viral."[332]

Speaking to reporters aboard Air Force One the same morning while flying to the NATO summit in The Hague, Trump said that he would "like to see a deal with Russia" ending the hostilities in Ukraine, and disclosed that [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] had offered in a recent call to facilitate negotiations with Iran — help Trump said he did not need.[332]

A preliminary classified report disclosed on June 24 concluded that the American strikes had delayed Iran's nuclear program by less than six months, that entrances to two facilities had been sealed without collapsing their underground buildings, and that much of Iran's enriched uranium stockpile had been moved beforehand.[332] The strikes also drove domestic controversy: Rep. [[al-green|Al Green]] forced an impeachment vote that the House tabled 344–79, and Rep.
