[[jasmine-crockett|Jasmine Crockett]] said publicly that "I'm the one that's supposed to make the f---ing decision."[332] Separately, federal agents arrested 11 Iranian nationals in the United States in the days after the bombing, an action Trump framed as part of stopping the threat posed by the "world's number one state sponsor of terror."[332]

## August 2025: Witkoff mission, CPB defunding, and the BLS firing

Trump confirmed in early August 2025 that special envoy Steve Witkoff would travel to Moscow within days, ahead of the expiry of a 10-day ultimatum for Russia to reach a peace agreement with Ukraine, saying the goal was to "get a deal where people stop getting killed" while conceding Russians were "wily characters" adept at avoiding sanctions.[333] In May 2025 he signed an executive order directing the Corporation for Public Broadcasting to cease federal funding for PBS and NPR, a step that with congressional rescissions led the CPB to announce its wind-down in August.[333] On August 1, 2025 he ordered the dismissal of Bureau of Labor Statistics commissioner Erika McEntarfer after a weak July jobs report with large downward revisions, calling the numbers "faked" and promising a more "competent and qualified" replacement.[333] The same period saw the Office of Special Counsel open a Hatch Act review of former special counsel Jack Smith over his prosecutions of Trump, and the Justice Department file a misconduct complaint against Chief Judge James Boasberg.[333]

## Refusal of 2024 intelligence briefings

In an exclusive interview with DailyMail.com published in August 2024, Trump said he would not accept the intelligence briefings customarily offered to presidential nominees, for fear of being accused of leaking classified information.[337] U.S. spy agencies offer such briefings to candidates once they secure their party's nomination in order to prepare them for the presidency, but after the [[fbi|FBI]] recovered government documents including classified papers at his [[mar-a-lago-raid|Mar-a-Lago]] home in Florida, critics questioned whether Trump could be trusted with sensitive material.[337] Trump said he could get the briefings if he wanted them but sensed a trap: "I don't want them, because, number one, I know what's happening. It's very easy to see what's happening," he said, before criticizing President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] and Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]].[337]

Badlands commentary from [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] endorsed the refusal, arguing that the intelligence agencies had been wrong about nearly every public proclamation of the previous decade, that their propaganda had degraded into fabricated stories and anonymous quotes fed to media surrogates, and that their silence and refusal of transparency following the attempt on Trump's life discredited any claim to his trust.[337]

## August 2024 crime and law-enforcement platform

In a law-enforcement-centered speech on August 20, 2024, flanked by local Michigan sheriffs, Trump attacked Harris's "anti-police" policies while his campaign rolled out a tougher-on-crime platform that included pursuing the death penalty for child rapists and a nationwide return to [[stop-and-frisk|stop-and-frisk]] strategies.[337] He accused Harris of waging an "anti-police crusade" and of backing "extremist, pro-crime, anti-police" positions such as "defund the police," declaring, "We will never even think about defunding the police... The concept is so chilling and so horrible," and vowing, "We'll re-fund the police."[337] Trump also criticized low arrest rates under Biden and Harris, saying the administration "goes after guys like me but they don't go after people that kill people," and blamed Harris for a crime spike since he left office.[337]

Badlands read the platform as a direct threat to the [[deep-state|Deep State]], pairing Trump's promise to execute pedophiles and [[child-trafficking|child traffickers]] with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]]'s speeches accusing Western elites of satanism and of attempting to normalize pedophilia, and framing both as evidence of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] operating on all cylinders.[337]

## X shareholder disclosure commentary

Following a federal court order unsealing the shareholder list of [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]]'s [[x-corp|X]], [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Musk and Trump were executing what he called a "MIL-CIV pincer" on the [[cabal|Cabal]], with the timing of the disclosure on the eve of the final election push serving to catalyze influential fence-sitters and spook those who had chosen the wrong side since what Badlands called the realized coup on Joe Biden.[337] Badlands separately noted that investors [[andreessen-horowitz|Andreessen Horowitz]] had recently endorsed Trump.[337]

## July 2025: Epstein lawsuit, Syria pressure, and the crypto package

On July 17, 2025, Trump announced that he would sue the Wall Street Journal and its owner over a report alleging he had sent a racy birthday letter to Jeffrey Epstein, and simultaneously directed Attorney General Pam Bondi to begin the process of unsealing grand jury testimony in the disgraced financier's criminal case.[338] "Based on the ridiculous amount of publicity given to Jeffrey Epstein, I have asked Attorney General Pam Bondi to produce any and all pertinent Grand Jury testimony, subject to Court approval," Trump wrote on social media, adding that "this SCAM, perpetuated by the Democrats, should end, right now!"[338] He said he had personally warned the Journal's owner Rupert Murdoch and its editor in chief Emma Tucker that the letter was "fake" before publication, calling the story "false, malicious, and defamatory," and noted that he had "already beaten George Stephanopoulos/ABC, 60 Minutes/CBS, and others."[338] Badlands commentators read the episode as a demonstration of Trump's counter-striker method — setting narrative patterns as bait and letting opponents expose themselves — and argued that the Trump side had waged and won a twelve-day information war over the Epstein files.[338]

In the same period Trump's administration pressed Israel to halt strikes on Syrian government targets and to open direct talks with Damascus, with officials warning that the attacks could destabilize the new Syrian government.[338] Trump also angrily telephoned Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu over an IDF strike that killed three civilians in Gaza's only Catholic church, prompting a statement of regret from the Israeli premier's office.[338] On Russia policy, TIME argued that Trump had finally pivoted away from appeasement by announcing sales of U.S. weapons to NATO countries for transfer to Ukraine and by signaling possible sanctions on buyers of Russian oil and gas.[338] Domestically, his administration clawed back $4 billion in federal funds from California's bullet train, drawing a lawsuit from Governor Gavin Newsom alleging political retribution, while the House sent the GENIUS Act to his desk for signature.[338]

## Appeal bond on the New York civil fraud judgment (February 2024)

Following the $355 million civil fraud judgment entered against him in New York, Trump faced a requirement to post a deposit or bond covering the full amount of damages — close to half a billion dollars once roughly $100 million in interest was included — merely to file an appeal to the New York Court of Appeals.[341] Law professor [[jonathan-turley|Jonathan Turley]] argued that the threshold deposit rule magnifies the unfairness of a New York statute that requires no proof that anyone lost money, and can itself force insolvency on a party seeking review; he noted that Justice [[arthur-engoron|Arthur Engoron]] imposed the fine despite finding that the alleged victims made handsome profits, and that the amount represented roughly 14–17 percent of Trump's wealth.[341]

In the Republican primary, [[nikki-haley|Nikki Haley]] vowed on February 20, 2024 to stay in the race against Trump, telling a South Carolina crowd "I'm not going anywhere" and saying she had "no fear of Trump's retribution."[341] Commentators argued her purpose was to delay consolidation of the right behind Trump ahead of November.[341]

## Georgia case: Bradley text messages (February 2024)

On February 28, 2024, CNN reported on 413 text messages showing that [[terrence-bradley|Terrence Bradley]] played a deeper-than-known role in the defense push to disqualify District Attorney [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]] from the Georgia election case against Trump, working with [[ashleigh-merchant|Ashleigh Merchant]], an attorney for one of Trump's co-defendants.[342]

## Michigan benchmark

Commentators noted that the more than 100,000 "uncommitted" votes cast in the [[2024-michigan-democratic-primary|2024 Michigan Democratic primary]] were more than nine times Trump's 10,704-vote margin of victory over Hillary Clinton in the state in 2016, in a swing state worth 15 electoral votes.[342]

## Carlson interview and Ukraine remarks (April 2023)
In April 2023 Trump gave his first interview after being indicted on dozens of felony counts of business fraud to [[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]] on [[fox-news|Fox News]].[325] In it he said Ukraine "cannot win" the war with Russia and appeared to suggest the United States should stop sending military aid to Kyiv.[325] He described [[vladimir-putin|Putin]] as "very smart" while allowing that he had probably had a bad year, asked what the U.S. would do if Russia took over all of Ukraine given [[joe-biden|Biden]] commitment, and said Russia was "making massive amounts of ammunition, beyond anything they have ever made before. We do not have any ammunition. We have given it to Ukraine."[325]
Russian state television host [[evgeny-popov|Evgeny Popov]] replayed the segments and said Trump had proposed that America "surrender" and stop assisting Ukraine, concluding that Republicans understood they would have to make a deal and that "Donald is laying the groundwork," coverage that [[newsweek|Newsweek]] relayed under the framing that Russian state TV claimed Trump was preparing a Ukraine deal.[325] [[cnn|CNN]] separately attacked Carlson for failing to challenge Trump during the sit-down.[325]
Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump had long signalled the need for a peacemaker during this period of escalation and would fill that role when the time was right, and that Trump was leveraging Carlson platform to educate "Normie MAGA" audiences on foreign policy nuance.[325]

## February 2025: institutions, Gaza and Korea
In the second week of February 2025 Trump moved simultaneously against several Washington institutions. He announced the termination of multiple trustees of the [[john-f-kennedy-center-for-the-performing-arts|John F. Kennedy Center for the Performing Arts]], named himself chairman, and said he would dictate programming, specifically ending events featuring performers in drag.[326] The same day he dismissed [[colleen-shogan|Colleen Shogan]], the Archivist of the United States, an office overseeing the [[national-archives-and-records-administration|National Archives]], which he had criticized since it alerted the Justice Department to his handling of classified documents in 2022.[326] His administration also went "scorched-earth" against [[usaid|USAID]], which he called "corrupt," placing at least 8,000 staffers and contractors on leave or furlough, while [[doge|DOGE]] began taking over the [[department-of-education|Department of Education]], which Trump described as ineffective, wasteful and dominated by radical leftists.[326]
On the economy, the [[white-house|White House]] press secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] responded to January's jobs report by saying it revealed "the Biden economy was far worse than anyone thought," and citing Trump's national energy emergency declaration, his pledge to cut ten regulations for every new regulatory action, and his plan for "the largest tax cut in history."[326]
Abroad, Israeli President [[isaac-herzog|Isaac Herzog]] said Trump would begin talks with Arab leaders — first the king of Jordan and the president of Egypt, and also the crown prince of [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]] — over his proposal to relocate Palestinians from Gaza while the enclave is rebuilt as what Trump called "the Riviera of the Middle East."[326] Badlands commentary warned that Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]]'s alteration of ceasefire terms threatened the deal Trump had assembled "to stave off World War 3," and asked whether Trump would continue to stand by him if the deal collapsed.[326]
Trump also said he wanted to reestablish relations with North Korean leader [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong-un]], telling reporters "We will have relations with North Korea and with Kim Jong-un," recalling that "I got along with him very well" and claiming "I think I stopped the war," and describing the rapport as "a very big asset for everybody."[326] Separately, he ordered the [[us-secret-service|Secret Service]] to provide him "every bit of information" about his two would-be assassins, telling the New York Post, "I'm entitled to know... Why did the one guy have six cell phones and why did the other guy have [foreign] apps? No more holding back because of Biden."[326] At the Super Bowl in New Orleans his entourage included Eric Trump, Lara Trump and Ivanka Trump, while first lady [[melania-trump|Melania Trump]] was absent; Trump said in a statement that she "joins me in sending our best wishes for a great Super Bowl Sunday."[326]

## December 2024: envoys, canals and the funding fight
In December 2024, President-elect Trump named television producer [[mark-burnett|Mark Burnett]] — the man behind [[the-apprentice|The Apprentice]] — as special envoy to the United Kingdom, saying Burnett brought "a unique blend of diplomatic acumen and international recognition to this important role."[329] The selection was described as continuing his trend of staffing the incoming administration with figures from television or politics, alongside defense secretary pick [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]] and [[mehmet-oz|Mehmet Oz]].[329]
The same weekend, Trump threatened to demand the return of the [[panama-canal|Panama Canal]] if [[panama|Panama]] did not lower its transit fees, writing on Truth Social that "this complete 'rip-off' of our Country will immediately stop" and calling President [[jimmy-carter|Jimmy Carter]]'s 1979 treaty a foolish giveaway made "for One Dollar."[329] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed these moves as narrative "deployments" derived from The Art of the Deal and Sun Tzu's Art of War, noting that Trump's deal-making was on display across borders and oceans before he had even taken office — with [[justin-trudeau|Justin Trudeau]] in a Trump-provoked death spiral to the north and [[claudia-sheinbaum|Claudia Sheinbaum]] pressed to take the fight to the cartels to the south.[329]
Domestically, Trump's eleventh-hour intervention in the government funding fight — backed by [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] — led Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] to trash his initial deal with Democrats and brought the country to the brink of a holiday shutdown, with The Hill describing House Republicans as governed by an effort to appease "an audience of one."[329] His insistence that any package include a debt-ceiling hike infuriated House conservatives, and Rep. Bob Good, former head of the [[house-freedom-caucus|House Freedom Caucus]], said "trying to raise the debt limit was a mistake."[329]
329. Badlands Brief — "Our Boy Blue, the Apprentice and the Art of the Deal", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-d36
## June 2023 classified documents trial date
On 20 June 2023, Judge [[aileen-cannon|Aileen Cannon]] set 14 August 2023 as the first court date in Trump's [[2023-trump-classified-documents-indictment|classified documents case]] in the U.S. District Court for the Southern District of Florida, a little over a week before the first Republican primary debate, with pretrial motions due by 24 July.[330] Trump had been arraigned in Miami the previous week and pleaded not guilty to 31 counts of willful retention of national defense information alongside counts of conspiracy to obstruct justice, withholding and concealing records, a scheme to conceal, and false statements.[330] Badlands writer [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued the prosecution was politically motivated, contending that Trump's retention and declassification of records was governed by the [[presidential-records-act|Presidential Records Act]] and settled by the "Clinton sock drawer" litigation, and that the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] had resorted to the [[espionage-act|Espionage Act]] precisely because the records-act precedent favored him.[330] She cited a Harvard/Harris poll showing 55% of likely voters viewing the indictment as politically motivated and 56% as 2024 election interference, and a CNN poll putting the politically motivated figure at 71%, and argued the scheduling so close to the debate would push those numbers higher.[330] The simultaneous announcement of the [[hunter-biden-plea-agreement|Hunter Biden plea agreement]] was read by Badlands commentators as sharpening the [[two-tier-justice-system|two-tier justice system]] contrast between Trump and the establishment.[330]
330. Badlands Brief — "Badlands News Brief: Hunter's Slap & Trump's Trap", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-d24
## July 2023 indictment speculation
In July 2023, former Trump White House attorney [[ty-cobb|Ty Cobb]] predicted on CNN that a federal indictment of Trump in the January 6 investigation was "about to happen," describing the case as encompassing "all the attempts to cling to power and prevent the peaceful transfer of government."[331] Trump had already been indicted twice that year and awaited trial later in the summer.[331] The same month, the [[june-2023-harvard-caps-harris-poll|June 2023 Harvard CAPS-Harris Poll]] showed Trump with a net approval of −3%, tied with [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] on favorability at 45% but carrying a 48% unfavorable rating, while separately leading [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] by six points in a head-to-head.[331]

## Speaker race endorsement and MAGA extremist categorization (October 2023)
After the removal of Speaker [[kevin-mccarthy|Kevin McCarthy]] in October 2023, Trump was reported to be attending a closed GOP House candidate forum the following Tuesday, and after briefly teasing himself for the speakership he endorsed Representative [[jim-jordan|Jim Jordan]]'s bid.[334] Representative [[troy-nehls|Troy Nehls]] relayed the endorsement on X, saying Congress "should listen to the leader of our party."[334] Badlands contributor [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] argued that the affair was useful regardless of intent, because pushback against a Trump speakership exposed establishment Republicans; in his framing, Trump's America First populism and call for a return of the true rule of law act as a political X-ray revealing [[rino|RINOs]] as globalist assets, and Trump's constant presence at the center of the news cycle is itself a weapon in the information war.[334]
Separately, a Newsweek investigation that month reported that the federal government had quietly created a new extremist tracking category built around Trump's MAGA followers ahead of the 2024 election, with the [[fbi|FBI]] acknowledging that the vast majority of its "anti-government" investigations involved Trump supporters.[334] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] also noted Trump's allusions to the [[joe-biden|Biden]] administration as his "second administration" as an example of increasingly overt signaling in the [[fifth-generation-warfare|War of Stories]].[334]
## Colorado ballot disqualification fallout (December 2023)
The [[colorado-supreme-court|Colorado Supreme Court]]'s ruling of December 19, 2023 that Trump was ineligible to appear on the state's 2024 ballots by virtue of inciting an insurrection was described as another stress test for America's governing institutions and as evidence of a presidential campaign litigated as much in the courtroom as on the trail.[335] The suit was brought by [[citizens-for-responsibility-and-ethics-in-washington|Citizens for Responsibility and Ethics in Washington]], whose president [[noah-bookbinder|Noah Bookbinder]] sits on the [[homeland-security-advisory-council|Homeland Security Advisory Council]] advising Secretary [[alejandro-mayorkas|Alejandro Mayorkas]], a link the Daily Caller cast as a direct line to the administration of [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]].[335] Trump's pollster [[jim-mclaughlin|Jim McLaughlin]] predicted the decision would backfire on its authors.[335]
Reaction spread quickly: [[vivek-ramaswamy|Vivek Ramaswamy]] threatened to withdraw from the Colorado primary, the [[colorado-republican-party|Colorado Republican Party]] said it would abandon the primary for a pure caucus system, and California Lieutenant Governor [[eleni-kounalakis|Eleni Kounalakis]] asked Secretary of State [[shirley-weber|Shirley Weber]] to explore removing Trump from California's ballot.[335] Badlands writer [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] warned that write-in votes for Trump in Colorado would be discarded under state statute and that the appeal to the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|U.S. Supreme Court]] must contest the underlying insurrection findings rather than only the Fourteenth Amendment question.[335] [[paul-fleuret|Paul Fleuret]] called the removal "arguably the most disgusting miscarriage of justice" of his adult life and described the proceeding as lawfare.[335]
334. Badlands News Brief — "Speaker Deception, Ukraine Fatigue & FBI Corruption", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-d73
335. Badlands Media — "Badlands News Brief: Colorado Fallout (and Exposure) Continues", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-d5c
## Lawsuit against the Des Moines Register and Ann Selzer (December 2024)
On Monday, December 16, 2024, President-elect Trump sued [[des-moines-register|The Des Moines Register]] and pollster [[ann-selzer|J. Ann Selzer]] in Iowa state court in Polk County over a poll released just before Election Day showing Vice President Kamala Harris leading in Iowa by three percentage points; Trump went on to win the state by 14.[336] The suit accused the outlet and pollster of violating Iowa's consumer fraud laws through deception, asserting that "Selzer's polling 'miss' was not an astonishing coincidence - it was intentional," and sought unspecified damages, an order barring further "deceptive polls," and disclosure of the underlying data.[336] The complaint argued that "for too long, left-wing pollsters have attempted to influence electoral outcomes through manipulated polls."[336] It formed part of Trump's escalating legal fights with the media, alongside actions against ABC, CBS, journalist Bob Woodward and the Pulitzer Prize board, and followed the $15 million plus $1 million ABC settlement.[336] Badlands writer Ashe in America argued a win could set precedent and deter poll manipulation.[336]
In foreign affairs the same week, Trump said he wanted to speak with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]], declining to tell reporters whether the two had spoken since his election victory.[336] He also hosted [[nigel-farage|Nigel Farage]], [[nick-candy|Nick Candy]] and [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] at Mar-a-Lago, with the Reform UK figures thanking him for the use of the club.[336] Meanwhile his threatened tariffs featured in Canadian Finance Minister [[chrystia-freeland|Chrystia Freeland]]'s resignation letter as the "grave challenge" facing Canada.[336]

## Second-term opening week (January 2025)

By late January 2025 polling showed a sharp swing in public trust toward Trump and the Republican Party on immigration, with Republicans holding a 22-point margin over Democrats — a reversal from April 2017, when Democrats led by 11 points, as CNN data analyst [[harry-enten|Harry Enten]] noted on air.[339] Entering his second week in office, Trump pressed for early progress on his campaign vow to conduct the largest mass deportation in U.S. history, with [[immigration-and-customs-enforcement|ICE]] arrests climbing to nearly 1,200 in a single day and offices given targets of 75 arrests apiece.[339] He also floated shifting 88,000 IRS agents to border duties, while [[stephen-miller|Stephen Miller]] raised deputizing National Guard members.[339]

Trump was reported to be quietly reigniting a fight over the [[united-states-census-bureau|census]], having previously pushed to add a citizenship question to the 2020 count with the goal of excluding illegal immigrants from congressional apportionment — an effort the Supreme Court blocked largely on procedural grounds.[339] Late on Monday, January 27, his administration directed federal agencies to pause the disbursement of all federal financial assistance pending review, in a memo issued by acting OMB director [[matthew-vaeth|Matthew Vaeth]].[339] The same day, Acting Attorney General [[james-mchenry|James McHenry]] fired more than a dozen prosecutors from [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]]'s former team, and Trump was reported set to sign an executive order barring taxpayer-funded transgender accommodations in the military, including hormone treatments, surgeries and mixed-sex housing.[339]

Senator [[john-fetterman|John Fetterman]], defending his pre-inauguration visit to [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]] on "The View," described Trump as "kind" and "cordial," calling the meeting a positive experience free of political theater.[339]

## Westchester County case dropped (June 2023)

On a Thursday in June 2023, Trump announced that the district attorney's office in Westchester County, New York had dropped its criminal case against him and that no charges would be filed.[340] The matter concerned a Trump golf course and subpoenaed records, and according to the Westchester D.A.'s office the investigation produced nothing chargeable.[340] Badlands contributor [[johnny-q|Johnny Q]] called the outcome predictable, argued that Trump's poll numbers rose with each indictment, and wrote that New York Attorney General [[letitia-james|Letitia James]] had indicated the New York and Georgia criminal matters would likely be adjourned while the classified documents case took precedence.[340] He described Washington, D.C. as having become a nightmare for special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]], whose strongest documents-case option had instead opened in Florida, and contended that the cumulative effect of the prosecutions was to damage public opinion of the Biden administration and the Justice Department while making a fair trial for Trump difficult anywhere in the nation.[340]

## Zelensky criticism of Trump's war-ending pledge (June 2023)

In a June 2023 NBC News interview, Ukrainian President [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] attacked Trump's pledge to end the war in Ukraine, arguing that if Kyiv did not defeat Moscow, Russia would attack a [[nato|NATO]] member state and force the United States into direct conflict.[340] Asked about Trump's claim that he would immediately open talks with the Kremlin and bring the war to a negotiated settlement, Zelensky said, "Are they ready to start a war to send their children? Are they ready to die?"[340] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] wrote that seeing Zelensky mock Trump's appeals only solidified his belief that Trump would occupy the role of Peacemaker within the collective mind and the "War of Stories."[340] Commenting separately on NATO's stalled [[f-16-fighting-falcon|F-16]] deliveries, Johnny Q wrote that "Donald Trump is correct when he says the catastrophe needs to end."[340]
