342. Badlands Brief — "Hunter Hunted as Metrics Turn Sharply on Establishment", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-d8f

## Moscow invitation and the European leaders' summit (August 2025)

In August 2025, following his Anchorage, Alaska summit with Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]], Trump received a formal invitation to visit Moscow, confirmed by Russian Foreign Minister [[sergey-lavrov|Sergey Lavrov]] in an interview with Rossiya 24.[344] Putin had extended the invitation in person during the summit, and Lavrov recalled that Trump described the idea of visiting Russia as "interesting."[344] At the joint press conference Trump conceded he could "get a little heat" for traveling to Moscow but said he could "see it possibly happening."[344] Both leaders described the Alaska meeting — their first in-person encounter since 2019 — positively, Putin calling it "frank" and "substantive" and Trump hailing it as "productive."[344]

On Monday, August 18, 2025, seven European leaders joined Ukrainian President [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelenskyy]] at the [[white-house|White House]] in what NBC News described as a rare display of trans-Atlantic unity and the first time in decades that such a broad delegation of allies had assembled there under such pressing circumstances.[344] The heads of state and [[nato|NATO]] partners were met by Trump's chief of protocol, and in Zelenskyy's case by Trump himself; some had met earlier with Trump's special envoy for Ukraine.[344] The visit was intended to bolster Zelenskyy after his disastrous February 2025 White House appearance, when he clashed with Trump and Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]].[344]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump's evident satisfaction reflected a public "ready for peace," describing Trump's tariffs and Putin's battlefield attrition as "twin pincers" squeezing an "empire of endless war," and casting the episode as part of the "Arc of the Peacemakers."[344]

## Domestic actions of August 2025

The same period saw Trump administration pressure on the District of Columbia: federal prosecutors under U.S. Attorney [[jeanine-pirro|Jeanine Pirro]] opened an investigation into alleged manipulation of crime statistics by the [[metropolitan-police-department|Metropolitan Police Department]] amid a showdown over control of the department.[344] Director of National Intelligence [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] stripped security clearances from 37 current and former national security officials, a move described as the latest in a series of retributions against officials and political opponents, following Trump's March 2025 revocation of clearances for Joe Biden, Kamala Harris, Antony Blinken, Liz Cheney, and others.[344] Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] also announced that interviews for 11 candidates to replace Federal Reserve Chair [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] would begin after Labor Day, with the White House pressing for interest rate cuts.[344]

## Second transition: vetting, appointments, and the MAGAvengers

During the November 2024 transition, Trump's team [[trump-transition-fbi-background-check-bypass|bypassed traditional FBI background checks]] for at least some Cabinet picks, using private firms instead on the view that the bureau's process was slow and prone to surfacing material usable for political damage.[345] The approach coincided with contested selections including [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]] for attorney general and [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] for director of national intelligence, and reflected what reporting described as Trump's deep mistrust of the national security establishment he calls the [[deep-state|Deep State]].[345]

That same weekend Trump appeared at [[ufc-309|UFC 309]] at Madison Square Garden alongside [[dana-white|Dana White]], with [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]], [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]], [[donald-trump-jr|Donald Trump Jr.]], [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] and Gabbard in his entourage — a tableau Badlands Media dubbed the "MAGAvengers" and read as a public narrative arc of the [[great-awakening|Great Awakening]].[345] Separately, his transition was reported to be compiling a list of officers involved in the 2021 Afghanistan withdrawal for [[trump-transition-afghanistan-withdrawal-courts-martial-review|possible courts-martial]].[345] House Democrat [[wiley-nickel|Wiley Nickel]] responded to the incoming administration by proposing a [[shadow-cabinet-proposal-2024|shadow cabinet]] of 26 members to mirror and contest Trump's Cabinet.[345]

## June 2025 Iran ceasefire and Medvedev exchange

On June 23, 2025, Trump characterized Iran's retaliatory missile attack on the American [[al-udeid-air-base|Al Udeid Air Base]] in Qatar as a "very weak response," writing on Truth Social that of 14 missiles fired, 13 were knocked down and one was "set free" because it was headed in a nonthreatening direction.[347] He thanked Tehran for giving "early warning" of the attack to minimize casualties and predicted Iran had "gotten it all out of their 'system.'"[347] Later that night he announced a "complete and total ceasefire" between Israel and Iran, telling an interviewer, "I think the ceasefire is unlimited. It's going to go forever," and saying he did not believe the two countries would "ever be shooting at each other again."[347] A deadline he set for Iran to cease fire passed amid a further Iranian barrage that killed three people in Israel, and Israel did not acknowledge the announcement; Trump nonetheless declared on Truth Social that the "12 Days War" was over.[347] See [[twelve-day-war|Twelve Day War]].[347]

The same day, Trump hailed America's "powerful" and "lethal" nuclear-powered submarine fleet while condemning former Russian president [[dmitry-medvedev|Dmitry Medvedev]]'s suggestion that several countries were ready to supply Iran with nuclear warheads, writing: "Did I hear Former President Medvedev, from Russia, casually throwing around the 'N word' (Nuclear!)... If he did say that, and, if confirmed, please let me know, IMMEDIATELY."[347] Having campaigned on lower energy costs, Trump also used Truth Social to warn against higher oil prices amid the Middle East conflict, posting "I'M WATCHING!"; prices fell below $70 a barrel after Iran's strike spared crude infrastructure.[347]

In domestic matters that week, the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] allowed his administration to resume [[third-country-deportations|third-country deportations]] with limited notice, and Senate leaders worked to place the [[one-big-beautiful-bill-act|One Big Beautiful Bill Act]] on his desk by July 4 despite adverse rulings from the Senate parliamentarian.[347]


## October 30, 2025 developments

On Thursday, October 30, 2025, Trump called for the end of the Senate [[filibuster|filibuster]], urging Republicans on Truth Social to "initiate the '[[nuclear-option|nuclear option]]'" so that a government funding bill could pass with 50 votes rather than 60 during the [[october-2025-government-shutdown|October 2025 government shutdown]].[349] "Because of the fact that the Democrats have gone stone cold 'crazy,' the choice is clear — initiate the 'nuclear option,' get rid of the filibuster and make America great again!" he wrote.[349] Senate Republicans had privately asked Vice President Vance days earlier to get Trump to stop raising the filibuster, and the demand was seen as endangering fragile reopening negotiations.[349]

An August 2025 directive from Trump to War Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]] underpinned the [[pentagon|Pentagon]]'s order that every state and territory [[national-guard|National Guard]] stand up [[quick-reaction-force|"quick reaction" forces]] by January, trained and equipped for riots and civil unrest.[349] Badlands host [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] placed the order in a sequence beginning with Trump's targeting of NGOs and Soros funding in summer 2025 and the [[no-kings-protests|No Kings]] mobilizations that "tested readiness" and allowed mapping.[349]

The same day, Trump held a summit with Chinese leader [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] in South Korea at which the two broadly agreed to reduce trade tensions sparked by American tariffs and to stabilize relations between the world's two largest economies.[349] Separately, four Republican senators — [[rand-paul|Rand Paul]], [[mitch-mcconnell|Mitch McConnell]], [[susan-collins|Susan Collins]], and [[lisa-murkowski|Lisa Murkowski]] — joined Democrats in approving a resolution to repeal Trump's global tariffs, declaring terminated the national emergency he invoked on April 2, which he dubbed "[[liberation-day|Liberation Day]]."[349] Passage was symbolic, as Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] was seen as unlikely to bring it up in the House and Trump was certain to veto it.[349] The Tucker Carlson Network also highlighted the U.S. naval armada steaming toward Venezuela to pressure [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolas Maduro]] to leave power.[349]


## Asia Tour, Qatar Stop, and October 2025 Sanctions Diplomacy

En route to the [[asean|ASEAN]] summit in Malaysia in late October 2025, Trump made an unplanned refueling stop at Al Udeid Air Base in [[qatar|Qatar]], where Emir [[tamim-bin-hamad-al-thani|Sheikh Tamim bin Hamad Al Thani]] and Prime Minister Sheikh Mohammed bin Abdulrahman Al Thani boarded Air Force One to discuss the Israel-Hamas hostage deal and stability in Gaza.[351] Trump called the Emir "one of the great rulers of the world, not just the Middle East," and credited Qatari mediation: "What we've done is incredible. Peace to the Middle East... You have a safe Middle East right now."[351] Badlands commentary framed the stop as deliberate public support for Qatar against a coordinated campaign involving Laura Loomer, Mark Levin and [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]].[351]

The Malaysia stop produced a U.S.-China trade framework announced by Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]], removing the threat of 100% tariffs on Chinese imports from 1 November and including a final deal on TikTok's U.S. sale, ahead of a planned meeting with [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] in South Korea.[351] Trump said simply, "I think we're going to have a deal with China."[351]

Days earlier his administration had sanctioned Russia's largest oil companies, [[rosneft|Rosneft]] and [[lukoil|Lukoil]], while urging Moscow toward an immediate Ukraine ceasefire; [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] dismissed the measures, and Kremlin envoy [[kirill-dmitriev|Kirill Dmitriev]] nonetheless arrived in Miami for multi-day talks with [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]].[351] The administration also imposed sweeping sanctions on Colombian President [[gustavo-petro|Gustavo Petro]], his family and Interior Minister Armando Benedetti over cocaine trafficking, with Trump calling Petro "a lousy leader" and "a thug" and Colombia "a drug den."[351]

Domestically, the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]]'s plan to monitor November elections in California and New Jersey drew accusations from Governor [[gavin-newsom|Gavin Newsom]] that "Donald Trump's puppet DOJ" was interfering.[351] Separately, attorney [[mike-davis|Mike Davis]] reported that a federal grand jury had been empaneled in Fort Pierce, Florida, at the administration's request, to probe a decade-long conspiracy against Trump beginning with the 2016 Russia investigation.[351] Badlands commentary invoked Trump's 2017 inaugural line — "The oath of office I take today is an oath of allegiance to all Americans" — as a contract still awaiting delivery.[351]

## June 2023: polling surge and debate posture

By late June 2023, Trump's poll numbers were climbing despite escalating legal pressure. On June 8, 2023 the [[department-of-justice|U.S. Department of Justice]] filed a federal indictment against him for allegedly mishandling classified records, but Special Counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]]'s 37 charges appeared to strengthen rather than weaken his reelection odds.[352] A [[quinnipiac-university|Quinnipiac]] poll taken largely after the indictment showed [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] at 48 percent to Trump's 44 percent — a statistical tie — which [[abc-news|ABC News]] anchor [[jonathan-karl|Jonathan Karl]] called astonishing for the first former president to face federal prosecution, and Trump's showing was stronger still against his rivals for the GOP nomination.[352] Badlands writer [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued the numbers reflected public awakening to the weaponization of government.[352]

Trump also weighed skipping the first [[republican-national-committee|Republican National Committee]] primary debate, scheduled for August 23, 2023 in Milwaukee, and was reported by NBC News to be exploring counterprogramming — a competing event held during the broadcast.[352] In an April 25, 2023 [[truth-social|Truth Social]] post he asked why, when "leading by seemingly insurmountable numbers" against "hostile Networks with angry, TRUMP & MAGA hating anchors," he should subject himself to being libeled and abused, and objected that the second debate would be held at the Reagan Library, chaired by [[washington-post|Washington Post]] publisher [[fred-ryan|Fred Ryan]].[352] On June 26 he accused [[fox-news|Fox News]] of bias in favor of [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]], noting the network carried the governor's border news conference in full while ignoring Trump's weekend speeches.[352]

The same week, the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] struck down [[affirmative-action|affirmative action]] in college admissions, which [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed as another pre-Independence Day victory for the MAGA movement following two Trump indictments, and possibly a teaser for [[agenda-47|Agenda 47]].[352] [[candace-owens|Candace Owens]] also abandoned DeSantis and returned to Trump, an event Burning Bright described as the death blow to the public facade of the DeSantis campaign.[352]

## Third 2023 indictment and the January 6 grand jury

In late July 2023, Trump's legal and political teams prepared for the possibility that a federal grand jury would vote on charges against him as early as Thursday, July 27, according to three sources familiar with the thinking of his inner circle.[353] Trump had said on his social media platform on July 18 that he was notified he is a target of the investigation by special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] into the January 6, 2021 attack on the U.S. Capitol and efforts to overturn the 2020 election.[353] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] of [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] observed that the Truth Community greeted the prospect of Trump's third indictment of 2023 with more excitement than trepidation, took that reaction as evidence that more was afoot than the media portrayed, and noted that Trump was promoting his coming legal battles more than any other public figure — a choice Burning Bright attributed to his showmanship and to a possible intent to use the trial to press his [[2020-election-fraud-claims|2020 election fraud claims]].[353]

A Harvard-Harris poll conducted July 19-20, 2023 gave Trump a 45 percent favorable and 49 percent unfavorable rating, showed him winning the Republican primary and defeating [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] 45 to 40 percent in a general election, and put him ahead of Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] 47 to 38 percent, though he trailed [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] on net favorability.[353]

## Second apparent assassination attempt (September 2024)

Trump was unharmed on Sunday, September 15, 2024, in what authorities investigated as a potential assassination attempt after a man pointed a rifle into his West Palm Beach golf course while he was playing.[354] A [[us-secret-service|Secret Service]] agent positioned one or two holes ahead spotted a rifle muzzle in the fence line and opened fire, and Trump's detail moved him to a holding room; [[ryan-wesley-routh|Ryan Wesley Routh]] was arrested shortly afterward.[354] It was the second time in just over two months that a man had a high-powered rifle within range of Trump, renewing concerns about his safety in the final stretch of the campaign before Election Day on November 5.[354] The Secret Service had repeatedly denied requests from the Trump campaign for additional security at public events, citing limited resources.[354] Badlands treated the episode as part of a pattern it labelled the [[2024-trump-golf-course-assassination-attempt|second attempt on Trump's life]], reading its timing against the Establishment's reaction to [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]]'s ascent.[354]

## Election-integrity claims and media framing

During the September 10, 2024 presidential debate Trump said that "our elections are bad, and a lot of these illegal immigrants coming in, they're trying to get them to vote," a claim NPR characterized as an echo of the [[great-replacement-theory|great replacement]] theory and as an attempt to undermine the results of the 2024 election.[354] Badlands responded by pointing to federal data showing only 35 prosecutions of [[noncitizen-voting|noncitizen voting]] between 2001 and 2021 despite thousands of aliens registered to vote.[354]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] also described Trump as "the tribune of the people commenting on the sidelines" while a "Crisis Cascade" — Russian nuclear exercises, Chinese pressure on Taiwan, and Federal Reserve rate paralysis between inflation and stagflation — unfolds under Establishment management.[354]

## 2024 election victory and its immediate aftermath

Trump won the 2024 presidential election on November 5, 2024, taking both the Electoral College and the popular vote and sweeping the contested states of Georgia, Pennsylvania, Michigan and Wisconsin, with Arizona and Nevada projected to follow.[355] Decision Desk HQ projected his return to the White House, making him the first president to win non-consecutive terms since Grover Cleveland in 1892.[355] Republicans simultaneously recaptured the Senate for the first time in four years, while control of the House remained undecided the day after the election.[355] [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] conceded on November 6.[355]

Commentators emphasized the scale of the comeback: Trump prevailed after being impeached and acquitted twice, indicted four times, surviving two assassination attempts and absorbing what Just the News described as an unparalleled avalanche of lawfare, media, Big Tech and Democratic opposition.[355] Beyond his personal vindication, the result was framed as a once-in-a-generation realignment deeper than 2016, as black men, Hispanic voters and young voters shifted toward him while Arab and Muslim voters underperformed for Harris — an exodus dubbed "D-Exit," the American analogue of [[brexit|Brexit]].[355]

Foreign reaction was immediate. Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] was the first world leader to congratulate him, hailing "history's greatest comeback" and "a powerful recommitment to the great alliance between Israel and America."[355] Ukrainian President [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelenskyy]], European Commission President [[ursula-von-der-leyen|Ursula von der Leyen]] and British Prime Minister [[keir-starmer|Keir Starmer]] followed with congratulations, while Moscow offered only guarded optimism.[355] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that legacy-media anxiety focused conspicuously on Western Europe and Israel rather than the United States itself, reading the globalist alarm as recognition that "the US is patient zero for the sovereign restoration."[355] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] cast Trump and [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] as co-captains of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] and noted Trump's threat to impose tariffs marking up EU goods by 100–200 percent.[355]

On the domestic legal front, Justice Department officials moved to "wind down" the two federal criminal cases brought by special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] before Inauguration Day, citing the post-Watergate policy against prosecuting a sitting president.[355] In the Middle East, the Yemeni [[houthis|Ansar Allah]] movement told Newsweek that Trump could spare the American economy further damage by reining in Israel's war in Gaza, while expressing doubt he would honor his promises to Arab voters.[355]

## Woodward audiobook lawsuit (2023)

At the end of January 2023, Trump sued journalist [[bob-woodward|Bob Woodward]], publisher [[simon-and-schuster|Simon & Schuster]] and parent company [[paramount-global|Paramount Global]] for at least $49,980,000 over the commercial release of audio from Woodward's interviews with him.[357] Trump claimed he had never agreed to allow the recordings to be sold to the public; the damages estimate was based on the audiobook "The Trump Tapes" selling more than two million copies at $24.99 each.[357] Badlands Media commentary on the [[trump-v-woodward-lawsuit|Trump v. Woodward lawsuit]] argued that Woodward had altered Trump's words and their context, and that the recordings omitted a conversation refuting the basis of the Ukraine impeachment case.[357]

## Transition-period developments, November 2024

### Cabinet building and the Gaetz withdrawal

In late November 2024 [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]] withdrew from consideration as Trump's attorney general, saying he did not wish to be "a distraction" for the transition team, and then confirmed he would not join the 119th Congress despite winning re-election.[358] Badlands writer Ashe in America linked the episode to Trump's pre-election aside to Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] at the Madison Square Garden rally about "our little secret," and read the whole cabinet slate — together with the work of the [[department-of-government-efficiency|Department of Government Efficiency]] — as staffing for an 18-month program to re-limit the federal government under the Constitution by winding down unconstitutional bureaucratic institutions.[358]

Trump also announced hedge fund founder [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] as his choice for Treasury Secretary and Johns Hopkins surgeon [[marty-makary|Marty Makary]] to lead the [[food-and-drug-administration|FDA]] under [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]], saying the agency had "lost the trust of Americans" and that Makary would "course-correct" it.[358]

### "Negotiator in Chief" on Gaza

Hostage families and deal-minded Israeli officials placed their hopes in Trump to succeed where [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] had failed in convincing [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] to end the war in Gaza in exchange for the release of hostages held by [[hamas|Hamas]]; with a ceasefire looking unlikely before inauguration, Trump was expected to inherit the crisis and responsibility for the seven Americans held, four believed alive.[358] Incoming press secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] said Trump would restore stricter sanctions on Iran, fight terrorism and support Israel, declaring that "President Trump will serve as America's Negotiator in Chief and work to get innocent civilians held hostage home."[358] When Israeli President [[isaac-herzog|Isaac Herzog]] called to congratulate him on the election, Herzog said releasing the 101 hostages was "an urgent issue."[358] Badlands read Trump as unwilling to indulge what it called the false narratives sustaining the conflict, and framed the [[mike-huckabee|Mike Huckabee]] appointment and other pro-Israel picks as a poker play designed to maximise leverage over Netanyahu.[358]

### UFC 309 and the Saudi signal

At UFC 309 in Madison Square Garden, Trump sat between [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] and [[yasir-al-rumayyan|Yasir Al-Rumayyan]], governor of Saudi Arabia's [[public-investment-fund|Public Investment Fund]] and chairman of [[saudi-aramco|Aramco]], as Jon Jones defeated Stipe Miocic.[358] Musk and Al-Rumayyan were reported to control $1.2 trillion in assets; with Trump the trio's combined net worth was put at about $1.8 trillion, equal to the latest US Congress spending bill, and the photograph was dubbed "the richest picture ever seen in world sport."[358] Badlands, discussing it on [[devolution-power-hour|Devolution Power Hour]], treated the appearance as a deliberate message.[358]

### Ukraine escalation before the handover

Western officials reportedly believed the Kremlin would not escalate significantly before Trump's January swearing-in, and the outgoing Biden administration weighed serious escalations including the [[proposed-return-of-nuclear-weapons-to-ukraine|return of nuclear weapons to Ukraine]].[358] Much of the American political class had cast Trump and [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] as Russian agents, though extensive investigations into Trump's Kremlin ties had come up empty.[358] Badlands writer Burning Bright tied Trump's repeated invocation of a "mandate" to a mandate not merely in the vote but to rebuild the world.[358]

## Netanyahu meeting and Abbas letter (July 2024)

On Tuesday, July 23, 2024, Trump announced that he would host Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu that Friday at his Mar-a-Lago resort in Palm Beach, Florida, writing on Truth Social that he was "looking forward to welcoming Bibi Netanyahu."[359] It would be their first meeting since the end of Trump's presidency, and came amid strains between Netanyahu and President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] over Israel's war against Hamas in Gaza.[359] Late the same day, Trump posted a letter written to him by Palestinian President [[mahmoud-abbas|Mahmoud Abbas]] the day after the July 13 rally shooting, in which Abbas wished Trump "strength and safety"; Trump said he looked forward to meeting Netanyahu and working toward peace in the Middle East.[359]

Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued that Trump and Netanyahu are in fact adversaries, citing Netanyahu's early congratulation of Biden after the 2020 election — which Trump has never forgiven and has attacked in Israeli media — the February 15, 2017 joint press conference at which Trump publicly pressed Netanyahu to hold back on West Bank settlements, the tense body language of Trump's May 2017 visit to Israel, Netanyahu's late withdrawal from the January 2020 [[qassem-soleimani|Soleimani]] strike, and Netanyahu's attempt to back out of the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]] via ambassador [[ron-dermer|Ron Dermer]] before Trump personally forced the signing.[359] The release of the Abbas note on the eve of Netanyahu's congressional address was read as a deliberate signal.[359]

## Post-shooting political standing

Polling in late July 2024 showed Trump benefiting from a strong post-convention bump: a Forbes/[[harrisx|HarrisX]] survey taken July 19–21 had him leading Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] 51%–40% and Biden 49%–39% among likely voters.[359] A Reuters/[[ipsos|Ipsos]] poll released the same week showed Harris ahead 44%–42%, a result [[zerohedge|ZeroHedge]] attributed to oversampling of Democratic respondents.[359] Badlands commentary also noted that the [[united-states-secret-service|Secret Service]] director resigned on July 23 over the failure to prevent the attempt on Trump's life, and asked openly who had tried to kill "the most popular politician in the world."[359]

## April 2024: trial, Bud Light and rising retrospective approval

On Monday, April 15, 2024, the first day of the Manhattan hush-money trial, Justice [[juan-merchan|Juan Merchan]] barred Trump from attending his son's high school graduation and from attending a Supreme Court immunity hearing in Washington, prompting Trump to call the proceeding a "scam," a "political witch hunt" and "election interference."[360]

In the same period Trump ended his boycott of [[anheuser-busch|Anheuser-Busch]]. As the final holdout still hammering the company in February 2024 over the [[bud-light|Bud Light]] controversy, he was called by [[ufc|UFC]] chief executive [[dana-white|Dana White]]; according to people briefed on the conversation Trump was sheepish about causing headaches, and within days suggested on social media that Anheuser-Busch deserved "a second chance," using talking points that closely echoed White's.[360] A [[new-york-times|New York Times]]/[[siena-college|Siena College]] poll released in mid-April 2024 found views of his presidency had grown more positive since he left office, with nearly half of voters saying he improved things during his term, up from the 39 percent who said the country was better off after he took office ahead of the 2020 election.[360]


## Greenland and Panama Canal Proposals (December 2024)

In a Truth Social post on Monday, December 23, 2024, President-elect Trump revived the proposal that the United States take control of [[greenland|Greenland]], declaring that "for purposes of National Security and Freedom throughout the World, the United States of America feels that the ownership and control of Greenland is an absolute necessity."[362] The statement accompanied his nomination of PayPal cofounder [[ken-howery|Ken Howery]] as U.S. Ambassador to [[denmark|Denmark]], the NATO ally of which Greenland is an autonomous territory.[362] Earlier the same day Trump attacked the fees charged for use of the [[panama-canal|Panama Canal]], writing that "the fees being charged by Panama are ridiculous, especially knowing the extraordinary generosity that has been bestowed to Panama by the U.S.," calling it a complete "rip-off" and warning that if Panama failed to observe "moral and legal principles" the United States would demand full control of the canal, adding: "To the Officials of Panama, please be guided accordingly!"[362] Badlands analyst Burning Bright read the posts as deliberate signal rather than trolling, noting that the Panama deployment tracked the same memetic and media structure as Trump's earlier Canada messaging — which, he argued, prompted near-immediate capitulation from Justin Trudeau and the apparent collapse of the government in Ottawa — and that his tariff threats against Claudia Sheinbaum preceded Mexico's largest fentanyl bust in years.[362] The effect, Burning Bright wrote, was to push global conversation toward the [[monroe-doctrine|Monroe Doctrine]] and the Panama Papers, and to raise the question of why relatively small European powers control vast distant land masses.[362]

## September 2025: Bagram, Erdoğan, gold card, and the Kirk memorial

In September 2025 Trump renewed his demand that the [[taliban|Taliban]] return [[bagram-air-base|Bagram Air Base]] to American control, saying "we're talking now to Afghanistan," declining to rule out deploying troops, and warning that if the base were not handed back, "BAD THINGS ARE GOING TO HAPPEN!!!"[363] He cited the base's proximity to China as a strategic rationale; Kabul rejected the ultimatum as a violation of the [[doha-agreement|Doha Agreement]].[363]

On 19 September he announced he would host Turkish President [[recep-tayyip-erdogan|Recep Tayyip Erdoğan]] at the [[white-house|White House]] on 25 September, writing on Truth Social that the two governments were working on trade and military deals including a large-scale [[boeing|Boeing]] purchase, a major F-16 deal, and continued [[f-35|F-35]] talks he expected to conclude positively.[363]

That same week Trump rolled out the [[trump-gold-card-visa|gold card]], a $1 million fast-track for wealthy foreigners to live and work in the United States, which Commerce Secretary [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] said would replace the EB-1 and EB-2 programs with an initial 80,000 cards.[363] He also filed a [[trump-passport-gender-marker-supreme-court-petition|Supreme Court petition]] seeking to block a lower-court ruling permitting an "X" gender designation on U.S. passports, and announced further [[september-2025-narcoterror-boat-strikes|lethal strikes on alleged narcotrafficking vessels]] in the [[southcom|USSOUTHCOM]] area of responsibility.[363]

Suggestions by Trump and [[federal-communications-commission|FCC]] Chairman [[brendan-carr|Brendan Carr]] that broadcaster licenses could be revoked over disfavored on-air views were reported as potentially conflicting with [[national-rifle-association-of-america-v-vullo|NRA v. Vullo]].[363] With federal funding due to expire on 30 September, Senate Democratic Leader [[chuck-schumer|Chuck Schumer]] publicly urged Trump to negotiate a bipartisan bill to avert the [[october-2025-government-shutdown|shutdown]].[363] On 21 September Trump closed the [[charlie-kirk-memorial|memorial]] for [[charlie-kirk|Charlie Kirk]] with a eulogy calling him a "martyr" and declaring that "the gun was pointed at him, but the bullet was aimed at all of us," vowing that his Justice Department would find those who fund and perpetrate political violence; at the same event he publicly reconciled with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]].[363]

## 2024 debate and campaign narrative (September 2024)

In the [[2024-trump-harris-presidential-debate|first Trump–Harris presidential debate]] on September 10, 2024, Trump went through what [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] called "the greatest hits of the 2024 campaign, which mirror the greatest hits of 2020 & 2016."[366] Badlands argued this does not win voters with Trump Derangement Syndrome but presents the one thing the Establishment cannot: consistency of presence, of vision, and of principles.[366] Trump, the analysis held, has been telling the same story since his ascension in the American Zeitgeist, even before he came down the golden escalator, and Americans know what he is fighting for.[366] On immigration, Trump was contrasted with [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]]'s "earned pathway to citizenship" by his claim that he will enact a large deportation initiative to expel illegal migrants from the United States.[366]

Badlands also predicted that Trump would allow the Media Industrial Complex to promote a Russia–China–Iran "New Axis" framing for narrative reasons before codifying peace and trade deals it described as already done.[366] Separately, Trump had publicly thanked [[brittany-mahomes|Brittany Mahomes]] for her support, a fact [[variety|Variety]] cited in its column pressuring [[taylor-swift|Taylor Swift]] to endorse Harris.[366]

## Butler shooting and the Apollo Affair

In its coverage of [[arnon-milchan|Arnon Milchan]] and Israeli nuclear procurement, Badlands noted as narrative synchronicity that the stage in Butler, Pennsylvania where Trump was shot in the head sits less than thirty miles from the facility involved in the [[apollo-affair|Apollo Affair]].[366]

## Government funding fight (September 2024)

A Trump-backed plan to avert a partial government shutdown and crack down on election security cleared the House Rules Committee 9–4 on Monday, September 9, 2024, combining a six-month continuing resolution at fiscal year 2024 funding levels with a House GOP bill mandating proof of citizenship in voter registration; it advanced toward a chamber-wide vote amid Republican defections.[366]

## May 2024 hush money trial cross-examination

During [[donald-trump|Trump]]'s Manhattan "hush money" trial, defense attorney [[todd-blanche|Todd Blanche]] destroyed the credibility of star prosecution witness [[michael-cohen|Michael Cohen]] on May 16, 2024, confronting him with text messages showing that a pivotal October 2016 phone call — used by prosecutors to tie Trump to reimbursements for the payment to [[stormy-daniels|Stormy Daniels]] — was actually with Trump's bodyguard [[keith-schiller|Keith Schiller]].[367] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued the wider lawfare campaign should be read as Trump and his team baiting the charges and the presiding judges, and above all baiting the media into "one last gasp" of predictions of his downfall.[367] Burning Bright separately contended that the multipolarity, anti-hegemony and anti-sanctions themes advanced jointly by [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] at their May 2024 Beijing summit aligned closely with Trump's own positions, making future peace and trade deals easier for him to sell.[367]

## November 2023 Stack Data Strategy electoral college survey

A [[stack-data-strategy|Stack Data Strategy]] analysis of 15,205 registered voters, conducted October 13 to November 3, 2023 and released through [[politico|Politico]] on November 13, found that Trump would win 292 electoral votes to [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s 246 in a hypothetical general election rematch, returning him to the White House even as Biden narrowly won the popular vote 49 percent to 48 percent.[370] The same survey showed Trump defeating Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] and California Governor [[gavin-newsom|Gavin Newsom]] in hypothetical matchups, while Biden decisively beat Florida Governor [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]].[370]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] said he paid little attention to polls but found this one worth noting for internal media consistency, agreeing that Trump would trounce Biden while conceding that Biden would likely beat DeSantis, and arguing that "Con Inc." cared only about promoting DeSantis and starting a new forever war rather than America First priorities.[370] Separately, an [[american-thinker|American Thinker]] item promoted a speculative [[trump-tucker-ticket|Trump-Tucker ticket]] pairing Trump with [[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]] for 2024.[370]

## Epstein files bill, Saudi diplomacy, and Mamdani meeting (November 2025)

On Wednesday, November 19, 2025, President Trump signed legislation ordering the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] to release documents from its long-running investigation into sex offender [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]], files sought by both his political opponents and members of his own base who had pressed for greater transparency.[372] The material could shed more light on Epstein's activities; Epstein socialized with Trump and other notable figures before his 2008 conviction for soliciting a minor for prostitution.[372] Reuters described the scandal as a thorn in Trump's side for months, partly because he had amplified conspiracy theories about Epstein to his own supporters, many of whom believe his administration covered up Epstein's ties to powerful figures.[372]

The same week Trump hosted Saudi Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] for a black-tie White House dinner and an Oval Office meeting, followed by the [[saudi-us-investment-forum|US-Saudi Investment Forum]] at the Kennedy Center, where Trump said $270 billion of Saudi investment had already been negotiated and locked in as the first portion of a promised $1 trillion.[372] At the forum Trump said the crown prince had asked him to help end the war in [[sudan|Sudan]]: "His majesty would like me to do something very powerful having to do with Sudan... it was not on my charts to be involved in... But I just see how important that is to you... And we're going to start working on Sudan."[372] He later condemned the "tremendous atrocities" in Sudan's civil war and said he would work with Saudi Arabia, the United Arab Emirates and Egypt to end it.[372]

Trump also announced on Truth Social that he had agreed to meet New York City Mayor-elect [[zohran-mamdani|Zohran Mamdani]] in the Oval Office on Friday, November 21, writing: "Communist Mayor of New York City, Zohran 'Kwame' Mamdani, has asked for a meeting."[372] Trump had urged New York voters to back Mamdani's opponent, former Governor Andrew Cuomo, and had repeatedly attacked Mamdani in public statements.[372] Separately, Rep. [[eugene-vindman|Eugene Vindman]] demanded Trump release the transcript of a call with the Saudi crown prince held after the killing of [[jamal-khashoggi|Jamal Khashoggi]].[372]

## May 2024 battleground state polling

A set of polls released on 13 May 2024 by the [[new-york-times|New York Times]], [[siena-college|Siena College]] and the [[philadelphia-inquirer|Philadelphia Inquirer]] found Trump leading President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] in five of six critical battleground states — Michigan, Arizona, Nevada, Georgia and Pennsylvania — with Biden barely clinging to Wisconsin.[374] The top grievances among disaffected voters were the cost of living, immigration and the war in Gaza, amid what the Times described as widespread dissatisfaction with the state of the country and serious doubts about Biden's ability to deliver major improvements to American life.[374]

Nearly 70 percent of voters said the country's political and economic systems needed major changes or to be torn down entirely, and only 13 percent of Biden's own supporters believed he would bring major change in a second term, while even many voters who disliked Trump acknowledged he would shake up an unsatisfying status quo.[374] More than half of voters still described the economy as "poor," down only a single percentage point since November 2023 despite cooling inflation, an end to rate hikes and significant stock market gains.[374]

[[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] commentator [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] highlighted the near-unspoken fear among CNN commentators discussing the results, arguing that the Rust Belt minority counties Biden campaigned on in 2020 were sharply turning toward Trump, and predicting that the administration and allied media might pivot to narrative attacks on American minorities and the working class.[374]

## Alleged dismantling of the CIA proxy network (Badlands analysis)

In a May 2024 Badlands feature, contributor Joe Lange argues that Donald Trump, together with the military, has been systematically dismantling a worldwide network of proxy states controlled by the [[central-intelligence-agency|CIA]] and shadow government, following an order Lange derives from [[qanon|Q]] drop 1245: [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]], then [[north-korea|North Korea]], then [[armenia|Armenia]], then [[iran|Iran]].[382] The 2017 arrest of [[alwaleed-bin-talal|Prince Alwaleed]] and hundreds of Saudi allies is presented as the removal of the most powerful puppet master and the severing of his funding to subordinate puppets.[382] Lange credits Trump with freeing [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong Un]] from CIA captivity, with installing [[mike-pompeo|Mike Pompeo]] at the CIA to locate and shut down the agency's [[snow-white-supercomputers|Snow White supercomputers]], with removing CIA control of Armenia — an achievement he says went completely under the radar and for which Trump received no credit — and with driving coordinated European raids against the [[armenian-mafia|Armenian mafia]] near the end of his first term.[382] He also cites the killing of Qasem Soleimani and the cancellation of the Iran nuclear deal as measures that weakened Iran and reduced Iranian control of the Middle East.[382]

## Fed–Treasury merger and the BlackRock appointment (Carlson commentary)

In his April 2023 essay "Get Woke, Go Broke," [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] revisited the claim that while Trump was president he merged the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] with the [[us-department-of-the-treasury|US Treasury]], understood in that community as dissolving the corrupt Fed into the Treasury.[383] What Carlson found confusing was that Trump appointed [[blackrock|BlackRock]] and [[larry-fink|Larry Fink]] — in his view unambiguous members of the elite club — to administer the merger, raising the possibility that Trump was forcing the deep state to buy up soon-to-be-worthless government assets and thereby destroy itself.[383] Carlson separately described Trump as, in his opinion, the greatest president who has ever lived.[383]

## Heroes of the Great Awakening

The February 2023 Badlands Media essay "Heroes of the Great Awakening" casts Trump as the contemporary successor to the Kennedys, arguing that the same power structure that killed [[john-f-kennedy|John F. Kennedy]] and [[robert-f-kennedy|Robert F. Kennedy]] is "battling Trump, his family and his allies now."[385] The author notes that this structure goes by several names — "the 'Establishment', the 'Cabal' or the '[[deep-state|Deep State]]'" — and that whatever it is called, it does "their best—and worst—work in the shadows."[385]

The essay also emphasises Trump's personal connection to [[jfk-jr|JFK Jr.]], stating that according to Trump himself the two men were friends, and citing a 1996 remark in which Kennedy Jr. appeared to predict a Trump presidency two decades in advance.[385] Trump is thus positioned as an ally in what the article calls the effort "to counter this 'repugnant' and secretive cabal."[385] At the same time the author cautions against personality-driven expectations, writing that "there are charismatic figures that come and go" but that "it was not one man who beat fascism in the 1940's, and it will not be one man in the current epoch either."[385]

## The feud with John McCain

Badlands contributor Joe Lange argues that Trump deliberately picked public fights with specific individuals in order to expose the lies of the media, which had promoted those people as patriots, and that the largest of those fights was the [[trump-mccain-feud|feud with John McCain]].[386] It began on June 30, 2015, two weeks after Trump's candidacy announcement, when [[john-mccain|McCain]] criticised his remarks about Mexican immigrants; Trump answered at a Phoenix rally on July 11, 2015, calling McCain an "incompetent politician" and "very weak on immigration," and on July 18, 2015 said, "He's not a war hero. He was a war hero because he was captured. I like people who weren't captured."[386] Contrary to universal expectation, the remark did not destroy his campaign, which the article attributes to widespread public awareness of governmental corruption and to Trump's role as the champion of voters who had lacked the power to change it.[386] On March 19, 2019, months after McCain's death, Trump said, "I was never a fan of John McCain and I never will be."[386]

The article ties the feud to Trump's border agenda: securing the southern border was one of his administration's most important objectives from the beginning, and McCain was his biggest obstacle to getting [[us-congress|Congress]] to fund the wall, proposing competing legislation in the form of a revived [[gang-of-eight-immigration-bill|Gang of Eight]] amnesty bill while the White House backed the [[raise-act|RAISE Act]].[386] Press secretary Sean Spicer confirmed in February 2017 that Trump opposed the Gang of Eight bill as amnesty.[386] Trump's pressure for repeal of the [[affordable-care-act|Affordable Care Act]] was likewise defeated by McCain's decisive [[skinny-repeal-vote|thumbs-down vote]].[386]

## "Wartime president" and irregular warfare

Badlands contributor Joe Lange argues in "[[irregular-warfare|Irregular Warfare]]" that Trump has been a "wartime president" since he was first sworn in in 2017, waging a covert campaign with military intelligence against an embedded insurgency directed by foreign patrons — chief among them, in Lange's account, [[alwaleed-bin-talal|Prince Alwaleed bin Talal]].[388] Because the adversary is described as infiltrating institutions rather than invading, the conflict is characterized as irregular warfare, defined in U.S. joint doctrine as a violent struggle among state and non-state actors for legitimacy and influence over relevant populations.[388]

Lange frames Trump's sustained attacks on the press as "Fake News" and the "enemy of the people" as a deliberate, pre-planned strategy to contest the narrative battlefield, credited with driving public trust in media to record lows.[388] He argues Trump deliberately allowed the enemy to "walk through the front door" — welcoming the impeachment attempt and the appointment of a Special Counsel — while [[jeff-sessions|Jeff Sessions]] quietly launched RICO investigations in the background.[388] In this reading Trump nominated [[rod-rosenstein|Rod Rosenstein]] as Deputy Attorney General knowing his Clinton entanglements, because a compromised deputy could be leveraged and would give the enemy a false sense of security, and he confronted [[robert-mueller|Robert Mueller]] with evidence of his crimes at a May 2017 meeting held under the pretext of an FBI director interview.[388]

The article also asserts that Trump's repeated declarations of national emergency, combined with the [[war-powers-act|War Powers Act]], let him deploy the [[united-states-marine-corps|Marines]] without congressional approval or a governor's consent — the mechanism said to explain military construction of the border wall and the decline of street violence.[388] These claims are the author's interpretation rather than documented fact.

## Covfefe and cryptographic communication

On 31 May 2017 Trump posted a Tweet containing the word "covfefe," deleted it, and then six hours and three minutes later publicly asked who could figure out its true meaning.[389] More than two years afterwards he challenged the characterization of the post as a "mistweet," and he and others have continued to suggest the word carries a deeper significance.[389] Badlands Media commentary treats the episode as a deliberate cryptographic act rather than a typographical error, and applies the [[a1z26-cipher|A1Z26 cipher]] to [[covfefe|Covfefe]] to derive the number sequence 3, 15, 22, 6, 5, 6, 5.[389] Separately, the signature "4, 10, 20" first used in Q drop #35 decodes under the same cipher to the initials D, J, T — Donald John Trump.[389]

## "On Infiltration and Israel" (2025)

The May 6, 2025 Badlands essay "On Infiltration and Israel" by [[american-hypnotist|American Hypnotist]] was written expressly to push back against the popular narrative that Trump is a puppet of [[israel|Israel]].[393] The author argued that it is a mistake to take Trump at face value, and expressed surprise that so many anons are still fooled by surface-level appearances.[393]

On the essay's account Trump uses the propaganda directed against him to his advantage, sometimes by leaning into it rather than openly defying it: by appearing friendly to Israel he can maneuver the Israeli state into positions unavailable to a declared enemy, since declaring open enmity forfeits all negotiating leverage.[393] The author cited the Israeli spy [[jonathan-pollard|Jonathan Pollard]], who he said regards Trump as anything but a friend to Israel because everything Trump does to benefit it comes at a cost Israel sorely wishes not to pay.[393] He also pointed to the [[jfk-files-release|JFK files release]], in which the portions implicating Israel in crimes against the United States were left unredacted, as something no other administration would have permitted.[393] A further consideration offered was that after a decade of mainstream media portraying Trump as a Nazi, his public support for Israel makes his critics look like nutjobs.[393]

The essay also defended Trump's executive order of January 29, 2025 on combating anti-Semitism, which affirms and strengthens [[executive-order-13899|Executive Order 13899]] of December 11, 2019.[393] Having read the order, the author rejected claims that it strips Americans of free speech, noting that EO 13899 explicitly reaffirms the primacy of the First Amendment and that the 2025 order is to be implemented consistent with applicable law and subject to the availability of appropriations.[393] His reading was that the true target is the criminal and terror cells behind the campus anti-Israel protests that erupted after October 7, 2023, and that Trump is using the cabal's own [[antisemitism|antisemitism]] weapon against groups operating through the university system.[393]

## Signals to the anon movement (October 2022)

In an October 21, 2022 Badlands Media essay, contributor [[pepe-lives-matter|Pepe Lives Matter]] argued that Trump had recently affirmed supporters who take [[qanon|Q]] seriously, playing at rallies a song he knew would be linked to the anon movement and doing so on purpose.[394] The writer noted that less than a month earlier Trump had re-posted on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] the assurance that nothing can stop what is coming, and asked readers whether they took him seriously given that he possessed more information than they did.[394] The essay also observed that Trump and Q had highlighted the same video repeatedly, treating the repetition as intentional.[394]

## CPAC 2023: "Call to Arms"

At the [[conservative-political-action-conference|CPAC]] gathering of March 4, 2023, Trump delivered an address that Badlands contributor [[brad-getz|Brad Getz]] called "one of his most raw speeches to date" and read as the opening of a new phase of the [[great-awakening|Great Awakening]].[396] In the [[2023-trump-cpac-speech|speech]] Trump repudiated the pre-2016 Republican leadership — "We had a Republican Party that was ruled by Freaks, Neocons, Globalists, Open Border Zealots, and Fools... But we are never going back to the party of Paul Ryan, Karl Rove, and Jeb Bush" — and rejected "unlimited money to fight foreign wars, that are endless wars, that are stupid wars."[396] He told the audience that a second term would be more effective because of what he had learned in office: "I now know the good ones, the bad ones, the weak ones, the strong ones—I know em all!"[396] Trump also stressed his financial independence from donors, saying that his enemies "cannot stand that they do not own me" and could therefore never control the voters through him, and closed with the formulation "I am your warrior, I am your justice, and for those who have been wronged and betrayed, I am your retribution," together with the pledge "I will totally obliterate the [[deep-state|Deep State]]."[396]


## "Profiting From War"

Joe Lange's "Profiting From War" places Trump at the centre of an alleged campaign of [[money-flow-disruption|money flow disruption]] against entrenched financial networks.[398] The article argues that the [[bush-family|Bush family]] feared him because a sitting president holds the constitutional power to declassify evidence held in presidential libraries under classification markings, and that this fear explains [[george-w-bush|George W. Bush]]'s October 2017 speech at the [[george-w-bush-institute|George W. Bush Institute]] — described by the [[los-angeles-times|Los Angeles Times]] as an extraordinary if veiled attack suggesting Trump had promoted bigotry and falsehoods.[398] Hours before the speech, Trump had suggested via Twitter that the FBI, Russian officials and the Democratic Party colluded to create a dossier about him during the 2016 race, a claim the newspaper then called an unfounded theory; Lange argues it has since been vindicated.[398]

On Saudi Arabia, the article recounts Trump's refusal to accept the [[central-intelligence-agency|CIA]] assessment that Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] ordered the killing of [[jamal-khashoggi|Jamal Khashoggi]], his use of an emergency declaration to conclude an $8.1 billion arms deal with Saudi Arabia, the United Arab Emirates and Jordan without congressional approval, and his veto of the bipartisan legislation that sought to block those sales.[398] Lange frames these decisions as Trump funding his most important ally in an unconventional war to end CIA control of the Middle East and bring peace to the region.[398] The article also credits the administration from its outset with cutting off slush funds and money streams, including the settlement payout program terminated by Attorney General [[jeff-sessions|Jeff Sessions]].[398]

## Q drops #15–22 in Q for Dummies

Chapter 3 of [[q-for-dummies|Q for Dummies]] reads [[qanon|Q]] drops #15 through #22 as a sustained set of assurances about Trump's position in late 2017.[408] Drop #15 is read as establishing that his election disrupted a [[16-year-plan-to-destroy-america|sixteen-year plan]] premised on [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] winning, the [[deep-state|Deep State]] having never expected to lose the presidency again as it had with [[john-f-kennedy|JFK]] or [[ronald-reagan|Reagan]].[408] Drop #16 states that POTUS is safe, that anyone believing he is not in control is mistaken, and that he was insulated with zero risk of removal from office — an assurance the author judges to have proved "bang-on."[408] The same drop is read as explaining that Trump's public quarrel with Attorney General [[jeff-sessions|Jeff Sessions]] was staged for optics.[408]

Later drops in the chapter cover Trump's meeting with [[robert-mueller|Robert Mueller]], the warning about surveillance delivered by Admiral [[michael-rogers|Mike Rogers]] and the associated [[white-house|White House]] renovations, and the argument that Trump's 2023 arrest and mugshot increased his standing among minority voters.[408]

## Economic Record (Badlands "Accomplishments" Series, Part 2)

Badlands Media contributor AbsoluteTruth1776 devoted the second installment of a serialized survey of Donald Trump's presidency, published April 28, 2023, to what the author called the "understated and suppressed" economic accomplishments of the Trump administration, framing them both as evidence of material governance and as steps toward dismantling the Deep State.[412] The stated purpose of the series was persuasive: to compile a citable record that could be used to "red pill" readers on Trump's first-term results and argue for his return to office ahead of 2024.[412]

The compilation credits Trump's domestic policy with a labor market that continued to expand into 2020 and exceeded forecaster expectations, the longest sustained economic recovery in U.S. history, an unemployment rate at a 50-year low, and roughly 6.4 million jobs added during his administration.[412] It further cites a record-high median household income for the middle class in 2017 as reported by the Census Bureau, the lowest Black unemployment rate since the government began tracking the series in the early 1970s, worker compensation rising to a near-ten-year high in the second quarter of 2018, and household wealth reaching a record $1.7 trillion gain in the second quarter of 2017.[412]

On trade and industry, the article highlights Trump's August 2017 authorization of an inquiry into Chinese theft of American intellectual property — his administration's first direct trade measure against Beijing — and a separate executive order directing a study of abuses of existing U.S. trade agreements, including an examination of American participation in the World Trade Organization.[412] Domestically, it credits his deregulatory executive order aimed at scrapping "outdated, unnecessary, bad for workers, or contrary to the national interest" rules with enabling job creation, and points to manufacturer optimism above 93 percent, consumer confidence at a 17-year high, and an eight-quarter Dow winning streak — the first in twenty years — as market validation.[412]

Other cited results include a 22 percent surge in U.S. housing starts in July 2020 during the COVID-19 period, America becoming the world's largest crude oil producer for the first time since 1973, a 1.2 percent reduction in the debt-to-GDP ratio in Trump's first year, Apple's pledge of a $350 billion U.S. contribution and 20,000 new jobs attributed to the new tax law, WIC welfare participation falling to a 17-year low, and Trump's role in securing the 2026 World Cup for North America.[412] The author presented these items as a deliberate contrast with the economy under Joe Biden.[412]


## Government accomplishments record (Badlands, 2023)

A March 2023 Badlands Media article by contributor AbsoluteTruth1776 catalogued twenty government-related accomplishments of the Trump presidency, written explicitly to rebut critics who claimed Trump achieved little or nothing in office.[413] The list opened with his nomination and confirmation of three justices to the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] — [[neil-gorsuch|Neil Gorsuch]], [[brett-kavanaugh|Brett Kavanaugh]], and [[amy-coney-barrett|Amy Coney Barrett]] — all three of whom the author credited with a pivotal role in the overturning of [[roe-v-wade|Roe v. Wade]].[413] It also noted that he nominated well over 170 federal judges, whose rulings the author said proved important in overturning what he called draconian COVID-era mandates and executive orders.[413]

On trade, the article credited Trump with signing the [[usmca|USMCA]] agreement to replace NAFTA, arguing it opened the job market and returned jobs to the United States.[413] On trafficking, it asserted that more human trafficking arrests and prosecutions occurred under Trump than under the previous three presidents combined, that he created a new White House position dedicated to fighting human trafficking, and that he signed legislation stripping liability protections from platforms enabling [[child-trafficking|child trafficking]], leading to the takedown of [[backpage-com|Backpage.com]].[413]

Several items concerned veterans and first responders: a law protecting disability benefits for veterans who declare bankruptcy, a bill aimed at preventing veteran suicide, and an extension of the [[september-11th-victim-compensation-fund|September 11th Victim Compensation Fund]].[413] Others targeted the pharmaceutical industry, including a bill lowering prescription drug costs, moves to lower the cost of insulin and increase Medicare benefits, and a law lifting the gag order preventing pharmacists from telling patients how to save money on prescriptions.[413] The article also cited an opioid bill promoting research into non-addictive pain management, the [[elie-wiesel-genocide-and-atrocities-prevention-act|Elie Wiesel Genocide and Atrocities Prevention Act]], an executive order keeping the [[guantanamo-bay|Guantanamo Bay]] military prison open, legislation creating the [[martin-luther-king-jr-national-historical-park|Martin Luther King Jr. National Historical Park]], a bill expanding hate crime protections to religious institutions, an executive order penalizing violence against police and targeting drug cartels, a measure reducing federal funding for abortions, the [[jfk-files-release|declassification of the JFK files]], and the creation of the [[united-states-space-force|United States Space Force]].[413]

The author summarized Trump as "perhaps the most accomplished President of the past 40 years," pointing to his actions against Big Pharma, his support for veterans and law enforcement, his expansion of job markets, and his record of avoiding new wars, including his 2019 meeting with [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong Un]] at the Korean DMZ.[413] The piece was billed as Part 1 of a series, with subsequent parts to cover economic accomplishments and national security.[413]


## Ehret's warning on the British trap

Badlands contributor Matthew Ehret writes that Trump indicated openness to [[king-charles-iii|King Charles III]]' offer for the United States to rejoin the British Commonwealth as an "associate member," saying "I Love King Charles. Sounds good to me!"[422] Ehret asks whether Trump understands that the trap being set involves not turning Canada into a republic or annexing it, but the risk of the USA itself falling back into the British Empire, and whether he knows of the [[club-of-rome|Club of Rome]]'s 1973 "Regionalized and Adaptive Model of the Global World System" program dividing the world into "Ten Kingdoms."[422]

Ehret calls Trump's endorsement of [[mark-carney|Mark Carney]]'s Liberals in the 2025 Canadian election questionable, and faults his tendency to treat the British Crown as something separate from the British deep state that worked to sabotage his first term.[422] He also criticizes Trump's trust in transhumanists such as [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]], [[peter-thiel|Peter Thiel]], Alex Karp and [[larry-ellison|Larry Ellison]].[422] The so-called [[golden-dome|"Golden Dome"]] missile shield, supported by both Carney and Trump, is presented as envisioning integrated military control across North America.[422] While allowing that Trump may have a plan behind the scenes, Ehret argues that ignoring these facts out of blind faith in "The Plan" is not constructive.[422]

## Role in the January 2025 Gaza ceasefire

Badlands Media credited [[donald-trump|Trump]] with securing the January 2025 [[gaza-ceasefire|Gaza ceasefire]] before he had even been sworn in for his second term, accomplishing what [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] "couldn't—or wouldn't" over 15 months of war.[444] Rather than dispatching a career diplomat, Trump sent his longtime friend, the New York real estate billionaire [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]], as Middle East envoy — a choice the article read as a signal that Trump did not trust standard State Department procedure to produce results.[444]

Trump had insisted publicly that the fighting in Gaza, as well as in Syria and Lebanon, end before he assumed office so he could focus on domestic concerns and the populist base that elected him.[444] A week before the deal he warned that "all hell will break out in the Middle East" if the hostages were not released by his January 20 inauguration, declining to specify what he meant beyond saying it would not be good for [[hamas|Hamas]] or "frankly, for anyone."[444] The Wall Street Journal reported both sides had been galvanized by his imminent return to office.[444]

The article noted Trump's stated ambition to broker wider agreements involving Saudi Arabia, [[qatar|Qatar]], the Emirates and Israel as a revival of the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]], which required an end to the bombardment of Gaza first.[444] It speculated that Trump may have offered [[benjamin-netanyahu|Netanyahu]] concessions such as West Bank annexation in exchange, and concluded that if Trump were to secure a lasting Middle East peace it would cement his legacy and reshape future American foreign policy.[444] Even Trump's own description of the outcome was characteristically superlative — that he did the deal, that it was a great deal, and that nobody had ever seen anything like it.[444]

Trump's success also produced friction on his own side: pro-Israel Republicans in Congress objected to the terms, and Israeli hardliners including [[itamar-ben-gvir|Itamar Ben-Gvir]] and commentator [[erel-segal|Erel Segal]] blamed him directly for the deal.[444]

Trump Doctrine: Peace Through Space

The February 2024 Badlands article "[[trump-doctrine-peace-through-space|Trump Doctrine: Peace Through Space]]" argues that Trump's greatest and most overlooked accomplishment was a concealed reorientation of American power toward non-kinetic warfare.[445] Under the policy of [[peace-through-strength|Peace Through Strength]] he poured more than $2.2 trillion into the US military, announced the [[united-states-space-force|Space Force]] as the sixth branch on June 18, 2018 (signed into law December 21, 2019), and elevated [[us-cyber-command|Cyber Command]] to a major warfighting command.[445] The article contends that because Department of Defense finances are effectively unauditable, those funds could have been directed to building out [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]]'s launch capability out of public view.[445] It further asserts that Trump knew in advance, partly through pandemic tabletop exercises such as [[clade-x|Clade X]] and [[event-201|Event 201]], what would occur during COVID, and that by insisting responsibility rested with state and local officials he allowed a $14.1 trillion expansion of M1 that devalued the dollar and disadvantaged rival space programs.[445] Quiet monetary moves are cited as part of the same pattern: on May 21, 2020 Trump and Treasury Secretary [[steven-mnuchin|Steve Mnuchin]] appointed Bitcoin maximalist [[brian-brooks|Brian Brooks]] to head the OCC, and within two months banks were permitted to custody bitcoin.[445]

## Fulton County booking (August 2023)
Trump surrendered at the [[fulton-county-jail|Fulton County Jail]] in Atlanta on August 24, 2023, following his indictment on racketeering and conspiracy charges brought by District Attorney [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]], and was released on a $200,000 bond roughly twenty minutes after handing himself over at about 7:36 p.m.[343] During processing he had his fingerprints and mugshot taken, producing the first mugshot of a former American president.[343] Trump, then 77, called the booking a "terrible experience," told Newsmax he had "never heard the words mug shot" before because "they didn't teach me that at the Wharton School of Finance," and said the accumulating prosecutions were an attempt to "wear you out."[343] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] of [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] argued the resulting image had become "the most memetic image in American history" and that Trump's confrontation with the establishment was not a distraction from his 2024 campaign but was the campaign itself.[343] See [[2023-trump-fulton-county-booking|2023 Trump Fulton County booking]].[343]

## Super Tuesday 2024
On [[2024-super-tuesday|Super Tuesday]], March 5, 2024, Trump romped through the opening contests, piling up wins in states including Texas, the second-largest delegate prize of the night, moving inexorably toward the Republican nomination and a November rematch with [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]].[346] His only defeat came in Vermont, which [[nikki-haley|Nikki Haley]] secured according to the Associated Press, denying him a clean sweep; he nevertheless won eleven other states by 11 p.m. Eastern time, when polls closed in California.[346] Speaking at Mar-a-Lago, his Palm Beach home, Trump made no mention of Haley and instead called for unity: "We want to have unity, and we're going to have unity, and it's going to happen very quickly."[346]
Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued the night was never about formally codifying Trump's nomination but about demonstrating that he — and the movement behind him — had never left, and about the existential threat that movement posed to what he calls the System of Systems.[346] In that reading Trump functions as a symbol of what has been taken from the public and of what would be reclaimed politically, administratively and culturally.[346]

## Late December 2024 developments
### Statement on Jimmy Carter's death
Following the death of [[jimmy-carter|Jimmy Carter]] at age 100 on December 29, 2024 — 22 days before the inauguration — Democrats noted that the 30-day half-mast flag protocol for a deceased former president would overshadow the ceremony, with critics calling it Carter's parting gift after their longstanding feud.[348] Trump's statement said "the challenges Jimmy faced as President came at a pivotal time for our country and he did everything in his power to improve the lives of all Americans. For that, we all owe him a debt of gratitude," and that while he "strongly disagreed with him philosophically and politically," Carter "truly loved and respected our Country" and "was a truly good man," adding that Carter "was also very consequential, far more than most Presidents, after he left the Oval Office."[348] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read these remarks as deliberate signal about the constraints on sovereign leadership rather than routine platitude.[348]
### Endorsement of Mike Johnson
Days before the January 3 Speaker vote, Trump gave Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] his "complete and total endorsement," writing on Truth Social: "Speaker Mike Johnson is a good, hard working, religious man. He will do the right thing, and we will continue to WIN. Mike has my Complete & Total Endorsement. MAGA!"[348] The endorsement was seen as potentially decisive given Johnson could afford only a handful of GOP defections, and followed Rep. [[mike-lawler|Mike Lawler]]'s argument that no [[house-freedom-caucus|Freedom Caucus]] member could reach 218 votes.[348]
### Transition social media directive
On December 29, incoming chief of staff [[susie-wiles|Susie Wiles]] ordered all intended nominees to refrain from public social media posts without prior approval of the incoming White House counsel, reiterating that "no member of the incoming administration or Transition speaks for the United States or the President-elect himself."[348] Commentators read the timing as a signal that Trump did not share [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]]'s enthusiasm for the [[h-1b-visa-program|H-1B visa program]], amid Musk's clash with Trump supporters over immigration.[348]
### E. Jean Carroll appeal
On December 30, 2024, a unanimous three-judge panel of the 2nd U.S. Circuit Court of Appeals upheld the $5-million verdict [[e-jean-carroll|E. Jean Carroll]] had won against Trump, rejecting his argument that jurors should not have heard evidence of alleged past sexual misconduct.[348] The court said that evidence, including the "Access Hollywood" video, established a "repeated, idiosyncratic pattern of conduct" consistent with Carroll's allegations, and found no error affecting Trump's substantial rights.[348]

## 2024 rematch, Musk overture, J6 pardon vow and RNC takeover (March 2024)
With both [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] and Trump securing their parties' nominations in March 2024, the United States set up its first presidential rematch since 1956, when Dwight D. Eisenhower again defeated Adlai Stevenson; Grover Cleveland, the 22nd and 24th president, had won non-consecutive elections in 1884 and 1892.[350] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued the "official" rematch solidified encouraging plot points in the War of Stories — Americans love rematches and comebacks, and voters now had three-plus years of the Biden administration against which to compare Trump's record — while dismissing the primaries and the coming election as largely stage-managed and urging maximum turnout to make any repeat of 2020 absurd on its face.[350]
The Washington Post reported that in the summer of 2023 Trump asked [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] whether he would be interested in buying [[truth-social|Truth Social]], an overture that did not lead to a deal but showed the two men had communicated more than was previously known.[350] Their contacts also included a Palm Beach meeting earlier in March 2024 attended by high-powered Republican donors, after which Musk wrote on X that he was "not donating money to either candidate for US President."[350] At the time of the earlier discussion, Trump's media company was trapped in a long-delayed merger process.[350] [[simon-esler|Simon Esler]] treated anything emerging from Trump–Musk private meetings as intentional narrative seeding, potentially designed both to steer public awareness and to draw fire from Deep State operations.[350]
In a March 11, 2024 Truth Social statement, Trump vowed that his first acts as president would be to "Close the Border, DRILL, BABY, DRILL, and Free the January 6 Hostages being wrongfully imprisoned!"[350] He had said at a 2022 Texas rally that he would consider pardons for those convicted over the January 6 Capitol breach, and told a CNN town hall at Saint Anselm College that he was inclined to pardon a "large portion" of those charged, while stressing he would not pardon all of them because "a couple of them, probably they got out of control."[350]
Trump's campaign also took operational control of the [[republican-national-committee|Republican National Committee]] on March 11, 2024, triggering a staff purge of more than 60 officials after [[michael-whatley|Michael Whatley]] and [[lara-trump|Lara Trump]] were elected chair and co-chair and [[chris-lacivita|Chris LaCivita]] was named chief of staff.[350]

## Iran diplomacy and strike deliberations (June 2025)
In mid-June 2025, with Israel's [[operation-rising-lion|Operation Rising Lion]] underway against Iran, Trump told reporters at the White House that "really, nothing is too late" regarding a resumption of US-Iranian talks, and said the Iranians were in contact with the Americans and "even suggested they come to the White House" for negotiations.[356] He declined to say when talks might resume or how long the Israel-Iran hostilities would last, asserting that the Iranians were "totally defenseless," had "no air defense whatsoever," and that "we have totally captured the air" over Iran.[356] Asked whether the United States was nearing a decision to strike, he said: "I may do it. I may not do it. I mean, nobody knows what I'm going to do. I can tell you this: Iran's got a lot of trouble, and they want to negotiate."[356]
The [[wall-street-journal|Wall Street Journal]], citing people familiar with the deliberations, reported that Trump had told senior aides late on Tuesday, June 17 that he approved of attack plans for Iran but was holding off to see whether Tehran would abandon its nuclear program, with the deeply buried [[fordow-fuel-enrichment-plant|Fordow]] enrichment facility named as a possible US target.[356] He repeated his insistence on Iran's unconditional surrender, saying "the next week is going to be very big, maybe less than a week," while Supreme Leader [[ali-khamenei|Ali Khamenei]] said Iran would not surrender and warned that US military intervention would bring irreparable consequences.[356] The [[pentagon|Pentagon]] described the accompanying American build-up — a third destroyer in the eastern Mediterranean and a second carrier strike group heading toward the Arabian Sea — as purely defensive.[356]
Badlands commentary from [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] noted that Trump had raised the prospect of an Iranian delegation at the White House more than once and had added that he "may do that," predicting such a meeting would occur with Israel excluded — to the fury of figures such as [[mark-levin|Mark Levin]] and [[ted-cruz|Ted Cruz]].[356] Other commentary held that Trump had told the public he wants peace with Iran and that the resulting pressure was forcing Americans to choose between war and peace, while [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] accused the Wall Street Journal of publishing anonymously sourced stories designed to drag the United States into the war.[356]
## Trump administration actions on speech and agencies (June 2025)
Trump's April 2025 executive order directing agencies to eliminate duplicative and unnecessary functions covered the State Department's [[foreign-information-manipulation-and-interference-office|Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference Office]], and Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] subsequently announced the office's closure; a June 13, 2025 injunction from U.S. District Judge [[susan-illston|Susan Illston]] temporarily blocked the shutdown.[356]
