## January 2024 momentum after Iowa
Following his overwhelming win in the January 2024 Iowa caucuses, Trump's dominance reshaped the New Hampshire primary calendar: both [[cnn|CNN]] and [[abc-news|ABC News]] canceled their planned Republican debates after he continued to skip every debate of the cycle and [[nikki-haley|Nikki Haley]] refused to appear without him.[361] An Atlanta Journal-Constitution poll of registered voters in Georgia — a state [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] flipped in 2020 — showed Trump leading Biden 45 percent to 37 percent, an eight-point margin, after the two had been nearly tied at 45–44 in the same survey in November 2023.[361] Similar swing-state polling in Arizona, Pennsylvania and Michigan pointed the same direction amid persistently low Biden approval ratings.[361]
[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the establishment's escalating alarm over "Trump Admin 2.0" as a major rhetorical shift that coincided with the turn of the calendar to 2024 and intensified after Iowa, while cautioning that none of it addressed underlying concerns about the U.S. election system.[361]
Trump's influence extended to legislative fights: Rep. [[jamie-raskin|Jamie Raskin]] accused House Republicans of blocking a bipartisan Senate border deal on "orders from" Trump, with Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] declaring the package dead on arrival.[361] Abroad, [[blackrock|BlackRock]] vice chairman [[philipp-hildebrand|Philipp Hildebrand]] warned from Davos that a Trump return posed a "fundamental" challenge to Europe.[361]
In litigation, [[e-jean-carroll|E. Jean Carroll]] admitted during her January 2024 damages trial that [[george-conway|George Conway]] had convinced her to sue Trump and that she had publicized her allegations partly because "the book was not selling."[361] In Maine, Superior Court Justice [[michaela-murphy|Michaela Murphy]] stayed a decision on Trump's ballot status pending the Supreme Court's ruling in the Colorado case, leaving him on the state's primary ballot.[361] Meanwhile a number of his former administration lawyers, including [[pat-cipollone|Pat Cipollone]], joined [[bill-barr|Bill Barr]]'s [[torridon-group|Torridon Group]].[361]
362. Badlands Brief — "Badlands News Brief: Biden Commutations and Mossad Confessions", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-e79

## February 2025 developments
On February 18, 2025 Trump expressed disappointment at Kyiv's displeasure over the [[2025-riyadh-us-russia-talks|Riyadh talks]] between Russian and American officials, telling reporters after signing executive orders at Mar-a-Lago that Ukrainians were "upset about not having a seat" though "they've had a seat for three years," and that "just a half-baked negotiator could have settled this years ago."[364] Badlands commentary read the remarks as throwing [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] and the Kyiv government under the bus and as branding Zelensky illegitimate.[364] Analysts separately criticized the administration's rollout of its Ukraine framework, saying it had alarmed Kyiv and European allies while pleasing Moscow.[364]
The same week the [[united-states-senate|Senate]] confirmed [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] as Trump's Commerce Secretary and advanced [[kash-patel|Kash Patel]]'s nomination to lead the [[fbi|FBI]].[364] Trump also congratulated [[centcom|CENTCOM]] and General [[michael-kurilla|Michael Kurilla]] on Truth Social for a weekend precision airstrike in Syria that killed a senior finance and logistics official of [[hurras-al-din|Hurras al-Din]], an [[al-qaeda|Al-Qaeda]] affiliate.[364]
On Gaza, Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] said he was "committed to US President Trump's plan" to relocate the Strip's roughly two million residents and rebuild it, while rejecting any governing role for [[hamas|Hamas]] or the [[palestinian-authority|Palestinian Authority]].[364] Badlands commentary asserted that Trump had given President [[mahmoud-abbas|Mahmoud Abbas]] his word that he would support him as the Palestinian leader signing the "final and most important" peace agreement with Israel, describing Abbas as a "father-like figure" in a December 2021 interview with Israeli journalist Barak Ravid, and cited *The Art of the Deal* in arguing Trump would keep that promise.[364]
Trump's 20 January 2025 executive order imposing a 90-day freeze on all US foreign aid, together with the reinstated [[global-gag-rule|Global Gag Rule]], prompted the [[international-commission-of-jurists|International Commission of Jurists]] and more than 60 civil society groups to appeal to the European Union for emergency funding.[364] Meanwhile his tariff policy dominated relations with [[india|India]]: after Prime Minister [[narendra-modi|Narendra Modi]] failed to win an exemption in Washington, Trump publicly identified India as among the worst offenders on import tariffs and vowed reciprocal tariffs.[364]
364. Badlands News Brief — "Disagreements Among Allies & Pausing Kash for Cash", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-e31
## September 2023 general election polling
A pair of polls released in mid-September 2023 — from CBS News/YouGov and Harvard Harris, the latter no longer disclosing how many Democrats were oversampled — showed Trump defeating President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] in a national matchup.[363] Broken down by party identification, 57% of independents chose Trump against Biden's 42%, while just 34% of all respondents thought Biden would be able to finish a second term if reelected; Biden also trailed Trump badly on job approval at the equivalent point in their terms.[363] The polling ran alongside [[realclearpolitics|RealClearPolitics]] commentary on the collapse of [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]]'s primary campaign, which [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read as panic in Washington over the rejection of the establishment alternative by the MAGA and America First base.[363]

## Allied preparations for a second term and the Manhattan trial (April 2024)
By April 2024, U.S. allies across five continents were taking concrete steps to prepare for a possible Trump return to power in the November elections, an outcome described as an even chance based on recent swing-state polling.[368] Germany was reported to be waging a charm offensive inside the Republican Party, Japan lining up its own Trump interlocutor, Mexican government officials talking to his camp, and Australia legislating to insulate its U.S. defense ties.[368] Governments sought to avoid a repetition of the first term's "America First" policies, which had brought trade wars, a shakeup of security alliances, an immigration crackdown and withdrawal from a global climate accord.[368]
Reporting described Mexican deliberations over a Trump-savvy foreign minister, an Australian envoy's rush to protect a submarine deal, and a German official's talks with Republican state governors.[368] Some foreign leaders contacted Trump directly despite the risk of irritating President Joe Biden: Saudi Arabia's crown prince reportedly telephoned him, while Hungary's prime minister and Poland's president met him in person in the preceding weeks.[368] Badlands commentary read these approaches as world leaders showing Trump's team more deference than the sitting administration, and forecast the dismantling of the unipolar order in favor of a [[multipolar-world|multipolar]] arrangement of regional responsibility.[368]
The same period saw Trump on trial in Manhattan, where prosecutors under [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]] advanced [[new-york-election-law-17-152|New York Law 17-152]] as a predicate offense.[368] Badlands writers framed the prosecutions as a war of attrition best understood through game theory, in which the lawfare apparatus was caught between continuing inquiries that would set unwelcome precedent and abandoning them in full view of an awakening public.[368]
368. Badlands Brief — "Badlands News Brief: Trump 2.0, FBI Enablers & Collegiate Idiots", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-f0e
## September 2025: New York Times lawsuit and the Kirk memorial
In September 2025 Trump announced that he had filed a $15 billion defamation and libel lawsuit against [[new-york-times|The New York Times]], accusing the paper of serving as a "mouthpiece" for Democrats and of decades of false reporting about him, his family, his business, and the America First movement.[369] He claimed the paper's front-page endorsement of Kamala Harris amounted to the largest illegal campaign contribution in history, likened the case to prior settlements with ABC, Disney and [[cbs|CBS]], and filed the suit in Florida, vowing to end what he called unacceptable and illegal media abuse.[369] The case is covered at [[trump-defamation-lawsuit-against-the-new-york-times|Trump defamation lawsuit against The New York Times]].[369]
That same week, [[turning-point-usa|Turning Point USA]] announced that Trump and Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] would deliver remarks at the Arizona memorial service for [[charlie-kirk|Charlie Kirk]], joining Kirk's widow [[erika-kirk|Erika Kirk]] and a slate of administration officials.[369]
369. Badlands Brief — "Badlands News Brief: Israeli Arms, Bondi's Back Peddle, & Assassins' Creeds", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-ed9
370. Badlands Brief — "Badlands News Brief: House Passes Gas & Country Refuses to Rejoice", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-f25
## Transition and early Cabinet selections (November 2024)
On November 13, 2024, Trump met President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] in the Oval Office for roughly two hours to discuss the handover of power, telling reporters beforehand that the "transition will be so smooth" after Biden welcomed him back.[371] In the same period he moved swiftly to assemble a Cabinet, naming Fox News host [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]] as Secretary of Defense, Representative [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]] as Attorney General, and former congresswoman [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] as Director of National Intelligence.[371] Trump's inner circle described the attorney general post as the most important in the administration after the presidency itself, key to plans for mass deportations, pardons for January 6 defendants, and retribution against prosecutors who had pursued him.[371]
Badlands analysis tied the personnel choices to an announced eighteen-month drive to streamline the federal bureaucracy, executed alongside the [[department-of-government-efficiency|Department of Government Efficiency]] under [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] and [[vivek-ramaswamy|Vivek Ramaswamy]], and argued that success would require agency heads willing to dismantle entrenched fiefdoms.[371] Separately, confidants of the president-elect were reported to be weighing changes to the James Brady Press Briefing Room that would give newer MAGA-aligned outlets access to daily briefings, extending a first-term pattern of restricting outlets the administration viewed as hostile and of blocking reporters from campaign events.[371] Meanwhile special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] and his team prepared to resign before Trump took office, winding down the two outstanding federal criminal cases against him.[371]
European and domestic reaction to Trump's victory over [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] was markedly subdued compared with 2016, with muted resignation replacing the street protests, #Resist hashtags and Never Trump organizing of his first win.[371]

## September 2025 news cycle
On September 2, 2025, Trump announced that [[united-states-space-command|U.S. Space Command]] would move from [[colorado|Colorado]] to [[huntsville-alabama|Huntsville, Alabama]], reversing a Biden-era decision and capping a four-year interstate fight over the headquarters.[373] He said Huntsville won because "they fought harder for it than anybody else," and justified leaving Colorado by citing its all-mail voting, which he said means "they have automatically crooked elections."[373] Badlands hosts read the move as a "harsh measure" against Colorado over the state-level prosecution of [[tina-peters|Tina Peters]], whom Trump cannot pardon.[373]
In a Daily Caller interview reported the same week, Trump said he was surprised that Israel had lost control of [[congress|Congress]], calling Israel's lobby formerly "the strongest lobby I've ever seen" and noting that "today, it doesn't have that strong a lobby."[373] On Truth Social, during a Beijing military parade marking victory over Japan in World War II, Trump accused China, Russia and [[north-korea|North Korea]] of conspiring against the United States, writing to [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]]: "Please give my warmest regards to [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong-un]], as you conspire against the United States of America."[373] Badlands commentary suggested Trump may be honest except that "the US" refers to the [[deep-state|Deep State]], citing his history of high-profile kayfabe with Kim Jong-un.[373]
Trump also made his first official public appearance in a week on September 2 after online speculation about his health over the Labor Day weekend, blaming the "so fake" media for rumors that he had died and dismissing footage of a bag thrown from an upper White House window as AI, saying the windows are sealed.[373] He has said the U.S.-brokered [[democratic-republic-of-the-congo|DR Congo]]–[[rwanda|Rwanda]] pact, including a joint security mechanism, gives Washington rights to local mineral wealth.[373]
373. Badlands Brief — "Badlands News Brief: Sinking Confidence & A Loss Of Command", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-f76
374. Badlands Brief — "Badlands News Brief: Economic Strife Prompts a Squeeze on the Establishment", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-f8a
## COVID variant fear-mongering claim and VP speculation (August 2023)
In a video posted to [[truth-social|Truth Social]] on August 30, 2023, Trump accused "left-wing lunatics" of fear-mongering about new COVID-19 variants in order to justify reintroducing lockdown and mandate policies - including drop boxes and mail-in ballots as used in 2020 - in a bid to rig the 2024 election.[375] He said his message should serve as a warning to every COVID-19 "tyrant" who wanted to "take away our freedom," adding that "the left-wing lunatics are trying very hard to bring back COVID lockdowns and mandates with all of their sudden fear-mongering about the new variants that are coming."[375] Badlands contributor [[johnny-q|Johnny Q]] wrote that lockdowns and vaccine mandates "did wonders as far as redpilling is concerned" and contrasted Trump's stance with the incumbent administration's.[375]
Days earlier, in an interview with talk show host Glenn Beck, Trump praised [[vivek-ramaswamy|Vivek Ramaswamy]] when asked whether he would consider the 38-year-old as a running mate, saying "I think he's great" and calling him "smart," "young" and possessed of "good energy."[375] The remarks came shortly after the first GOP debate, which Trump skipped while pledging to observe for potential vice presidential contenders.[375] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the praise as a dig that simultaneously showed Trump wielding control over the 2024 election cycle while being "hunted" by a weaponized DOJ, and as evidence that Ramaswamy's rise was proportional to [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]]'s fall.[375] Separately, [[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]] warned in the same period that if indictments failed to stop Trump, assassination and a hot war with Russia could follow.[375]
375. Badlands Brief - "Badlands News Brief: Trump Trolls, Tucker Trends and Ukraine Rages", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-f9f
## September 2024 debate aftermath and second-debate challenge
Following his September 10, 2024 debate with [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] in Philadelphia, several House Republicans privately told The Hill that Trump's performance was "not good," saying Harris had "knew exactly where to cut to get under his skin" by attacking the size of his rallies, the magnitude of his family fortune and world leaders "laughing" at him.[376] One lawmaker said "the road just got very narrow"; a second said "many" in the GOP conference were disappointed he could not stay on message; a third called the result "not devastating – but it's not good."[376]
Harris's campaign challenged Trump to a second debate immediately afterward.[376] In a live post-debate interview on [[fox-news|Fox News]], Trump said he would "have to think about it" and that Harris wanted a rematch "because she lost," but when [[sean-hannity|Sean Hannity]] pressed him he replied, "Maybe if it was on a fair network," adding that a Hannity-moderated debate "would be fair."[376] Fox News had proposed an October debate moderated by Martha MacCallum and Bret Baier.[376]
Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] rejected the criticism, arguing that Trump won "the fake debate" and that the debate's very occurrence was another humiliation ritual imposed on the American people, who are "not just tired … they're angry, and Trump is their totem."[376] The commentary framed the shifting mood — from the memetic shock of the Trump–Biden debate to "stretched and cracking tension" — as a feature of the [[great-awakening|Great Awakening]] rather than a bug.[376]
Trump also complicated Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]]'s government funding effort that week by insisting Republicans pass no funding bill without addressing "election security," alleging Democrats were "trying to 'stuff' voter registrations with illegal aliens."[376]
376. Badlands Brief — "Kamalaflation, Sovereign Meetings & MAGA Joe?", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/badlands-news-brief-fe3
## Framing in "BRIC by BRIC" (August 2023)
In an August 2023 essay on the [[2023-brics-johannesburg-summit|BRICS Johannesburg summit]], [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump would be remembered not as a destroyer or conqueror but as "a unifier, a builder and a deal-maker," and predicted he would return to public power amid a rebuilding international order that he had "helped to coax along from the shadows" before, during and after his first term.[377] He described Trump as the de facto leader of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] and credited him with paving the way to ending the Forever War in the Middle East.[377]
The essay treated Trump's absence from the presidency as itself a [[devolution|Devolution]] proof: had he remained Commander-in-Chief, the author reasoned, he could not have permitted Russian kinetic incursions nor the growth of a bloc challenging the dollar without contradicting his own economic rhetoric.[377] It also predicted that Trump would be transformed "very slowly, and then all at once" in the eyes of the reachable middle of American society from a bloviating show-off into a truth-teller, and pointed to his relationship with Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] and the 2017 Saudi sword dance as elements of a much larger plan.[377]
377. Badlands Brief — "BRIC by BRIC", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/bric-by-bric
## Posture on the Middle East during the 2024 campaign
In August 2024, Badlands contributor [[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] noted that Trump had made clear he believed [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] and [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] had not done enough to support [[israel|Israel]] — an accusation DeLarme called wild given that the Biden administration had given Israel essentially everything it asked for.[378] DeLarme observed that Trump, like Biden and Harris, had not addressed how he would handle the escalation following the [[assassination-of-ismail-haniyeh|assassination of Ismail Haniyeh]], leaving only a series of displays of loyalty to Israel to go on.[378]
DeLarme allowed for the possibility that Trump was playing an intricate game against Israel's own deep state, and noted that support of this kind may be an unavoidable feature of American presidential politics given the influence of [[aipac|AIPAC]].[378] Against the argument advanced by journalist [[michael-tracey|Michael Tracey]] — that Trump's affection for Israel presaged continued U.S.-supplied bombing across the Middle East — DeLarme pointed to a Trump interview with [[israel-hayom|Israel Hayom]] in which Trump urged Israel to "finish the job" rather than prolong the offensive, and described how his administration had rendered [[iran|Iran]] too broke to fund militants.[378] He further cited Trump's first term, during which Trump maintained amicable relations with most perceived adversaries, with the exceptions of [[abu-bakr-al-baghdadi|Abu Bakr al-Baghdadi]] and Qasem Soleimani, whom he removed surgically rather than through the grinding conflict preferred by the U.S. security state.[378]
378. Badlands Brief — "Cataclysmic Silence", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/cataclysmic-silence
## November 2022: "Death Blossom" and the announcement sequence
In "Death Blossom," published November 23, 2022, [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described Trump as "the closest thing we have to an anointed commander and leader of the Global — yes, the worldwide — [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] that is rising to beat back the forces of Marxist Communism," and as extremely active on the Fifth-Generation War game board in late 2022.[379] Drawing on his earlier essay "The Master," he argued that Trump's deployments are almost always Potential rather than Actual in nature: rarely kinetic even when he was public Commander-in-Chief of the most powerful military in the world, and rarely overt even in the political realm, with his strongest and most frequent moves made in the realm of the Narrative.[379]
Burning Bright defined Trump's mastery as the ability to provoke all sides into strong engagement with whatever story he chooses to tell, and observed that even allies frequently fall prey to the cleverness of these deployments — reacting in ways that look silly or embarrassing in hindsight, but that leave the game board rendered in starker detail once the emotional tide recedes.[379]
He identified three purposes served at once by Trump's November 2022 sequence — the November 7 announcement of an announcement, the November 15 declaration of his 2024 candidacy, and a November 18 deployment aimed at countering the weaponization of the [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]].[379] First, by appearing weak while in fact stronger than ever, Trump drew out the controlled opposition in [[con-inc|Con Inc.]] and the Alternative Media, who backed the first America First figure other than Trump they thought they could control.[379] Second, the eight-day advance notice put the [[media-industrial-complex|Media Industrial Complex]] on the back foot and forced the premature expenditure of anti-Trump and anti-MAGA narrative ammunition.[379] Third, Burning Bright suggested the announcements may have provoked an Actual Event on the other side of the world: the attempt to frame an errant missile strike on [[poland|Polish]] — and therefore [[nato|NATO]] — territory as the work of an aggressive and unhinged [[russia|Russia]].[379]
Taken together, he called these objectives "a veritable coup on the part of Trump and his allies," simultaneously smoking out charlatans within the movement and forcing hot factions to expose themselves on the international board.[379] While crediting Trump with getting out ahead of the attempt to drag humanity into another war, Burning Bright insisted that the decentralized public — not any single leader — holds the key to stopping such deployments, since patriots at the highest levels of the military and political structures "cannot be everywhere at once."[379]
379. Badlands Brief — "Death Blossom", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/death-blossom
## Carlson's "read between the lines" thesis (May 2023)
In a May 15, 2023 Badlands Media essay on [[energy-consolidation|energy consolidation]], contributor [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] set out an interpretive framework for Trump's public statements, writing that he does not believe half of what comes out of Trump's mouth — not from distrust, but because saying things he does not believe is necessary given how the media twists his words.[380] Carlson argued that if Trump spoke plainly, the coverage would be far worse, so audiences are forced to read between the lines.[380]
He applied the framework to several cases. Trump's mockery of electric cars was, in Carlson's reading, not opposition to EVs but ridicule aimed at the people pushing carbon-reduction policies, since noting that an EV can only go so far before charging is equally true of a gasoline car.[380] Trump's statement that "vaccines are safe" was read as a constrained utterance, paired with his insistence on individual choice, signalling to supporters that he cannot say what he actually thinks without being blamed for millions of deaths.[380] Carlson argued that a direct declaration that climate change is a hoax would trigger blanket media coverage, riots in liberal cities, and enough lost votes to cost Trump the 2024 election — hence the careful selection of what to say, how, and when.[380]
Carlson also reinterpreted Trump's elevation of [[anthony-fauci|Anthony Fauci]] during the Covid period, arguing that Trump paraded him not out of confidence but so the public could see that Fauci, the government health agencies, and Big Pharma were frauds — turning a narrative designed to destroy him politically against the [[deep-state|Deep State]].[380] He credited Trump's fast-tracking of and support for a vaccine with forcing the economy to reopen years earlier than the cabal intended, describing a possible five-year shutdown plan that collapsed as a result.[380]
On energy, Carlson credited Trump with leaving office having achieved [[american-energy-independence|American energy independence]], with gasoline at $2.38 a gallon and the national reserves refilled "to the tippy top."[380] He also suggested Trump knowingly scheduled his CNN town hall for the day after his defamation verdict, having anticipated the outcome, in order to contrast his own composure with the behaviour of his opponents.[380]
380. Badlands Brief — "Energy Consolidation", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/energy-consolidation
## Compared with George Washington (July 2024)
Following the [[attempted-assassination-of-donald-trump-2024|attempted assassination]] at Butler, Pennsylvania on July 13, 2024, Badlands contributor [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] framed Trump's survival as divine intervention and drew an explicit parallel with [[george-washington|George Washington]]'s escape at the [[battle-of-the-monongahela|Battle of the Monongahela]] of July 9, 1755 — an engagement fought about 30 miles from Butler, 269 years and four days earlier.[381] Carlson concluded that both men had been chosen and protected by God: "One was chosen to make America, and the other to make America great again."[381]
In an interview with the [[new-york-post|New York Post]] published July 14, 2024, conducted aboard his plane en route to Milwaukee for the Republican National Convention, Trump described the shooting as a "very surreal experience," said the treating doctor "never saw anything like this, he called it a miracle," and repeated, "I'm not supposed to be here, I'm supposed to be dead."[381] He wore a large loose white bandage over his right ear and his staff barred photographs.[381]
Carlson read Trump's response at the rally — rising with a raised fist and shouting "fight, fight, fight!" — as a message both to those who wanted him dead, that he refused to go away, and to Americans, that they no longer needed to hide their support and must refuse to cower.[381] He argued the incident forced a reappraisal of Trump among detractors: those who thought him a [[deep-state|Deep State]] puppet had watched him come within an inch of death; those who thought him hate-filled saw a humbled and grateful man; and those who thought him motivated by money saw a man willing to die for his country.[381] Carlson also noted that Trump's protective detail was conspicuously larger at the subsequent [[2024-republican-national-convention|Republican National Convention]], and that [[trump-media-and-technology-group|DJT]] stock had fallen after the [[2024-presidential-debate|presidential debate]] amid short selling he interpreted as evidence of foreknowledge of the attempt.[381]
381. Badlands Brief — "Donald Trump and George Washington", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/donald-trump-and-george-washington
382. Badlands Brief — "Everything Is Connected", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/everything-is-connected
383. Badlands Brief — "Get Woke, Go Broke", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/get-woke-go-broke
## "Has Trump's Whole Life Been a Sting Operation?" (Carlson, 2024)
In a June 15, 2024 Badlands feature, contributor [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] advanced the thesis that Trump's entire public life has been a decades-long covert operation against the [[deep-state|Deep State]].[384] Carlson began with Trump's uncle [[john-g-trump|John G. Trump]], the MIT physicist who analysed [[nikola-tesla|Nikola Tesla]]'s papers for the [[fbi|FBI]] in 1943, imagining the young Donald — whose middle name came from his uncle — captivated by the older man's stories.[384] He speculated that Trump learned of advanced medical procedures, cheap or free energy and powerful weapons from his uncle, and that he avoids publicising those achievements so as not to alert Big Pharma, Big Energy and the military-industrial complex to what remains undisclosed.[384]
Carlson then traced Trump's New York real estate career, noting that the mafia controlled every aspect of construction — concrete in particular — and that Trump found ways to work around mob extortion and complete projects on time and on budget.[384] He set out a timeline: Trump began building his first Atlantic City hotel and casino in 1982; [[rudy-giuliani|Rudy Giuliani]] took a demotion to become U.S. Attorney for the Southern District of New York in 1983; the casino opened in 1984; and Giuliani prosecuted the five New York mafia families under RICO in 1985 with heavy FBI involvement.[384] From this Carlson asked whether Trump, like his uncle, had been working with the Bureau.[384]
On the casino business, Carlson wrote that the [[2008-financial-crisis|2008 financial crisis]] triggered a sudden decline and that within five years Trump sought to exit the sector; his 2013 attempt to sell the Trump Plaza Hotel and Casino was blocked by [[carl-icahn|Carl Icahn]], the senior lender on its mortgage.[384] The Plaza closed in 2014, Icahn bought the deed in 2018 and demolished the property in 2021.[384]
Carlson argued that Trump had to plan his whole life carefully, because had he decided later to confront the Deep State there would have been compromising material in his past to control him; when he announced his candidacy in 2015 his opponents were shocked to find nothing usable and had to manufacture endless accusations instead.[384] He cited the 2011 purchase of the Truth Social domain — four years before Trump descended the escalator — as evidence of long-range planning against anticipated social-media bans, and compared Trump's elite-friendly public persona to Crockett and Tubbs of Miami Vice posing as drug dealers.[384]
The feature also linked Trump to [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]], asking how far the two go back, where Musk obtained his technology, and whether he was asked to buy Twitter.[384] Carlson described Trump as Deep State public enemy number one and Musk as number two since 2022, and noted reporting that the Biden administration authorised lethal force during the [[mar-a-lago-raid|Mar-a-Lago raid]].[384] On the New York hush-money case, he wrote that Trump publicly posted a signed statement from his accuser saying the affair was a hoax and was nonetheless convicted on 34 felony counts, which he read as an unmistakable political hit designed to remove the leading presidential candidate — "the equivalent of hitting your opponent in the shins with a lead pipe before an ice-skating competition."[384] Carlson closed by urging hope, arguing that Trump and his allies have been game-planning this operation for multiple decades and that the tide would turn on Election Day.[384]
384. Badlands Brief — "Has Trump's Whole Life Been a Sting Operation?", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/has-trumps-whole-life-been-a-sting

## "Reagan 2.0" and the Generation X coalition
A December 21, 2024 Badlands essay by [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] described Trump as "Reagan 2.0," writing that both he and [[ronald-reagan|Ronald Reagan]] restored a sense of pride in America and a belief that real change was possible, but that where Reagan sought to subdue the [[deep-state|Deep State]] and tried to make deals with it, Trump plans to annihilate it and has shown reluctance to repeat Reagan's mistakes.[387]
Carlson argued that Trump's 2024 victory depended on [[generation-x|Generation X]], citing exit statistics in which 42% of voters aged 18–29, 46% aged 30–44, 53% aged 45–64 and 49% aged 65 and over backed him; the 45–64 bracket was the only age group to give him a majority, and without it, Carlson wrote, Trump would not have been elected.[387] He offered conventional explanations — high earners preferring lower taxes, parents reacting against woke school policies, business leaders preferring deregulation — but argued the deeper reason is that Gen X could see through propaganda that shaped the [[baby-boomers|Boomers]], [[millennials|Millennials]] and [[generation-z|Gen Z]].[387]
The essay also noted that Trump, himself a Baby Boomer, is removing a Deep State cast drawn largely from the Boomer and Silent generations — the Obamas, Clintons, Bidens, Pelosi, Schumer and McConnell — and replacing them with appointees who are predominantly Gen X, which Carlson said "can't be a total coincidence."[387] He speculated Trump was probably ready to fight the battle decades earlier but had to wait for his supporting cast to be ready.[387]
387. Badlands Brief — "Is Generation X Saving America?", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/is-generation-x-saving-america

## Trump in the "Mirrored Quadrant" (December 2022)

The Badlands Media essay "Mirror, Mirror" (December 14, 2022) treated [[donald-trump|Donald Trump]] as one leg of a "Trinity" of sovereign leaders alongside [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]], analysed through four shared attributes the author called a Mirrored Quadrant.[390] It dated the Deep State's decision to go "ALL IN" in defence of its system to the moment "a certain Orange Man came riding down a golden escalator."[390]

On populist rise, the essay described Trump as the first American leader — perhaps the first major world leader — in decades to wage a narrative war on the globalist class, breaking from decades of subversive and administrative tradition by extolling independence, strength and idealism in a manner closely mirroring Presidents Reagan and Kennedy.[390] That rise was said to have awoken a sleeping giant in the American populace, one tickled and ultimately tricked by the Bush Jr. administration and demoralized under [[barack-obama|Obama]]; Trump did not create the preconditions of sovereignty in the American zeitgeist but tapped a well already gushing beneath the surface.[390]

On military backing, the piece noted that Trump has no public military background although he attended the New York Military Academy in the 1960s, and that he tapped the same military and nationalist base Bush Jr. had subverted.[390] It argued that Trump recalled Kennedy's cabinet appointments and civilian advisory councils by leaning heavily on his support within the US military, specifically the [[united-states-marine-corps|Marines]], and that his inauguration speech — delivered ringed by Marines — was widely read in this community as a shot across the bow to the collectivists.[390]

On the war against the oligarchy, the essay credited Trump with breaking from the tradition of leaders who wear national pride as a "subversive skin suit" en route to Forever Wars enriching the oligarchic class, instead going to war on a narrative level with the US political class and using the [[media-industrial-complex|Media Industrial Complex]] to boost his own messaging while putting corporate and political cronies on the defensive.[390] The result claimed for this campaign was the steady and complete exposure of a plurality of the American elite, as Fortune 100 companies and opposition and intra-party officials alike closed ranks against a man and a movement they considered an outsider threat to their systemic illusion.[390] Against the globalists, Trump's contribution was framed as highlighting the inherent weakness and corruption of the globalist-aligned system to a steadily waking American public.[390]

390. Badlands Brief — "Mirror, Mirror", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/mirror-mirror
391. Badlands Brief — "Money and Oil", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/money-and-oil

## "Money and Oil" (November 2022)

Joe Lange's essay "Money and Oil" casts Trump as the figure who set out to wrest control of both the world's money supply and its oil supply from the [[cabal|cabal]].[391] Lange asks readers to recall Trump "rolling the Fed into the Treasury" and then who controls the Treasury, presenting the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]]'s aggressive 2022 rate rises — which strengthened the dollar and forced bailouts of three foreign central banks — as the mechanism by which the [[global-debt-system|global debt system]] is being bankrupted.[391] He cites a Q post he reads as revealing Trump's strategy to destroy that system and return the United States to the [[gold-standard|gold standard]].[391]

On the energy side the essay credits a coordinated plan involving other world leaders, especially those of the two largest oil producers: [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] in [[russia|Russia]] and Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] in [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]], the latter having reset the oil price by massively increasing production during Trump's term.[391] Lange concludes that this "great transition" is happening while Trump is offstage, that he has taken away the two weapons the central banks used to control the world — money and [[oil|oil]] — and that "the best is truly yet to come."[391]

## "Golden bridge" interpretation of his immigration posture (January 2025)

In "On Golden Bridges and the Art of War," published January 28, 2025, the pseudonymous Badlands contributor [[american-hypnotist|American Hypnotist]] argued that Trump's expressed support for the [[h-1b-visa-program|H-1B visa program]] should not be taken at face value, holding that neither Trump nor [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] could plausibly be unaware of the harms of mass immigration or of the availability of domestic technical talent.[392] The author read their statements as an application of [[sun-tzu|Sun Tzu]]'s [[golden-bridge-strategy|golden bridge]] maxim, in which an enemy is shown an apparent way out so that he can be struck from advantage, and as part of the [[we-are-watching-a-movie|scripted "movie"]] intended to move mass opinion against immigration.[392]

An editorial note appended before publication observed that the article had been written several weeks before the inauguration and that, on day one of his presidency, Trump had already begun fighting back "with a vengeance" against what the author called the [[deep-state|Deep State]]'s campaign of destruction-via-immigration.[392] The essay further cited Trump's elevation of [[anthony-fauci|Anthony Fauci]] to world fame as a precedent for exposing a figure by promoting him, and argued that Trump cannot raise "far out conspiracy" topics publicly until citizen journalists have made them widely known — the division of labour the author termed the [[mil-civ-alliance|MIL-CIV alliance]].[392]

## March 2024 Israel Hayom interview

In March 2024, [[israel-hayom|Israel Hayom]] sent editor-in-chief Omer Lachmanovitch and journalist Ariel Kahana to Mar-a-Lago seeking a definitive statement from Trump on the Gaza war.[395] The Badlands essay "Our 'Greatest Ally'" notes that Trump, entangled in what it calls the most intense lawfare campaign in American history, had remained relatively silent on the topic despite widespread assumptions that he would fully back the [[benjamin-netanyahu|Netanyahu]] government.[395]

Asked whether he supported the complete destruction of [[hamas|Hamas]], Trump replied that the October 7 attack would never have occurred had he still been president, because [[iran|Iran]] was broke: he had told 47 nations personally that buying Iranian oil would cost them access to the U.S. market and invite tariffs, leaving Tehran with no money for Hamas or [[hezbollah|Hezbollah]].[395] He contrasted that with Iran's subsequent position — "$221 billion in cash" and effective control over Iraq, which he described as "like a subsidiary" — blaming the U.S. invasion of Iraq under George W. Bush and the bolstering of Iran under [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]].[395]

Trump then urged Israel to "finish up your war" and "get on to peace," warning that "Israel has to be very careful because you're losing a lot of the world."[395] Pressed on the rise in antisemitic attacks since October 7, he answered that Israel "made a very big mistake," saying he had wanted to call and tell them not to broadcast "moving shots of bombs being dropped into buildings in [[gaza|Gaza]]," which he called "a very bad picture for the world," adding: "Go and do what you have to do. But you don't do that."[395]

[[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] praised the performance, writing that Trump "danced around that minefield with the grace of an omniscient ballerina," and argued that Trump's record shows a preference for ending conflicts by decapitating hostile leadership with minimal collateral damage — citing the strikes on [[qasem-soleimani|Qasem Soleimani]] and [[abu-bakr-al-baghdadi|Abu Bakr al-Baghdadi]] — rather than following the doctrine of endless war favored by the [[military-industrial-complex|Military Industrial Complex]].[395]

395. Badlands Brief — "Our 'Greatest Ally'", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/our-greatest-ally

## "Project Trump Card" (December 2022)

On December 14, 2022, Trump posted a video to [[truth-social|Truth Social]] promising a "major announcement," prompting overnight speculation about his campaign, a running mate, or a bid for Speaker of the House.[397] The announcement delivered the following morning was the [[trump-digital-trading-cards|Trump Digital Trading Cards]] NFT collection, which many supporters treated as a "nothingburger" and which drew derision from crypto and mainstream outlets alike.[397] The collection sold out within hours.[397]

Hours later Trump's campaign released, via [[jack-posobiec|Jack Posobiec]], the major planks of his free-speech policy proposal, dubbed the [[digital-bill-of-rights|Digital Bill of Rights]].[397] [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] contributor [[kitty-gillespie|Kitty Gillespie]] argued the pairing was deliberate misdirection — "LOOK HERE/NOT THERE" — with the NFT drop absorbing hostile media attention while the policy rollout passed largely unnoticed, and that [[steve-bannon|Steve Bannon]] and [[steve-cortes|Steve Cortes]] played assigned roles in disparaging the NFTs.[397]

Gillespie further noted that the NFT checkout accepted ETH (WETH) and delivered cards to a "digital wallet," despite Trump's earlier public hostility to cryptocurrency — he had told Fox Business in August 2021 that crypto was "potentially a disaster waiting to happen" and that he preferred "the currency of the United States," and told Maria Bartiromo in December 2021 that crypto was "very dangerous."[397] She concluded the launch, like [[melania-trump|Melania Trump]]'s December 2021 NFT, was intended to normalize [[digital-wallets|digital wallets]] ahead of a shift away from [[fiat-currency|fiat currency]], and read his boast that he would be "better than Lincoln, better than Washington" as signalling a new currency tied to his presidency — [[abraham-lincoln|Lincoln]] for coinage, [[george-washington|Washington]] for the dollar.[397] She also speculated that Trump had quietly aligned the United States with [[brics|BRICS]] and may have been an early architect of the bloc.[397]

397. Badlands Brief — "Project Trump Card", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/project-trump-card
398. Badlands Brief — "Profiting From War", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/profiting-from-war

## Q for Dummies Chapter 15: November 2017 operations and the July 2024 landscape

Chapter 15 of the [[q-for-dummies|Q for Dummies]] series reads Q drop #103 of November 5, 2017, as a message relayed on Trump's behalf thanking operators waging what the author calls a kinetic war against the [[deep-state|Deep State]] out of public sight.[399] The chapter states that at the time Trump was preparing for talks with Asian leaders while [[barack-obama|Barack Obama]] travelled the region ahead of him in an alleged attempt to torpedo those talks.[399]

The chapter's central claim about Trump concerns the [[2017-trump-twitter-account-deactivation|deactivation of his Twitter account]]: the author argues that the takedown was a [[central-intelligence-agency|CIA]]-directed attempt to stop Trump from transmitting a coded go-code tweet targeting the Saudi royal family, that Trump subsequently sent the tweet and the [[saudi-purge|Saudi purge]] followed, and that because an attack on a sitting president constitutes an act of war it gave Trump legal precedent to act as a "war time President" — potentially via the [[insurrection-act|Insurrection Act]], [[continuity-of-government|Continuity of Government]] or [[devolution|Devolution]].[399]

Writing in July 2024, the same chapter surveys the contemporaneous political landscape: the presidential debate described as a train wreck for [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], Democratic pressure for a new nominee, the Supreme Court's [[presidential-immunity|presidential immunity]] ruling and the reversal of [[chevron-deference|Chevron deference]], speculation over Trump's vice-presidential pick, his newly declared support for [[bitcoin|Bitcoin]], and a planned meeting with Hungary's Viktor Orbán at [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]].[399]

399. Badlands Brief — "Q for Dummies - Chapter 15", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/q-for-dummies-chapter-15

## Insulation and relative power in the first Q drops

The opening [[q-operation|Q]] drops of October 2017 repeatedly assert that Trump was never outmatched by his opponents.[400] Drop #2 states that no [[deep-state|deep state]] player possessed more power than the president, and Drop #4 reiterates that the belief that [[george-soros|Soros]], [[hillary-clinton|Clinton]], the Rothschilds and their associates held more power than Trump was, in [[paul-fleuret|Paul Fleuret]]'s phrase, "a complete pipe dream."[400] Drop #4 also describes POTUS as "insulated," which Fleuret reads in Chapter 1 of [[q-for-dummies|Q for Dummies]] to mean that Trump was protected from harm, and cites the drop's references to military generals and [[military-intelligence|military intelligence]] as indicating that those at the very top supported him.[400]

Drop #3 notes Trump's meeting with [[robert-mueller|Robert Mueller]], ostensibly to interview him for the [[fbi|FBI]] directorship — a role Mueller could not legally have taken — which Fleuret treats as an open question about the meeting's real purpose.[400] Writing in December 2023, Fleuret tied these early drops to Trump's then-pending trial, which he expected to produce verifiable evidence that the 2020 election was stolen.[400]

400. Badlands Brief — "Q for Dummies - Chapter 1", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/q-for-dummies-chapter-1

## "Q for Dummies" Chapter 2 readings

Badlands' "Q for Dummies" Chapter 2 argues, from Q drop #6, that contrary to widespread expectations in the movement Trump would never "take over the airwaves" or hold a grand press conference announcing arrests of Deep State figures, in any medium.[401] The chapter explains the caution by reference to predecessors who confronted the same forces — John F. Kennedy, who "went head-on with the deep state," and Ronald Reagan, who was shot — concluding that white hats first had to remove corrupt military leadership around the President in order to insulate and protect him.[401]

Discussing drop #12, the author speculates that Trump was himself a long-term infiltrator of the Democratic Party, explaining his roughly thirty years as a Democrat and his social proximity to its figures: "sometimes you have to work in the dark to serve the light."[401] On drop #14, the chapter asserts that Trump knows "where the bodies are buried," that he was asked and possibly groomed to run, that he is uniquely equipped temperamentally to absorb attacks, and that he "cannot be bought."[401] The chapter further claims [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] was supposed to win in 2016, that the election was targeted for theft, and that white hats safeguarded it — predicting a repeat in 2024 whose effect would be to spotlight 2020 and Joe Biden's "81mm" votes.[401] Chapter 2 also cites Trump's rising support among Black voters, noting he won in 2016 with a very low share of the Black vote but was polling near 20% at the time of writing.[401]


## Chapter 4 of Q for Dummies

Chapter 4 of the [[q-for-dummies|Q for Dummies]] series, published January 19, 2024, presents Trump as the first president to turn the post-9/11 [[unacknowledged-special-access-programs|special access program]] architecture toward benevolent ends, arguing that because the military held the same programs as the civilian agencies he could exercise his authority as Commander in Chief to see any and all intelligence he wished.[402] The chapter holds that he began draining the swamp on day one at [[central-intelligence-agency|CIA]] headquarters, one day after his inauguration and six weeks after his Al Smith Dinner remarks, and reads his survival — set against [[john-f-kennedy|John F. Kennedy]]'s fate — as evidence of the protection he possesses.[402] Wins cited in the chapter's introduction include Trump's victory in the [[2024-iowa-caucus|Iowa Caucus]] and federal court rulings requiring that he be allowed on ballots.[402]

The author argues that Trump's elevation of [[anthony-fauci|Anthony Fauci]] to public prominence was not an endorsement but a means of placing him in the spotlight so the public would dig into and expose his conduct, offered as an illustration of the "40,000 foot view."[402] He also states that he has not ruled out that Trump invoked the [[insurrection-act|Insurrection Act]] at some point in 2020, while acknowledging he has no solid proof and therefore assumes he did not.[402] On trafficking, the chapter observes that names arrested after drop #29 include [[jeffrey-epstein|Epstein]], [[harvey-weinstein|Weinstein]], [[ghislaine-maxwell|Maxwell]], Jean-Luc Brunel and Peter Nygard, and asserts that Trump pursued such figures harder than any president before him.[402] Finally, drop #30 is read as showing that surveillance and attacks on Trump and his family — including spying, the framing of his national security adviser, and [[russiagate-hoax|Russiagate]] — were more expansive than the public imagines, and that the 2017 [[white-house|White House]] renovation likely served to remove listening devices.[402]

402. Badlands Media — "Q for Dummies - Chapter 4", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/q-for-dummies-chapter-4

## Alleged target of a domestic color revolution

Badlands contributor [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] argued in June 2023 that Trump is the political opponent the [[central-intelligence-agency|CIA]] needed to remove and keep out of power, through election rigging, propaganda and violent street protests, in what Carlson called the Rainbow [[color-revolution|Color Revolution]].[403] Drawing a parallel with the [[1953-iranian-coup|1953 overthrow of Mohammad Mosaddegh]] — who was convicted of treason after the CIA installed the Shah — Carlson noted that Trump, whom he said the CIA helped remove from office, had just been indicted under the [[espionage-act|Espionage Act]].[403] He also referenced his prior essay on early Communist-fighting patriots and their ties to Trump.[403]

## Alignment between Trump's tweets and the Q drops

The June 27, 2024 essay "Q is Real, Because Math Says So" by [[american-hypnotist|American Hypnotist]] treats correspondences between Trump's Twitter output and the [[q-operation|Q]] drops as the strongest available evidence that Q is a genuine insider connected to the president.[404] Its central example is December 9, 2017: Q posted at 10:54 a.m. "We love our U.S. Military. On behalf of an entire Nation, THANK YOU for your sacrifice and service", and Trump posted the same 98 characters verbatim to Twitter — capitalisation and punctuation included — at 1:37 p.m. the same day.[404] The author argues that the odds of Trump unknowingly reproducing that text are already beyond coincidence, and adds a second case in which a Q post of April 9, 2020 was echoed by many politicians besides Trump, which he says he verified against the actual Twitter accounts.[404]

On the essay's reasoning, if Q and Trump are coordinating their posts then Q cannot be a [[larp|LARP]]; the [[q-proofs|proofs]] are therefore treated as evidence about Trump as much as about Q.[404] The article also invokes Trump's earlier outspokenness on vaccines to argue that his promotion of the Covid vaccine under [[operation-warp-speed|Operation Warp Speed]] must have had a strategic rationale within what it calls an optics battle.[404] It further complains that critics who call Q supporters stupid or crazy behave exactly as the left does toward Trump supporters.[404]


## "Reagan 2.0" parallels

A February 18, 2025 Badlands feature by [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] titled "Reagan 2.0" argued that Trump is an improved successor to [[ronald-reagan|Ronald Reagan]], with both men enemies of the [[deep-state|Deep State]].[405] On February 6, 2025, the 114th anniversary of Reagan's birth, Trump added a portrait of the 40th president to his Oval Office and issued a birthday message calling Reagan "a giant of American history, a lion of freedom and liberty," crediting the Reagan Revolution with a boom economy and the collapse of Communism, and pledging to carry the torch of liberty as America enters its "Golden Age."[405]

Carlson catalogues the correspondences he sees: the [[make-america-great-again|MAGA]] slogan derives from Reagan's 1980 campaign line "Let's make America great again"; Iran released 52 American hostages as Reagan took office, while a plan to free [[hamas|Hamas]]-held American and Israeli hostages was initiated once Trump returned to power; Reagan refused to fund [[jimmy-carter|Jimmy Carter]]'s new [[department-of-education|Department of Education]] while Trump plans to shut it down entirely; Los Angeles hosted the Olympics in [[1984-los-angeles-olympics|1984]] under Reagan and will host again in [[2028-los-angeles-olympics|2028]] under Trump; both were Hollywood figures, with Carlson arguing Trump is taking over Hollywood via the [[skydance-media|Skydance Media]]–[[paramount-global|Paramount Global]] merger; both were former Democrats who won blue-collar and minority support; both used deregulation and tax cuts, with Carlson believing Trump aims to eliminate the income tax; both pursued immigration restriction, Reagan by making it illegal to hire undocumented workers in 1986 and Trump by driving a 2025 mass exodus of illegal aliens.[405]

Further parallels include a principal foreign adversary — the Soviet Union under Reagan, Russia under Trump, with Carlson predicting Americans will soon stop viewing Russia as their greatest enemy; shared [[jacksonian-populism|Jacksonian Populism]], with both men hanging portraits of [[andrew-jackson|Andrew Jackson]] in the Oval Office; opposition to [[affirmative-action|Affirmative Action]], which Carlson says Trump has extended by decimating the DEI agenda from day one; staffing agencies with officials opposed to them, such as [[linda-mcmahon|Linda McMahon]] and [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]]; surviving assassination attempts, Reagan six weeks into his term and Trump while campaigning; professed Christian faith, which Carlson says strengthened in Trump after the attempt on his life; and blended families, Reagan's from two marriages and Trump's from three.[405] Carlson also notes that [[generation-x|Generation X]], which grew up under Reagan, is by far the most Trump-supporting generation.[405]

Carlson rejects attempts to separate the two men, including [[liz-cheney|Liz Cheney]]'s 2024 ABC News statement that "there is absolutely no chance that Ronald Reagan would be supporting Donald Trump," which he characterizes as a Neocon and RINO effort to keep Trump from exposing and dismantling Deep State operations.[405] He closes with [[anthony-fauci|Anthony Fauci]], who oversaw the AIDS response beginning in 1981 with [[azt|AZT]] and the Covid response before the 2020 election, asking whether Fauci was used twice to derail an America First president.[405]

405. Badlands Brief — "Reagan 2.0", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/reagan-20

## NATO withdrawal threats and European rearmament (2025)

During his second term Trump renewed threats to abandon [[nato|NATO]] and paused funding to Ukraine, sending shockwaves through Europe's elite circles.[406] In March 2025 he questioned whether the United States would defend NATO allies who "don't pay," and suggested Washington might not honor Article 5's collective defense clause.[406] His skepticism dated to his first term, when he called the alliance "obsolete" and pressured members to raise defense spending; he later called for a 5% GDP commitment, far above the standing 2% target.[406]

The pressure, compounded by his televised White House confrontation with [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]], triggered what Badlands contributor Ryan DeLarme described as a collective freak-out among European leaders, producing nuclear-sharing proposals from Friedrich Merz, Polish nuclear talk from [[donald-tusk|Donald Tusk]], and the [[rearm-europe|ReArm Europe]] program announced by [[ursula-von-der-leyen|Ursula von der Leyen]].[406] DeLarme argued Trump had scrambled the centuries-old system variously called the "liberal world order" or the "international rules-based order," and that the floundering efforts of figures like [[kaja-kallas|Kaja Kallas]] and von der Leyen to keep the war machine running proved the system does not work without the United States.[406]

## Role in the COVFEFE Dig

"The COVFEFE Dig," published by Badlands Media in March 2023, treats [[donald-trump|Donald Trump]]'s 31 May 2017 [[covfefe|covfefe]] Tweet as a deliberate signal rather than a typing error, and builds an argument that Trump's monetary policy was aimed at destroying the central banking system.[421] The dig notes that Trump's first act of interior decoration in the White House was to hang a portrait of [[andrew-jackson|Andrew Jackson]], the president associated with the [[bank-war|Bank War]] and with the phrase "Drain the Swamp."[421] It observes that 3 × 15 = 45 and that Trump is the 45th president, that D is the fourth letter so "D5" reads as 45, and that [[satoshi-nakamoto|Satoshi Nakamoto]]'s stated birthday of 4/5/1975 produces the same figure.[421] Trump is nicknamed the Orange Man and [[bitcoin|Bitcoin]] the "orangecoin," a correspondence the author treats as meaningful.[421]

The article addresses Trump's July 2019 statement that he was "not a fan of Bitcoin and other Cryptocurrencies, which are not money," arguing that an open declaration of a Bitcoin strategy would have defeated the plan.[421] Trump's earlier posting of himself as [[pepe-the-frog|Pepe the Frog]] on 10/13/2015 is read as another 3-1-5 marker.[421] The dig's central financial claim is that during the COVID-19 market crisis of 2020 Trump, Treasury Secretary [[steven-mnuchin|Steve Mnuchin]] and the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] effectively merged the two institutions, accomplishing what [[john-f-kennedy|John F. Kennedy]] had attempted with [[executive-order-11110|Executive Order 11110]].[421]

## "Trump Knows": the rule-of-law thesis

In a March 2023 essay, [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] argued that Trump's [[first-inaugural-address-of-donald-trump|2017 inaugural address]] was a deliberate signal to the criminal networks that covertly exert influence over free peoples, and that on January 20, 2017 the then President-elect "uttered words that would send shockwaves" through a secret group of blackmailers and debt peddlers who weaponize vice, run international banking schemes and manufacture crises to accumulate power.[442] Rather than continue the tradition of parasitically draining freedoms from hardworking Americans, the article says, "45" began the process of giving power back to the people.[442]

The essay's refrain is that "Trump knows": that he recognizes the American people are effectively a conquered people, that the government is made to serve interests hostile to the public, and that the country operates a [[two-tier-justice-system|two-tiered justice system]].[442] Deschamps proposed that Trump could see the profile of the corrupt actors and the [[event-201|warning signs of a pandemic conspiracy]] because the same modus operandi had been used many times before — "the way a parent knows that their kid has been in the cookie jar."[442] Trump is further credited with understanding the propaganda and [[fifth-generation-warfare|fifth-generation warfare]] campaign waged against the pillars of freedom.[442]

Trump's repeated public invocations of the [[rule-of-law|rule of law]] are read as more than rhetoric. The article collects his statements that "a country must enforce its borders" and that "respect for the rule of law is at our country's core," his 2018 pledge not to rest until borders were secured and the rule of law restored, and his description of the rule of law as "our Nation's proud heritage" and "the cornerstone of our Freedom" during the Brett Kavanaugh Supreme Court nomination, as posted by Dan Scavino.[442] Deschamps concluded that Trump and the patriots are preparing the world for a genuine rule-of-law society in which the people reclaim knowledge of the law, closing with Trump's own line, "The best is yet to come."[442]

## Treatment in "Q for Dummies" Chapter 5

Chapter 5 of [[q-for-dummies|Q for Dummies]], published February 2, 2024, frames its survey of Q drops #31–40 around Trump's position at the start of that election year.[407] Its author describes the New York civil judgment against Trump as a sham "almost certain to be overturned in an appeals court," and reads footage from Davos as evidence that global elites were panicked at the prospect of his return.[407] From that he argues that if the [[deep-state|Deep State]] retained the electoral control it exercised in 2020, it would not be expending such effort against him.[407]

Within the drops themselves, the chapter reports that drop #34 mentions POTUS declaring a state of temporary military control, which it connects to contemporary alarm over what Trump might do on returning to office, and that drop #35 assures readers Trump would be well insulated and protected during the operation — a statement the author suggests kept the Deep State on edge whenever Trump travelled aboard [[air-force-one|Air Force One]].[407] Drop #35's "4, 10, 20" sign-off is explained in drop #40 as Trump himself signing as DJT through letter-number substitution, making it, on this reading, a post authored by the president rather than by Q.[407] The chapter also reads Trump's [[calm-before-the-storm|"calm before the storm"]] remark, made three weeks before the [[q-operation|Q operation]] began, as a deliberate signal, and points to inauguration imagery in which Trump is surrounded by personnel wearing insignia tied to [[military-intelligence|Military Intelligence]] and the [[judge-advocate-generals-corps|Judge Advocate General's Corps]].[407]

## Narrative strategy as read by Badlands (January 2023)

In "Right the Ship" (January 12, 2023), [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described Trump as a master of Narrative Warfare whose focus on the "story" emerging from events such as the January 2023 [[2023-speaker-of-the-house-election|Speaker of the House contest]] and the surfacing [[biden-classified-documents-scandal|Biden documents scandal]] is deliberate rather than frivolous.[409] The reasoning he attributed to Trump and his allies is that, in order to visit justice on the enemies of the American people, those people must first recognize who their enemies are.[409]

Burning Bright referred to Trump as "our chosen and acknowledged POTUS" and cited a statement Trump released during the Speaker firestorm that framed the episode as an "event" in quotation marks, treating it as evidence that Trump and company were fully in control of the play being watched.[409] He described the use of narrative framing by Trump and his "chosen scions" in the [[america-first-movement|America First]] movement as a necessary evil — using the enemy's own weapons in service of an agenda of exposure, disclosure and eventually justice — and credited that mastery, alongside the truth community's improving discernment, with eroding the [[deep-state|Deep State]]'s illusory control over the American Mind.[409]

The same essay credited Trump with signing the [[doha-agreement|Doha Agreement]] and the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]], which together with the beginnings of the [[great-awakening|Great Awakening]] it identified as the developments that began to reverse the Middle East [[forever-wars|Forever War]].[409] It also placed Trump's entry into politics within a longer arc of backlash from the [[media-industrial-complex|Media Industrial Complex]].[409]

## "Revolution Founded at the Crossroads" (October 2024)

An October 31, 2024 Badlands essay by the [[patriots-in-progress|Patriots in Progress]] author built its argument for peaceful revolution around the 2016 campaign compilation "This Video Will Get Donald Trump Elected," in which Trump classified the moment as "a crossroads in the history of our civilization that will determine whether or not We the People reclaim control over our government."[410] Other lines quoted include "our campaign represents a true existential threat, like they haven't seen before," "this is not simply another 4-year election," "this is a struggle for the survival of our nation, and this will be our last chance to save it," and "our great civilization has come upon a moment of reckoning."[410]

The essay read Trump's description of the incumbent order as a definition of tyranny: a Washington establishment and the financial and media corporations funding it existing "for only one reason, to protect and enrich itself," and "a global power structure that is responsible for the economic decisions that have robbed our working class."[410] It highlighted his claim that [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] "meets in secret with international banks to plot the destruction of US sovereignty."[410]

Trump's pledge to replace "a failed and corrupt political establishment, with a new Government controlled by you, the American people" was read as a deliberate reference to the [[declaration-of-independence|Declaration of Independence]]'s right of the people "to institute new Government," and therefore as an invocation of revolution by irregular and unconventional means.[410] The author also noted Trump's 2017 framing of his inauguration as the return of power to We the People, his discussion of a strategic Bitcoin stockpile, and his attendance at the Bitcoin conference, connecting the movement to [[bitcoin]] and to [[dan-scavino|Dan Scavino]]'s March 21, 2016 "#BTC for the #Trump Train" tweet.[410]

## Carlson's "big picture" case for a second term (October 2024)

In "Stay Focused on the Big Picture," published October 15, 2024, Badlands contributor [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] framed the choice between Trump and [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] as the most consequential in American history — prosperity never known before against the end of the country as we know it — and argued that earlier "uniparty" elections had merely determined which [[deep-state|Deep State]] agenda, wars or moral decay, would be pursued.[411] Relaying [[qanon|Q]] claims, he described Trump as working with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] to take down the Deep State Cabal, and speculated that Trump had handpicked [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] as his 2020 opponent while Xi supplied the leverage that kept Biden controllable.[411] He read the placement of [[tim-walz|Tim Walz]] on the 2024 Democratic ticket as a Trump-led trap whose brilliance lay in the timing of Walz's exposure.[411]

Carlson also defended [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] as working with Trump to take down the system rather than building a robot army, and predicted that when Trump becomes president again, technology the public believes does not yet exist — free energy and medical breakthroughs among it — will be released to the world.[411] He cautioned nonetheless that any good done during a second Trump term would be only a temporary reprieve unless the financial system itself is replaced, since arrested puppets are simply succeeded by others willing to sell their souls for power and wealth.[411] The article closed with a call for "all hands on deck" to return Trump to the White House, and with the injunction to trust the plan.[411]

## As memetic signaller in "Signal Collapse" (June 2025)

The June 26, 2025 Badlands essay "Signal Collapse" by [[observing-consciousness|Observing Consciousness]] presented Trump as an operator in [[fifth-generation-warfare|fifth-generation warfare]] whose public statements function as timed memetic devices rather than policy announcements.[414] The essay's central claim is that Trump "speaks in completed tense" and "doesn't forecast — he closes loops," citing "We caught them all," "They should have signed the deal," and [[2025-trump-regime-change-remarks-on-iran|"Regime change"]] as instances of language confirming events that had already concluded out of public view.[414]

On this reading his [[miga|MIGA]] post was "a memetic activator" rather than branding, and his posts generally are "timed memetic explosives" and "psychological disruptors" deployed when the system is weakest — words "placed like scalpel blades into collective illusions."[414] The essay also grouped Trump with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] as "alignment agents" or "global chiropractors" said to be releasing pressure in a collective field, and argued that Trump "does not struggle to dismantle" the existing order but "marks its end."[414] These are interpretive assertions of the essay's [[canon-of-collapse|Canon of Collapse]] framework rather than documented facts about Trump's intent.[414]
