## "He didn't hand over the car keys" (Carlson, May 2023)

In "Stuck in a Groove of Negativity and Hopelessness" (May 4, 2023), [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] framed his entire optimistic reading of the post-2020 period around a refusal to believe that Trump had simply conceded.[415] He wrote sardonically that he did not believe Trump threw "a kegger on January 19th, 2021, at the White House," nor that on January 20 he threw up his hands and told the [[deep-state|Deep State]] "okay, you win" — that is, that after four-plus years of fighting with everything he and his family had, Trump would hand "the car keys to a drunk driver who also has dementia."[415] Carlson maintained instead that Trump had put in place a system with the power to intervene before permanent damage occurs and still retains some control of the country.[415]

On the 2016 campaign, Carlson argued the cabal did its best to keep Trump out of the White House by lying about him, ridiculing prospective voters, and publishing polls implying that only the uneducated supported him — and then lost because it believed its own fake polling and under-calculated the number of votes to flip.[415] He described the subsequent attacks as non-stop and baseless, aimed at removing Trump from office or slowing him to the point of accomplishing very little, and read [[mail-in-voting|mail-in ballots]] in 2020 as the contingency built to prevent a repeat.[415]

Carlson expressed confidence that whatever damage the [[joe-biden|Biden]] administration's policies inflict will not be permanent, that Trump will "right the ship" on returning to office, and that if damage began to become irreversible "Trump and his people will step in to stop it."[415] He asked readers to recall which countries Trump visited in 2017 — the soccer ball, the sword dance — as evidence for that reading, and criticised members of his own community who insist Trump cannot win in 2024 because elections are too rigged.[415]

## The "boomerang" strategy (Badlands account)

[[joe-lange|Joe Lange]]'s "[[the-biggest-boomerang-in-history|The Biggest Boomerang In History]]" argues that Trump deliberately inverted the operation mounted against him.[416] The first move was firing [[james-comey|James Comey]], which cost the opposing network control of the [[fbi|FBI]], allowed a housecleaning, and forced them to secure a Special Counsel before a new director could be confirmed; Trump is also said to have required [[rod-rosenstein|Rod Rosenstein]] to write the memo enumerating grounds for the dismissal.[416]

The second and decisive instrument was the presidential power to declassify.[416] Fox News reported that Trump issued a memo giving Attorney General [[william-barr|William Barr]] authority to declassify any documents related to surveillance of the Trump campaign in 2016 and ordered the intelligence community to cooperate fully; Q drop 3784 rendered the chain as POTUS to Barr, Barr to [[john-durham|Durham]].[416] Lange contends Trump distanced himself from the investigations, and publicly criticised Barr as he had earlier criticised [[jeff-sessions|Jeff Sessions]], in order to blunt claims of political bias — deception he describes as required in war.[416]

Lange further claims Trump ordered an early raid on the office of the Obama Presidential Library for classified documents, and that he had hoped [[barack-obama|Obama]] and the media would make an issue of it, since Obama's 2009 Executive Order forbade classification used to conceal violations of law.[416] The article closes by arguing that Trump used [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]]'s own instrument — Comey — against her, and predicts Comey will be the first big unsealed indictment.[416] These claims reflect Badlands Media interpretation rather than established fact.

416. Badlands Brief — "The Biggest Boomerang In History", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-biggest-boomerang-in-history

## Plans to transform the banking system

In "The Battle for Control," [[joe-lange|Joe Lange]] continues a series arguing that Trump plans to transform the banking system in the United States and give control of it back to the people, preventing the government from being controlled by private bankers whether foreign or domestic.[417] Safeguards, Lange writes, are the key to that future, and many will be put in place to maintain control.[417] He predicts specifically that Trump will set up a public banking option to force the banking system to serve the people and protect their savings, in contrast to private banks whose focus is always profit for their owners.[417]

Lange also argues that Trump will end the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] and return control of the banking system to the people, and suggests Trump focused on [[andrew-jackson|Andrew Jackson]] during his first term because Jackson waged war against the banking cabal.[417] Speculating that Trump may follow Jackson's [[specie-circular|Specie Circular]] precedent, he asks what better way there is to limit foreign purchases of American land, farms and corporations than to demand payment in gold and silver, which he calls both honest money and a safeguard for sovereignty.[417] The article further states that Trump took [[alwaleed-bin-talal|Prince Alwaleed bin Talal Al Saud]] "off the chessboard," describing him as the biggest threat to Trump on the day he was inaugurated.[417]

417. Badlands Brief — "The Battle for Control", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-battle-for-control

## The "chosen one" remark and Lange's inversion (January 2023)

Trump's self-description as "the chosen one" — delivered while discussing China's theft of American manufacturing and intellectual property, and his intention to stop what previous presidents had permitted — was characterised by [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] contributor [[joe-lange|Joe Lange]] as one of his favourite examples of Trump trolling his opponents, and as a remark that badly rattled the left.[418]

In "The Chosen One" (January 18, 2023), Lange inverted the phrase, proposing that [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] was the true chosen one and that Trump had chosen him.[418] The essay restated Lange's recurring claim that Trump and the military exercise far more control over events than the public realizes, citing the [[2023-speaker-of-the-house-election|January 2023 Speaker of the House contest]] as a case where Trump was "directing the whole play": Lange said Trump wanted [[kevin-mccarthy|Kevin McCarthy]] as Speaker, that [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]] and [[marjorie-taylor-greene|Marjorie Taylor Greene]] were acting rather than grandstanding, and that Trump's use of the words "GOOD" and "GREAT" in Truth Social posts echoed a [[qanon|Q]] post reading "Good movie. Great actors."[418] Through negotiation — "the art of the deal" — the drama exposed how the House had operated under [[nancy-pelosi|Nancy Pelosi]] and returned power to members.[418]

On the [[2020-democratic-presidential-primary|2020 Democratic primary]], Lange argued Trump chose his own opponent by attacking Biden relentlessly and thereby elevating him with Democratic voters, while anonymous leaks from purported campaign insiders that Biden was the only rival Trump feared functioned as free advertising.[418] He cited the earlier [[elizabeth-warren-dna-test|Elizabeth Warren DNA test episode]] — the Montana rally challenge, the 1/1024th result and the Republican "100% Liar" campaign — as proof of Trump's ability to set the national narrative and destroy a rival.[418]

Lange further asserted that the plan had been to allow Biden to take the 2020 election so that Democrats would absorb the blame and a civil war, said to have been the enemy's aim from January 6, 2021, would be avoided; the enemy, he wrote, "never expected Trump to just walk away."[418] He described Trump as a "Master 5D chess player" who had installed a [[judas-goat|Judas goat]] at the head of the opposition and set "the biggest sting in world history."[418]

418. Badlands Brief — "The Chosen One", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-chosen-one

## The Uranium One trap and the 2025 clearance order

In "The Biggest Cover-up in American History," [[joe-lange|Joe Lange]] argues that Trump's public feud with Attorney General [[jeff-sessions|Jeff Sessions]] over the latter's recusal from the Russian collusion investigation was a deliberate illusion of division, designed to make Trump look weak and Sessions neutered while the [[huber-investigation|Huber investigation]] proceeded in secret.[419] Citing [[qanon|Q]] drop #1711, which asks whether Trump would allow a complete takeover of his [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] if nothing were being done behind the scenes, Lange concludes that the plan was always to trap the enemy using Trump as bait, and that Trump was never in real danger of removal from office.[419]

Lange also points to Trump's March 2025 [[executive-order-rescinding-security-clearances|memorandum rescinding security clearances]] from a named list of individuals including [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]], Joseph R. Biden Jr. and members of his family, describing it as unprecedented and as proof of an ongoing [[racketeer-influenced-and-corrupt-organizations-act|RICO]] investigation approaching the unsealing of [[sealed-indictments|sealed indictments]].[419] He notes that Trump had earlier tried to draw attention to media protection of the Clintons and [[barack-obama|Obama]] over the [[uranium-one-deal|Uranium One deal]], and argues that Trump's claims to have "caught them all" and to be draining the swamp should be taken literally.[419]

419. Badlands Brief — "The Biggest Cover-up in American History", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-biggest-cover-up-in-american

## Carlson's 2024 thesis on Trump and the CIA

Erik Carlson's "The CIA is Being Thrown Under the Bus" places Trump in a line of four presidents — with [[john-f-kennedy|Kennedy]], [[richard-nixon|Nixon]] and [[ronald-reagan|Reagan]] — who were alarmed by the [[central-intelligence-agency|CIA]]'s unchecked power and were attacked for it, Trump having had everything thrown at him but the kitchen sink.[420] Carlson writes that since Trump took office a spotlight has been shone on the agency like never before, and that the illumination only intensified afterwards.[420]

He notes that Trump stopped running intelligence through the CIA once [[gina-haspel|Gina Haspel]] replaced [[mike-pompeo|Mike Pompeo]] as director, and that the agency managed to get America into zero new wars during his term — evidence, in Carlson's view, that Trump contained the agency even while it worked against him, and might yet follow through on Kennedy's desire to splinter it.[420] He also credits Trump with shifting control of America's banking system from the [[city-of-london|City of London]] to the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] by switching from [[libor|LIBOR]] to [[sofr|SOFR]].[420]

The article's central prediction is that the Deep State expects Trump back in the White House in 2025 and is therefore transferring power to [[nato|NATO]], the [[united-nations|United Nations]] and the [[european-union|European Union]], all outside his control.[420] Carlson argues that Trump has spent years working out whom he can trust so that moles such as [[alexander-vindman|Alexander Vindman]], [[olivia-troye|Olivia Troye]] and [[john-kelly|John Kelly]] cannot sabotage a second administration, and dismisses reports of drugs found in the Trump White House as absurd given that Trump neither drinks nor smokes.[420] He also cites Trump's remark that banning TikTok would make [[mark-zuckerberg|Mark Zuckerberg]] twice as powerful.[420]

420. Badlands Brief — "The CIA is Being Thrown Under the Bus", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-cia-is-being-thrown-under-the
421. Badlands Brief — "The COVFEFE Dig", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-covfefe-dig

## Response to the Kirk assassination (September 2025)

In "The Exploitation of Charlie Kirk," [[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] called the Trump administration's initial response to the murder of [[charlie-kirk|Charlie Kirk]] troubling, writing that Trump's remarks began as a thoughtful tribute before shifting halfway through to set the tone for what became the right's own brand of [[cancel-culture|cancel culture]] and calls for repressive measures.[423] DeLarme argued the premise that villainizing political opponents makes one responsible for violence against them is the same sentiment the establishment left has used across the West to justify censoring conservatives.[423] He listed measures taken under the administration including [[state-department|State Department]] visa revocations, [[pentagon|Pentagon]] tracking of service members, Attorney General [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]]'s "hate speech" pledge, and a reported [[white-house|White House]] executive order on political violence.[423]

DeLarme explicitly declined to claim that Trump and his administration were intentionally advancing the goals of a deep state or the financial elite, saying that would be as dishonest as calling every early War on Terror supporter a shill for the military-industrial complex; his suggestion was that no regime is perfect and mistakes are made when emotions run high.[423] He also entertained the alternative that the measures were deliberate — a move in a [[war-of-stories|war of stories]] designed to give the left's voters a taste of their own medicine.[423] DeLarme separately noted that Kirk had been the most influential figure in the coalition that returned Trump to office, and that [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] had spent Trump's second term trying to wrangle him into Middle East wars that Kirk opposed.[423]

423. Badlands Brief — "The Exploitation of Charlie Kirk", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-exploitation-of-charlie-kirk

## Silence on escalation during the 2024 transition

During the interval between the 2024 election and inauguration, Trump appeared preoccupied with his appointments, rocket launches, and attendance at UFC events, while remaining uncharacteristically silent about the [[joe-biden|Biden]] administration's efforts to escalate tensions with [[russia|Russia]].[424] [[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] argued this reticence suggested confidence rather than indifference, noting Trump's visit to Saudi Arabia and participation in the ceremonial sword dance, and his relationship with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]], who at a press conference expressed sympathy for Trump and his family while distinguishing them from "the Political System of the United States."[424] DeLarme suggested that if anyone could broker terms with Russia — and by extension China and Iran — keeping [[nato|NATO]] from encroaching while producing an acceptable deal for [[ukraine|Ukraine]], it would be Trump, particularly if a "Truth and Reconciliation Commission" promised in [[agenda-47|Agenda 47]] exposed U.S. State Department and [[cia|CIA]] backing of the [[euromaidan|Euromaidan]] regime change.[424]

The same essay characterized the [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]] attorney general nomination and the placement of [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] as DNI as signals sent across the gameboard, and concluded that some of Trump's more establishment appointments could be figureheads destined to be fired or compelled to play ball — "about as real as Santa Claus," alongside the perceived threat of nuclear war.[424]

424. Badlands Brief — "The Fake and the Gaetz", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-fake-and-the-gaetz

## "I HATE TAYLOR SWIFT" and the media as enemy of the people

In a September 17, 2024 Badlands Media essay, [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] argued that Trump's all-capitals Truth Social post "I HATE TAYLOR SWIFT!", published shortly before a second assassination attempt on his life, should not be read as an impulsive insult but as a deliberate act of misdirection.[425] Carlson's thesis was that everything Trump communicates has a purpose, and that what critics read as missteps repeatedly "explodes in the Deep State's faces" rather than backfiring on Trump.[425] On this reading, Trump was not attacking [[taylor-swift|Taylor Swift]] the person but the [[cabal|Cabal]] machinery that manufactured her and uses celebrity to steer young women toward what Carlson called unfulfilling lives.[425]

Carlson also credited Trump's popularization of the phrase "[[fake-news|fake news]]" with being the moment when large numbers of Americans first entertained the possibility that televised information was not the whole truth.[425] He attributed the two assassination attempts against Trump in two months not to controversial statements but to elite fear that Trump would dismantle the system, and closed by saying he would vote for "the man the Satanic Cabal desperately wants dead."[425]

425. Badlands Brief — "The Media is the Enemy of the People", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-media-is-the-enemy-of-the-people

## The Master Builder thesis (Lange, 2023)

In "The Master Builder" (February 2, 2023), [[joe-lange|Joe Lange]] framed Trump's pre-political career as preparation for a far larger project.[426] The article recalls that before becoming president Trump was known worldwide as a real-estate mogul, that he took over his father's real-estate business in 1971 and renamed it [[trump-organization|The Trump Organization]], and that he constructed or restored some of the most iconic buildings in the world.[426] Trump, Lange writes, is a master builder — but what he is now building is a new global financial system, one no longer controlled by the [[rothschild-family|Rothschilds]] and an allied cabal, in which people are no longer born into debt slavery.[426]

Lange points to Trump's placement of a portrait of [[andrew-jackson|Andrew Jackson]] in the White House and his comparisons of himself to the president who removed America's central bank, quoting Jackson's warning about the power the "monied interest" derives from a paper currency it can control.[426] He also invokes [[thomas-jefferson|Thomas Jefferson]]'s statement that banking establishments are more dangerous than standing armies.[426] Building on his earlier article "Master and Commander," Lange asserts that Trump effectively merged the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] into the U.S. Treasury and now controls it, while [[devolution|devolution]] has left [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] without control of the main functions of the U.S. military.[426]

The article describes the resulting process as a [[controlled-demolition-of-the-global-debt-system|controlled demolition]] of the global central-bank debt system, requiring an international coalition because the enslavement is global.[426] It argues that [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] is allied with Trump, citing the unprecedented [[forbidden-city|Forbidden City]] state dinner during Trump's November 2017 Beijing visit and ambassador [[cui-tiankai|Cui Tiankai]]'s stated need to find a private channel to Trump — found in [[jared-kushner|Jared Kushner]], who arranged the April 2017 [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]] summit at a property equipped with a [[sensitive-compartmented-information-facility|SCIF]].[426]

On Russia, the article cites Washington Post reporting that Trump concealed details of his conversations with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]], on one occasion taking possession of his interpreter's notes after a 2017 Hamburg meeting attended by then-Secretary of State [[rex-tillerson|Rex Tillerson]], leaving no detailed record even in classified files of five face-to-face encounters over two years.[426] Lange interprets this not as impropriety but as back-channel statecraft comparable to [[john-f-kennedy|John F. Kennedy]]'s conduct during the [[cuban-missile-crisis|Cuban Missile Crisis]], and reads the [[2018-helsinki-summit|Helsinki summit]] — where Putin handed Trump a World Cup soccer ball and said the ball was now in Trump's court — as the symbolic start of the takedown.[426]

Finally, the article credits Trump with preparing the economic battlefield by forcing [[nato|NATO]] members, above all [[germany|Germany]], to fund their own defense, ending what Lange calls an American subsidy of Europe that benefited European central banks.[426] With the [[russia-ukraine-war|war in Ukraine]] and the resulting [[sanctions|sanctions]] boomeranging onto Europe, and with rapid Federal Reserve rate increases forcing central banks to seek bailouts, the piece concludes that the [[brics|BRICS]] coalition now offers the alternative system.[426]

426. Badlands Media — "The Master Builder", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-master-builder
427. Badlands Brief — "The Midas Touch", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-midas-touch

## Gold and the "Midas Touch" thesis (May 2023)

In "The Midas Touch," Joe Lange argues that Trump has always loved gold and understands that it, not the dollar, is the one true store of value.[427] In September 2011, long before announcing his presidential run, Trump accepted gold instead of dollars as a rent deposit from a new tenant, saying: "It's a sad day when a large property owner starts accepting gold instead of the dollar. The economy is bad, and Obama's not protecting the dollar at all … If I do this, other people are going to start doing it, and maybe we'll see some changes."[427] Lange reads the episode as a purposeful early warning about [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] money printing and the superiority of gold over the dollar.[427]

Lange points to Trump's transition-team choice of [[judy-shelton|Judy Shelton]], a longtime [[gold-standard|gold standard]] advocate later nominated to the Fed's Board of Governors, and his meetings with [[john-allison|John Allison]], former head of BB&T and of the [[cato-institute|Cato Institute]], as evidence that Trump surrounded himself with people committed to hard money and to abolishing the central bank.[427] He also cites Treasury Secretary [[steven-mnuchin|Steven Mnuchin]]'s rare 2017 visit to [[fort-knox|Fort Knox]] as a signal that the gold reserves had been verified.[427]

More broadly, Lange presents Trump's emphasis on states' rights and decentralizing power from Washington, and his prioritizing of judicial appointments, as preparation for a constitutional showdown over money in which the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] would overturn the [[legal-tender-cases|Legal Tender Cases]] and [[gold-clause-cases|Gold Clause Cases]].[427] In this framing, "Trump's Great Reset" would give power back to the people, in contrast to the [[world-economic-forum|World Economic Forum]]'s version of the [[great-reset|Great Reset]].[427]


## 2024 Democratic framing as an existential threat

In January 2024, Badlands Media contributor [[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] argued that the Democratic campaign against Trump had been reduced to deflection and name-calling, citing [[biden-valley-forge-speech|Joe Biden's Valley Forge speech]] in which the president compared Trump to [[adolf-hitler|Adolf Hitler]] and his supporters to Nazis, and [[cnn|CNN]] commentary asserting that Trump "poses the greatest threat to democracy that we've ever seen."[428] CNN separately reported that aides on Biden's reelection campaign joked about when to "go full Hitler" in their attacks.[428] DeLarme noted that Trump was the highest-polling candidate in the nation at the time while Democratic operatives sought to have him incarcerated or removed from state ballots, which he characterized as an unelected bureaucracy deciding whom Americans may vote for.[428] Rebutting predictions that a second Trump term would bring the execution of political rivals, he pointed out that during Trump's first term he had not built camps, rounded up Democrats, or started a third world war, contrary to warnings issued in 2017.[428] Biden also criticized Trump for calling January 6 defendants patriots and promising to pardon them.[428]

428. Badlands Brief — "The President That Never Was", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-president-that-never-was

## "The Sting" and the DeSantis announcement (Carlson, May 2023)

[[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]]'s May 30, 2023 essay "The Sting" argued that [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]]'s presidential campaign — however it was intended — would end up serving Trump.[429] Carlson wrote that whether Trump, [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] and DeSantis were working together or the latter two were working against Trump was ultimately unimportant, because the process would force [[rino|RINOs]], [[neoconservatives|NeoCons]] and [[con-inc|Con Inc.]] to "out" themselves and show who funds and controls them.[429]

Reflecting on Trump's first term, Carlson asked whether Trump, sitting down behind the Oval Office desk in January 2017, had anticipated the scale of the pushback he would face — from [[russiagate-hoax|Russiagate]] and the impeachment attempts to Covid.[429] He posed the alternatives: either Trump knew his presidency would be sabotaged by both Democrats and Republicans, or he expected only Democratic sabotage; either he believed he could fix everything in four years, or he understood from the outset that it was a process requiring him to leave office before returning.[429] Carlson allowed the further possibility that the Covid narrative forced Trump to devise a new plan.[429]

Carlson noted that Trump had said he would become president again, recalling that Trump had predicted a landslide in 2020 that would be stolen and had stated he would win again in 2024.[429] He predicted that a second Trump residency in the [[white-house|White House]] would be completely different: the Democrats already exposed, the RINOs and NeoCons exposed and in many cases replaced, "European overlords" no longer controlling the American banking system, and Trump no longer spending four years dealing with his own side obstructing him.[429] Americans, he wrote, would have to recalibrate their expectations because they were "currently unable to accept how amazing things can actually be."[429]

429. Badlands Brief — "The Sting", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-sting

## Cited in "The Steering Wheel of Civilization"

The February 24, 2023 Badlands Media essay "The Steering Wheel of Civilization" cites Trump twice.[430] It names him alongside [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] and [[leonardo-da-vinci|Leonardo da Vinci]] as an example of a highly productive person able to coordinate multiple ambitions so that they advance together rather than conflict.[430] It also describes arguably positive groups working with Trump under the banner of a [[great-awakening|Great Awakening]] or America First ideological foundation as seeking to restore a benevolent political identity to the United States and the world at large, and credits Trump — and increasingly [[qanon|Q]] — with a prominent role in reigniting American patriotism as part of a broader movement toward freedom and away from autocratic tyranny-style governance.[430]

430. Badlands Brief — "The Steering Wheel of Civilization", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-steering-wheel-of-civilization

## The Magician of Narrative (November 2022)

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]]'s essay "The Unreality Paradox" identified Trump as the figure in the movement who best understands that fake news is itself part of a real war.[431] Trump was said to wield control of the public narrative with such vigor and surety that he can get large swaths of his own voter base to criticise him in line with the very [[media-industrial-complex|Fake News Media Complex]] he seeks to provoke, forcing that complex to engage with, promote and highlight the stories he wants at the forefront of the [[collective-mind|Collective Mind]] of American society.[431]

For this reason the author called Trump a Magician of sorts — a diametrically opposed mirror to the [[deep-state|Deep State]] illusionists, using misdirection, rhetoric and paradoxical verbiage not to hide the truth but to reveal core truths pivotal to a collective escape from the enemy matrix he called the [[system-of-systems|System of Systems]].[431] Trump's chosen [[narrative-warfare|Narrative Deployments]], he observed, often appear one way when first actioned before evolving and turning en route to suggesting far more consequential and encouraging revelations in hindsight.[431]

Cited examples included Trump's presumed innocence in the [[russiagate-hoax|Russia Collusion narrative]], described as both an exoneration of him and his administration and an educational model his base could apply to better-evidenced foreign collusion allegations against opponents; the "[[perfect-phone-call|Perfect Phone Call]]" with [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelenskyy]] and the timing of media attempts to frame it as laden with quid pro quo; and the reversals by opponents on election fraud and on political violence.[431] The essay concluded that Trump and his Anons had turned the War of Stories around on its originators, using narrative vehicles, templates and sensationalism to weaponize the enemy's own psychological deployments against it.[431]

431. Badlands Brief — "The Unreality Paradox", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-unreality-paradox

432. Badlands Brief — "The TikTok Ban", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-tiktok-ban

## Reversal on the TikTok ban (2020-2024)

Trump's position on [[tiktok|TikTok]] reversed between his first term and the 2024 campaign.[432] On August 6, 2020 he invoked his emergency economic powers to impose broad sanctions on the app, an order taking effect in 45 days that would have outlawed transactions between [[bytedance|ByteDance]] and U.S. citizens on national security grounds; experts told NPR the measure would cut the service off from American advertising, force its removal from the Apple and Google app stores, and leave the more than 100 million Americans who had downloaded it without software updates until the app became unusable.[432]

By early 2024 Trump had reversed course, saying a ban would make [[mark-zuckerberg|Mark Zuckerberg]] twice as powerful and give him more "Zuckerbucks" to spend on stealing the 2024 election as he had in 2020.[432] Badlands Media contributor [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] interpreted the change as Trump concluding that an empowered Meta posed a greater danger than TikTok itself — analogous, in Carlson's reading, to Trump's judgment that the COVID lockdowns threatened America more than the virus — and as an instance of Trump's willingness to "take one step back to avoid having to take five steps back."[432] Carlson noted for contrast that Meta stock had risen 56 percent since [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] took office while Tesla had fallen 40 percent, and argued that supporters who believed Trump was sincere in opposing the ban should oppose it as well.[432] He also placed Trump within a coalition he saw forming with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]], [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] against the [[deep-state|Deep State]], and questioned whether the [[uniparty|Uniparty]] might eventually pressure Trump to divest from [[truth-social|Truth Social]].[432]

## The vanguard of the War of Stories (December 2022)

In "The War of Stories" (December 30, 2022), [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] cast Trump as the leading figure of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] whose America First rhetoric and exposure of the [[deep-state|Deep State]] [[system-of-systems|System of Systems]] were pushing the [[overton-window|Overton Window]] back toward freedom one [[narrative-deployment|deployment]] at a time.[433] He described Trump's role as that of the vanguard — wading into battle ahead of the occupying force, taking the heaviest casualties but inflicting them in equal or greater number — and observed that although the narrative attacks on Trump since he "came down the escalator" had been difficult to witness, the bite of the media talking heads aligned against him had lost much of its force, with many of his most vocal critics rendered irrelevant even to their own bases.[433]

The essay attributed this less to Trump being better at [[narrative-warfare|Narrative Warfare]] than his globalist counterparts — though Burning Bright maintained he is far better at it — than to the fact that "he simply speaks the truth," which, though buried under false deployments at birth, is exposed by time.[433] Trump was also credited with pulling the United States out of the forever war inherited from the Bush and Obama eras, and named alongside [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and potentially [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] as sovereign actors in the global play.[433]

433. Badlands Brief — "The War of Stories", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-war-of-stories

## The Transition Integrity Project and the 2020 election

In a September 2023 Badlands Media essay, [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] argued that Trump was targeted before the 2020 election by the [[transition-integrity-project|Transition Integrity Project]], a group founded in late 2019 by [[rosa-brooks|Rosa Brooks]] and [[nils-gilman|Nils Gilman]] out of concern that the Trump Administration might manipulate, ignore, undermine or disrupt the election and transition.[434] TIP's June 2020 war games, involving more than 100 participants, predicted in every scenario that Trump and his supporters would subvert the legal system and turn violent — forecasting that he would declare victory on election night, refuse to count mail-in ballots, use Attorney General [[william-barr|Bill Barr]] and the [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]] to stay in office, federalize the [[national-guard|National Guard]] or invoke the [[insurrection-act|Insurrection Act]], force the Postmaster General to seize ballots, launch "MAGA TV" under [[jared-kushner|Jared Kushner]], pardon himself and his associates, and ultimately refuse to leave the White House.[434] Carlson notes that essentially none of these predictions came true.[434]

Carlson's central claim is that Trump deliberately allowed the 2020 election to be stolen.[434] He argues that TIP previewed its own side's plan — a nationwide corporate strike to force the counting of every mail-in ballot, and scenarios including [[cascadia-secession-scenario|Cascadia secession]], new statehood for DC and Puerto Rico, and abolition of the [[electoral-college|Electoral College]] — leaving Trump a choice between halting the count and triggering economic collapse, or conceding.[434] Comparing the decision to King Solomon's judgment, Carlson holds that Trump temporarily surrendered the country to the [[deep-state|Deep State]] rather than destroy it.[434]

Carlson likewise defended Trump's push to fast-track a Covid-19 vaccine as necessary to reopen the economy, citing a European Journal of Clinical Investigation finding that 71% of adverse reactions traced to 4.2% of batches and arguing that Trump instructed Big Pharma to accelerate a vaccine, not to poison a fraction of the batches.[434] On the indictments, he argued that Trump needs to go to trial, that the televised Georgia proceeding will exonerate him and expose his accusers, and drew an analogy to the [[johnny-depp|Johnny Depp]] v. [[amber-heard|Amber Heard]] trial in which public opinion reversed once evidence was heard — noting an Emerson poll showing 64% of District of Columbia residents would find Trump guilty of 2020 election tampering before trial.[434]

434. Badlands Brief — "The Transition Integrity Project", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/the-transition-integrity-project

## The Werewolf Game series (2024)

[[american-hypnotist|American Hypnotist]]'s [[the-werewolf-game|Werewolf Game]] series posits that Trump and the [[kennedy-family|Kennedy family]] are royal heirs of an ancient benevolent civilization identified with [[tartaria|Tartaria]], locked in a centuries-long struggle with a werewolf-themed death cult descended from the [[koryos|Koryos]].[435] The series treats the establishment's reaction to the [[2016-united-states-presidential-election|2016 election]] as a black swan event proving Trump really was an outsider and an existential threat to the [[deep-state|Deep State]], reasoning that the media would not have lost composure so completely or deployed "all assets" against a merely minor threat.[435]

The article documents a claimed [[trump-kennedy-connection|Trump-Kennedy connection]]: Trump's famous friendship with [[jfk-jr|JFK Jr.]], his possession of [[john-f-kennedy|JFK]]'s rocking chair, his children's friendship with the children of Justice [[anthony-kennedy|Anthony MacLeod Kennedy]], the two families' continuing co-investments, JFK Jr.'s reported advice that Trump run for office, and Trump's brief appointment of [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|RFK Jr.]] to oversee vaccine policy.[435] It also notes that Trump's mother [[mary-anne-macleod-trump|Mary Ann Macleod]] was born on the Isle of Lewis near the Isle of Skye, where a 15th-century Ulric Kennedy is said to have settled and where the Macleod clan's Dunvegan Castle stands.[435]

Finally, the series argues that [[john-oliver|John Oliver]]'s mockery of Trump's ancestral surname *Drumpf* inadvertently revealed its German origin, which the author links to [[erhard-landmann|Erhard Landmann]]'s claim that ancient German was once spoken by all peoples of the world — evidence, on this reading, that Trump descends from a globally influential culture.[435]

## Aftermath of the 2024 assassination attempt (Badlands commentary)

Part 8 of [[the-werewolf-game|The Werewolf Game]] series at [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]], "Modern Day Cyber-Necromancy" (August 20, 2024), states that ever since the assassination attempt on Trump the author had observed a major uptick in what he calls the mass [[zombification|zombification]] efforts of the cabal, describing a distinct atmosphere on social media that felt like a last-ditch attempt to retain control over the minds of the masses.[436] As evidence of coordinated inauthentic activity he cites dozens of posts by progressives and Democrats in the wake of the attempt that were upvoted past 100,000 while their comment sections were entirely negative, which he offers as an illustration of the [[dead-internet-theory|dead internet theory]].[436]

## "Can Trump Alter Time?" (December 2024)

Part 3 of [[the-white-pill-emporium|The White Pill Emporium]], published 24 December 2024, asks whether Trump can alter time and answers largely in the affirmative within a metaphysical framework drawn from [[chris-langan|Chris Langan]]'s [[cognitive-theoretic-model-of-the-universe|CTMU]].[437] The essay reports the belief, held by many, that the Trump family knows how to time travel, owing to [[john-g-trump|John Trump]]'s receipt of [[nikola-tesla|Tesla]]'s papers after Tesla's death.[437] In support, it cites the 1890s novels of [[ingersoll-lockwood|Ingersoll Lockwood]] — *The Last President* and *Baron Trump's Marvelous Adventure* — whose character Baron Trump lives in "Trump Castle" with a dog named Bulger, alongside the real [[barron-trump|Barron Trump]], [[trump-tower|Trump Tower]] and the Trump family dog named for [[george-s-patton|Patton]], victor of the Battle of the Bulge.[437] In *The Last President* a riot occurs on Fifth Avenue at the site of the present-day New York Trump Tower, and Baron receives mission-critical instructions from a "master of all masters" named Don.[437]

On [[operation-warp-speed|Operation Warp Speed]], the essay argues that although many blame Trump for vaccine mandates, Trump never mandated that anybody take anything, and that the program's hidden purpose was to force the cabal to rush its plans and thereby prevent mass internment in [[fema-camps|FEMA camps]].[437]

[[american-hypnotist|American Hypnotist]] concludes that Trump, "more than anyone else I've ever seen, could be described as a living avatar of the American spirit itself," and that the Trump/Q operation appears to be a response to the prayers of millions of patriots.[437] As a purported sign, the essay notes that Trump's first full day as president occurred exactly 70 years, 7 months and 7 days after his birth.[437]

## "Teflon Don" and Divine intervention

Part 2 of [[the-white-pill-emporium|The White Pill Emporium]] (2 November 2024) discusses Trump under the nickname "Teflon Don," attributed to his appearing impossible to take out or defeat.[438] The essay recounts that from the very beginning his opponents accused him of colluding with Russia, then impeached him multiple times, and subsequently subjected him to what it calls absurd levels of lawfare clearly intended to destroy him or at least incapacitate him so that he would never again pose a threat to the [[deep-state|Deep State]].[438] It notes that the public watched him dodge a bullet on television several months before publication, and wagers that he continues to dodge all manner of literal or metaphorical bullets daily in episodes never reported.[438]

[[american-hypnotist|American Hypnotist]] treats this survival as evidence of [[divine-intervention|Divine intervention]], while explicitly denying that he is calling Trump or Q a savior.[438] He states there is no doubt in his mind that Trump has "successfully contacted the miraculous," and speculates that Trump, Q and the [[white-hats|white hats]] remain several steps ahead of the [[cabal|cabal]] because they can access intelligence across potential timelines — a reading he ties to the "4D chess" meme, which he regards as a deliberate communication rather than a coincidence.[438]

## German name and claimed royal lineage

Part 5 of [[the-werewolf-game|The Werewolf Game]] series, "The Ancient Origin of Modern Conflict" (30 May 2024), builds on the [[ancient-german-language-thesis|claim that ancient German was once spoken worldwide]] to argue that Trump descends from a powerful and benevolent ancient society.[439] The author concedes that a German surname alone proves nothing, but asks how an outsider such as Trump could have amassed the worldly power he holds, noting that one thing separating the "elites" from the rest of humanity is that they know how to gain such power.[439]

The essay combines three observations — that Trump is hated by the mainstream media and the [[deep-state|Deep State]], that he clearly knows how to wield power, and that his name harks back to a language once spoken by the entire planet — and concludes that together they suggest a royal lineage of some kind.[439] The ancestral form of the family name, *Drumpf*, is used earlier in the same article as an illustration of how a word's essential structure survives shifts in pronunciation, and is later cited alongside "Schwaub" as an example of ancient German names found among elite families.[439] The installment also recalls the preceding article's claim of a Celtic/Druid royal connection between Trump and the [[john-f-kennedy|Kennedys]], and notes that many researchers equate the Druids with the [[illuminati|Illuminati]], which reportedly began in Bavaria.[439]

## Depiction in "Trump Did It" (April 2023)

In the April 10, 2023 Badlands Media feature "Trump Did It," [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] wrote an explicitly fictional retrospective from the year 2050 in which Trump, elected the 47th President in 2024, is remembered as the figure who ended globalist rule.[440] The narrative places his likeness beside those of famous patriots and ordinary Americans, captioned with the phrase attributed to him throughout the piece: "Without the true rule of law, there can be no freedom."[440] It has him repeat the line most memorably in a 2038 address to a newly formed global body for preserving unalienable rights.[440]

In the story, Trump responds to a 2025 collapse engineered by the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] and "globalist bankers" with a [[sound-money-initiative|Sound Money initiative]] that cancels trillions in fraudulent debt and restarts the financial system from a clean slate, declaring that "we can manage our own money supply, just like the founding fathers did."[440] He is blamed for hyperinflation while in office and abandoned by supporters and RINOs alike, answering only, "It couldn't be any other way."[440] He leaves office in 2028 and dies in 2039 at the age of 93.[440]

A fictional final interview has Trump explain that he withheld what he knew because "people would never accept the truth while they were still attached to their old lives," describing a spring 2025 in which cornered elites in "the DC swamp and on lake Geneva" attempted hyperinflation, civil and religious war, nuclear detonations and attacks on infrastructure.[440] He says he had been communicating silently with patriots since exposing those who spied on his campaign and, in his account, stole the 2020 election, and credits his endurance to faith: "I didn't do it alone. I had God with me."[440] A second recollection has him crediting a Jesuit teacher at [[fordham-university|Fordham University]] with teaching him that "true law and God's will are one and the same."[440]

Deschamps's analytical conclusion casts Trump as a "wise king and patient steward" who, unlike his rivals in Washington, chose the country over self-enrichment, destroying his reputation and forgoing millions in assets and perhaps billions in deals rather than accept the establishment's rewards.[440] He argues that Trump privately understands the true [[rule-of-law|rule of law]], that "Trump's rule is God's Rule, God's Will," and that Trump has been preparing the world to rediscover that law — a project he links to accelerating the [[great-awakening|Great Awakening]] and to the political order he calls [[magaism]].[440]

440. Badlands Brief — "Trump Did It", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/trump-did-it

## Post-election interviews and "Trump 2.0" (December 2024)

In "Trump 2.0 and the 'Opposition' Plot," Badlands Media contributor [[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] argued that the version of Trump seen after his November 2024 victory was more focused, sincere and unrestricted than the vaguer, party-pleasing campaign figure, pointing to two lengthy post-election interviews: an hour-long sit-down with [[kristen-welker|Kristen Welker]] on [[meet-the-press|Meet the Press]] and a printed interview with [[time-magazine|TIME]] after it named him Person of the Year.[441] DeLarme attributed the shift to the absence of a third election, which freed Trump from the need to appease party orthodoxy and the donor class.[441] He described Trump as the "ultimate RINO," leveraging the Republican Party to reach the Oval Office in pursuit of cleansing institutions, uniting the country and redirecting attention to deeper issues rather than traditional partisan combat.[441]

On transgender policy, Trump told TIME, "I don't want to get into the bathroom issue. Because it's a very small number of people we're talking about, and it's ripped apart our country, so they'll have to settle whatever the law finally agrees," and agreed with incoming Delaware representative [[sarah-mcbride|Sarah McBride]] that lawmakers should focus on more important matters.[441] DeLarme linked the answer to Trump's 2016 Today Show remarks and argued that the campaign's "Kamala's for they/them; President Trump is for you" ad worked not through hostility to transgender people but by highlighting how out of touch the Democratic Party's priorities were.[441]

On Ukraine, Trump said briefers had given him staggering and roughly equal Russian and Ukrainian casualty figures and stated, "I disagree very vehemently with sending missiles hundreds of miles into Russia. Why are we doing that? We're just escalating this war and making it worse."[441] DeLarme contrasted this with the establishment Republican position voiced by [[keith-kellogg|General Keith Kellogg]] on Fox News and shared by [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] and [[elise-stefanik|Elise Stefanik]], whose criticism of [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] was that he had not done enough - a divergence DeLarme read as evidence that Trump intended his own policies, not his appointees', to shape the administration.[441]

On Israel and Gaza, Trump declined to reaffirm a two-state solution, saying, "I support a plan of peace, and it can take different forms" and "what I want is a deal where there's going to be peace and where the killing stops," and confirmed that he had previously stopped [[benjamin-netanyahu|Netanyahu]] from annexing the West Bank.[441] Asked whether he trusted Netanyahu, Trump replied, "I don't trust anybody" - a statement DeLarme called almost unheard of in Washington outside figures such as [[thomas-massie|Thomas Massie]] and former members of Congress displaced by [[aipac|AIPAC]].[441]

On Meet the Press, Trump gave what DeLarme described as a rational, nondogmatic answer on whether [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|RFK Jr.]] would be allowed to ban pediatric vaccines, and expressed willingness to strike a deal with Democrats on the Dreamers despite his mass-deportation platform.[441] DeLarme argued that Trump's first-term energy had been consumed by vendettas and allegations such as Russiagate, whereas the second-term figure appeared focused on the plan and unmoved by the noise, deftly sidestepping media traps laid to portray him as a monster and to supply ammunition to [[the-opposition|the Opposition]].[441]

## Modern heir to the anti-Communist patriots (June 2023)

In "Trump, JFK, Patton & McCarthy," [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] argued that Trump fully understands that the destruction of America is a Communist plot, but rarely says so overtly, preferring terms such as "the Deep State," "the swamp," corruption and wokeness while seldom explaining why the destructive events are happening.[442] Carlson attributed the reticence to conditioning: Americans have been trained to dismiss anyone who raises Communist infiltration as a crazy conspiracy theorist or a fascist, and to react to any mention of Russia with 75 years' worth of accumulated fear and paranoia.[442] He noted Trump had recently begun using "Marxist" and "fascist" to describe the DOJ and FBI, reading this as a transition toward naming the Communists directly.[442]

The essay traced two connections between Trump and Senator [[joseph-mccarthy|Joseph McCarthy]].[442] The first was linguistic: Trump's nickname "De-Sanctimonious" for [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]] echoed McCarthy's habitual "Sanctimonious Stu" for his critic [[stuart-symington|Stuart Symington]], used even in official Senate hearings — an unusual insult in both cases, implying that its target makes a show of being morally superior, that is, is not really as conservative, as America First, or as moral as claimed.[442] The second was biographical: Trump's mentor as a young New York real estate developer was [[roy-cohn|Roy Cohn]], who had been McCarthy's chief counsel during the 1954 [[army-mccarthy-hearings|Army–McCarthy hearings]].[442] Carlson noted that critics cite Cohn's later reputation as a "mob" lawyer as evidence of Trump's ties to organized crime, but held the opposite view: that it is evidence Trump helped the FBI take down the Mob in the 1980s.[442] He paired this with [[james-forrestal|James Forrestal]]'s mentorship of [[john-f-kennedy|John F. Kennedy]] as a matching pattern.[442]

Carlson placed Trump at the end of a sequence of Americans who fought [[communism|Communism]] and were destroyed for it — Forrestal labelled mentally unwell and dead mysteriously, McCarthy demonized and dead mysteriously, [[george-s-patton|General George S. Patton]] attacked by the press and dead mysteriously, Kennedy assassinated and then subjected to postmortem character assassination — with Trump demonized by the world's media and governments and, in Carlson's account, the subject of multiple believed assassination attempts.[442] Within his scheme of 30-year destruction cycles, Trump is "another imperfect man, this time from New York" who ruined the Cabal's plan for the 2010s just as [[ronald-reagan|Ronald Reagan]] had ruined the plan for the 1980s, forcing the [[deep-state|Deep State]] Cabal to quadruple its efforts and compress its timetable because too many people are waking up.[442] Carlson also read Trump's nickname shift for [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] from "Sleepy Joe" to "Crooked Joe" as a deliberate redirection of public attention from Biden's mental and physical deficiencies to his corruption, in the manner of the perennial "Crooked Hillary," and suggested Biden's recent fall may have been staged to reverse that focus.[442] His summation was that Trump is "a modern-day Joseph McCarthy, James Forrestal, General Patton and JFK all rolled into one."[442]

## In "Trump Cuts the Head off the Snake"

Joe Lange writes that from day one Trump and [[jared-kushner|Jared Kushner]] began planning the transformation of the Middle East, a project Lange presents as complete by mid-2023.[446] In this account Trump warned every leader in the region that they were either on the American side against Islamic extremism or on the enemy's side, and pursued a sequence that began with the [[saudi-purge|November 2017 arrests]] of [[alwaleed-bin-talal|Alwaleed bin Talal]] and hundreds of others.[446]

After protesters attacked the [[2019-baghdad-embassy-attack|US embassy in Baghdad]] on December 31, 2019, Trump tweeted that [[iran|Iran]] would be "held fully responsible for lives lost, or damage incurred, at any of our facilities" and that "they will pay a very BIG PRICE! This is not a Warning, it is a Threat."[446] Speaking at Mar-a-Lago that evening he said he did not want war but that a conflict "would go very quickly."[446] He also told Iraqi Prime Minister [[adil-abdul-mahdi|Adil Abdul-Mahdi]] by telephone that [[iraq|Iraq]] had a responsibility to protect the embassy and American personnel.[446] Three days later he ordered the [[assassination-of-qasem-soleimani|drone strike]] that killed [[qasem-soleimani|Qasem Soleimani]] and [[abu-mahdi-al-muhandis|Abu Mahdi al-Muhandis]], and stated publicly that Soleimani should have been killed many years earlier.[446] Lange argues that by taking down the puppet master first and then the biggest remaining piece on the board, Trump freed Iran from cabal control and set the conditions for the [[2023-china-brokered-saudi-iran-normalization|2023 Saudi-Iran normalization]].[446]

## "Trump's 2025 Vision" (Badlands commentary, June 2023)

In a June 1, 2023 essay, Badlands Media contributor [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] analyzed an [[axios|Axios]] survey of Trump's plans for a second term, treating each policy the outlet framed as alarming as evidence of an institution already captured by [[globalism|globalist]] interests.[447] The planks discussed included presidential authority to hire and fire federal workers at will, dismissal of "radical left" university accreditation officials and rewards for schools abolishing tenure, removal of diversity, equity and inclusion programs, an auditing system to ensure intelligence agencies are not spying on citizens, removal of "Marxist prosecutors," Justice Department investigation of pharmaceutical companies and hospital networks over "sex transitions," exclusion of gender-affirming care providers from Medicaid and Medicare, use of the military against drug cartels and street crime, repeal of an Obama-era fair-housing rule tied to federal grants, baby bonuses, ten [[freedom-cities|Freedom Cities]], and national concealed-carry reciprocity.[447]

Deschamps argued that Trump's first term had been hampered by [[deep-state|Deep State]] holdover staffers, and that the proposed workforce authority was a remedy rather than a threat.[447] He read the intelligence-auditing and prosecutorial reforms as an assault on [[lawfare|lawfare]], and predicted that Trump's reforms would transform the legal landscape of the country and, by extension, the world.[447] He also framed Trump's positions on crime and firearms as restorations of a working [[rule-of-law|rule of law]], arguing that crime falls and investment rises where citizens are armed and trained and police work alongside them.[447]

An update to the article covered Trump's May 30, 2023 Truth Social announcement of a year-long celebration of the 250th anniversary of American independence, including a "Great American State Fair" that would "put forth innovative visions for America's future."[447] Deschamps interpreted this, together with the March 2023 Freedom Cities announcement, as a "white-hat America First World Fair" intended to pre-empt a [[great-reset|Great Reset]]-style exposition and to make the transhumanist vision of the future appear absurd by comparison.[447] He characterized Trump as having dropped "a massive turd in the globalist 'Agenda 2030 Own Nothing and be Happy' punch bowl," and dated the shift from exposing criminality to articulating a positive program to 2022, culminating in the November 2022 announcement of his 2024 presidential run.[447]

447. Badlands Brief — "Trump's 2025 Vision [Updated]", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/trumps-2025-vision

## Arctic rail and the Alaska summit

In the early hours of September 26, 2020, Trump announced federal support for reviving the 2,570 km [[alaska-canada-railway|Alaska–Canada rail connection]], a continental project long thought dead and buried.[447] [[matthew-ehret|Matthew Ehret]] writes that, other than this announcement, the only North American Arctic discussions of note in recent decades had centred on anti-Russian militarization and anti-ballistic missiles.[447] Ehret also credits Trump's flushing of [[nafta|NAFTA]] as a vital precondition for giving sovereign nation states power over private corporations, while arguing that much more remains to be done, including the restoration of [[glass-steagall|Glass-Steagall]] and a national banking system.[447] He framed the [[2025-trump-putin-alaska-summit|August 15, 2025 meeting with Vladimir Putin in Alaska]] as a potential opening for reviving the [[bering-strait-tunnel|Bering Strait Tunnel]] corridor, while warning that neoconservative war hawks and "[[palantir|Palantir]] Transhumanists" had shaped strategic thinking on the [[golden-dome|Golden Dome]].[447]

## Trump and *The Art of War*

Trump is described by [[absolutetruth1776|AbsoluteTruth1776]] as a lifelong student of [[sun-tzu|Sun Tzu]]'s [[the-art-of-war|*The Art Of War*]], which the author calls his favourite book and the key to understanding how he has gone about draining the Swamp — a process the author holds will lead to the eventual defeat of the [[deep-state|Deep State]].[449] The February 13, 2023 essay reads Trump's conduct through Sun Tzu's Chapter 1, noting how often he has appeared five steps ahead of his enemies and set and played the game board so as to turn the battlefield in his favour.[449] It interprets his seeming support of the [[covid-19-vaccines|vaccines]] as psychological warfare that deprived the enemy of a valuable weapon, his habitual trolling and baiting of opponents as the tactician who plays with his adversary as a cat plays with a mouse, and his improbable run to the Presidency as the precept of appearing where one is not expected, since the Deep State did not expect him to run, much less win.[449] On that reading the author takes Trump's statement "We caught them all" literally, treating his consistent rhetoric and method as more informative than any single action taken or not yet taken.[449]

449. Badlands Brief — "Understanding the Art of War - Part 1", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/understanding-the-art-of-war-part
## The 2025 counteroffensive thesis

Badlands Media's "Trump's Final Offensive" presents Trump as orchestrating the final phase of a multi-decade counterinsurgency against the [[deep-state|Deep State]], fought not with bullets but with peace, tariffs, legal strikes, and a [[continuity-of-government|Continuity of Government]] framework already in motion.[450] Its central claim is that what the mainstream press reads as chaos is in fact methodical: the collapse of the old guard by design rather than accident, converging on a sovereign global reset driven by law rather than by technocrats or bankers.[450]

On the diplomatic front the article identifies three moves. In April 2025 Trump quietly proposed a peace framework in London that would have Ukraine accept Russian control of Crimea and occupied Donbas in exchange for a full ceasefire, withdrawal of Wagner-linked militias, and reinstatement of gas and grain trade routes through eastern corridors.[450] The New York Times warned it would "reward Russian aggression and fracture European solidarity" and Slate called it "a surrender wrapped in a handshake," while Hungary's [[viktor-orban|Viktor Orbán]], Brazil's [[luiz-inacio-lula-da-silva|Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva]], and a reluctant Germany welcomed the effort.[450] Separately the administration secured the release of eleven American hostages in the first quarter of 2025 — six from Venezuela, two from Afghanistan, one from Russian captivity in the Caucasus, and two through backchannel negotiations with Hamas — using trade leverage, digital surveillance intelligence, and third-party brokers such as Qatar and Turkey rather than airstrikes or public threats.[450] The third was the [[2025-riyadh-middle-east-vision-speech|Riyadh address of May 13, 2025]].[450]

Economically the counteroffensive rests on the [[reciprocal-tariff-doctrine|Reciprocal Tariff Doctrine]] of April 17, 2025, paired with capital-alignment diplomacy and domestic re-shoring measures.[450] Legally it rests on [[executive-order-13818|EO 13818]], [[executive-order-13848|EO 13848]], [[executive-order-13912|EO 13912]], and [[executive-order-14151|EO 14151]], together with [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]]'s restructuring of the [[public-integrity-section|Public Integrity Section]].[450] Financially it points toward [[maga-baby-accounts|MAGA Baby Accounts]], a diminished [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]], and managed engagement with [[brics|BRICS+]] and its [[aurumnet|AurumNet]] settlement ledger.[450] In his inaugural address Trump had promised to "move with purpose and speed to bring back hope, prosperity, safety, and peace for citizens of every race, religion, color, and creed."[450]

450. Badlands Brief — "Trump's Final Offensive", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/trumps-final-offensive

## Deschamps's "master move" theory (December 2023)

In "Vivek, DeSantis, and Trump's Master Move," published December 20, 2023, [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] argued that Trump had been working with [[vivek-ramaswamy|Vivek Ramaswamy]] in some capacity, assisting him in playing the insurgent role Trump himself had played in 2016, and that Ramaswamy's pledge to leave the Colorado primary ballot rather than benefit from Trump's removal was evidence of it.[451] Deschamps also proposed that [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]] was functioning as a double agent for Trump while appearing to serve the [[deep-state|Deep State]], with Trump propping DeSantis up early and then publicly breaking with him in a way that played into the media caricature of Trump as an egotist unable to share the limelight — a maneuver that forced the Deep State to commit its card.[451]

Extending the theory backward, Deschamps wrote that he suspected [[mike-pence|Mike Pence]] and Trump, along with many others, had been orchestrating a carefully worked-out plan to win hearts and minds globally, one designed to expose Deep State assets, the failures of the justice system and media, and what he called the facade of the geopolitical field over the eighty years since the Second World War.[451] He speculated further that [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] might be in on the plan or, more likely, being used by Trump as a prop without his knowledge.[451]

On Deschamps's account, Trump's plan required both Trump and Ramaswamy to be removed from ballots, something only Colorado had achieved at that point; the [[colorado-ballot-disqualification-case|Colorado disqualification]] was anticipated, and indeed needed to happen.[451] Trump, he argued, knew the January 6 litigation would be pursued and wanted it pursued, because it was vital to expose the hijacking of the law and legal system in the United States and worldwide.[451] Deschamps maintained that no crime beyond property damage occurred on January 6 and that Trump knew it, and read the alarm over J6 footage being released on [[rumble|Rumble]] — followed by a series of cyber attacks — as confirmation.[451]

Deschamps further contended that Trump understands what he calls the true rule of law, grounded in free will, God-given rights, contract and trust, and that all of Trump's efforts had been in alignment with those principles.[451] Citing his own News Brief for December 20th, he argued that a righteous actor cannot change a legal system through contempt or vigilante methods — "pay to Caesar what is owed to Caesar" — and that Trump therefore had to exhaust every legal avenue before actions outside the system could be pursued, a long and deliberate process whose purpose was to rouse the people into exercising free will.[451] The larger goal he attributed to Trump and the patriots was to de-tooth the old political theatre system that requires candidates merely to look presidential, and to build toward a moment when the people dissolve what he called the defunct government and restore the Republic.[451]

451. Badlands Brief — "Vivek, DeSantis, and Trump's Master Move", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/vivek-desantis-and-trumps-master

## One-year retrospective on the Mar-a-Lago raid (August 2023)

In August 2023, [[alpha-warrior|Alpha Warrior]] marked one year since the [[mar-a-lago-raid|FBI search]] of Trump's [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]] estate by arguing that the ensuing case against him bore the hallmarks of a politically motivated assault on an opposing leader.[452] Trump, who denies wrongdoing, stands as the only former president ever charged with a crime, and faced a [[2023-trump-classified-documents-indictment|37-count indictment]] alleging he retained sensitive records after leaving office, shared them with unauthorised individuals and obstructed efforts to recover them, including an alleged instruction to his attorney to "hide or destroy" materials sought by subpoena.[452] He and his supporters maintained he had acted within the [[presidential-records-act|Presidential Records Act]] of 1978.[452]

On [[truth-social|Truth Social]] Trump wrote: "I have been indicted, seemingly over the Boxes Hoax. I never thought it possible that such a thing could happen to a former President of the United States, who received far more votes than any sitting President in the History of our Country, and is currently leading, by far, all Candidates, both Democrat and Republican, in Polls of the 2024 Presidential Election. I AM AN INNOCENT MAN!"[452]

452. Badlands Brief — "Unveiling the Mar-a-Lago Raid – 1 Year Delta", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/unveiling-the-mar-a-lago-raid-1-year

## Challenge to the NED-USAID apparatus (2025)

"Two Wings, One Ugly Bird" casts Trump and [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]]'s [[doge|DOGE]] as outsiders to the [[central-intelligence-agency|CIA]] cut-out network who are attempting to remedy some of its excesses, drawing public scrutiny toward it with help from the research of [[mike-benz|Mike Benz]], but facing heavy pushback both legally and in the narrative war.[453] On March 5, 2025 the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] ruled 5-4 against Trump's bid to freeze $2 billion in [[usaid|USAID]] payments, restoring grants to the [[national-democratic-institute|NDI]], the [[international-republican-institute|IRI]] and their sister organisations.[453] The article also observes that before Trump, a Republican presidential aspirant effectively had to be IRI-approved, and concludes that he will be judged by whether he overcomes and reforms the apparatus — a job the author compares to the still-pending release of Epstein and JFK files.[453]

453. Badlands Brief — "Two Wings, One Ugly Bird", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/two-wings-one-ugly-bird

## The dealmaker and spotlight readings of the 2024 cabinet (Carlson)

In "Where the Art of the Deal Meets the Spotlight" (December 3, 2024), [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] defended Trump against a [[maga-coalition|MAGA]] base alarmed by his second-term cabinet nominations, arguing that his return to office with majorities in [[congress|Congress]] and the [[united-states-senate|Senate]] was not luck and that there was "a method to his madness."[454]

Carlson read Trump's picks through [[the-art-of-the-deal|The Art of the Deal]]: a good deal leaves all parties satisfied, which requires concessions and setting pride aside, so some appointments were the price of keeping [[rino|RINO]] and [[neoconservatives|NeoCon]] legislators cooperative during the roughly two years his majorities were assured.[454] He suggested Trump's most loyal supporters — including those jailed rather than testify against him — were being kept out of the cabinet temporarily, possibly as part of that bargain, and that some nominees were placeholders or were never intended to be confirmed.[454]

A second thesis held that certain picks were spotlights, exposing a person or an office to public judgment as, in Carlson's account, Trump had done with [[anthony-fauci|Anthony Fauci]] during the Covid pandemic.[454] Carlson also defended Trump's handling of Covid — the fifteen-day lockdown, the devolution of decisions to the states, and [[operation-warp-speed|Operation Warp Speed]], which he likened to the torpedo maneuver in *The Hunt for Red October* — and noted that Trump had maneuvered through a rigged election system, a corrupt political system and assassination attempts on his way back to the Oval Office.[454] See [[2024-trump-cabinet-selection-controversy]].[454]

454. Badlands Brief — "Where the Art of the Deal Meets the Spotlight", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/where-the-art-of-the-deal-meets-the

## Lange's counterinsurgency framing (December 2022)

In "What's Taking So Long?" (December 28, 2022), [[joe-lange|Joe Lange]] argued that a military plan was in place long before Trump announced he was running for president, and that Trump became a wartime president the moment he was sworn in, fighting an unconventional war whose main battlefield is the media.[455] Lange presented Trump's record judicial appointments — filling vacancies he attributes to obstruction during the [[barack-obama|Obama]] years — as the central move of the strategy, intended to end the [[two-tier-justice-system|two-tier justice system]] and to have the Constitution restored by the justice sector rather than by Trump personally.[455]

Measuring Trump against the [[counterinsurgency-guide|Counterinsurgency Guide]]'s components, Lange credited him with building popular support (citing a ten-million-vote increase at re-election), with the [[make-america-great-again|MAGA]] political strategy that forced opponents to defend the Obama-Biden record, with an economic record of job creation, energy independence, record tax cuts and deregulation, and with establishment-phase measures including cancelled trade deals, tariffs on China and Europe, the border wall, expanded ICE enforcement, the Remain in Mexico policy and travel restrictions.[455] On foreign policy he credited Trump with peace with North Korea, the [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]], ending the wars in Afghanistan and Syria, destroying ISIS and rebuilding the military.[455]

Lange also argued that Trump waged war on the press by branding it "Fake News" and speaking directly to the public over it, that Trump set up [[devolution|Devolution]] and [[continuity-of-government|continuity of government]] before leaving office so the plan would continue under [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], and that Trump deliberately walked away and allowed the 2020 election to be taken as part of the strategy.[455] He contended Trump welcomes being spied on, framed, impeached, investigated and raided because it sets legal precedents ensuring no president is above the law, and described the [[mar-a-lago-raid|Mar-a-Lago raid]] as an operation Trump himself announced while playing the victim for optical advantage.[455]

455. Badlands Brief — "What's Taking So Long?", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/whats-taking-so-long

## "Who Controls Whom?" — three audiences and the Netanyahu question

Badlands contributor [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] argued in August 2024 that Trump and the patriots speak constantly to three distinct audiences — the awake, the asleep, and the [[deep-state|Deep State]] — and that readers bear the responsibility of determining which messages are meant for them and which are aimed at opponents.[456] Applying this to Trump's warnings of a stock market crash in early August 2024, Carlson maintained that Trump knew perfectly well the market had not crashed — it finished the week up roughly 3% — and that the alarm was propaganda directed at sleeping normies, intended to make them associate economic collapse with [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]]'s elevation to the ticket.[456] Carlson compared the tactic to the U.S. Army's inflatable decoy tanks used to fool the Nazis, and argued the timing three months before the election was deliberate because voters decide on the state of their wallets and have short memories.[456]

Carlson also used [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]]'s July 2024 trip to argue that Trump is not Israel's puppet: Netanyahu travelled to [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]] as the final stop of the visit, and Trump greeted him with a handshake that pulled the prime minister off balance — "not how one interacts with their master."[456] He suggested Netanyahu "doesn't do anything without Trump's permission," linked the visit to the [[assassination-of-ismail-haniyeh|killing of the Hamas leader in Tehran]] five days later, and proposed that Trump had manoeuvred the Deep State into replacing [[joe-biden|Biden]] with Harris precisely because Harris was at odds with Netanyahu, deepening internal conflict within the [[deep-state-cabal|Deep State Cabal]].[456] Carlson noted the incoherence of critics who call Trump both a power-hungry dictator and a controlled puppet — "you can't say he is both the puppet and the puppet master" — and concluded that Trump and the patriots are in control while the Deep State is in panic mode.[456]

456. Badlands Brief — "Who Controls Whom?", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/who-controls-whom

## Role in the Epstein prosecutions (January 2024 commentary)

A January 2024 Badlands feature by [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] observed that Trump worked with the prosecution team that convicted [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]] the first time, in 2008, and that Trump was president when Epstein was arrested again in 2019 and when [[ghislaine-maxwell|Ghislaine Maxwell]] was arrested a year later.[457]

457. Badlands Brief — "Who's the U-Boat Commander?", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/whos-the-u-boat-commander

## The 2019 Afghanistan history remarks

"Who Is The Real Enemy?" builds much of its argument around remarks Trump made after a January 2019 Cabinet meeting, in which he said Russia used to be the Soviet Union, that "Afghanistan made it Russia, because they went bankrupt fighting in Afghanistan," that "the reason Russia was in Afghanistan was because terrorists were going into Russia," and that "they were right to be there."[458] The [[washington-post|Washington Post]] called it a "bizarre history lesson" and asserted the claim about terrorists was "simply not true," arguing the Soviets intervened to prop up communism abroad and that Trump — who was using the anecdote to argue for withdrawal from Afghanistan and Syria — was straining for causation.[458]

Badlands Media argues Trump is a master of narrative who deliberately invites media attacks to draw attention to a subject, wording statements so the press takes the bait, and that repeated mentions of "Russia" functioned as a siren call to a press corps still invested in Russian collusion after [[robert-mueller|Robert Mueller]]'s exoneration and Trump's first impeachment.[458] On the substance, the article holds that the war drained Soviet resources and produced the bankruptcy that preceded the union's breakup — and that Trump was drawing the parallel to a United States around $35 trillion in debt after its own twenty-year Afghan war.[458]

The article frames Trump's central strategy as money flow disruption: from day one he focused on turning off the enemy's money spigots, on the principle that money equals power and cutting off the money leaves the enemy powerless.[458] It also cites [[hamid-karzai|Hamid Karzai]] noting Trump's disclosure that America had paid $35 billion to [[pakistan|Pakistan]] over nineteen years while that country sheltered the [[taliban|Taliban]].[458] Badlands Media contends Trump declassified extensively, knows what has been hidden, and that the "terrorists" he referred to were the [[central-intelligence-agency|CIA]] — which is why he casts the media as the enemy of the people.[458]

458. Badlands Brief — "Who Is The Real Enemy?", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/who-is-the-real-enemy

## "Wilted Ivy" and the asset-seizure thesis

Badlands Media's May 2025 essay "Wilted Ivy" credits Trump with quietly building the legal machinery behind what it calls a controlled demolition of captured institutions.[459] It states that while the corporate press covered "meaningless scandals and theatrical distractions," Trump signed [[executive-order-13818|EO 13818]] in December 2017 and [[executive-order-13848|EO 13848]] in September 2018, creating authority to freeze and seize assets tied to corruption, human rights abuse and election interference.[459] The article writes that he "didn't just tweet about draining the swamp" but "codified the tools to drain it, then waited for the right time to activate them," letting the system reach "peak arrogance" before the trap sprang shut in 2023-2024.[459]

The essay quotes his 2019 UN General Assembly line, "the future does not belong to globalists. The future belongs to patriots," and asks whether he "merely foresaw this collapse, or quietly engineered the opportunity for sovereign reclamation," citing the sequence of executive orders, public awakenings, asset-manager realignment and forced liquidations at [[harvard-university|Harvard]] as evidence of careful timing.[459]

459. Badlands Brief — "Wilted Ivy", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/wilted-ivy

## Call for a 21st-century Glass-Steagall

[[matthew-ehret|Matthew Ehret]] notes that at the very beginning of Trump's first term the new president called for a 21st-century [[glass-steagall|Glass-Steagall]], saying, "We support reinstating the Glass-Steagall Act of 1933, which prohibits commercial banks from engaging in high-risk investment."[460] According to Ehret, the deep state in play at that time crushed any further discussion of the topic, and Trump was unable to advance the program beyond mere words.[460]

460. Badlands Brief — "Why America Needs to Break Wall Street's Kneecaps", URL: https://badlands.substack.com/p/why-america-needs-to-break-wall-streets
